# Zenith2 normative statement catalogue

Generated by the session coverage checker (`check.mjs docs`) from the tagged bullets of the Architecture, Rendering, Scripting, Contracts and Budgets document families. 2154 statements. Do not edit by hand.

| Z2-N-ARCH-001 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture.md`:11 | Zenith2 Architecture › Purpose and scope › What this document binds | Zenith2 code conforms to this document family (this entry document and every file under `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture/`); a ticket, design document or code change that contradicts it is reported as a contradiction and changes nothing that picks a side until the document is revised in the same diff as the change (Zenith2/Docs/Contracts.md → "Review and change management"). |
| Z2-N-ARCH-002 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture.md`:12 | Zenith2 Architecture › Purpose and scope › What this document binds | A change to any contract, API, format or rule specified here edits the affected section of this document family in the same diff, and a new or changed ruling is appended to `Zenith2/Docs/DecisionLog.md` in that diff with an explicit `Z2-D-NNN` (Z2-D-085, Z2-D-097). |
| Z2-N-ARCH-003 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture.md`:51 | Zenith2 Architecture › Purpose and scope › Scope | No engine library implements a capability classified excluded above, and no engine library implements quest, dialogue, inventory or interaction rules; those exist as Luau gameplay modules inside `Games/OpenWorldSlice/` (Z2-D-077). |
| Z2-N-ARCH-004 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture.md`:52 | Zenith2 Architecture › Purpose and scope › Scope | Deferred capabilities are designed now so that their stage adds code without changing the Stage 1–6 contracts: render views keep a stereo view kind reserved (Zenith2/Docs/Rendering.md → "Render views"), input goes through actions and contexts, and simulation is fixed-tick with replayable commands and network IDs (§ "Multiplayer foundations"). |
| Z2-N-ARCH-005 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture.md`:102 | Zenith2 Architecture › Purpose and scope › Assumed types | A type this family uses and does not declare is either declared by Zenith2/Docs/Contracts.md or listed in the table "Assumed types" with the design document that declares it, and the owning design document adopts the name and semantics given there or raises a contradiction. |
| Z2-N-ARCH-021 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture.md`:273 | Zenith2 Architecture › Repository layout › Ownership per directory | Every compiled-language source under `Engine/`, `Player/`, `Tools/`, `Benchmarks/` and `Tests/` belongs to exactly one target by its directory (Architecture/BuildSystem.md → "The zenith_add_library helper"), and the `SourceLayout` gate step fails on a source in a directory no target globs. |
| Z2-N-ARCH-022 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture.md`:274 | Zenith2 Architecture › Repository layout › Ownership per directory | Each engine library is one directory directly under `Engine/` whose name equals its CMake target, and a library is never nested inside another library's directory; the one exception is `ZenithPlayer`, whose directory is `Zenith2/Player/` (Architecture/BuildSystem.md → "The zenith_add_library helper"). |
| Z2-N-ARCH-023 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture.md`:275 | Zenith2 Architecture › Repository layout › Ownership per directory | A library's public API is the set of headers at its directory root; everything else is under `Internal/`, platform code under `Internal/Windows/`, `Internal/Android/` or `Internal/Linux/`, and tests under `Tests/` (Zenith2/Docs/Contracts.md → "API boundary"). |
| Z2-N-ARCH-024 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture.md`:276 | Zenith2 Architecture › Repository layout › Ownership per directory | Games live only under `Zenith2/Games/<Name>/`, in this repository, and no compiled-language source is authored or tracked under `Games/<Name>/` (Z2-D-002); the one generated TU of each game lives under `out/` (bullet 206). |
| Z2-N-ARCH-025 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture.md`:277 | Zenith2 Architecture › Repository layout › Ownership per directory | Everything a build, cook, test or gate step produces is written under `Zenith2/out/` (or the `linux-tsan` tree under `$HOME/.zenith2/out/`), which the nested `Zenith2/.gitignore` ignores, so a gate run never changes the tracked or untracked tree outside `out/`. |
| Z2-N-ARCH-026 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture.md`:278 | Zenith2 Architecture › Repository layout › Ownership per directory | `Zenith2/Docs/Board/` holds Vault records only; no document, script or tool output is written there by anything other than the Vault writer. |
| Z2-N-ARCH-027 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture.md`:279 | Zenith2 Architecture › Repository layout › Ownership per directory | `Zenith2/Tools/Gates/**` and `Zenith2/CLAUDE.md` are protected paths edited only by PROC `needs-human` tickets; ordinary tickets run gate steps by name and never edit them. |
| Z2-N-ARCH-028 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture.md`:280 | Zenith2 Architecture › Repository layout › Ownership per directory | Engine assets that every game needs (default configuration, engine shaders' cooked blobs, fallback textures) are produced by the cook from `Zenith2/Engine/*` libraries' declared content and `Zenith2/Shaders/`, never copied into a game's directory. |
| Z2-N-ARCH-029 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture.md`:298 | Zenith2 Architecture › Repository layout › Games in the repository | Every game under `Games/` builds in every preset its `game.json` platforms allow on every change, because every game is part of the one CMake tree; a change that breaks any game's build fails the Build step. |
| Z2-N-ARCH-071 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture.md`:384 | Zenith2 Architecture › Library dependency graph › Declared direct dependencies | Every library's `zenith_add_library(… DEPENDS …)` lists exactly the declared direct dependencies of the table above, and a change to a library's dependencies edits the table in the same diff. |
| Z2-N-ARCH-072 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture.md`:385 | Zenith2 Architecture › Library dependency graph › Declared direct dependencies | The declared graph is acyclic, and every edge points from a higher layer to a strictly lower layer. |
| Z2-N-ARCH-073 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture.md`:386 | Zenith2 Architecture › Library dependency graph › Declared direct dependencies | No engine library, module or backend depends on a tool, a game, the Player or a test target, and no library below `ZenithRuntime` depends on a module. |
| Z2-N-ARCH-074 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture.md`:387 | Zenith2 Architecture › Library dependency graph › Declared direct dependencies | A module may depend on another module only as the table declares (AI on Physics, Animation on Physics, Cinematics on Animation), and a game selecting a module selects its module dependencies too (§ "Engine modules and game projects"). |
| Z2-N-ARCH-075 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture.md`:388 | Zenith2 Architecture › Library dependency graph › Declared direct dependencies | A backend depends on its API library and on libraries at or below the API's layer, never on another backend or a sibling API's backend; it may also depend on one engine library of its own family that depends only on the family's API library and libraries at or below the API's layer, sits strictly between the API's layer and the backend's, is never itself a backend (no `BACKEND_OF`), and is linked into a game only through that backend's `DEPENDS` (and into tests through `TEST_DEPENDS`), as `ZenithOnline_SteamCore` and `ZenithOnline_GooglePlayCore` are. |
| Z2-N-ARCH-076 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture.md`:392 | Zenith2 Architecture › Library dependency graph › Flux never sees Scene | `ZenithFlux` declares no dependency on, includes no header of, and links no symbol of `ZenithScene`, `ZenithECS`, `ZenithScript`, `ZenithSave`, `ZenithRuntime` or any module; `ZenithRuntime` extracts render state into `Flux_RenderWorld` and modules contribute through registered extractors (Architecture/CPUFrameGraph.md → "Render extraction and pipelining"). |
| Z2-N-ARCH-077 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture.md`:393 | Zenith2 Architecture › Library dependency graph › Flux never sees Scene | `ZenithRHI` and its backends know nothing of the render graph's passes, of assets, or of any simulation library; they expose devices, resources, queues and command recording to `ZenithFlux`. |
| Z2-N-ARCH-078 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture.md`:397 | Zenith2 Architecture › Library dependency graph › Upward notifications | A lower library that needs to notify a higher one declares an interface that the higher library implements and the composition root passes down — `Zenith_LifecycleListener` (ZenithScene), `Zenith_MemoryConsumer` (ZenithCore), the asset loader interface (ZenithAssets, implemented by ZenithFlux), `Zenith_ModuleInstance` (ZenithRuntime) — and never stores a pointer into a higher library's code otherwise (Zenith2/Docs/Contracts.md → "Layering DAG"). |
| Z2-N-ARCH-079 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture.md`:419 | Zenith2 Architecture › Library dependency graph › What libgraph enforces | The rules of the table "What libgraph enforces" marked fail are hard failures of the `Libgraph` gate step (Z2-D-007, Z2-D-042), and a rule exception exists only as an allowlist entry in a protected gate file with a decision ID. |
| Z2-N-ARCH-080 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture.md`:420 | Zenith2 Architecture › Library dependency graph › What libgraph enforces | Libgraph writes the swap-contract report for every backend family to `${ZENITH_OUT}/gates/libgraph/swap_<family>.json` on every run, and a backend whose consumers use a symbol or type outside the family's API library fails the swap-contract check. |
| Z2-N-ARCH-081 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture.md`:432 | Zenith2 Architecture › Library dependency graph › Generated code attribution | Generated code is attributed to the target whose build produces it, recorded in `declared_deps.json`, and libgraph counts its includes as that target's edges (Zenith2/Docs/Contracts.md → "Layering DAG"); the symbol rule for the INTERFACE target `ZenithShadersGenerated` is Architecture/BuildSystem.md bullet 243. |

| Z2-N-ARCH-083 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture.md`:378 | Zenith2 Architecture › Library dependency graph › Declared direct dependencies | Generated shader headers use only ZenithCore types, ZenithRHI descriptor and view value types and ZenithVertexFormat layout-table types, so `ZenithShadersGenerated` has no dependency above layer 3 and every library including a generated header declares `ZenithShadersGenerated`. |

| Z2-N-ARCH-085 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture.md`:380 | Zenith2 Architecture › Library dependency graph › Declared direct dependencies | The Luau-free binding ABI is the public header `ZenithReflect/Zenith_ScriptBinding.h`; a library or module that owns script bindings depends on `ZenithReflect` for them and never on `ZenithScript`, and the bindings of libraries below `ZenithReflect` live in `ZenithRuntime/Internal/ScriptBindings/` (Zenith2/Docs/Scripting.md → "Modules, bindings and generated types"). |
| Z2-N-ARCH-086 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture.md`:382 | Zenith2 Architecture › Library dependency graph › Declared direct dependencies | `ZenithPathTracer` is linked by render-test and physical-accuracy test targets alone and never appears in any Player's link closure; the Shipping link-closure check covers it as a tool target (Architecture/BuildSystem.md → "The zenith_add_library helper"). |
| Z2-N-ARCH-087 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture.md`:381 | Zenith2 Architecture › Library dependency graph › Declared direct dependencies | `ZenithDocument` performs no host file I/O: every document read and write goes through its `Zenith_DocumentStorage` interface, and the host-filesystem implementation is the tool library `ZenithDocumentHost`, which the editor, the cook and `ZenithNewGameScaffold` link and no Player links, so a runtime target that links `ZenithDocument` gains no path outside the virtual filesystem (Architecture/PlatformLayer.md bullet 923). |

A selection candidate that a harness measures before its ruling has an entry with `owner: "benchmark"` (linked into benchmark targets alone, Architecture/BuildSystem.md → "The manifest dependencies section"), and the ticket that implements the ruling moves the admitted candidate's `owner` to the library and its `usage` to `runtime` in the same diff; a dependency that does not build with exceptions and RTTI off is not admitted unless a recorded decision amends ForbiddenCheck rule F17 for it (Zenith2/Docs/Contracts.md → "ForbiddenCheck rules"). |

| Z2-N-ARCH-125 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture.md`:496 | Zenith2 Architecture › Third-party dependencies › Dependencies admitted by open selections | The SHIP licence inventory is generated from the manifest's `licence` fields and shipped with every packaged build; a dependency whose licence the inventory cannot state blocks packaging. |

| Z2-N-ARCH-152 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture/BuildSystem.md`:14 | Architecture — Build system and presets › Settled rules governing the build | Source files are discovered by `file(GLOB_RECURSE … CONFIGURE_DEPENDS)` inside the shared helpers; no `CMakeLists.txt` in the tree lists a source file by name. |

| Z2-N-ARCH-155 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture/BuildSystem.md`:17 | Architecture — Build system and presets › Settled rules governing the build | Every backend (ECS, RHI, physics, compression, online services) is its own library target, and the consumer selects a backend by linking it; no preprocessor symbol selects a backend. |
| Z2-N-ARCH-156 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture/BuildSystem.md`:60 | Architecture — Build system and presets › Top-level tree and helper modules | `zenith_add_subdirectories(<Dir> [GROUPS <Group>...])` globs `<Dir>/*/CMakeLists.txt` (and `<Dir>/CMakeLists.txt` when present) and, for each named group, `<Dir>/<Group>/*/CMakeLists.txt`, with `CONFIGURE_DEPENDS`, merges and sorts the result by byte order, and calls `add_subdirectory` in that order, so adding a library, harness or game directory never edits a parent `CMakeLists.txt`; a `CMakeLists.txt` directly inside a group directory is a configure error, `zenith_add_subdirectories(<Dir>): <Dir>/<Group> is a group directory and holds no CMakeLists.txt`. |

| Z2-N-ARCH-159 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture/BuildSystem.md`:66 | Architecture — Build system and presets › Top-level tree and helper modules › Checkout hash and output root | The checkout hash is the first 16 lower-case hexadecimal digits of the SHA-256 of the checkout root's absolute path, lower-cased, with `\` replaced by `/` and no trailing slash, encoded as UTF-8. |
| Z2-N-ARCH-160 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture/BuildSystem.md`:68 | Architecture — Build system and presets › Top-level tree and helper modules › Checkout hash and output root | Every build tree, report, generated file and cooked output lives under `Zenith2/out/<checkout-hash>/` (Windows presets) or `$HOME/.zenith2/out/<checkout-hash>/` (the `linux-tsan` preset), and nothing a build or gate step produces is written anywhere else inside the checkout. |
| Z2-N-ARCH-161 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture/BuildSystem.md`:163 | Architecture — Build system and presets › The zenith_add_library helper › Directory rules and globbing | Public headers of a library sit directly in the library's root directory; a subdirectory of a library other than `Internal/` and `Tests/` that contains a compiled-language source is a configure error (non-source directories such as the Player's `GradleTemplate/` are ignored by the glob), `zenith_add_library(<Name>): directory '<dir>' is not allowed; public headers live at the library root, private code under Internal/`. One signposted carve-out exists: ZenithCore's three per-platform threading headers `Platform/Windows/Zenith_PlatformThreading.h`, `Platform/Android/Zenith_PlatformThreading.h` and `Platform/Linux/Zenith_PlatformThreading.h`, and no other file under `Platform/` (`Zenith2/Docs/Contracts.md` → "Library directory layout" [Z2-N-CONTRACT-583], [Z2-N-CONTRACT-584]; Z2-D-141). |
| Z2-N-ARCH-162 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture/BuildSystem.md`:164 | Architecture — Build system and presets › The zenith_add_library helper › Directory rules and globbing | A directory named `Windows`, `Android` or `Linux` containing a compiled-language source anywhere other than directly below `Internal/` is a configure error, and platform-specific code lives only under `Internal/Windows/`, `Internal/Android/` or `Internal/Linux/`, except ZenithCore's three per-platform threading headers under `Platform/<Platform>/` (the carve-out of [Z2-N-ARCH-161], [Z2-N-CONTRACT-583]), for which `zenith_add_library()` adds `${CMAKE_CURRENT_SOURCE_DIR}/Platform/${CMAKE_SYSTEM_NAME}` to ZenithCore's public include directories; a `Tests/` tree therefore has no platform partition, and test code that needs a platform API calls ZenithTestSupport's child-process and own-executable facility (`Zenith_RunChildTestCase`, `Zenith_TestProcess`, `Zenith_GetTestExecutablePath` in `ZenithCore/Tests/Support/Zenith_TestProcess.h`), whose platform bodies are ordinary ZenithCore `Internal/<Platform>/` files (ruling X349 (a); Zenith2/Docs/Design/CORE/Memory.md → "Test child processes"). |
| Z2-N-ARCH-163 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture/BuildSystem.md`:165 | Architecture — Build system and presets › The zenith_add_library helper › Directory rules and globbing | Test code lives only under a library's `Tests/` directory (or an integration suite under `Zenith2/Tests/`), and nothing under `Tests/` is ever compiled into the library target itself. |
| Z2-N-ARCH-164 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture/BuildSystem.md`:166 | Architecture — Build system and presets › The zenith_add_library helper › Directory rules and globbing | The include root of every engine library is `Zenith2/Engine`, so every include of an engine header reads `#include "<Library>/<Header>.h"`, except the bare `#include "Zenith_PlatformThreading.h"` of ZenithCore's `Zenith_Mutex.h`, `Zenith_ConditionVariable.h` and `Zenith_Semaphore.h`, resolved through the per-platform include directory of [Z2-N-ARCH-162] (the signposted carve-out of `Zenith2/Docs/Contracts.md` [Z2-N-CONTRACT-583], [Z2-N-CONTRACT-584]); tool libraries use `Zenith2/Tools` the same way, and the Player library uses `Zenith2/` so its headers read `#include "Player/<Header>.h"`. |
| Z2-N-ARCH-165 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture/BuildSystem.md`:167 | Architecture — Build system and presets › The zenith_add_library helper › Directory rules and globbing | A third-party header is included only from files under the owning library's `Internal/` directory; libgraph's leakage rule and `ForbiddenCheck` both fail an include of a manifest dependency's header from any other file. |
| Z2-N-ARCH-166 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture/BuildSystem.md`:191 | Architecture — Build system and presets › The zenith_add_library helper › Target creation steps | A library target is always `STATIC`; no Zenith2 engine, module, backend or Player library is ever built as a shared library, and Android's single shared object is the game executable produced by `zenith_add_game`. |
| Z2-N-ARCH-167 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture/BuildSystem.md`:192 | Architecture — Build system and presets › The zenith_add_library helper › Target creation steps | `DEPENDS` lists exactly the library's direct dependencies: a library whose files include a header of, or reference a symbol of, library X names X in `DEPENDS` even when X is reachable transitively, and libgraph fails a used-but-undeclared edge. |
| Z2-N-ARCH-168 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture/BuildSystem.md`:193 | Architecture — Build system and presets › The zenith_add_library helper › Target creation steps | A `KIND MODULE` library names `ZenithRuntime` in `DEPENDS` and exports exactly two entry points, `Zenith_Module_<MODULE_NAME>_RegisterTypes` and `Zenith_Module_<MODULE_NAME>_Start`, from its root header `Zenith_Module_<MODULE_NAME>.h`, with the signatures given in Architecture.md → "Engine modules and game projects". |
| Z2-N-ARCH-169 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture/BuildSystem.md`:194 | Architecture — Build system and presets › The zenith_add_library helper › Target creation steps | A `KIND BACKEND` library names its `BACKEND_OF` library in `DEPENDS`, declares the `FACTORY` function in a public root header named `Zenith_<Family>_<BACKEND_NAME>.h`, and exports no other public function; the API library itself exports no factory. |
| Z2-N-ARCH-170 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture/BuildSystem.md`:195 | Architecture — Build system and presets › The zenith_add_library helper › Target creation steps | `ZenithTestSupport`, built by the helper from `Engine/ZenithCore/Tests/Support/**`, is linked by test, conformance, fuzz, benchmark and integration-suite targets and by nothing else; `TestSeparationCheck` fails when any other target links it. |
| Z2-N-ARCH-171 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture/BuildSystem.md`:250 | Architecture — Build system and presets › The zenith_add_library helper › The ZENITH_SUPPORTED matrix | Every `zenith_add_library` and `zenith_add_tool` call states its matrix through `SUPPORTED` or accepts its kind's default, and the `CompileCoverage` gate step checks every source against the matrix its target declares, narrowed by its `Internal/<Platform>` directory; a library whose record carries `unavailable` is reported as declared-but-unavailable, naming the absent entry, instead of failing. |
| Z2-N-ARCH-172 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture/BuildSystem.md`:251 | Architecture — Build system and presets › The zenith_add_library helper › The ZENITH_SUPPORTED matrix | A target that depends on a library absent from the current preset is itself absent from that preset; a mismatch is a configure error, `zenith: <Target> is supported in <preset> but depends on <Library>, which is not`. A `TEST_DEPENDS` entry is not such a dependency: `<Name>_Tests` and `<Name>_Conformance` link the entries the preset supports, skip the others and define `ZENITH_TEST_LINKS_<TARGET>` to `1` or `0` for each (argument row `TEST_DEPENDS`), so a library's tests exist in every preset its library does. |
| Z2-N-ARCH-173 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture/BuildSystem.md`:252 | Architecture — Build system and presets › The zenith_add_library helper › The ZENITH_SUPPORTED matrix | GPU libraries (RHI, its backends, Flux, Runtime, Player and every module) exclude `linux-tsan`, so the `linux-tsan` tree contains no GPU library, window system or audio device code. |
| Z2-N-ARCH-174 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture/BuildSystem.md`:256 | Architecture — Build system and presets › The zenith_add_library helper › Exceptions and the firewall toggle | Every Zenith2 target compiles with exceptions disabled: MSVC `/EHs-c- /D_HAS_EXCEPTIONS=0`, clang `-fno-exceptions`. |
| Z2-N-ARCH-175 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture/BuildSystem.md`:257 | Architecture — Build system and presets › The zenith_add_library helper › Exceptions and the firewall toggle | `EXCEPTIONS INTERNAL` is accepted for `ZenithScript` and `ZenithScriptCompiler` alone (the two Luau firewall libraries, Zenith2/Docs/Scripting.md → "Luau integration and the exception firewall"); any other library passing it fails the configure with `zenith_add_library(<Name>): EXCEPTIONS INTERNAL is reserved for ZenithScript and ZenithScriptCompiler (Z2-D-017)`. |
| Z2-N-ARCH-176 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture/BuildSystem.md`:258 | Architecture — Build system and presets › The zenith_add_library helper › Exceptions and the firewall toggle | The `ZenithScript_EH` and `ZenithScriptCompiler_EH` object libraries compile with `/EHs` on MSVC — never `/EHsc` — and with `-fexceptions` on clang, and the Luau third-party targets compile with the same flag: exactly the `targets` elements of the manifest entry `luau` that carry `"exceptions": true` (`Luau.VM`, `Luau.Ast`, `Luau.Compiler`, `Luau.CodeGen` and every further Luau target the entry lists, § "The manifest dependencies section"), which is the set Zenith2/Docs/Scripting.md → "Exception-enabled target list" enumerates. |
| Z2-N-ARCH-177 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture/BuildSystem.md`:259 | Architecture — Build system and presets › The zenith_add_library helper › Exceptions and the firewall toggle | `ForbiddenCheck` reads the CMake File API compile groups and fails when `/EHs`, `/EHsc`, `/EHa` or `-fexceptions` appears on any compile group other than `ZenithScript_EH`, `ZenithScriptCompiler_EH` and the Luau targets whose manifest `targets` element carries `"exceptions": true` ([Z2-N-ARCH-176]). |
| Z2-N-ARCH-178 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture/BuildSystem.md`:276 | Architecture — Build system and presets › The zenith_add_library helper › Test, conformance and fuzz targets | A backend family's conformance executable links every backend of the family that the preset supports, and runs every conformance scenario once per backend; a backend that fails a scenario fails the test. |
| Z2-N-ARCH-179 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture/BuildSystem.md`:278 | Architecture — Build system and presets › The zenith_add_library helper › Test, conformance and fuzz targets | Each doctest `TEST_CASE` is registered as its own CTest test through doctest's CTest discovery, and conformance cases once per backend, named `<doctest name>[<Backend>]` (Zenith2/Docs/Contracts.md → "Tests and code hygiene gates"); the test main excludes the `z2-death-*`, `z2-sanitizer-defect`, `z2-selftest`, `z2-fixture-setup`, `z2-*-cooked` and `z2-acceptance-*` suites from the default run and from discovery unless a suite or case is named (`z2-fixture-setup` and `z2-*-cooked` are registered as fixture entries, [Z2-N-ARCH-257]; each `z2-acceptance-<area>` suite is registered as one CTest entry for stage acceptance only, which `Run-Committed.ps1 -Acceptance -Stage N` (`Zenith2Acceptance`) runs and the `Test` step never does, for example CONTENT's `z2-acceptance-content`, `Zenith2/Docs/Design/CONTENT/Tests.md`), and the test registry `CMake/Tests/ZenithCMakeTests.cmake` registers each case of those suites as its own CTest entry with the pass condition of Zenith2/Docs/Contracts.md → "Tests and code hygiene gates" (table "Death and sanitizer-defect tests") as `PASS_REGULAR_EXPRESSION`: `z2-death-assert`, and any `z2-death-check` case whose check is compiled only with `ZENITH_ASSERTS_ENABLED=1`, only in the asserts-enabled configurations (`Debug`, `Sanitize`, `Hwasan`), and `z2-sanitizer-defect` only when `ZENITH_SANITIZER` is set. |
| Z2-N-ARCH-180 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture/BuildSystem.md`:287 | Architecture — Build system and presets › The zenith_add_library helper › Test, conformance and fuzz targets | On Android presets the test and conformance executables are built (so `CompileCoverage` covers them) and are never registered with CTest; on-device test runs belong to acceptance and `needs-human` device tickets, and every such run pushes the committed fixture trees, the built shader containers and the target's generated test inputs to the target's device directories before it starts ([Z2-N-ARCH-250], [Z2-N-ARCH-254]; `CMake/ZenithPushTestApkFiles.cmake`, `Zenith2/Docs/Design/MOD/GameBuild.md` → "Test APK device files"), which is the one device-file rule a device ticket cites. |
| Z2-N-ARCH-181 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture/BuildSystem.md`:288 | Architecture — Build system and presets › The zenith_add_library helper › Test, conformance and fuzz targets | Test, conformance, fuzz, benchmark and integration-suite targets never enter the `Shipping` configuration's build presets (`win-shipping`, `android-arm64-shipping`); the `ShippingTest` configuration, which builds test, conformance and integration-suite targets with Shipping's code ([Z2-N-ARCH-231]), is never a Player's: no build preset that names `zenith_players` uses it, and neither the Shipping link closure ([Z2-N-ARCH-240]) nor `TestSeparationCheck` reads it; and `TestSeparationCheck` fails when a shipping Player's link closure contains any of them or `ZenithTestSupport`. |
| Z2-N-ARCH-182 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture/BuildSystem.md`:348 | Architecture — Build system and presets › The zenith_add_library helper › The declared_deps.json record | `declared_deps.json` is deterministic: arrays sorted by byte order of `name` (and of `file` for `generated`), keys in the order shown, four-space indentation, LF line endings, and the file is rewritten only when its bytes change, so an unchanged configure never touches its timestamp. |
| Z2-N-ARCH-183 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture/BuildSystem.md`:349 | Architecture — Build system and presets › The zenith_add_library helper › The declared_deps.json record | The record contains every declaration of the tree regardless of which preset is being configured, so every preset writes identical bytes; `supported` records where each target exists. |
| Z2-N-ARCH-184 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture/BuildSystem.md`:350 | Architecture — Build system and presets › The zenith_add_library helper › The declared_deps.json record | Every generated file consumed by a compile is listed under `generated` with the library it is attributed to (the library whose helper call produced it: `ZenithShadersGenerated` for shader headers, the declaring library for `GENERATES` files), and libgraph attributes the file's includes to that library. |
| Z2-N-ARCH-185 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture/BuildSystem.md`:372 | Architecture — Build system and presets › Compile and link flags › MSVC flags for the win and win-asan presets | `zenith_apply_flags` applies the warning sets of `CMake/ZenithWarnings.cmake` (warnings as errors, specified in Zenith2/Docs/Contracts.md → "Tests and code hygiene gates") to every Zenith2 target, and third-party headers are included as system headers so their warnings never apply. |
| Z2-N-ARCH-186 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture/BuildSystem.md`:373 | Architecture — Build system and presets › Compile and link flags › MSVC flags for the win and win-asan presets | The `Debug` configuration of the `win` preset (the `win-debug` build preset) always compiles with `/Gy-` and never with `/ZI`, so libgraph's symbol stage reuses the `win-debug` build and no fourth build flavour exists. |
| Z2-N-ARCH-187 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture/BuildSystem.md`:374 | Architecture — Build system and presets › Compile and link flags › MSVC flags for the win and win-asan presets | MSVC debug information is `/Z7` in every configuration, because sccache does not cache compiles that write a shared PDB (`/Zi`); libgraph detects function-level linking from the File API's compile fragments, never from the debug-information flag. |
| Z2-N-ARCH-188 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture/BuildSystem.md`:375 | Architecture — Build system and presets › Compile and link flags › MSVC flags for the win and win-asan presets | No Zenith2 target enables RTTI (`/GR-`, `-fno-rtti`) or floating-point contraction (`/fp:precise` without `/fp:contract`; clang `-ffp-contract=off`), because the determinism guarantee depends on identical per-entity instruction sequences (Architecture.md → "Determinism guarantee and mechanisms"). |
| Z2-N-ARCH-189 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture/BuildSystem.md`:400 | Architecture — Build system and presets › Compile and link flags › Clang flags for the android and linux-tsan presets | The configuration defines (`ZENITH_CONFIG_*`, `ZENITH_PLATFORM_*`, `ZENITH_SANITIZER_*`) are read only through the `constexpr` functions of `ZenithCore/Zenith_BuildInfo.h` (`Zenith_IsDebugBuild()`, `Zenith_IsShippingBuild()`, `Zenith_IsSanitizerBuild()`, `Zenith_GetBuildPlatform()`), and never change a type's layout (Zenith2/Docs/Contracts.md → "No layout-changing conditionals"). |
| Z2-N-ARCH-190 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture/BuildSystem.md`:575 | Architecture — Build system and presets › Presets › Preset reference | Every Build gate step builds `win-tools` before any other build preset of the phase, and every custom command that runs a host tool names the tool's file under `ZENITH_HOST_TOOLS_DIR` in its `DEPENDS`, so a tool change re-runs the commands that used it. |
| Z2-N-ARCH-191 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture/BuildSystem.md`:576 | Architecture — Build system and presets › Presets › Preset reference | The `zenith_host_tools`, `zenith_players` and `zenith_tests` utility targets are created by `zenith_end_build`: `zenith_host_tools` depends on every `zenith_add_tool` target supported in the preset, `zenith_players` on every game executable, and `zenith_tests` on every test, conformance and integration-suite target supported in the preset (the target set of `win-shipping-test`, [Z2-N-ARCH-231]). |
| Z2-N-ARCH-192 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture/BuildSystem.md`:577 | Architecture — Build system and presets › Presets › Preset reference | `ZENITH_HOST_TOOLS_DIR` is the one path by which a build reaches the host tools (ZenithShaderCompiler, ZenithCook); Android presets never build a host tool, and every Android custom command runs the Windows tool from that directory. |
| Z2-N-ARCH-193 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture/BuildSystem.md`:578 | Architecture — Build system and presets › Presets › Preset reference | The Configure gate step configures every configure preset whose `condition` holds on the host; a preset whose condition is false is reported as `skipped (condition: hostSystemName)` and is never an error. |
| Z2-N-ARCH-194 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture/BuildSystem.md`:611 | Architecture — Build system and presets › Toolchain pins and the setup script › The toolchain section | `CMake/toolchains/msvc-x64.cmake` and `CMake/toolchains/clang-linux-x64.cmake` read the compiler pin (`msvc.clVersion`, the `llvm` pin's `version`) and select the pinned compiler, and `zenith_begin_build` compares `CMAKE_CXX_COMPILER_VERSION` against that pin (a toolchain file runs before the compiler is identified) and stops the configure on a mismatch with `Zenith2: compiler <found> does not match the pinned <pinned> in ThirdParty/manifest.json`; on Android presets `zenith_begin_build` checks `ANDROID_NDK_REVISION` against the `ndk` pin's `pkgRevision` the same way. |
| Z2-N-ARCH-195 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture/BuildSystem.md`:612 | Architecture — Build system and presets › Toolchain pins and the setup script › The toolchain section | `Zenith2/Tools/Setup/Setup-Zenith2.ps1` is the clean-machine setup script: it installs or verifies every `toolchain` pin, downloads and verifies every `dependencies` source, installs the WSL distro with its pinned packages, and applies the `linux-tsan` kernel setting; `-Check` verifies without changing anything and exits 0 when everything matches or 1 on any mismatch, listing each mismatch. |
| Z2-N-ARCH-196 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture/BuildSystem.md`:613 | Architecture — Build system and presets › Toolchain pins and the setup script › The toolchain section | Every download is verified against its manifest SHA-256 before use, and a mismatch deletes the download and fails the setup; no configure step downloads anything (no `FetchContent` or `ExternalProject` download at configure time). |
| Z2-N-ARCH-197 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture/BuildSystem.md`:617 | Architecture — Build system and presets › Toolchain pins and the setup script › Compiler cache | sccache is the compiler launcher for MSVC and for NDK and Linux clang (`CMAKE_C_COMPILER_LAUNCHER` and `CMAKE_CXX_COMPILER_LAUNCHER` set by the toolchain files and the Android preset), pinned by the manifest. |
| Z2-N-ARCH-198 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture/BuildSystem.md`:618 | Architecture — Build system and presets › Toolchain pins and the setup script › Compiler cache | The cache directory is machine-wide and shared by every checkout and lane: `%LOCALAPPDATA%\Zenith2\sccache` on Windows and `$HOME/.zenith2/sccache` in WSL, for every compiler. |
| Z2-N-ARCH-199 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture/BuildSystem.md`:694 | Architecture — Build system and presets › Third-party integration › The manifest dependencies section | Upstream sources are integrated as released, never forked, rewritten or reorganised; a local change is a patch file listed in the entry's `patches` array with the decision ID that approved it, applied by the setup script after verification. |
| Z2-N-ARCH-200 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture/BuildSystem.md`:695 | Architecture — Build system and presets › Third-party integration › The manifest dependencies section | A dependency's headers are visible only to its `owner` library, which is the one library naming it in `THIRD_PARTY` (for a dependency with several targets, such as Luau, each target has one owner); a second library naming the same target is a configure error unless it is a test, benchmark or tool target, or the entry is `header-only` and its `owner` array lists both libraries, every one a `KIND BACKEND` of the same `BACKEND_OF` library; the manifest's ownership kinds are checked at the end of the configure against the declaration store (`zenith_add_tool` records `ZENITH_DECL_<Name>_TOOL_KIND`), the setup script's script mode checking structure only, and an owner a pin names before the ticket that declares it is reported at the end of the configure (one `STATUS` line per entry and owner), not refused, until it is declared, while a case-insensitive near miss of a declared name is refused naming the declared spelling (`Zenith2/Docs/Design/SHIP/Manifest.md` → "Validation rules", rule K4). |
| Z2-N-ARCH-201 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture/BuildSystem.md`:696 | Architecture — Build system and presets › Third-party integration › The manifest dependencies section | In the `win-asan`, `linux-tsan` and `Hwasan` builds every C and C++ dependency compiled into a runtime target (Jolt, PhysX, Luau, flecs, EnTT, zlib, zstd, SDL3 and the rest) is compiled from its pinned source with the sanitizer's flags; a `prebuilt` C or C++ library linked into a runtime target of a sanitizer build is a configure error. |
| Z2-N-ARCH-202 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture/BuildSystem.md`:699 | Architecture — Build system and presets › Third-party integration › The manifest dependencies section | A sanitizer suppression-file entry is admitted only with a decision ID in a comment on the line above it, and suppression files live in `Zenith2/Tools/Gates/Suppressions/` (`asan.supp`, `tsan.supp`, `ubsan.supp`, `hwasan.supp`). |
| Z2-N-ARCH-203 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture/BuildSystem.md`:700 | Architecture — Build system and presets › Third-party integration › The manifest dependencies section | No runtime target links Slang or DXC; they are `host-tool` entries used by `ZenithShaderCompiler`, and libgraph's link-surface stage fails when a Slang or DXC symbol appears in any runtime target. |
| Z2-N-ARCH-204 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture/BuildSystem.md`:704 | Architecture — Build system and presets › Third-party integration › The dependency cache | Verified dependency sources live in the machine-wide cache `%LOCALAPPDATA%\Zenith2\thirdparty\<name>\<dir>\` (Windows) or `$HOME/.zenith2/thirdparty/<name>/<dir>/` (WSL, and the native Linux CI host that `Setup-Zenith2.ps1`'s Linux host mode provisions, `Zenith2/Docs/Design/SHIP/Manifest.md` → "Linux host mode"), shared by every checkout, where `<dir>` is the source `<sha256>` of an unpatched entry, `<sha256>-p<first 16 hex digits of the entry's patch-set SHA-256>` of a patched one (so two lanes whose manifests differ only in patches never share a tree) and `<abi>-<sha256 of that ABI's archive>` of a `prebuilt` or `host-tool` item with its own archive; a `fromToolchain` entry has no `thirdparty` directory (its tree is the toolchain install). Each tree's stamp `<dir>.zenith2-stamp.json` sits beside it, never inside (`Zenith2/Docs/Design/SHIP/Manifest.md` → "Cache layout"). `CMake/ZenithThirdParty.cmake` derives the path from the manifest and fails the configure with `Zenith2: <name> <version> missing from the dependency cache; run Tools/Setup/Setup-Zenith2.ps1` when it is absent, and with `Zenith2: <name> <version>: dependency cache stamp does not match ThirdParty/manifest.json; run Tools/Setup/Setup-Zenith2.ps1` when the stamp's source or patch-set SHA-256 differs from the manifest's. |
| Z2-N-ARCH-205 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture/BuildSystem.md`:728 | Architecture — Build system and presets › Games in CMake › The zenith_add_game helper | A game directory contains no compiled-language source: `SourceLayout` fails on any `.cpp`, `.c`, `.h`, `.hpp` or `.inl` under `Games/`. |
| Z2-N-ARCH-206 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture/BuildSystem.md`:729 | Architecture — Build system and presets › Games in CMake › The zenith_add_game helper | The game executable's only source is the generated composition TU; every other object comes from linked engine, module, backend and Player libraries. |
| Z2-N-ARCH-207 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture/BuildSystem.md`:730 | Architecture — Build system and presets › Games in CMake › The zenith_add_game helper | Cooking and type generation run through the host tools (`ZenithCook --game <Name> --platform <windows\|android> --out ${ZENITH_OUT}/Cooked/<Name>/<platform>` and `ZenithCook --game <Name> --step luau-types`, which writes `${ZENITH_OUT}/Generated/Luau/<Name>/` (Zenith2/Docs/Scripting.md → "Modules, bindings and generated types")) with `DEPFILE` outputs, so an unchanged content root re-cooks nothing. |
| Z2-N-ARCH-208 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture/BuildSystem.md`:731 | Architecture — Build system and presets › Games in CMake › The zenith_add_game helper | Generated `.d.luau` files are written under `${ZENITH_OUT}/Generated/Luau/<Name>/` and never into `Games/<Name>/`, so a build never leaves an untracked file in the source tree. |
| Z2-N-ARCH-209 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture/BuildSystem.md`:780 | Architecture — Build system and presets › Games in CMake › The game.json schema | `zenith_add_game` fails the configure on a missing key, a wrong type, a value outside its allowed set, or any top-level or nested key that is neither in the table nor prefixed `x-`, naming the key path in the one format `Games/<Name>/game.json: <keyPath>: <expected>, found <actual>` (a missing key: `<keyPath>: missing`; an unknown key: `<keyPath>: unknown key`), for example `Games/OpenWorldSlice/game.json: backends.physics.android: expected "Jolt" or "PhysX", found "Havok"`; the `schemaVersion` and PhysX-on-Android messages are instances of this format with their own detail text in place of `expected <expected>, found <actual>`, and the module-dependency message (bullet 267 of Architecture.md), which concerns the list as a whole, is `Games/<Name>/game.json: ` followed by its own text with no key path. |
| Z2-N-ARCH-210 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture/BuildSystem.md`:781 | Architecture — Build system and presets › Games in CMake › The game.json schema | `game.json` names no RHI and no compression backend: a Windows game links both RHI backends and selects one at run time through configuration, an Android game links `ZenithRHI_Vulkan`, and every game links both compression backends. |
| Z2-N-ARCH-211 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture/BuildSystem.md`:782 | Architecture — Build system and presets › Games in CMake › The game.json schema | When D-SEL-PHYSX-ANDROID (open, Stage 1) rules that PhysX is not an Android backend, `backends.physics` naming `PhysX` for `android` fails the configure with `Games/<Name>/game.json: backends.physics.android: PhysX is not an Android physics backend (capability matrix); use Jolt`. |
| Z2-N-ARCH-212 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture/BuildSystem.md`:786 | Architecture — Build system and presets › Games in CMake › Android packaging | The Android toolchain is NDK r27c with `ANDROID_PLATFORM android-34` (minSdk 34), `ANDROID_STL c++_static`, `ANDROID_SUPPORT_FLEXIBLE_PAGE_SIZES=ON`, and every shared object linked with `-Wl,-z,max-page-size=16384`. |
| Z2-N-ARCH-213 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture/BuildSystem.md`:787 | Architecture — Build system and presets › Games in CMake › Android packaging | The APK is packaged by the Gradle project that `zenith_add_game` generates from `Zenith2/Player/GradleTemplate/` into `${ZENITH_OUT}/Games/<Name>/<configure-preset>/android/`. |
| Z2-N-ARCH-214 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture/BuildSystem.md`:790 | Architecture — Build system and presets › Games in CMake › Android packaging | The `ApkCheck` gate step runs `llvm-readelf -l` on every `.so` inside the APK (including any prebuilt middleware shared object) and fails when any `PT_LOAD` segment's alignment is below `0x4000`. |
| Z2-N-ARCH-215 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture/BuildSystem.md`:793 | Architecture — Build system and presets › Games in CMake › Android packaging | The HWASan APK is the Gradle build type `hwasan`, fed by the `Hwasan` configuration: `debuggable true`, `packaging.jniLibs.useLegacyPackaging = true` (so `extractNativeLibs=true`), and `lib/arm64-v8a/wrap.sh` containing `#!/system/bin/sh` and `LD_HWASAN=1 exec "$@"`; it is exempt from the zip-alignment check and still passes the ELF alignment check. |
| Z2-N-ARCH-216 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture/BuildSystem.md`:794 | Architecture — Build system and presets › Games in CMake › Android packaging | The `android-x86_64` preset exists for emulator smoke runs alone; no acceptance, budget or parity result is ever taken from it, and no gate line of the base category builds it. |

| Z2-N-ARCH-218 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture/BuildSystem.md`:810 | Architecture — Build system and presets › The linux-tsan host | The `linux-tsan` binary directory is on the Linux filesystem (`$HOME/.zenith2/out/<checkout-hash>/linux-tsan`), never on DrvFS (`/mnt/…`). |
| Z2-N-ARCH-219 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture/BuildSystem.md`:811 | Architecture — Build system and presets › The linux-tsan host | The setup script sets `vm.mmap_rnd_bits` to at most 28 in the WSL distro and on the native Linux CI host its Linux host mode provisions (`sysctl -w vm.mmap_rnd_bits=28`, persisted in `/etc/sysctl.d/99-zenith2-tsan.conf`), and `Sanitizers.ps1` reads the live value before running and fails with a message naming the setting when it is higher. |
| Z2-N-ARCH-220 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture/BuildSystem.md`:812 | Architecture — Build system and presets › The linux-tsan host | The sanitizer runtime options are fixed: `TSAN_OPTIONS=halt_on_error=1:second_deadlock_stack=1:history_size=7:suppressions=<Suppressions/tsan.supp>` and `UBSAN_OPTIONS=halt_on_error=1:print_stacktrace=1:suppressions=<Suppressions/ubsan.supp>`. |
| Z2-N-ARCH-221 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture/BuildSystem.md`:813 | Architecture — Build system and presets › The linux-tsan host | CI runs the `linux-tsan` preset natively on a Linux runner, and the Linux job runs only while a `linux-tsan` gate line is active in the committed Z2 category gate lists. |
| Z2-N-ARCH-222 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture/BuildSystem.md`:817 | Architecture — Build system and presets › Gate-budget rules that shape the build | A warm gate phase (measure or verify) completes within 30 minutes of wall-clock time; the cap is the category's `phaseTimeoutMinutes`, and the build layout (one configure tree per toolchain, sccache, incremental Ninja builds) exists to keep a warm phase under it. |
| Z2-N-ARCH-223 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture/BuildSystem.md`:818 | Architecture — Build system and presets › Gate-budget rules that shape the build | The Build step detects a cold start (an empty or invalidated binary directory: a missing `CMakeCache.txt`, or a changed hash of `CMakePresets.json`, `ThirdParty/manifest.json` or `CMake/**`) and reports `cold: true` with its reason in the gate output, so the cold cap from the gate-budget rows applies (Zenith2/Docs/Budgets.md → "Gate budget rows"). |
| Z2-N-ARCH-224 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture/BuildSystem.md`:819 | Architecture — Build system and presets › Gate-budget rules that shape the build | Every step has its own hard timeout sized for a cold build, recorded in the gate-budget rows; the step kills its process tree when the timeout passes. |

| Z2-N-ARCH-319 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture/ZenithGraph.md`:185 | Architecture — ZenithGraph shared core › Public API › The graph object | Graph captures and every object stored in a schedule arena are trivially destructible, because arenas release memory without running destructors (Zenith2/Docs/Contracts.md → "Ownership and allocation"); `Zenith_Graph` itself is not (it owns `Zenith_Array`s), so its owner destroys it with `Zenith_Destroy` before resetting or destroying the arena it was built in. |

| Z2-N-ARCH-322 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture/ZenithGraph.md`:255 | Architecture — ZenithGraph shared core › Hazard derivation › The version chain | A write access consumes the resource's latest version v and produces v+1; a second write declared with a handle whose version is not the latest (two writers of the same version) is `GRAPH_ERROR_VERSION_FORK`, reported with both nodes and the version. |

| Z2-N-ARCH-325 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture/ZenithGraph.md`:272 | Architecture — ZenithGraph shared core › Hazard derivation › The derivation algorithm | The derivation is deterministic: resources are processed in declaration order, accesses within a version bucket in node declaration order, and the resulting edge list is sorted by `(m_uFrom, m_uTo, m_eReason, m_uResource)`, so the same declarations always produce byte-identical schedules. |
| Z2-N-ARCH-326 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture/ZenithGraph.md`:273 | Architecture — ZenithGraph shared core › Hazard derivation › The derivation algorithm | Hazard derivation runs in O((A + R) log A) time for A accesses and R resources (interval-map operations) plus O(E log E) to sort E edges. |
| Z2-N-ARCH-327 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture/ZenithGraph.md`:281 | Architecture — ZenithGraph shared core › Hazard derivation › Culling | With `m_bCullUnusedNodes` set, a node is culled when it lacks `GRAPH_NODE_FLAG_SIDE_EFFECT` and no non-culled node and no `GRAPH_RESOURCE_FLAG_OUTPUT` resource depends on any version it writes; culling is computed by a reverse reachability walk from side-effect nodes and output resources, and culled nodes are absent from `m_auOrder` with `uGRAPH_INVALID_INDEX` positions. |
| Z2-N-ARCH-328 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture/ZenithGraph.md`:282 | Architecture — ZenithGraph shared core › Hazard derivation › Culling | The CPU frame graph compiles with `m_bCullUnusedNodes = false`, because every system runs every tick whether or not a subsequent node reads its output. |
| Z2-N-ARCH-329 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture/ZenithGraph.md`:286 | Architecture — ZenithGraph shared core › Cycle detection and diagnostics | `Compile` runs Tarjan's strongly-connected-components algorithm over the deduplicated edge set in O(N + E); any component with more than one node is `GRAPH_ERROR_CYCLE`, and the compile fails with one diagnostic per component. |
| Z2-N-ARCH-330 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture/ZenithGraph.md`:287 | Architecture — ZenithGraph shared core › Cycle detection and diagnostics | A cycle diagnostic names one concrete cycle inside its component, found by a breadth-first search from the component's lowest-index node back to itself, and lists every edge of that cycle with its reason, resource name, version and the two accesses that created it. |
| Z2-N-ARCH-331 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture/ZenithGraph.md`:367 | Architecture — ZenithGraph shared core › Cycle detection and diagnostics › Error codes and message formats | `Compile` reports every error it finds in one pass, not just the first, sorted by code and then by first node index, so one failed compile lists every broken declaration. |

| Z2-N-ARCH-333 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture/ZenithGraph.md`:392 | Architecture — ZenithGraph shared core › Ordering, priorities and dependency counts › Critical-path priority | A node's priority is its cost plus the maximum priority of its successors (its bottom level): computed in reverse topological order in O(N + E), summed in `uint64`, with `m_uCost` clamped to at least 1. |
| Z2-N-ARCH-334 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture/ZenithGraph.md`:399 | Architecture — ZenithGraph shared core › Ordering, priorities and dependency counts › Transitive reduction and dependency counts | With `m_bTransitiveReduction` set and at most `m_uTransitiveReductionNodeLimit` non-culled nodes, `Compile` removes every edge u → v for which another path from u to v exists, using per-node reachability bitsets in reverse topological order (O(N · E / 64) time, O(N² / 8) bytes of schedule-arena scratch); above the limit, reduction is skipped, `m_bReductionSkipped` is set, and the front end logs it once. |
| Z2-N-ARCH-335 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture/ZenithGraph.md`:400 | Architecture — ZenithGraph shared core › Ordering, priorities and dependency counts › Transitive reduction and dependency counts | `m_auDependencyCount[n]` equals the number of distinct predecessors of n in the edge set the schedule keeps, and every edge the schedule keeps appears in n's successor range of its source; the conformance test `Test_GraphConformance_DependencyCountsMatchEdges` checks both properties on every scenario. |
| Z2-N-ARCH-336 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture/ZenithGraph.md`:404 | Architecture — ZenithGraph shared core › Lifetime intervals | For each resource, `m_axLifetimes` records the positions (in `m_auOrder`) of its first and last non-culled access; a resource with no non-culled access has both positions `uGRAPH_INVALID_INDEX`. |
| Z2-N-ARCH-337 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture/ZenithGraph.md`:405 | Architecture — ZenithGraph shared core › Lifetime intervals | Lifetimes are the one input either front end uses for memory reuse: Flux places transients by lifetime per `(queue, position)` (Zenith2/Docs/Rendering.md → "Transient aliasing"), and the CPU front end reuses frame-arena scratch between two transient resources whose lifetimes do not overlap and where every accessor of the one first in position order reaches every accessor of the other through the kept edges, because concurrently running nodes are not ordered by position alone. |
| Z2-N-ARCH-338 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture/ZenithGraph.md`:413 | Architecture — ZenithGraph shared core › Structure hash | `m_ulStructureHash` is `Zenith_Hash64` over the canonical little-endian serialization of: node count, each node's name bytes, lane and flags; resource count, each resource's name bytes, subresource count and flags; each access record (node, resource, mode, versions, subranges, backend usage); each ordering (before, after); in declaration order. Costs and backend payloads are excluded, so a cost change never changes the hash. |
| Z2-N-ARCH-339 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture/ZenithGraph.md`:433 | Architecture — ZenithGraph shared core › Recompilation and caching support | `Zenith_GraphReprioritise` produces the same order `Compile` would produce for the same declarations with the new costs, and never alters edges, dependency counts or the accesses that bound each resource's lifetime; positions, and therefore `m_axLifetimes`, follow the new order; the conformance test `Test_GraphConformance_ReprioritiseMatchesCompile` checks the equality on random graphs. |
| Z2-N-ARCH-340 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture/ZenithGraph.md`:511 | Architecture — ZenithGraph shared core › JSON dump | The dump is deterministic for identical inputs: keys in the order shown, arrays in declaration (or schedule) order, 64-bit values as `0x`-prefixed 16-digit lower-case hexadecimal strings, integers below 2^53 as JSON numbers, and flags as arrays of upper-case names without the `GRAPH_*_FLAG_` prefix. |
