| Z2-N-ARCH-343 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture/ZenithGraph.md`:565 | Architecture — ZenithGraph shared core › Conformance suite | Every scenario in the table "Graph conformance tests" runs through every builder its "Builders" cell names: the five scenarios that exercise the core API itself (`Test_GraphConformance_ArenaOnlyAllocation`, `Test_GraphConformance_CapacityErrorReported`, `Test_GraphConformance_ConcurrentCompileOfOneGraph`, `Test_GraphConformance_ReprioritiseMatchesCompile`, `Test_GraphConformance_JsonDumpSchema`) run through the core builder alone and every other scenario through all three, except that a scenario whose entry sets `m_bAssertsDisabledOnly` has no case where `ZENITH_ASSERTS_ENABLED` is 1, so of the presets bullet 344 names it runs only in `win-release`; a difference between any two builders' edge sets, orders, dependency counts or lifetimes fails the test. |
| Z2-N-ARCH-344 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture/ZenithGraph.md`:566 | Architecture — ZenithGraph shared core › Conformance suite | The graph conformance suite runs in the `Test` step on `win-debug` and `win-release`, and under `win-asan` and `linux-tsan` whenever the `Zenith2Memory` category's sanitizer lines run. |
| Z2-N-ARCH-345 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture/ZenithGraph.md`:380 | Architecture — ZenithGraph shared core › Cycle detection and diagnostics › Error codes and message formats | Each front end maps core error codes as the table "Error codes by front end" states and carries the core diagnostic text unchanged in its own diagnostics. |
| Z2-N-ARCH-346 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture/ZenithGraph.md`:381 | Architecture — ZenithGraph shared core › Cycle detection and diagnostics › Error codes and message formats | The GPU front end declares every transient resource with `GRAPH_RESOURCE_FLAG_REQUIRE_DEFINED_READS` and `GRAPH_RESOURCE_FLAG_FIRST_WRITE_DISCARD_PER_SUBRESOURCE`, which is how the first-write rule of Zenith2/Docs/Rendering.md → "Transient aliasing" reaches the core: each first write of a subresource interval is declared with its real mode over its real range, so first writers of disjoint subresources of one transient gain no ordering edge; the CPU front end declares every persistent resource `GRAPH_RESOURCE_FLAG_IMPORTED` and sets no first-write flag on arbitration staging resources. |
| Z2-N-ARCH-347 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture/ZenithGraph.md`:513 | Architecture — ZenithGraph shared core › JSON dump | A front end's dump embeds the core document of this section verbatim under the key `"core"` and adds its own data under sibling keys (the GPU front end's placement, barriers and queues; the CPU front end's affinity and timings), so every reader of the core schema reads every front-end dump. |
| Z2-N-ARCH-348 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture/ZenithGraph.md`:313 | Architecture — ZenithGraph shared core › Cycle detection and diagnostics | Cycle diagnostics use exactly the header, detail-line and subrange forms above, so `Test_GraphConformance_CycleDiagnosticNamesAccesses` compares them byte for byte. |
| Z2-N-ARCH-349 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture/ZenithGraph.md`:368 | Architecture — ZenithGraph shared core › Cycle detection and diagnostics › Error codes and message formats | A failed `Compile` writes every diagnostic into `xReportOut`, whose entries stay valid until `xScheduleArena` is reset, and returns a `Zenith_GraphCompileError` carrying the first diagnostic's code and the diagnostic count; the record carries no text (Zenith2/Docs/Contracts.md → "Errors and results"). |
| Z2-N-ARCH-350 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture/ZenithGraph.md`:382 | Architecture — ZenithGraph shared core › Cycle detection and diagnostics › Error codes and message formats | A resource carries at most one of `GRAPH_RESOURCE_FLAG_FIRST_WRITE_DISCARD` and `GRAPH_RESOURCE_FLAG_FIRST_WRITE_DISCARD_PER_SUBRESOURCE`; a resource declared with both triggers a programmer-error assert in builds with asserts on and is checked by the per-subresource rule in every build, and that check runs over the interval map of the derivation algorithm in the same O((A + R) log A) bound. |
| Z2-N-ARCH-381 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture/CPUFrameGraph.md`:20 | Architecture — CPU frame graph and scheduler › Settled rules and ownership | `ZenithJobs` knows no ECS, phase, component or render concept: it schedules nodes grouped into ordered stages over opaque resources, and `ZenithECS` and `ZenithRuntime` translate systems, phases and engine stages into those terms. |
| Z2-N-ARCH-382 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture/CPUFrameGraph.md`:21 | Architecture — CPU frame graph and scheduler › Settled rules and ownership | There are no fibers and no blocking waits between jobs: a job never waits on another job, and every dependency is expressed as a graph edge, a sub-job join or a continuation (Zenith2/Docs/Contracts.md → "Threading and concurrency contract"); the one exception is a development-build debugger stop of a Luau VM, bracketed by `Zenith_MarkThreadAsJobWorker(false)` and `Zenith_MarkThreadAsJobWorker(true)`, which the blocking-wait hook reports under `szPrimitive = "debugger-stop"` (Zenith2/Docs/Scripting.md → "Debugging and profiling"). |
| Z2-N-ARCH-383 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture/CPUFrameGraph.md`:45 | Architecture — CPU frame graph and scheduler › Frame structure | Tick instances within one frame run strictly in sequence: tick instance t+1 starts after every node of tick instance t has completed, and each world's tick boundary node is the first node of its tick. |
| Z2-N-ARCH-384 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture/CPUFrameGraph.md`:46 | Architecture — CPU frame graph and scheduler › Frame structure | Phase 12 (render extraction) runs once per frame, in the epilogue after the frame's last tick instance; when T is 0 (paused, or no accumulated time) the epilogue still runs and extracts with the updated interpolation alpha. |
| Z2-N-ARCH-385 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture/CPUFrameGraph.md`:47 | Architecture — CPU frame graph and scheduler › Frame structure | The render subgraph of frame k−1 runs concurrently with the tick and epilogue subgraphs of frame k in the same worker pool, with no edge between them and no lock on game state, because it reads only the sealed `Flux_RenderWorld` k−1. |
| Z2-N-ARCH-386 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture/CPUFrameGraph.md`:48 | Architecture — CPU frame graph and scheduler › Frame structure | Iteration k+1 begins only after every subgraph of iteration k (including RENDER(k−1)) has completed, so simulation never runs more than one frame ahead of rendering. |
| Z2-N-ARCH-387 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture/CPUFrameGraph.md`:159 | Architecture — CPU frame graph and scheduler › Job system › Public types | Per-worker state (deque, arenas, steal RNG, profiler buffer handle) is reached only through the `Zenith_JobContext&` passed to every job; no job-system state is `thread_local` or global (Zenith2/Docs/Contracts.md → "No mutable global state"). |
| Z2-N-ARCH-388 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture/CPUFrameGraph.md`:160 | Architecture — CPU frame graph and scheduler › Job system › Public types | Every job function, sub-job body and continuation returns normally (it carries no exception specification, and exceptions are off outside the Luau firewall, Z2-D-141), and a job that must stop early returns; there is no job-level exception, abort or wait API. |
| Z2-N-ARCH-389 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture/CPUFrameGraph.md`:170 | Architecture — CPU frame graph and scheduler › Job system › Threads | The main thread runs the SDL platform pump and every world's main Luau VM (behaviours); per-worker chunk-system VMs belong to their workers and need no pinning (Zenith2/Docs/Scripting.md → "VM model and threading"). |
| Z2-N-ARCH-390 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture/CPUFrameGraph.md`:171 | Architecture — CPU frame graph and scheduler › Job system › Threads | The render-submit thread executes only render-submit jobs and never steals: the `JOB_AFFINITY_ANY` jobs its jobs make ready go to the injection queue and it never runs one on any path, so a submit or present is never delayed behind simulation work. |
| Z2-N-ARCH-391 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture/CPUFrameGraph.md`:183 | Architecture — CPU frame graph and scheduler › Job system › Queues | Every job queue is bounded with the capacity and full policy of the table above, and counts its full events and high-water mark under the counter names `jobs.<queue>.full` and `jobs.<queue>.high_water` (Zenith2/Docs/Contracts.md → "Threading and concurrency contract"). |
| Z2-N-ARCH-392 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture/CPUFrameGraph.md`:184 | Architecture — CPU frame graph and scheduler › Job system › Queues | The affinity queues and the injection queue are sized from the compiled schedule, so their capacity bounds exactly the jobs one frame can enqueue into them; reaching a fatal full policy is a scheduler defect, never a load condition. |
| Z2-N-ARCH-393 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture/CPUFrameGraph.md`:197 | Architecture — CPU frame graph and scheduler › Job system › Worker loop | A background job never delays frame work by more than one slice: a background job body runs for at most 2 ms of wall-clock time before it re-submits its remainder as a continuation, and review checks every background job for this; bullet 443 is the one exception. |
| Z2-N-ARCH-394 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture/CPUFrameGraph.md`:199 | Architecture — CPU frame graph and scheduler › Job system › Worker loop | The only blocking waits in the scheduler are a worker parking when it has nothing to run, the render-submit thread waiting for the GPU timeline or present, and the main thread parking when the frame is incomplete and nothing it may run is visible; none of them occurs inside a job body, except the development-build debugger stop of bullet 382, which runs outside the job-worker bracket and is excluded from `Test_JobSystem_NoBlockingWaitInJobs`. |
| Z2-N-ARCH-395 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture/CPUFrameGraph.md`:207 | Architecture — CPU frame graph and scheduler › Dynamic sub-jobs and continuations › ParallelFor | Sub-jobs spawned by a node are joined before the node completes: the node's completion is signalled after its body has returned, every sub-job it (or its sub-jobs, transitively) spawned has finished, and every continuation it registered has finished; successors see a finished node. |
| Z2-N-ARCH-396 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture/CPUFrameGraph.md`:208 | Architecture — CPU frame graph and scheduler › Dynamic sub-jobs and continuations › ParallelFor | A sub-job's results are merged in a deterministic order defined by the caller — index order for plain ranges, the merge key of the ECS model for simulation work (Architecture.md → "ECS model") — never in completion order. |
| Z2-N-ARCH-397 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture/CPUFrameGraph.md`:209 | Architecture — CPU frame graph and scheduler › Dynamic sub-jobs and continuations › ParallelFor | `ParallelFor` over ECS chunks passes the chunk count as `uCount`; the chunk-to-worker assignment is dynamic and varies between runs, and nothing observable to simulation depends on it. |
| Z2-N-ARCH-398 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture/CPUFrameGraph.md`:215 | Architecture — CPU frame graph and scheduler › Dynamic sub-jobs and continuations › Continuations | Anything that looks like waiting inside a node is written as a continuation: a node that needs its sub-jobs' results registers a continuation to consume them, and the node's deterministic merge step is its final continuation. |
| Z2-N-ARCH-399 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture/CPUFrameGraph.md`:402 | Architecture — CPU frame graph and scheduler › Frame graph builder › Declarations at the ECS level | A system reaches component data, world resources, queries, command buffers and event emitters only through the `Zenith_SystemContext&` it is invoked with, and the context grants exactly what its registration declared; an undeclared access is a programmer-error assert in builds with asserts on and is rejected by the `Zenith_SystemContext` accessor in every build. |
| Z2-N-ARCH-400 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture/CPUFrameGraph.md`:403 | Architecture — CPU frame graph and scheduler › Frame graph builder › Declarations at the ECS level | Declaring a whole component (`Read(xType)` or `Write(xType)`) expands to every property of that component when the schedule compiles, so access and authority are always checked per property. |
| Z2-N-ARCH-401 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture/CPUFrameGraph.md`:404 | Architecture — CPU frame graph and scheduler › Frame graph builder › Declarations at the ECS level | `Before`/`After` name systems or stages by their registered names; an unknown name is a schedule-compile error, while `BeforeIfPresent`/`AfterIfPresent` are ignored when the named node is absent (for example, a module the game did not select). |
| Z2-N-ARCH-402 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture/CPUFrameGraph.md`:419 | Architecture — CPU frame graph and scheduler › Frame graph builder › Translation into the graph core | Edges are derived automatically from read-after-write, write-after-read and write-after-write hazards on the resources above, plus the stage orderings and the explicit `Before`/`After` constraints; no other mechanism orders two nodes. |
| Z2-N-ARCH-403 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture/CPUFrameGraph.md`:420 | Architecture — CPU frame graph and scheduler › Frame graph builder › Translation into the graph core | Two writers of one frame resource in one stage without an arbitration node are a jobs-level compile error (`FRAME_GRAPH_ERROR_UNORDERED_WRITERS`); for ECS properties the authority check of Architecture.md → "Frame phases and authority" reports it first, with the authority message. |
| Z2-N-ARCH-404 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture/CPUFrameGraph.md`:421 | Architecture — CPU frame graph and scheduler › Frame graph builder › Translation into the graph core | An explicit ordering that contradicts the stage order — `Before('<B>')` declared by a node in a stage subsequent to `<B>`'s, or `After('<B>')` declared by a node in a stage preceding `<B>`'s — is `FRAME_GRAPH_ERROR_ORDERING_CONTRADICTS_STAGE`, reported as `schedule: '<A>' (phase <P>) declares Before('<B>') but '<B>' runs in preceding phase <Q>` or `schedule: '<A>' (phase <P>) declares After('<B>') but '<B>' runs in subsequent phase <Q>` respectively. |
| Z2-N-ARCH-405 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture/CPUFrameGraph.md`:422 | Architecture — CPU frame graph and scheduler › Frame graph builder › Translation into the graph core | A system's order index — the merge-key component "system order" of the ECS model — is its registration index in the world's system registry, which the composition root's fixed module order makes identical on every run; it never depends on the compiled execution order. |
| Z2-N-ARCH-406 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture/CPUFrameGraph.md`:445 | Architecture — CPU frame graph and scheduler › Schedule compilation and caching | The frame graph is compiled once into a static schedule — per subgraph a topological order, per-node dependency counts and critical-path priorities, from `ZenithGraph` — and each frame executes that schedule without recomputing any of it. |
| Z2-N-ARCH-407 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture/CPUFrameGraph.md`:446 | Architecture — CPU frame graph and scheduler › Schedule compilation and caching | The compiled schedule is cached and keyed by its structure hash; it is recompiled only when a trigger in the table "Schedule recompile triggers" fires. |
| Z2-N-ARCH-408 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture/CPUFrameGraph.md`:447 | Architecture — CPU frame graph and scheduler › Schedule compilation and caching | Recompilation runs on the main thread in step 8 of the frame prologue, compiles into a second schedule buffer, and swaps under `uLOCK_LEVEL_JOBS_SCHEDULE` before `RunFrame` of that frame; the previous schedule is released after the render subgraph that used it completes. |
| Z2-N-ARCH-409 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture/CPUFrameGraph.md`:448 | Architecture — CPU frame graph and scheduler › Schedule compilation and caching | A recompile that fails keeps the previous schedule, rejects the change that triggered it (for example, a hot-reloaded script system whose new access declaration breaks authority) and reports the schedule-compile diagnostics; a first compile that fails stops startup with the diagnostics. |
| Z2-N-ARCH-410 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture/CPUFrameGraph.md`:461 | Architecture — CPU frame graph and scheduler › Schedule compilation and caching | Disabling a failing system or behaviour (from the next tick boundary, Zenith2/Docs/Scripting.md → "Commit and failure semantics") never recompiles the schedule: the disabled node stays in place and completes immediately without running its body. |
| Z2-N-ARCH-411 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture/CPUFrameGraph.md`:488 | Architecture — CPU frame graph and scheduler › Execution | Ready nodes are pushed in descending critical-path priority, so among simultaneously ready nodes the one on the longest remaining path is taken first; priority changes the wall-clock schedule only, never a result. |
| Z2-N-ARCH-412 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture/CPUFrameGraph.md`:489 | Architecture — CPU frame graph and scheduler › Execution | A cancelled schedule instance (`RequestCancel`) still completes every node: nodes that have not started skip their bodies and complete immediately, so every counter reaches zero and no thread waits for work that will never run. |
| Z2-N-ARCH-413 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture/CPUFrameGraph.md`:520 | Architecture — CPU frame graph and scheduler › Execution › Simulation clock | Simulation runs on fixed ticks: every tick advances simulated time by exactly `GetTickDurationUs()`, systems read simulated time only from the tick index and tick duration, and no simulation code reads wall-clock time. |
| Z2-N-ARCH-414 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture/CPUFrameGraph.md`:521 | Architecture — CPU frame graph and scheduler › Execution › Simulation clock | Catch-up is bounded by `m_uMaxCatchUpTicks` per frame; dropped ticks increment the counter `sim.dropped_ticks` and never run, and a replay records the tick sequence actually executed, so dropped real time never affects replay equality (Architecture.md → "Multiplayer foundations"). |
| Z2-N-ARCH-415 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture/CPUFrameGraph.md`:523 | Architecture — CPU frame graph and scheduler › Execution › Simulation clock | Rendering interpolates between the previous and current tick's world transforms with `GetInterpolationAlpha()` at extraction, and a teleport resets the interpolation history of the teleported entity (Architecture.md → "Lifecycle commands"). |
| Z2-N-ARCH-416 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture/CPUFrameGraph.md`:561 | Architecture — CPU frame graph and scheduler › Render extraction and pipelining › The hand-off object | The extraction node of each world (phase 12) snapshots every render-relevant value into `Flux_RenderWorld` k by copying it; the render world holds no pointer, view or handle into ECS storage, script memory or any other simulation-owned memory, only copied values, asset handles and device handles. |
| Z2-N-ARCH-417 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture/CPUFrameGraph.md`:562 | Architecture — CPU frame graph and scheduler › Render extraction and pipelining › The hand-off object | `Flux_RenderWorld` is immutable after `Render.Seal`; the render subgraph reads it without locks, and no simulation node reads or writes any render world. |
| Z2-N-ARCH-418 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture/CPUFrameGraph.md`:563 | Architecture — CPU frame graph and scheduler › Render extraction and pipelining › The hand-off object | The render worlds live in a ring of `uRENDER_WORLD_RING_SIZE` (2) arenas: frame k's epilogue writes slot `k % 2` while RENDER(k−1) reads slot `(k−1) % 2`, and a slot is reset only at the start of the epilogue that reuses it, after the render subgraph that read it has completed (guaranteed by the iteration boundary). |
| Z2-N-ARCH-419 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture/CPUFrameGraph.md`:564 | Architecture — CPU frame graph and scheduler › Render extraction and pipelining › The hand-off object | Modules contribute to extraction only through extractors registered with `Zenith_WorldAttachContext::AddExtractor` (Architecture/CompositionRoot.md → "Startup sequence"); each extractor declares its reads like any system, runs as a phase-12 node, and writes only its own `Flux_RenderWorldWriter` partition, merged in extractor registration order and then chunk order. |
| Z2-N-ARCH-420 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture/CPUFrameGraph.md`:565 | Architecture — CPU frame graph and scheduler › Render extraction and pipelining › The hand-off object | Flux never includes a header of or links `ZenithScene`, `ZenithECS` or `ZenithScript`; the render world is the one data path from simulation into Flux, and libgraph fails any edge from `ZenithFlux` to those libraries. |
| Z2-N-ARCH-421 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture/CPUFrameGraph.md`:573 | Architecture — CPU frame graph and scheduler › Render extraction and pipelining › Pipelining and latency | The CPU pipeline depth is exactly one frame: simulation of frame N+1 overlaps rendering of frame N and never runs further ahead, and telemetry records `pipeline.depth = 1` with every frame. |
| Z2-N-ARCH-422 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture/CPUFrameGraph.md`:574 | Architecture — CPU frame graph and scheduler › Render extraction and pipelining › Pipelining and latency | The extra frame of latency is accounted for in the input-latency budget row: each frame records the input sample time, simulation end, extraction seal, submit and present timestamps, and the input sequence number travels from the Input phase through `Flux_RenderWorld` to the present so the latency measurement can attribute each present to its input (Zenith2/Docs/Budgets.md → "Measured in every workload run"). |
| Z2-N-ARCH-423 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture/CPUFrameGraph.md`:578 | Architecture — CPU frame graph and scheduler › Worlds in the graph | Each world contributes its own tick-boundary, phase, sync-point and extraction nodes in its own group; nodes of different worlds share no frame resource, so worlds tick concurrently and one world's failure or pause never blocks another world's nodes. |
| Z2-N-ARCH-424 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture/CPUFrameGraph.md`:579 | Architecture — CPU frame graph and scheduler › Worlds in the graph | A world being loaded in isolation for a world transition contributes no nodes to the running schedule until its swap at a tick boundary (Architecture.md → "Lifecycle commands"). |
| Z2-N-ARCH-425 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture/CPUFrameGraph.md`:583 | Architecture — CPU frame graph and scheduler › Cancellation and shutdown | Background jobs are cancelled cooperatively through their `Zenith_OperationHandle`: `RequestCancel` sets the flag that `Zenith_JobContext::IsCancelled` reports, the job returns at its next check (within one 2 ms slice), releases what it holds on its own thread, and only then reports `OPERATION_STATUS_CANCELLED` (Zenith2/Docs/Contracts.md → "Threading and concurrency contract"). |
| Z2-N-ARCH-426 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture/CPUFrameGraph.md`:584 | Architecture — CPU frame graph and scheduler › Cancellation and shutdown | Scheduler shutdown is orderly and follows the shutdown ownership order of Zenith2/Docs/Contracts.md → "Threading and concurrency contract": the executor first drains the in-flight frame (simulation N+1 finishes, rendering N submits). |
| Z2-N-ARCH-427 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture/CPUFrameGraph.md`:590 | Architecture — CPU frame graph and scheduler › Profiling | Every frame-graph node, sub-job, continuation and background job emits a profiling zone through the installed profiler hooks: nodes as `<NodeName>`, sub-jobs as `<NodeName>/[<begin>,<end>)`, continuations as `<NodeName>/cont<n>`, background jobs as `bg/<submitter name>`; the zones feed the on-screen profiler's per-worker timeline and Tracy. |
| Z2-N-ARCH-428 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture/CPUFrameGraph.md`:591 | Architecture — CPU frame graph and scheduler › Profiling | The scheduler publishes these counters every frame: `jobs.steals`, `jobs.steal_failures`, `jobs.inline_runs`, `jobs.idle_ns.<worker>`, `jobs.parked`, `jobs.ready_high_water`, the queue counters of § "Queues", `sim.ticks`, `sim.dropped_ticks`, `sim.alpha`, and `frame.schedule_recompiles`. |
| Z2-N-ARCH-429 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture/CPUFrameGraph.md`:593 | Architecture — CPU frame graph and scheduler › Profiling | The frame schedule's JSON dump (the `zenith2.graph-dump` schema of Architecture.md → "ZenithGraph shared core", one document per subgraph, with timings) is available to the on-screen profiler every frame in development builds and is written on demand by the console command `jobs.dump <path>`. |
| Z2-N-ARCH-430 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture/CPUFrameGraph.md`:599 | Architecture — CPU frame graph and scheduler › Required tests | Every test in the table "CPU frame graph tests" exists under the name given and asserts the case given. |
| Z2-N-ARCH-431 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture/CPUFrameGraph.md`:585 | Architecture — CPU frame graph and scheduler › Cancellation and shutdown | After the drain, the executor cancels every background operation and runs jobs until each reports completion. |
| Z2-N-ARCH-432 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture/CPUFrameGraph.md`:586 | Architecture — CPU frame graph and scheduler › Cancellation and shutdown | The job system's destructor then wakes every thread and joins them in reverse dependency order: the render-submit thread first (it consumes the workers' output), then the workers, which are peers, in descending index order. |
| Z2-N-ARCH-433 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture/CPUFrameGraph.md`:592 | Architecture — CPU frame graph and scheduler › Profiling | The on-screen profiler's contention diagnostics (steal rate, idle time per worker, queue high-water marks) are computed from the counters of bullet 428 and nothing else. |
| Z2-N-ARCH-434 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture/CPUFrameGraph.md`:72 | Architecture — CPU frame graph and scheduler › Frame structure › Audio update | Audio is updated by one epilogue stage node per world, `Audio.Update@W<tag>`, registered through `Zenith_WorldAttachContext::AddEpilogueStage`, which drains its world's `Zenith_AudioCommandQueue` (declared as a write), reads simulation state only through its declared access and updates that world's audio state alone; the module's engine-wide stage `Audio.Mix` (bullet 442), ordered after every `Audio.Update@W<tag>`, hands the device thread one mixer snapshot per frame through a bounded lock-free channel; no simulation node touches the audio device thread's state. |
| Z2-N-ARCH-435 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture/CPUFrameGraph.md`:73 | Architecture — CPU frame graph and scheduler › Frame structure › Audio update | The audio device thread never waits on, locks against or allocates for any job or engine thread; a missing snapshot makes it mix the previous one again. |
| Z2-N-ARCH-436 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture/CPUFrameGraph.md`:522 | Architecture — CPU frame graph and scheduler › Execution › Simulation clock | `m_uMaxCatchUpTicks` is filled by the composition root from the configuration key `sim.maxCatchUpTicks`, whose value is the budget row "Bounded catch-up fixed steps per frame" (Z2-B-JOBS-002, pending, allocated by the Stage 1 per-system budget ticket for JOBS) written into the platform configuration layer; a value of 0 stops startup with exit code 2 naming the key. |
| Z2-N-ARCH-437 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture/CPUFrameGraph.md`:63 | Architecture — CPU frame graph and scheduler › Frame structure › The frame prologue | The frame prologue is exactly the eight main-thread steps above (step 1 including its step 1b), in that order, with no frame-graph job running; it is not an executor subgraph, and every document that places work "in the frame prologue" means one of these steps. |
| Z2-N-ARCH-438 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture/CPUFrameGraph.md`:64 | Architecture — CPU frame graph and scheduler › Frame structure › The frame prologue | The frame prologue precedes every world's first tick boundary of its frame and is not itself a tick boundary; tick boundaries 2 to T of a catch-up frame are not prologues, and no prologue step runs at them. |
| Z2-N-ARCH-439 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture/CPUFrameGraph.md`:65 | Architecture — CPU frame graph and scheduler › Frame structure › The frame prologue | The EPILOGUE subgraph has two stages: stage 12 holds every world's extractors and epilogue stages, which run concurrently as their declared accesses allow; stage 13 holds the engine-wide `Render.Seal` node, which `Zenith_WorldSimulation::EmitInto` orders after every extractor by generated orderings and not after epilogue stages, because an epilogue stage writes nothing the render world holds. |
| Z2-N-ARCH-440 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture/CPUFrameGraph.md`:339 | Architecture — CPU frame graph and scheduler › Frame graph builder › Declarations at the jobs level | A failed `Zenith_FrameGraphBuilder::Compile` logs every diagnostic at the point of failure and keeps them, sorted by code then node, for `GetDiagnostics`, which returns them until the builder is destroyed; the returned `Zenith_FrameGraphError` carries the first diagnostic's code and the count and no text. |
| Z2-N-ARCH-441 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture/CPUFrameGraph.md`:87 | Architecture — CPU frame graph and scheduler › Frame structure › Engine stage nodes | `ZenithRuntime` registers `Assets.Service` in every configuration, headless included, as the one engine-wide EPILOGUE stage-12 node, declaring a write of `Zenith_AssetRegistry` alone, so no world node orders against it, and its body returns once it has spent `asset.service.frameBudgetUs` of node time in the frame. |
| Z2-N-ARCH-442 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture/CPUFrameGraph.md`:88 | Architecture — CPU frame graph and scheduler › Frame structure › Engine stage nodes | A module's engine-wide EPILOGUE stage, registered with `Zenith_ModuleStartContext::AddEngineEpilogueStage` (at most one per module, journaled and undone with the module), is a group-0 stage-13 node compiled into every frame schedule from the next compile on, in every configuration, headless included and with no world attached; its builder accepts a write of one world-independent resource alone, and the schedule compiler orders it after every world's epilogue stage named `m_szAfterWorldStage` through generated `After` edges, so a world attached in this frame's prologue is simply absent from its edges until its first stage runs. |
| Z2-N-ARCH-443 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture/CPUFrameGraph.md`:198 | Architecture — CPU frame graph and scheduler › Job system › Worker loop | In a tool host alone (the editor and the cook, whose composition roots create their job system with `Zenith_JobSystemDesc::m_bAcceptToolBackground`; `ZENITH_TOOLS_HOST` is a preset matrix value and selects no code), a background job submitted at the tools-host priority `JOB_PRIORITY_BACKGROUND_TOOL` may run past the 2 ms slice of bullet 393 (a lightmap tile, a generator region, a BVH build, a whole partition plan); at most `m_uMaxConcurrentToolBackground` workers run such jobs at once (default min(max(1, ⌊N / 2⌋), `m_uMaxConcurrentBackground`) for N workers), so frame work keeps at least half the workers plus the main thread, `jobs.bg.slice_overruns` does not count them, and a job system created without that field refuses it with `JOBS_ERROR_INVALID_DESC` (orchestrator ruling X350 (a), human may override; the API is `Zenith2/Docs/Design/JOBS/PublicAPI.md` → "Zenith_JobSystem header"). |
| Z2-N-ARCH-471 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture/FramePhases.md`:9 | Architecture — Frame phases and authority › Settled rules and ownership | Every simulation tick of every world runs the phases of the table "The twelve phases" in that order, and no simulation work of a world runs outside a phase, a sync point or the tick boundary. |
| Z2-N-ARCH-472 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture/FramePhases.md`:10 | Architecture — Frame phases and authority › Settled rules and ownership | Nodes inside one phase run in parallel as far as their declared accesses allow; the phase boundary is the only ordering between nodes of different phases other than explicit `Before`/`After` constraints, which cannot contradict it. |
| Z2-N-ARCH-473 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture/FramePhases.md`:66 | Architecture — Frame phases and authority › The twelve phases › Phase-specific rules | Behaviours declare exactly one of `PHASE_EARLY_SCRIPTS` or `PHASE_LATE_SCRIPTS` and run on the world's main Luau VM with main-thread affinity (Zenith2/Docs/Scripting.md → "Behaviours and chunk systems"). |
| Z2-N-ARCH-474 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture/FramePhases.md`:69 | Architecture — Frame phases and authority › The twelve phases › Phase-specific rules | Phase 1 reads the platform's input events and the per-player input contexts and writes action state; during replay it reads the replay log's input commands for the tick instead of platform events, and in both cases it appends the tick's input commands to the replay log (Architecture.md → "Multiplayer foundations"). |
| Z2-N-ARCH-475 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture/FramePhases.md`:70 | Architecture — Frame phases and authority › The twelve phases › Phase-specific rules | Phase 3 extracts root motion into root-motion deltas and never applies it to a transform; application happens in phase 4 under the arbitration rules. |
| Z2-N-ARCH-476 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture/FramePhases.md`:71 | Architecture — Frame phases and authority › The twelve phases › Phase-specific rules | Phases 11 and 12 are read-only for component properties and for every world resource except a phase-11 snapshot buffer ([Z2-N-ARCH-731]): no property may declare authority 11 or 12. |
| Z2-N-ARCH-477 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture/FramePhases.md`:74 | Architecture — Frame phases and authority › The twelve phases › Phase-specific rules | A save requested by a command is serialized in the phase 11 of the tick in which the request is played back, from the state as it stands after phase 10's sync point; the serialized bytes are handed to an asynchronous writer, and the write's completion publishes at a tick boundary (Zenith2/Docs/Scripting.md → "Merge-time capacity and asynchronous results"). |
| Z2-N-ARCH-478 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture/FramePhases.md`:75 | Architecture — Frame phases and authority › The twelve phases › Phase-specific rules | Phase 9 delivers the event queue merged at the sync points of phases 1–8 of the same tick; events emitted in phases 9 and 10 are merged at their sync points and delivered in phase 9 of the next tick, and delivery order is merge order. |
| Z2-N-ARCH-479 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture/FramePhases.md`:112 | Architecture — Frame phases and authority › Sync points and the tick boundary › End-of-tick playback | A sync point is a `FRAME_NODE_KIND_SYNC_POINT` node that runs after every node of its phase and before every node of the next phase of the same world, and it performs the playback steps above in the order listed. |
| Z2-N-ARCH-480 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture/FramePhases.md`:113 | Architecture — Frame phases and authority › Sync points and the tick boundary › End-of-tick playback | Chunk compaction and any other change of chunk layout happen only at sync points and at the end-of-tick playback, never while a phase's nodes run. |
| Z2-N-ARCH-481 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture/FramePhases.md`:114 | Architecture — Frame phases and authority › Sync points and the tick boundary › End-of-tick playback | A tick's playback is identical for identical inputs regardless of worker count and chunk layout, because every playback step iterates in merge order and never in completion or storage order (Z2-D-019). |
| Z2-N-ARCH-482 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture/FramePhases.md`:120 | Architecture — Frame phases and authority › Per-field authority › Rules | Every component property declares an authority: one phase from 1 to 10, or `PHASE_COMMANDS_ONLY`; a property without an authority (directly or through its component's default) fails registration with `REFLECT_ERROR_MISSING_AUTHORITY`. |
| Z2-N-ARCH-483 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture/FramePhases.md`:121 | Architecture — Frame phases and authority › Per-field authority › Rules | A system may declare a direct write only of properties whose authority is the system's own phase; any other write goes through a command (`Zenith_CommandBuffer::SetProperty` or a module command), applied at the next sync point. |
| Z2-N-ARCH-484 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture/FramePhases.md`:122 | Architecture — Frame phases and authority › Per-field authority › Rules | Two systems declaring a direct write of the same property in the same phase are a schedule-compile error, unless the property's component declares an arbitration rule covering it. |
| Z2-N-ARCH-485 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture/FramePhases.md`:123 | Architecture — Frame phases and authority › Per-field authority › Rules | A property with authority `PHASE_COMMANDS_ONLY` has no direct writer in any phase; every change arrives as a command. |
| Z2-N-ARCH-486 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture/FramePhases.md`:124 | Architecture — Frame phases and authority › Per-field authority › Rules | Reads are not restricted by authority: any system of any phase may declare a read of any property, subject to the cross-entity rule for script chunk systems (Zenith2/Docs/Scripting.md → "Transaction scope and the entity boundary"). |
| Z2-N-ARCH-487 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture/FramePhases.md`:125 | Architecture — Frame phases and authority › Per-field authority › Rules | Read and write sets and authority are checked per property when the schedule compiles, with one granularity; a component-level declaration expands to all its properties before any check. |
| Z2-N-ARCH-488 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture/FramePhases.md`:126 | Architecture — Frame phases and authority › Per-field authority › Rules | World resources declare an owning phase at registration the same way, one phase from 1 to 11 (Architecture/ECSModel.md bullet 731), and a direct write of a world resource from another phase is `SCHEDULE_ERROR_RESOURCE_AUTHORITY`. |
| Z2-N-ARCH-489 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture/FramePhases.md`:178 | Architecture — Frame phases and authority › Commands › Command semantics | A command recorded in phase p is applied at the sync point after phase p, before any node of phase p+1 runs; when the property's owning phase runs after p in the same tick, the owning phase may overwrite the commanded value, and that outcome is deterministic. |
| Z2-N-ARCH-490 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture/FramePhases.md`:179 | Architecture — Frame phases and authority › Commands › Command semantics | A command naming a stale or foreign entity handle is dropped at playback with the diagnostic counter `ecs.commands_stale_delta` incremented and a log entry naming the command and issuer; it never asserts, because scripts can produce it. |
| Z2-N-ARCH-491 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture/FramePhases.md`:180 | Architecture — Frame phases and authority › Commands › Command semantics | `AddComponent` on an entity marked `pending destroy` is dropped with the counter `ecs.commands_on_destroyed_delta`; `SetProperty` on such an entity is applied, because the entity stays readable until the end of its tick. |
| Z2-N-ARCH-492 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture/FramePhases.md`:181 | Architecture — Frame phases and authority › Commands › Command semantics | Module command kinds are registered with the type registry (kind ID, payload type, playback function, phase restrictions), and their playback functions run on the sync-point node, single-threaded per world, in merge order, except that the records of a kind registered with `COMMAND_KIND_FLAG_PLAY_LAST` play after every other step-7 record of the playback, in merge order among themselves. |
| Z2-N-ARCH-493 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture/FramePhases.md`:214 | Architecture — Frame phases and authority › Arbitration rules › Writer sources and default precedence | The default precedence is cinematic > ragdoll > physics > physics2D > animation > script; a component's arbitration declaration uses it unless it lists its own order, which names each source at most once. |
| Z2-N-ARCH-494 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture/FramePhases.md`:216 | Architecture — Frame phases and authority › Arbitration rules › Writer sources and default precedence | Every system that writes an arbitrated property declares its `m_eWriterSource`; a writer with `WRITER_SOURCE_NONE` is `SCHEDULE_ERROR_WRITER_SOURCE_MISSING`, and two writers of the same property with the same source in the same phase are `SCHEDULE_ERROR_DUPLICATE_WRITER_SOURCE`. |
| Z2-N-ARCH-495 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture/FramePhases.md`:274 | Architecture — Frame phases and authority › Arbitration rules › The arbitration merge | Arbitration is itself a deterministic merge step: its result for a row depends only on that row's staged values, weights and the declared order, never on which worker or in which order the writers ran. |

| Z2-N-ARCH-497 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture/FramePhases.md`:276 | Architecture — Frame phases and authority › Arbitration rules › The arbitration merge | Readers of an arbitrated property in the writers' phase read the arbitration node's result, never a staging column; staging columns are private to their writer and to the arbitration node; a writer of the property that also reads it runs before the arbitration node and reads the committed value (the property as the previous merge left it, through `Zenith_ChunkView::GetCommittedColumn`, `Zenith2/Docs/Design/ECS/Queries.md` → "Views and validation"), never its own or another source's staged value. |

| Z2-N-ARCH-505 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture/FramePhases.md`:90 | Architecture — Frame phases and authority › Sync points and the tick boundary › The tick boundary | The tick boundary node of each world that is not paused performs steps 1 to 4 above in that order, before any node of that world's phase 1 runs. |
| Z2-N-ARCH-506 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture/FramePhases.md`:353 | Architecture — Frame phases and authority › Schedule-compile errors | Each schedule-compile diagnostic is logged at the point of failure and kept in the world's `Zenith_SystemRegistry`, whose `GetDiagnostics` returns the diagnostics of the latest `Freeze` or `EmitInto` until the next one starts; the `Zenith_ScheduleError` result carries the first diagnostic's code, the world tag and the count, and no text. |
| Z2-N-ARCH-507 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture/FramePhases.md`:91 | Architecture — Frame phases and authority › Sync points and the tick boundary › The tick boundary | A paused world's tick boundary performs none of steps 1 to 4: no asynchronous result is published into it, its tick index does not advance, and results that complete while it is paused stay queued and publish in completion-ID order at the first tick boundary after it resumes, so a publication's recorded tick is always a tick that ran. |
| Z2-N-ARCH-508 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture/FramePhases.md`:92 | Architecture — Frame phases and authority › Sync points and the tick boundary › The tick boundary | Tick-boundary handlers receive the property-only `Zenith_TickBoundaryAccess` and no structural member; their property writes change no structural epoch, query structure version or query cache. |
| Z2-N-ARCH-509 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture/FramePhases.md`:215 | Architecture — Frame phases and authority › Arbitration rules › Writer sources and default precedence | `WRITER_SOURCE_PHYSICS_2D` (schema name `"Physics2D"`) is the writer source of `TwoD.Physics2DSyncTransforms`, which writes `Zenith.LocalTransform` properties 1 and 2 in phase 6, under either outcome of D-SEL-2DPHYS (open, Stage 2); existing values never move, and the two physics sources never write one entity: TWOD skips, and removes, the 2D body of an entity that carries `Zenith.Physics.RigidBody` (`Zenith2/Docs/Design/TWOD/Simulation.md` → "Physics 2D write-back and sync"). |
| Z2-N-ARCH-541 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture/LifecycleCommands.md`:10 | Architecture — Lifecycle commands › Settled rules and ownership | Every lifecycle command is recorded into a command buffer (or, for world transition and shutdown, requested from the runtime), is applied at the publication point of the table "Lifecycle command summary", and never takes effect immediately at the call. |
| Z2-N-ARCH-542 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture/LifecycleCommands.md`:11 | Architecture — Lifecycle commands › Settled rules and ownership | Lifecycle commands are dispatched to listeners at step 7 of sync-point playback, in merge order (except that the records of a kind registered with `COMMAND_KIND_FLAG_PLAY_LAST` play after every other step-7 record of the playback, in merge order among themselves), on the sync-point node of the world, so every listener sees the same sequence on every run. |
| Z2-N-ARCH-543 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture/LifecycleCommands.md`:66 | Architecture — Lifecycle commands › Listener interface | Listeners are registered per world by the composition root in module registration order and are invoked in that order for each event; the order is part of the determinism contract. |
| Z2-N-ARCH-544 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture/LifecycleCommands.md`:68 | Architecture — Lifecycle commands › Listener interface | A listener changes world state only through the `Zenith_WorldPlaybackAccess` it receives (property writes allowed at playback) and through follow-up commands, which are applied at the next sync point; it never records into the buffer being played back. |
| Z2-N-ARCH-545 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture/LifecycleCommands.md`:88 | Architecture — Lifecycle commands › Spawn | A spawned entity is created at the sync point after the issuing phase and is visible to every node of the next phase; its entity handle and stable simulation ID are assigned at playback in merge order, so they are identical on every run (Zenith2/Docs/Scripting.md → "Determinism rules and randomness"). |
| Z2-N-ARCH-546 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture/LifecycleCommands.md`:89 | Architecture — Lifecycle commands › Spawn | A spawn rejected at merge-time capacity (entity capacity, the per-entity snapshot cap of its issuer) creates nothing, consumes no ID, and resolves its ticket to the null handle; commands naming a null-resolved ticket are dropped with the counter `ecs.commands_stale_delta`. |
| Z2-N-ARCH-547 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture/LifecycleCommands.md`:93 | Architecture — Lifecycle commands › Destroy | `Destroy` marks the entity `pending destroy` at the sync point after the issuing phase and fires `LIFECYCLE_EVENT_DESTROY_PENDING` there. |
| Z2-N-ARCH-548 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture/LifecycleCommands.md`:96 | Architecture — Lifecycle commands › Destroy | Render extraction skips entities marked `pending destroy`, so a destroyed entity is never drawn in the frame whose tick destroyed it. |
| Z2-N-ARCH-549 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture/LifecycleCommands.md`:97 | Architecture — Lifecycle commands › Destroy | Destroying an entity destroys its hierarchy children in the same playback, in depth-first order of stable simulation ID, and each child is marked and removed like its parent. |
| Z2-N-ARCH-550 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture/LifecycleCommands.md`:125 | Architecture — Lifecycle commands › Teleport | A teleport resets physics velocity, interpolation, the animation root and the render motion-vector history of the entity and of every descendant, so no system observes a velocity or a smear from the jump. |
| Z2-N-ARCH-551 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture/LifecycleCommands.md`:126 | Architecture — Lifecycle commands › Teleport | A teleport of an entity in ragdoll moves every ragdoll body rigidly with the root and zeroes their velocities unless `TELEPORT_FLAG_KEEP_VELOCITY`, and the ragdoll blend state is kept. |
| Z2-N-ARCH-552 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture/LifecycleCommands.md`:158 | Architecture — Lifecycle commands › Ragdoll enter and recover | Ragdoll enter creates the ragdoll bodies from the current pose at the playback of the sync point after the issuing phase; a command issued in phases 1–4 is therefore simulated by phase 5 of the same tick, and one issued in phases 5–10 by phase 5 of the next tick. |
| Z2-N-ARCH-553 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture/LifecycleCommands.md`:161 | Architecture — Lifecycle commands › Ragdoll enter and recover | An enter received while a recover blend runs (or a recover while an enter blend runs) reverses the blend from its current weight, never jumping to 0 or 1. |
| Z2-N-ARCH-554 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture/LifecycleCommands.md`:201 | Architecture — Lifecycle commands › Cinematic start, interrupt and skip | A cinematic takes arbitration precedence over every other writer source while it plays, and on end, interrupt or skip it restores gameplay state from the pre-cinematic snapshot of owned fields at the next tick boundary. |
| Z2-N-ARCH-555 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture/LifecycleCommands.md`:202 | Architecture — Lifecycle commands › Cinematic start, interrupt and skip | Properties written by a cinematic track and not marked `restoreAfterCinematic` keep the cinematic's final value at restore, so a sequence can leave a character where it ended; the choice is declared per property in the component schema or registration, never at run time. |
| Z2-N-ARCH-556 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture/LifecycleCommands.md`:203 | Architecture — Lifecycle commands › Cinematic start, interrupt and skip | A bound entity destroyed during a cinematic is dropped from its bindings at the destroy playback, its snapshot entry is released, and the sequence continues for the remaining bindings. |
| Z2-N-ARCH-557 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture/LifecycleCommands.md`:230 | Architecture — Lifecycle commands › World transition | A new world is loaded in isolation and swapped in only in step 7 of a frame prologue, before every world's first tick boundary of that frame; no tick ever observes a half-loaded world or both worlds' state mixed. |
| Z2-N-ARCH-558 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture/LifecycleCommands.md`:233 | Architecture — Lifecycle commands › World transition | Asynchronous results addressed to a world that has been swapped out are discarded when they complete and never published into any world, and the discard is counted in `world.async_discarded`, published by the world host alone: the swapped-out world's `Zenith_AsyncResultTable` is retired, not destroyed, and outlives the world, together with the runtime sinks producers complete through, until no producer can still complete into it (`GetInFlightCount()` is 0, or shutdown after every job has run), so a completion after the world's destruction reaches the retired table, which discards and counts it (`Zenith2/Docs/Design/GAME/WorldHost.md` → "Retired world asynchronous results"); a world destroyed with results in flight other than at shutdown retires its table the same way. |
| Z2-N-ARCH-559 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture/LifecycleCommands.md`:234 | Architecture — Lifecycle commands › World transition | The memory governor reserves the world-transition peak (both worlds resident) before loading starts, and a refused reservation fails the transition request with `RUNTIME_ERROR_TRANSITION_PEAK_REFUSED` instead of loading (Architecture.md → "Memory governor"). |
| Z2-N-ARCH-560 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture/LifecycleCommands.md`:238 | Architecture — Lifecycle commands › Shutdown | `RequestShutdown` takes effect when the current frame-loop iteration returns: every tick that iteration started drains (every phase runs), the commands of its last sync points are flushed by their own playback, no further tick starts, and each world is torn down in reverse phase order (extraction state, snapshot state, scripts, events, the modules' per-world state in reverse canonical order (AI, post-physics, physics, movement, animation), transforms and the scene world state, input, then the world's asynchronous result table), followed by the process shutdown order of Zenith2/Docs/Contracts.md → "Threading and concurrency contract"; the scripts entry keeps the world's asynchronous result table, which is destroyed, or retired while results are in flight ([Z2-N-ARCH-558]), only after the modules' per-world state, the scene world state and the input resources, because producers complete into it from their `DetachWorld` and from background jobs, and the scene world state, which owns the lifecycle dispatcher the modules' listeners are registered with, is destroyed after the modules' `DetachWorld`. |
| Z2-N-ARCH-561 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture/LifecycleCommands.md`:239 | Architecture — Lifecycle commands › Shutdown | No lifecycle command recorded in the final tick is dropped silently: commands recorded after the final sync point of a world are counted in `world.commands_after_shutdown` and logged. |
| Z2-N-ARCH-562 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture/LifecycleCommands.md`:251 | Architecture — Lifecycle commands › Scenario tests | Every scenario in the table "Lifecycle scenario tests" exists under the name given and asserts every listed condition. |
| Z2-N-ARCH-563 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture/LifecycleCommands.md`:266 | Architecture — Lifecycle commands › Scenario tests | Every per-command test named in the table "Lifecycle command summary" exists and asserts that command's publication point and first observer as the table states. |
| Z2-N-ARCH-564 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture/LifecycleCommands.md`:159 | Architecture — Lifecycle commands › Ragdoll enter and recover | Ragdoll enter blends ownership from animation to physics over `m_fBlendSeconds` of simulated time, advanced in phase 6 of each tick. |
| Z2-N-ARCH-565 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture/LifecycleCommands.md`:160 | Architecture — Lifecycle commands › Ragdoll enter and recover | Ragdoll recover blends ownership from physics back to animation over its own `m_fBlendSeconds` and removes the ragdoll bodies at the playback after the blend reaches 1. |

| Z2-N-ARCH-567 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture/LifecycleCommands.md`:232 | Architecture — Lifecycle commands › World transition | Carry-over entities are serialized from the old world after its final end-of-tick playback and deserialized into the new world with new handles and unchanged persistent IDs before the new world's first tick boundary. |
| Z2-N-ARCH-568 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture/LifecycleCommands.md`:252 | Architecture — Lifecycle commands › Scenario tests | Each scenario is recorded once per backend and its log replayed with 1, 2, 8 and all workers, and the replays produce bit-identical per-tick world-state hashes on that backend; each suite carries its own replay-matrix case (`Test_Scenario_ReplayMatrix`, `Test_Scenario_ReplayMatrixCharacters`, `Test_Scenario_ReplayMatrixOpenWorld`), and the COHERENT case of `Test_Scenario_SaveAtSnapshotDuringStreaming` is not part of it. |
| Z2-N-ARCH-569 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture/LifecycleCommands.md`:94 | Architecture — Lifecycle commands › Destroy | An entity marked `pending destroy` stays fully readable and queryable for the rest of its tick. |
| Z2-N-ARCH-570 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture/LifecycleCommands.md`:95 | Architecture — Lifecycle commands › Destroy | The end-of-tick playback removes every pending-destroy entity, fires `LIFECYCLE_EVENT_DESTROYED` and increments its slot generation, so its handles are stale from the next tick. |
| Z2-N-ARCH-571 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture/LifecycleCommands.md`:98 | Architecture — Lifecycle commands › Destroy | The parent relation of entities is ECS's (held by `Zenith_EntityTable` and set by spawn playback and changed only by the `SET_PARENT` record ECS playback applies), so the destroy cascade of bullet 549 is ECS playback; ZenithScene's hierarchy reads the relation through `Zenith_EntityTable` and never stores a second copy. |
| Z2-N-ARCH-572 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture/LifecycleCommands.md`:67 | Architecture — Lifecycle commands › Listener interface | `LIFECYCLE_EVENT_ORIGIN_SHIFTED` (value 10) is dispatched by the `Scene.ShiftWorldOrigin` playback at the end of step 7 of its sync point, after every transform and flagged property is shifted, with a null entity and `m_pPayload` pointing at the played `Zenith_WorldOriginShift` for the duration of the dispatch, so a module holding position-valued state (the Physics module's backend world) shifts it at the same sync point as the transforms (`Zenith2/Docs/Design/SCENE/ModuleSeams.md` → "Origin shifted lifecycle event"). |
| Z2-N-ARCH-591 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture/DataDefinedComponents.md`:9 | Architecture — Data-defined components › Settled rules and ownership | `*.schema.json` is the one front end for game-defined component types; no second schema language, no Luau-side type declaration and no editor-only format defines a component. |
| Z2-N-ARCH-592 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture/DataDefinedComponents.md`:11 | Architecture — Data-defined components › Settled rules and ownership | A data-defined type is registered with `ZenithReflect` alongside the engine's C++-registered types, and no consumer — serialization, the inspector, migration, `.d.luau` generation, chunk-system column access, phase and authority checks, network replication — distinguishes the two (Zenith2/Docs/Contracts.md → "Reflection"). |
| Z2-N-ARCH-593 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture/DataDefinedComponents.md`:124 | Architecture — Data-defined components › The schema file › Field type grammar | The type ID of a data-defined type is `Zenith_Hash64` of its `stableName` (the same derivation as a C++-registered type's stable name, Zenith2/Docs/Contracts.md → "Reflection"), so renaming `component` changes no ID and changing `stableName` is a new type. |
| Z2-N-ARCH-594 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture/DataDefinedComponents.md`:125 | Architecture — Data-defined components › The schema file › Field type grammar | A field ID is never reused: removing a field adds its ID to `retiredFieldIds` in the same change, and a schema whose field ID appears in `retiredFieldIds`, or whose retired list shrinks relative to the committed previous version, fails validation; the committed previous version is the game's schema lock file `Games/<Name>/Schemas/schemas.lock.json`, written by the cook's `schema-lock` verb and committed with the schemas. |
| Z2-N-ARCH-595 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture/DataDefinedComponents.md`:126 | Architecture — Data-defined components › The schema file › Field type grammar | Containers are stored out of line and never in a component's inline bytes; nested containers (`container<container<…>>`, or a struct containing a container used as a container element) are rejected. |
| Z2-N-ARCH-596 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture/DataDefinedComponents.md`:127 | Architecture — Data-defined components › The schema file › Field type grammar | The schema limits are: at most 128 fields per component or struct, at most 1,024 inline bytes per component, struct nesting depth at most 4, `array` count at most 64, `maxCount` at most 65,535, and file size at most 1 MiB; exceeding any limit fails validation with the limit named. |
| Z2-N-ARCH-597 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture/DataDefinedComponents.md`:135 | Architecture — Data-defined components › The schema file › Inline layout | Serialization, saves, cooked data and replication never depend on the computed inline layout: they read and write fields by field ID through reflection (Z2-D-038), so a layout change from adding a field requires no data migration of its own. |
| Z2-N-ARCH-598 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture/DataDefinedComponents.md`:198 | Architecture — Data-defined components › Registration at game load › Validation errors | Every schema error names the source file, the JSON key path and the rule broken, in the form `<file>: <keyPath>: <message>`, and the cook reports every error of every schema in one run. |
| Z2-N-ARCH-599 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture/DataDefinedComponents.md`:199 | Architecture — Data-defined components › Registration at game load › Validation errors | An invalid schema fails the cook, and an invalid `schemas.zreg` at run time stops the game before its first tick with the error list and exit code 3; a data-defined type never silently degrades or registers partially. |
| Z2-N-ARCH-600 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture/DataDefinedComponents.md`:200 | Architecture — Data-defined components › Registration at game load › Validation errors | Unknown top-level or field keys that are not prefixed `x-` fail validation with `SCHEMA_ERROR_UNKNOWN_KEY` in the cook, and every tool that rewrites a schema preserves `x-` keys byte for byte in position. |
| Z2-N-ARCH-601 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture/DataDefinedComponents.md`:212 | Architecture — Data-defined components › Storage in every backend | Every ECS backend stores data-defined components natively in its own storage and passes the full `ZenithECS_Conformance` suite with a fixture that registers the same component once from C++ and once from a schema, asserting identical observable behaviour. |
| Z2-N-ARCH-602 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture/DataDefinedComponents.md`:213 | Architecture — Data-defined components › Storage in every backend | Fields whose type carries lifetime (asset handles and containers) are constructed, copied and destroyed through the generated hooks in every backend, so storage never copies such a field's bytes without its hook (Zenith2/Docs/Contracts.md → "Handle kinds"). |
| Z2-N-ARCH-603 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture/DataDefinedComponents.md`:222 | Architecture — Data-defined components › Generated Luau types | `.d.luau` definitions are generated from the frozen registry — engine types, the selected modules' types and the game's schemas together — by ZenithCook's `luau-types` step into `Zenith2/out/<checkout-hash>/Generated/Luau/<Game>/` (Zenith2/Docs/Scripting.md → "Modules, bindings and generated types"). |
| Z2-N-ARCH-604 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture/DataDefinedComponents.md`:225 | Architecture — Data-defined components › Generated Luau types | A schema's `component` and field `name` strings are the Luau names verbatim; field `id`s never appear in Luau source, so renaming a field is a script-visible change that the schema's version bump and migration record. |
| Z2-N-ARCH-605 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture/DataDefinedComponents.md`:247 | Architecture — Data-defined components › Migration | Authoring documents that contain a data-defined component are migrated through the authoring-document migration framework (preview, backup, journaled write, unknown-field preservation, per-version fixtures), and saves are migrated forward at load through the same `Zenith_MigrateFieldSet`; cooked data is re-cooked, never migrated. |
| Z2-N-ARCH-606 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture/DataDefinedComponents.md`:248 | Architecture — Data-defined components › Migration | Every `version` increment ships its migration entry and a migration fixture under `Games/<Name>/Schemas/Fixtures/<Component>/v<from>.json` whose migrated output is checked by the game's schema test run in the `Test` step. |
| Z2-N-ARCH-607 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture/DataDefinedComponents.md`:249 | Architecture — Data-defined components › Migration | A migration that cannot produce a valid target value (a `retype` whose conversion fails, a `script` that raises) fails the migration of that document or save with `Zenith_MigrationError` naming the component, field, versions and entity, and leaves the input unchanged. |
| Z2-N-ARCH-608 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture/DataDefinedComponents.md`:255 | Architecture — Data-defined components › Required tests | Every test in the table "Data-defined component tests" exists under the name given and asserts the case given. |
| Z2-N-ARCH-609 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture/DataDefinedComponents.md`:214 | Architecture — Data-defined components › Storage in every backend | Interned string IDs are plain values: the intern table is owned by `ZenithRuntime` for the process lifetime, never shrinks while any world exists, and is serialized by the strings' text, so a string field needs no hook and a row containing only plain values and string IDs may be copied bytewise. |
| Z2-N-ARCH-610 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture/DataDefinedComponents.md`:128 | Architecture — Data-defined components › The schema file › Field type grammar | A schema with `kind: event` carries no `authority`, `arbitration`, `networkAuthority`, `replicated` or `restoreAfterCinematic` key at any level (each is `SCHEMA_ERROR_UNKNOWN_KEY` for events) and no `container<T>` field; its fields' `serialized` flag decides what the replay log records of each event. |
| Z2-N-ARCH-611 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture/DataDefinedComponents.md`:223 | Architecture — Data-defined components › Generated Luau types | The generated definitions are deterministic: types in byte order of stable name, fields in ascending field ID. |
| Z2-N-ARCH-612 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture/DataDefinedComponents.md`:224 | Architecture — Data-defined components › Generated Luau types | Generated `.d.luau` files are never hand-edited and never tracked. |
| Z2-N-ARCH-613 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture/DataDefinedComponents.md`:197 | Architecture — Data-defined components › Registration at game load › Validation errors | A failed `RegisterSchemaJson` or `RegisterSchemaBlob` logs each diagnostic at the point of failure and keeps it in the registry, whose `GetSchemaDiagnostics` returns the diagnostics of the latest schema registration until the next one starts; the `Zenith_SchemaError` result carries the first code and the count, and no text. |
| Z2-N-ARCH-614 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture/DataDefinedComponents.md`:10 | Architecture — Data-defined components › Settled rules and ownership | Component types the engine and its modules ship (for example the lighting, camera and renderable-mesh components) are C++-registered structs registered from their library's registration function with explicit property IDs, never engine-content schemas; `*.schema.json` defines game types alone, under the game's `<Game>.<Name>` stable names. |
| Z2-N-ARCH-641 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture.md`:723 | Zenith2 Architecture › Reflection and runtime type registration › One reflection system | Every reflected type — engine and module types registered from C++, and game types registered from schemas — lives in one `Zenith_TypeRegistry` owned by the composition root, and every consumer reads types only through it. |
| Z2-N-ARCH-642 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture.md`:724 | Zenith2 Architecture › Reflection and runtime type registration › One reflection system | Registration is explicit and ordered (engine libraries, then modules in canonical order, then schemas) and completes with `Freeze`; nothing registers through static initialisation, and after `Freeze` the registry is immutable except through the hot-reload window of bullet 650 and safe to read from any thread (Architecture/CompositionRoot.md → "Startup sequence"). |
| Z2-N-ARCH-643 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture.md`:887 | Zenith2 Architecture › Reflection and runtime type registration › Registration API | Every consumer of type metadata — serialization, the inspector, migration, `.d.luau` generation, chunk-system column access, schedule authority checks, network replication and render extraction — works from `Zenith_TypeInfo` and `Zenith_PropertyInfo` alone and never branches on `m_eOrigin` (Z2-D-020). |
| Z2-N-ARCH-644 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture.md`:888 | Zenith2 Architecture › Reflection and runtime type registration › Registration API | Serialization reads and writes property values by property ID through the registry, never by copying an object's memory layout, so a layout change never changes a serialized format (Z2-D-038). |
| Z2-N-ARCH-645 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture.md`:889 | Zenith2 Architecture › Reflection and runtime type registration › Registration API | A type whose properties carry lifetime (asset handles and containers; interned string IDs are plain values) registers non-null lifecycle hooks, and every store of such a type (ECS columns, shadows, snapshots, save buffers) copies, moves and destroys through them. |
| Z2-N-ARCH-646 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture.md`:890 | Zenith2 Architecture › Reflection and runtime type registration › Registration API | `Freeze` validates the whole registry in one pass — ID uniqueness, stable-name uniqueness, missing authorities, arbitration validity, migration-chain completeness, lifecycle-hook presence — and reports every error; a frozen registry never contains a type that failed validation. |
| Z2-N-ARCH-647 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture.md`:891 | Zenith2 Architecture › Reflection and runtime type registration › Registration API | Looking up type metadata by `Zenith_TypeID` is data lookup and is allowed; the registry never returns a service object, and no subsystem uses it to find another subsystem (Zenith2/Docs/Contracts.md → "No mutable global state"). |
