| Z2-N-ARCH-649 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture.md`:929 | Zenith2 Architecture › Reflection and runtime type registration › Reflection error codes | The tests `Test_TypeRegistry_EachErrorCodeReported`, `Test_TypeRegistry_FrozenRejectsRegistration`, `Test_TypeRegistry_ConcurrentReadsAfterFreeze` (under `linux-tsan`), `Test_TypeRegistry_SerializationByPropertyID` and `Test_TypeRegistry_HooksBalanceReferences` exist in `Engine/ZenithReflect/Tests/` and assert what their names state. |
| Z2-N-ARCH-650 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture.md`:893 | Zenith2 Architecture › Reflection and runtime type registration › Registration API | After `Freeze`, the registry changes only inside a hot-reload window that `ZenithRuntime` opens on the main thread in step 6 of the frame prologue (Architecture/CPUFrameGraph.md → "Frame structure"; Architecture/CompositionRoot.md → "The frame loop"; Zenith2/Docs/Contracts.md → "Reflection"): the window admits replacement of `TYPE_KIND_BEHAVIOUR_STATE` types during play (Zenith2/Docs/Scripting.md → "Quarantine and hot reload") and of schema types in the editor between play sessions, and nothing else. |
| Z2-N-ARCH-651 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture.md`:894 | Zenith2 Architecture › Reflection and runtime type registration › Registration API | `EndHotReload` validates the changed types as `Freeze` does and publishes a new registry revision atomically; `Zenith_TypeInfo` pointers of the previous revision stay valid for two further frames and are then retired, and a validation failure publishes nothing and leaves the previous revision in effect. |
| Z2-N-ARCH-652 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture.md`:895 | Zenith2 Architecture › Reflection and runtime type registration › Registration API | Every reflected component, struct and event type that a module registers from C++ sets an explicit script name with `ScriptName`, unique programme-wide and derived from its C++ type name by rule 1 of Zenith2/Docs/Scripting/Bindings.md → "Name mapping from Zenith Hungarian" (for example `Zenith_RigidBody2DComponent` → `RigidBody2D`), so that two modules' types never fail `Freeze` with `REFLECT_ERROR_BAD_NAME` in a registry that holds both; MOD's integration suite registers the engine types and every module's types into one registry and freezes it, so a clash fails a gate instead of a game's start (ruling X353-SCRIPTNAMES, 2026-10-10; cross-doc item X353). |
| Z2-N-ARCH-681 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture/ECSModel.md`:16 | Architecture — ECS model › Settled rules and ownership | Every ECS operation outside a backend library is expressed through `ZenithECS` types keyed by reflection IDs (`Zenith_TypeID`, `Zenith_PropertyID`); no flecs or EnTT type, header or symbol appears outside that backend's `Internal/` directory (Zenith2/Docs/Contracts.md → "API boundary"). |
| Z2-N-ARCH-682 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture/ECSModel.md`:17 | Architecture — ECS model › Settled rules and ownership | The game selects its ECS backend at link time through `backends.ecs` in `game.json`; the conformance executable `ZenithECS_Conformance` links all three and runs every scenario on each. |
| Z2-N-ARCH-683 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture/ECSModel.md`:114 | Architecture — ECS model › Worlds and backends › The backend interface | A structural change (spawn, destroy, add or remove component) is applied only through `Zenith_WorldPlaybackAccess`, which requires a `Zenith_SyncPointToken`; systems and scripts reach structure only by recording commands. |
| Z2-N-ARCH-684 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture/ECSModel.md`:115 | Architecture — ECS model › Worlds and backends › The backend interface | A backend's factory unwinds everything it constructed when a subsequent construction step fails and returns the error (Zenith2/Docs/Contracts.md → "Threading and concurrency contract"); each construction step of each backend has a failure-injection test. |
| Z2-N-ARCH-685 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture/ECSModel.md`:210 | Architecture — ECS model › Worlds and backends › Backend storage models | Every backend presents chunks through the same `Zenith_ChunkView`: a row count, one `Zenith_RawColumn` per declared property (pointer, stride, optional row-index table), the entity handle and stable simulation ID of each row, the chunk's structural epoch and each column's change version. |
| Z2-N-ARCH-686 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture/ECSModel.md`:211 | Architecture — ECS model › Worlds and backends › Backend storage models | Chunk capacity is configurable per world (`m_uChunkCapacityRows`) and forced compaction is available to tests, so the determinism tests run the same replay with two different capacities and with compaction forced after every sync point. |
| Z2-N-ARCH-687 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture/ECSModel.md`:222 | Architecture — ECS model › Entity identity | The stable simulation ID is the identity every deterministic ordering and every script random key uses; it never depends on slot index, chunk, row or storage order (Zenith2/Docs/Scripting.md → "Determinism rules and randomness"). |
| Z2-N-ARCH-688 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture/ECSModel.md`:223 | Architecture — ECS model › Entity identity | Slot indices are allocated at spawn playback in merge order from the world's free list, lowest free index first, so slot assignment is itself deterministic; no ordering or result depends on slot index nonetheless. |
| Z2-N-ARCH-689 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture/ECSModel.md`:256 | Architecture — ECS model › Command buffers | Command buffers from parallel invocations are merged in lexicographic `Zenith_MergeKey` order — (system order, logical invocation ID = (stable simulation ID, invocation ordinal), sequence) — never in completion order. |
| Z2-N-ARCH-690 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture/ECSModel.md`:257 | Architecture — ECS model › Command buffers | A C++ system that records commands per entity records them under that entity's stable simulation ID (`Zenith_SystemContext::Commands(ulSimulationID)`), and a record not tied to an entity uses simulation ID 0 with an invocation ordinal the system derives deterministically from its inputs, never from a chunk index, worker index or completion order. |
| Z2-N-ARCH-691 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture/ECSModel.md`:258 | Architecture — ECS model › Command buffers | Command-buffer memory, including space for payloads and for the provisional handles of spawns, is reserved during the invocation, so appending a committed buffer to the world at merge never allocates and cannot fail (Zenith2/Docs/Scripting.md → "Commit and failure semantics"). |
| Z2-N-ARCH-692 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture/ECSModel.md`:259 | Architecture — ECS model › Command buffers | Every system that declares `EmitsCommands`, `EmitsEvent`, a reduction or `ConsumesMergeCapacity`, and every script system, gets a generated merge node after its system node (C++ systems without a merge function get the default merge), so all shared capacity is consumed on the merge chain; the merge nodes of one phase of one world are chained in the stage's cost-independent topological order — Kahn's algorithm over the stage's declared hazard and ordering edges, the chain itself excluded, ties broken by registration index — so a reader registered before the writer of a property it reads still schedules, capacity is consumed in chain order independent of which system finished first, and playback still applies accepted buffers in merge-key order. |
| Z2-N-ARCH-693 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture/ECSModel.md`:274 | Architecture — ECS model › Sync points and the structural epoch | A chunk's structural epoch is bumped only when structural commands are played back at a sync point (or by the end-of-tick playback), never by a property write, a commit or a merge. |
| Z2-N-ARCH-694 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture/ECSModel.md`:275 | Architecture — ECS model › Sync points and the structural epoch | Change-tracking versions are per column, separate from the structural epoch and from invocation IDs, and they advance at the merge point of the writing invocation, never at the write itself; a failed or rejected invocation advances no version. |
| Z2-N-ARCH-695 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture/ECSModel.md`:281 | Architecture — ECS model › Sync points and the structural epoch | A commit never invalidates another invocation's view: commits change column contents and change versions, which views do not validate against, and never structural epochs. |
| Z2-N-ARCH-696 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture/ECSModel.md`:351 | Architecture — ECS model › Views | Each view is scoped to the invocation ID that created it and becomes invalid when that invocation ends; `IsValid` compares the view's invocation ID with the current one and its structural epoch with the chunk's. |
| Z2-N-ARCH-697 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture/ECSModel.md`:352 | Architecture — ECS model › Views | A C++ system's use of an invalid view is a programmer error caught by `ZENITH_ASSERT` in builds with asserts on; a script's use of a retained or stale view raises a controlled Luau error in every build (Zenith2/Docs/Scripting.md → "Views epochs and misuse errors"). |
| Z2-N-ARCH-698 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture/ECSModel.md`:353 | Architecture — ECS model › Views | `GetColumn` returns a column only for properties the invocation declared, writable only for properties it declared as writes; an undeclared property yields a null column and, in builds with asserts on, an assert. |
| Z2-N-ARCH-699 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture/ECSModel.md`:354 | Architecture — ECS model › Views | String, entity-handle and asset-handle properties are read-only in chunk views and are changed through commands, and container properties are not exposed in chunk views at all; they are reached through the behaviour API's copy-on-write shadows (Zenith2/Docs/Scripting.md → "Component writes shadows and side-effect buffers"). |
| Z2-N-ARCH-700 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture/ECSModel.md`:383 | Architecture — ECS model › Queries | A query's matched-chunk list is cached and refreshed lazily at its first use after a sync point whose playback changed the world's query structure version, and never during a phase, so a system's chunk list is stable for the whole phase. |
| Z2-N-ARCH-701 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture/ECSModel.md`:384 | Architecture — ECS model › Queries | A system's queries and its access declaration agree: every property a query term names with `m_bWrite` is declared as a write of the system, and every other named property as a read, checked at `Freeze` of the system registry with `SCHEDULE_ERROR_QUERY_ACCESS_MISMATCH`. |
| Z2-N-ARCH-702 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture/ECSModel.md`:385 | Architecture — ECS model › Queries | The chunk order a query iterates is the backend's storage order and may differ between runs, capacities and backends; nothing that simulation observes depends on it, because merges, reductions and events are ordered by merge key and fixed reduction trees. |
| Z2-N-ARCH-703 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture/ECSModel.md`:389 | Architecture — ECS model › Deterministic reductions | Reductions and shared structures (sums, counts, spatial grids) are never written in place across entities by parallel invocations: each entity emits a contribution into its invocation's private buffer, and contributions are folded at the merge point in merge order. |
| Z2-N-ARCH-704 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture/ECSModel.md`:390 | Architecture — ECS model › Deterministic reductions | A parallel reduction whose result must not depend on worker count uses a fixed-order reduction tree over contributions sorted by merge key (a balanced binary tree over the sorted list, pair-summed left to right), never a per-worker partial sum. |
| Z2-N-ARCH-705 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture/ECSModel.md`:404 | Architecture — ECS model › Type-erased API surface | Every public ECS function is type-erased by reflection ID; a template in an ECS public header is an inline wrapper over a type-erased function and adds no storage behaviour, so data-defined components use exactly the same code paths as C++-registered ones. |
| Z2-N-ARCH-706 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture/ECSModel.md`:426 | Architecture — ECS model › World registry | World tags come from the `Zenith_WorldRegistry` object the composition root owns (Zenith2/Docs/Contracts.md → "Handle kinds"); a world never chooses its own tag, and no registry exists as global state. |
| Z2-N-ARCH-707 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture/ECSModel.md`:520 | Architecture — ECS model › Scheduling contract › The merge context | A system's callback runs once per tick per world as one frame-graph node; it iterates its query's chunks inline or fans out with `ParallelFor` over the chunk count, and every chunk it touches in a phase belongs to that phase's stable chunk list. |
| Z2-N-ARCH-708 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture/ECSModel.md`:521 | Architecture — ECS model › Scheduling contract › The merge context | Within one invocation, a system writes only the columns it declared, and two invocations that the graph runs concurrently never write the same column of the same chunk, because the schedule gives each (property, phase) a unique writer or an arbitration node. |
| Z2-N-ARCH-709 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture/ECSModel.md`:527 | Architecture — ECS model › Script lifetimes on the ECS side | The transaction boundary is the entity, never the chunk: the ECS supports per-row restore of a chunk's declared-writable columns (snapshot per chunk on first write, restore per row) and per-entity discard of side-effect sub-buffers, so a failure rolls back only the failing entity's writes. |
| Z2-N-ARCH-710 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture/ECSModel.md`:528 | Architecture — ECS model › Script lifetimes on the ECS side | A chunk-column snapshot covers only the columns the invocation declared writable (their pending change bits are private to the invocation and need no copy, bullet 720), and lives until the system's merge point, because merge-time capacity rejection can still roll back an entity's rows after the call returns. |
| Z2-N-ARCH-711 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture/ECSModel.md`:529 | Architecture — ECS model › Script lifetimes on the ECS side | A chunk system reads a column it writes in the same phase only for its own row, and other entities' rows only in columns not written in that phase; the schedule compiler marks each (system, column) pair own-row-only or any-row from per-field authority, and the view enforces it (Zenith2/Docs/Scripting.md → "Transaction scope and the entity boundary"). |
| Z2-N-ARCH-712 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture/ECSModel.md`:530 | Architecture — ECS model › Script lifetimes on the ECS side | Spawn IDs handed to a script during an invocation are provisional and are released on rollback; the entity IDs and stable simulation IDs of accepted spawns are assigned at playback in merge order. |
| Z2-N-ARCH-713 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture/ECSModel.md`:534 | Architecture — ECS model › Diagnostics | Every world publishes, once per tick after the end-of-tick playback, each suffixed `.<worldTag>` and with its kind in the counter hook's `eKind` (Zenith2/Docs/Contracts.md → "Threading and concurrency contract"), the gauges `ecs.entities`, `ecs.chunks`, `ecs.archetypes` and `ecs.snapshot_bytes` (the tick's peak) and the deltas `ecs.commands_delta`, `ecs.command_overwrites_delta`, `ecs.commands_stale_delta`, `ecs.commands_on_destroyed_delta`, `ecs.playback_us_delta` (the tick's sum of the sync-point and end-of-tick zones), `ecs.rollbacks_delta` and `ecs.capacity_rejections_delta` to the on-screen profiler; the full counter table, with its further rows, is `Zenith2/Docs/Design/ECS.md` → "Counters". |
| Z2-N-ARCH-714 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture/ECSModel.md`:540 | Architecture — ECS model › Required tests | Every test in the table "ECS conformance tests" exists under the name given, runs on all three backends, and asserts the case given. |
| Z2-N-ARCH-715 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture/ECSModel.md`:541 | Architecture — ECS model › Required tests | The determinism tests of the table run the same replay with 1, 2, 8 and all workers, with forced compaction and with two chunk capacities, on each backend, and assert bit-identical per-tick world-state hashes per backend (the hash of Zenith2/Docs/Scripting.md → "Determinism rules and randomness"). |
| Z2-N-ARCH-716 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture/ECSModel.md`:198 | Architecture — ECS model › Worlds and backends › Playback access and supporting types | Playback steps 5 to 7 of Architecture/FramePhases.md → "Sync points and the tick boundary" mutate storage only through `Zenith_WorldPlaybackAccess`, whose failures are recoverable results logged with the command that caused them, never asserts; its `GetWorldTag` names the world being played back, the one key by which a module command's playback (Architecture/CompositionRoot.md → "Module interfaces", `Zenith_PfnCommandPlayback`) finds its per-world module state. |
| Z2-N-ARCH-717 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture/ECSModel.md`:199 | Architecture — ECS model › Worlds and backends › Playback access and supporting types | Initial component values travel as `Zenith_ComponentInit` property-value sets keyed by property ID, never as bytes of a C++ layout, so a spawn or an added component is independent of every backend's storage layout (Z2-D-038). |
| Z2-N-ARCH-718 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture/ECSModel.md`:485 | Architecture — ECS model › Scheduling contract › The system context | A `Zenith_SystemContext` exists for one invocation, is created by the frame-graph node that runs the system, and is neither copied nor stored; a sub-job reaches it through the pointer its node passes in its job data, within that node's lifetime. |
| Z2-N-ARCH-719 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture/ECSModel.md`:486 | Architecture — ECS model › Scheduling contract › The system context | `GetResource` returns a world resource only when the system declared a read or write of that resource type and returns null otherwise; resources are injected by declaration, never looked up as services (Zenith2/Docs/Contracts.md → "No mutable global state"). |
| Z2-N-ARCH-720 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture/ECSModel.md`:276 | Architecture — ECS model › Sync points and the structural epoch | `Zenith_RawColumn::MarkRowChanged(uRow)` sets the row's pending change bit in the invocation's private bit set and changes nothing else; at the invocation's merge point a committed entity transaction's bits are folded into the column's change-tracking state and the column's change version advances, while a rolled-back or rejected entity's bits are discarded with its snapshot (Zenith2/Docs/Scripting.md → "Commit and failure semantics"). |
| Z2-N-ARCH-721 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture/ECSModel.md`:277 | Architecture — ECS model › Sync points and the structural epoch | Every write a script invocation makes through a chunk view calls `MarkRowChanged` for the written row, and a C++ system either calls it per written row or, when it writes every row it iterates, marks the whole column written, which advances the version at merge the same way. |
| Z2-N-ARCH-722 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture/ECSModel.md`:451 | Architecture — ECS model › Scheduling contract › The system registry | A system's registration index, returned by `GetRegistrationIndex` and used as the merge key's system order, is the order of its `AddSystem` or `AddEpilogueStage` call in the world's registry, which the canonical module order makes identical on every run. |
| Z2-N-ARCH-723 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture/ECSModel.md`:278 | Architecture — ECS model › Sync points and the structural epoch | The merge continuation of every invocation that wrote columns calls `Zenith_ChunkView::CommitRowChanges` once per written (chunk, column) with the row bits of its accepted entities alone, and the column's version becomes the precomputed merge version of the writing invocation's merge point (`Zenith_MergeContext::GetMergeVersion()`); the merge points of a stage are ordered by a deterministic topological order of the stage's declared hazards and orderings that ignores cost hints (ties by registration index), and a `QUERY_TERM_CHANGED` query compares against the version of the merge point immediately preceding its system's node in that order (`Zenith_MergeContext::GetReadVersion()`), its own merge's stamp excluded (Zenith2/Docs/Scripting.md → "Commit and failure semantics"). |
| Z2-N-ARCH-724 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture/ECSModel.md`:427 | Architecture — ECS model › World registry | `Zenith_WorldRegistry` lives in `ZenithECS` and reports failure as `Zenith_ECSError` (`ECS_ERROR_WORLD_TAGS_EXHAUSTED` when every tag is live); the composition root creates it in startup step 10, before the type registry's `Freeze`. |
| Z2-N-ARCH-725 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture/ECSModel.md`:428 | Architecture — ECS model › World registry | `AcquireTag` issues, with the tag, an initial generation in one of two cases: when the tag was issued before, one above the highest generation any previous world with that tag issued (as reported through `ReleaseTag`), a value above `uECS_MAX_GENERATION` wrapping to 1; when the tag was never issued, the creation-counter offset of Zenith2/Docs/Contracts.md → "Handle kinds" ([Z2-N-CONTRACT-099]); the creator copies it into `Zenith_WorldDesc::m_xEntityTable`, so a handle held from a destroyed world never resolves in a new world that reuses its tag. |
| Z2-N-ARCH-726 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture/ECSModel.md`:452 | Architecture — ECS model › Scheduling contract › The system registry | An epilogue stage registered with `AddEpilogueStage` (the one descriptor type is `Zenith_EpilogueStageDesc`, Architecture/CompositionRoot.md → "Startup sequence") is stored with `m_ePhase = PHASE_NONE` and is exempt from `SCHEDULE_ERROR_PHASE_NONE`, the authority checks and bullets 503–504; `EmitInto` places it in stage 12 of the EPILOGUE subgraph of its world's group, beside the extractors. |
| Z2-N-ARCH-727 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture/ECSModel.md`:453 | Architecture — ECS model › Scheduling contract › The system registry | An epilogue stage's builder accepts component reads, queries, world-resource reads and `WriteResource` of a world resource that it is the sole consumer of (the stage drains it, as `Audio.Update` drains `Zenith_AudioCommandQueue`); any component write is `SCHEDULE_ERROR_READ_ONLY_PHASE_WRITE`. |
| Z2-N-ARCH-728 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture/ECSModel.md`:279 | Architecture — ECS model › Sync points and the structural epoch | Property commands applied at a sync point mark rows through `Zenith_WorldPlaybackAccess::SetProperty`, and after playback step 5 the sync-point node calls `CommitRowChanges` once per written (chunk, column) with the playback's own `Zenith_InvocationScope` at the sync point's merge version; no call other than `CommitRowChanges` advances a column's change version, except the playback-version stamps made through `Zenith_WorldPlaybackAccess::SetPlaybackVersion` — rows a playback creates, property writes made outside the playback write set in step 6 (redundant additions, ticket-addressed records), columns a move adds to a row, and the destination column version of a moved or compacted row (the newer of its current value and the row's version) — and the re-stamping of `Zenith_World::SweepChangeVersions` (the version wrap sweep) and `Zenith_WorldSimulation::RestoreSimulationState`. |
| Z2-N-ARCH-729 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture/ECSModel.md`:280 | Architecture — ECS model › Sync points and the structural epoch | Tick-boundary handlers reach the world through the property-only `Zenith_TickBoundaryAccess` (`SetProperty`, `ReadProperty`, `ReadContainer`, `SetContainer`, `HasComponent`, `IsDestroyPending`, `FindByPersistentID`, `GetEntityTable`; no structural member; `SetContainer` is a property write that reserves its own container block and returns `ECS_ERROR_CAPACITY`, changing nothing, when the reservation is refused), and the property writes of tick-boundary steps 1 to 3 are committed after step 3 through `CommitRowChanges` at the tick boundary's merge version V(t, 0), with no epoch, query-structure-version or query-cache change (Architecture/FramePhases.md → "Sync points and the tick boundary"). |
| Z2-N-ARCH-730 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture/ECSModel.md`:454 | Architecture — ECS model › Scheduling contract › The system registry | `Zenith_SystemRegistry::AddSystem` (and a reload scope's `AddSystem` or `ReplaceSystem`) rejects `PHASE_RENDER_EXTRACTION`, `PHASE_COMMANDS_ONLY` and every value above `PHASE_SAVE_SNAPSHOT` at the call with `ECS_ERROR_PHASE_NOT_SCHEDULABLE`, because the TICK subgraph holds phases 1 to 11 and phase-12 work is EPILOGUE work registered through `AddEpilogueStage` or the extractor path; the rejection fails the registering module's attach with `MODULE_ERROR_REGISTRATION`, which the composition root reads through `GetRejectedRegistrations` (`Test_SystemRegistry_UnschedulablePhase_Rejected` in `Engine/ZenithECS/Tests/`). |
| Z2-N-ARCH-731 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture/ECSModel.md`:455 | Architecture — ECS model › Scheduling contract › The system registry | A world resource owns one phase from 1 to 11: a resource owning phase 11 (`PHASE_SAVE_SNAPSHOT`) is a snapshot buffer (SAVE's `Zenith.SaveSnapshotBuffer`, NET's `Zenith.Net.Capture`), the one kind of write phase 11 admits, no resource owns phase 12, and the phase passed to `Zenith_WorldAttachContext::AddWorldResource` equals the resource type's `m_eResourceOwningPhase` (otherwise `SCHEDULE_ERROR_RESOURCE_AUTHORITY`); a module whose systems advance world-clock-like state for the tick (the Environment module's clock, weather, wind and activation systems) owns its resources in `PHASE_INPUT`, so phase-2 scripts see the tick's state (Architecture/FramePhases.md → "The twelve phases"). |
| Z2-N-ARCH-732 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture/ECSModel.md`:516 | Architecture — ECS model › Scheduling contract › The merge context | The ECS public types this file declares (`Zenith_WorldPlaybackAccess` with its playback-write members and `SetPlaybackVersion`, `Zenith_MergeContext`, `Zenith_World` with `SweepChangeVersions`, `Zenith_SystemContext` with `GetPreviousReadVersion`, `Zenith_SystemRegistry` with `GetRejectedRegistrations`, `Zenith_CommandBuffer`, `Zenith_WorldSimulation`) also carry the members `Zenith2/Docs/Design/ECS/WorldAPI.md` and `Zenith2/Docs/Design/ECS/SystemAPI.md` mark elaboration, and those designs' headers are the complete declarations; a member this file declares keeps its name and meaning there. |
| Z2-N-ARCH-761 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture.md`:963 | Zenith2 Architecture › Worlds and isolation | Worlds are isolated: no entity handle, component handle, chunk view, command buffer, script value or physics body of one world is valid in another, and resolving one in the wrong world fails with the wrong-world error in every configuration (Zenith2/Docs/Contracts.md → "Handle kinds"). |
| Z2-N-ARCH-762 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture.md`:964 | Zenith2 Architecture › Worlds and isolation | Worlds share no mutable simulation state; the only cross-world data paths are serialization by persistent ID (world transition carry-over, editor copy and paste between edit and play worlds) performed at tick boundaries. |
| Z2-N-ARCH-763 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture.md`:965 | Zenith2 Architecture › Worlds and isolation | Each world is created and destroyed on the main thread at a frame boundary, receives a world tag from the `Zenith_WorldRegistry`, and contributes its own node group to the frame schedule (Architecture/CPUFrameGraph.md → "Worlds in the graph"). |
| Z2-N-ARCH-764 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture.md`:966 | Zenith2 Architecture › Worlds and isolation | A failure in one world (a script error, a quarantined behaviour, a merge rejection) affects no other world's tick, and pausing one world pauses no other. |
| Z2-N-ARCH-765 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture.md`:967 | Zenith2 Architecture › Worlds and isolation | Destroying a world releases its references to shared assets and GPU resources through their reference counts and never forces their destruction; assets and resources outlive every world (Zenith2/Docs/Contracts.md → "Handle kinds"). |
| Z2-N-ARCH-766 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture.md`:968 | Zenith2 Architecture › Worlds and isolation | Editor play worlds are independent: starting play copies the edit world by serialization into a new world, stopping play destroys the play world, and nothing the play world did reaches the edit world. |
| Z2-N-ARCH-767 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture.md`:969 | Zenith2 Architecture › Worlds and isolation | The test `Test_Worlds_TwoWorldsTickIndependently` (in `Tests/Determinism/`) runs two worlds of the same content with different pause and time-scale settings concurrently for 600 ticks and asserts that each world's tick hashes equal a solo run of that world. |
| Z2-N-ARCH-781 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture.md`:997 | Zenith2 Architecture › Multiplayer foundations › Network identity | Network IDs are distinct from entity handles, stable simulation IDs and persistent IDs; a world keeps a `Zenith_NetworkIDMap` from network ID to entity handle, and network messages carry network IDs only, never handles (Zenith2/Docs/Contracts.md → "Handle kinds"). |
| Z2-N-ARCH-782 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture.md`:998 | Zenith2 Architecture › Multiplayer foundations › Network identity | The authority model is server-authoritative by default: every component declares a network authority (`networkAuthority` in schemas, `NetworkAuthority` in C++ registration), defaulting to `NETWORK_AUTHORITY_SERVER`, and each replicated field declares `replicated`. |
| Z2-N-ARCH-783 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture.md`:1104 | Zenith2 Architecture › Multiplayer foundations › Fixed ticks and replayable commands | Gameplay input reaches simulation only as replayable input commands logged at phase 1, and asynchronous results reach it only as publications logged at tick boundaries, so a replay of the log reproduces the run bit for bit on the same build and backend (Z2-D-019, Z2-D-053). |
| Z2-N-ARCH-784 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture.md`:1105 | Zenith2 Architecture › Multiplayer foundations › Fixed ticks and replayable commands | Replaying a log whose build ID, composition hash, content hash, backends or tick rate (SAVE's `REPLAY_IDENTITY_FIELD_TICK_RATE`, the header's `sim.tickHz`) differ from the running game is refused with exit code 5 and a message naming the mismatched field. |
| Z2-N-ARCH-785 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture.md`:1112 | Zenith2 Architecture › Multiplayer foundations › Headless execution | A headless runtime configuration runs every simulation phase without Flux, the audio output device or platform windowing (Architecture/CompositionRoot.md → "Startup sequence"), and it is the configuration the loopback server, the device-free CI integration smoke and the determinism suites use. |
| Z2-N-ARCH-786 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture.md`:1130 | Zenith2 Architecture › Multiplayer foundations › The Stage 3 loopback proof | `Test_Net_LoopbackReplicatesTransformsAndCommands` passes when: the server's received command log equals the client's sent command log for every tick after reordering by tick and sequence; for every tick T for which the client holds a snapshot, every replicated transform equals the server's authoritative transform at T after the same quantisation; `net.late_commands` is 0 on an idle machine; and both processes exit with code 0. |
| Z2-N-ARCH-787 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture.md`:1135 | Zenith2 Architecture › Multiplayer foundations › The Stage 3 loopback proof | The loopback transport, snapshot format and possession exist to prove the foundations and are marked Stage 3 foundations in the NET design document; transport sessions, prediction, reconciliation, interest management and dedicated server builds are Stage 7 work specified there (Z2-D-073). |
| Z2-N-ARCH-788 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture.md`:1108 | Zenith2 Architecture › Multiplayer foundations › Fixed ticks and replayable commands | A world-transition load completion is logged as a `PUBLICATION_KIND_WORLD_LOADED` publication at the tick boundary that publishes it, and the swap is logged as a `REPLAY_RECORD_WORLD_SWAP` naming the old world's last executed tick; a replay swaps after exactly that tick. |
| Z2-N-ARCH-789 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture.md`:1106 | Zenith2 Architecture › Multiplayer foundations › Fixed ticks and replayable commands | A position in a replay log is the pair (ordinal of the `WORLD_BEGIN` record counted from 0 at the start of the file, tick index); a save written during a recorded run stores that pair in its header, and resuming a replay from the save seeks to it; a replay resumed from an EXACT save (no behaviour suspended mid-`Run`, no quarantined instance or instance of a disabled or disabling module, no in-flight additive load, every participant EXACT) compares its tick hashes with the recording, while one resumed from a COHERENT save (a behaviour suspended mid-`Run`, a quarantined or disabled instance, a loading additive instance, PhysX) runs without hash comparison and reports that, the save's coherence being checked by SAVE's coherence assertions (`Zenith2/Docs/Design/SAVE.md` → "Reproducibility boundaries"). |
| Z2-N-ARCH-790 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture.md`:1107 | Zenith2 Architecture › Multiplayer foundations › Fixed ticks and replayable commands | A replay stops before a `TICK` record whose flags carry `REPLAY_TICK_FLAG_NON_REPLAYABLE`, reports that tick, and exits with code 5. |
| Z2-N-ARCH-791 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture.md`:1102 | Zenith2 Architecture › Multiplayer foundations › Fixed ticks and replayable commands | A world-visible platform event reaches simulation only as an `ASYNC_SOURCE_PLATFORM` publication of kind `PUBLICATION_KIND_PLATFORM_RECORDED` at a tick boundary; its 36-byte little-endian payload (`uint16` the `Zenith_PlatformEventKind`, `uint16` 0, then 32 kind-specific bytes with platform-scoped IDs zeroed; the layout is `Zenith2/Docs/Design/PLAT.md` → "Threading lifetime and error contract") is recorded in the log, and a replay publishes the recorded payloads at the recorded ticks and ignores live platform events: under `--replay` the PLAT bridge allocates no completion ID for a live event, and the world's counter follows the recorded next completion ID of each `TICK` record ([Z2-N-ARCH-794]). |
| Z2-N-ARCH-792 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture.md`:1101 | Zenith2 Architecture › Multiplayer foundations › Fixed ticks and replayable commands | Every asynchronous result that reaches a world is completed into that world's `Zenith_AsyncResultTable` under a declared `Zenith_AsyncSourceKind`, and no other object publishes into a world; the source-kind values of the enumeration are assigned only by Zenith2/Docs/Scripting.md → "Asynchronous source kind registry" ([Z2-N-SCRIPT-924], [Z2-N-SCRIPT-925]), never renumbered, and at most `uASYNC_SOURCE_KIND_CAPACITY` (32) kinds exist. |
| Z2-N-ARCH-793 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture.md`:1113 | Zenith2 Architecture › Multiplayer foundations › Headless execution | GPU test executables and render tests are not headless runs: they create an RHI device directly on WARP (D3D12) or lavapipe (Vulkan, selected through the pinned ICD manifest), and those that need a swapchain first create a hidden window through `Zenith_Window` (`Zenith_WindowDesc::m_bHidden = true`, realised by the platform layer with `SDL_WINDOW_HIDDEN`), while every other GPU test creates no window; "boots headless on WARP and lavapipe" therefore means these test executables, never the headless configuration of bullet 785. |
| Z2-N-ARCH-794 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture.md`:1103 | Zenith2 Architecture › Multiplayer foundations › Fixed ticks and replayable commands | Every `REPLAY_RECORD_TICK` record carries the world's next completion ID at the tick boundary (after every frame-prologue allocation of the frame and before the tick's first merge-chain allocation); under `--replay` the world's `Zenith_AsyncResultTable` adopts the recorded value at that point, and a live counter above the recorded value is a replay divergence that halts the world with a diagnostic naming the tick, the recorded and the live value (Zenith2/Docs/Scripting.md [Z2-N-SCRIPT-341]). |
| Z2-N-ARCH-811 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture.md`:1326 | Zenith2 Architecture › Memory governor › Pressure levels | The governor derives the pressure level from the table "Pressure levels" and the hysteresis rule, applies level changes in the frame prologue, and notifies every registered consumer of every change in registration order on the main thread. |
| Z2-N-ARCH-812 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture.md`:1327 | Zenith2 Architecture › Memory governor › Pressure levels | Shedding changes caches, residency and presentation quality only, never simulation-visible state: no entity, component value, script-visible value or replayed quantity depends on the pressure level, so memory pressure cannot break the determinism guarantee. |
| Z2-N-ARCH-813 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture.md`:1328 | Zenith2 Architecture › Memory governor › Pressure levels | Process usage is measured every frame from the OS (`GetProcessMemoryInfo` working set on Windows, `/proc/self/statm` resident pages on Android) and from each consumer's `GetResidentBytes`. |
| Z2-N-ARCH-814 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture.md`:1348 | Zenith2 Architecture › Memory governor › Shedding per subsystem | Every consumer implements the shedding its row of the table "Shedding per subsystem" specifies for each level, and each consumer's design document lists the counters that prove its shedding ran. |
| Z2-N-ARCH-815 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture.md`:1354 | Zenith2 Architecture › Memory governor › Transition peaks | Streaming double residency, world transitions and texture swaps reserve their peak from the governor before they allocate; a refused reservation (`MEMORY_ERROR_PEAK_REFUSED`) defers the operation to a following frame (presentation prefetch of streaming, texture swap) or fails the request (world transition, Architecture/LifecycleCommands.md → "World transition"), and never allocates past the peak row; a simulation-required cell load never defers or waits: it reserves without deferral and holds the reservation for the whole load (until the cell holds content or the load ends), and on a refusal the consumer releases at once the presentation residency that doubles it (the displayed HLOD proxy), so no deferrable allocation exceeds the peak row. |
| Z2-N-ARCH-816 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture.md`:1355 | Zenith2 Architecture › Memory governor › Transition peaks | A reservation is released by its `Zenith_TransitionReservation` owner's destructor, and the governor reports every reservation still held at shutdown. |
| Z2-N-ARCH-817 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture.md`:1364 | Zenith2 Architecture › Memory governor › Forced-level tests | The tests `Test_MemoryGovernor_ForcedWarningShedsEachConsumer`, `Test_MemoryGovernor_ForcedCriticalShedsEachConsumer` (each subsystem's consumer, through its counters), `Test_MemoryGovernor_HysteresisReturnsToNormal`, `Test_MemoryGovernor_TransitionPeakRefusedAtCap`, `Test_MemoryGovernor_ShedsNoSimulationState` (tick hashes identical with and without forced CRITICAL), `Test_MemoryGovernor_OSSignalMapsToLevel` and `Test_MemoryGovernor_BadBudgetsRejected` exist in `Engine/ZenithCore/Tests/` or, for cross-system cases, `Tests/Memory/`, and assert what their names state; the S24 memory-pressure run is a `needs-human` acceptance device step. |
| Z2-N-ARCH-818 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture.md`:1329 | Zenith2 Architecture › Memory governor › Pressure levels | The measured process usage and every consumer's resident bytes are published each frame to the on-screen profiler's memory panel. |
| Z2-N-ARCH-819 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture.md`:1306 | Zenith2 Architecture › Memory governor › Budgets | The composition root fills every field of `Zenith_MemoryBudgets` from the configuration keys named in the table "Budgets", and `Zenith_MemoryGovernor::Create` fails with `MEMORY_ERROR_BAD_BUDGETS` (startup exit code 2, naming the key) when any budget or threshold is zero, when the warning threshold is not below the critical threshold, or when the per-subsystem budgets outside `m_uPendingSubsystemMask` exceed the process total. |
| Z2-N-ARCH-820 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture.md`:1309 | Zenith2 Architecture › Memory governor › Budgets | No threshold, hysteresis value or budget in the governor is a compiled-in number: each comes from a Budgets row or from a configuration key whose value the Stage 1 per-system budget ticket for CORE sets. |
| Z2-N-ARCH-821 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture.md`:1349 | Zenith2 Architecture › Memory governor › Shedding per subsystem | A consumer applies its response within one frame of the governor's level change and reverts it one frame after the level falls below that level. |
| Z2-N-ARCH-822 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture.md`:1350 | Zenith2 Architecture › Memory governor › Shedding per subsystem | The table "Shedding per subsystem" is the single source of shedding behaviour: another document that describes a subsystem's response to memory pressure cites this table and adds implementation detail only. |
| Z2-N-ARCH-823 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture.md`:1307 | Zenith2 Architecture › Memory governor › Budgets | The table "Budgets" is the single source of the mapping from subsystem to Budgets rows: each subsystem's budget is the sum, for the build platform, of exactly the rows its row lists (host and device-local values alike), a row it names as not summed contributes to no budget, and every other document that maps a subsystem to rows cites this table; which bound a row counts in is Zenith2/Docs/Budgets.md → "Purpose versioning and change rule" (table "Allocation sums"). |
| Z2-N-ARCH-824 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture.md`:1308 | Zenith2 Architecture › Memory governor › Budgets | A subsystem whose rows include a pending row takes the platform's process total as its budget (Z2-B-CORE-003 for its device-local share on Windows) and its bit in `m_uPendingSubsystemMask`, never sheds because of its own budget, and is reported as pending on the profiler's memory panel until the row is allocated. |
| Z2-N-ARCH-825 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture.md`:1359 | Zenith2 Architecture › Memory governor › Subsystem reservations | An operation that is not a transition and must not allocate past its subsystem's budget (a SCENE preparation whose decoded size exceeds `scene.prepareReserveThresholdBytes`, `Zenith2/Docs/Design/SCENE/Loading.md` → "Preparation operation") reserves its bytes with `ReserveSubsystemBytes(eSubsystem, ulBytes)` before it allocates. The governor grants the reservation when the subsystem's resident bytes at the last `Update` (the sum of its consumers' `GetResidentBytes`), plus the subsystem's outstanding reservations, plus `ulBytes`, stay within the subsystem's budget of the table "Budgets" (the process total for a subsystem in `m_uPendingSubsystemMask`, [Z2-N-ARCH-824]); otherwise it refuses with `MEMORY_ERROR_SUBSYSTEM_REFUSED` (with `m_eSubsystem`, the requested and the available bytes) and the operation fails or defers as its own design states (SCENE: `SCENE_ERROR_MEMORY_REFUSED`), never allocating past the budget. A world transition keeps the transition-peak reservation of [Z2-N-ARCH-815] and [Z2-N-ARCH-559] and takes no subsystem reservation for the same bytes. |
| Z2-N-ARCH-826 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture.md`:1360 | Zenith2 Architecture › Memory governor › Subsystem reservations | A `Zenith_SubsystemReservation` is released by its owner's destructor (for SCENE, with the preparation's arena) or earlier through `Shrink`; outstanding subsystem reservations count toward their subsystem's source and the process source of the table "Pressure levels" exactly as transition reservations count toward the process source, the double count while the reserved bytes are also reported by a consumer being conservative by design; the governor reports every subsystem reservation still held at shutdown, as [Z2-N-ARCH-816] does for transition reservations; and `Test_MemoryGovernor_SubsystemReservationRefusedAtBudget` (reservations up to the budget succeed, the next is refused with the available bytes, `Shrink` and destruction release) exists in `Engine/ZenithCore/Tests/`. |

the process has at most one account consumer and each module at most one engine-wide stage, a second registration of either is rejected (`MODULE_ERROR_REGISTRATION` for the registering module), and every one of them is journaled and undone with its module like every other registration (bullet 851); the runtime forwards a frame listener's calls in canonical module order (`OnStopAudio` and `OnResumeAudio` from its `Zenith_AppLifecycleListener` inside `Platform.Pump`, `OnPlatformEvents` after prologue step 1); `Zenith_WorldAttachContext::GetSceneState` returns the world's scene state, never null in a runtime world; `Zenith_WorldAttachContext::GetWorldPurpose` returns the value its world's creator set (`WORLD_PURPOSE_PLAY` for the runtime's and the world host's worlds, `WORLD_PURPOSE_EDIT` for the editor's, `WORLD_PURPOSE_PREVIEW` for an asset-preview host's), and `RegisterSaveParticipant` is `MAIN_THREAD`, valid during `AttachWorld` alone, rejected like any other attach registration when ZenithSave refuses it, and journaled and undone with its module; `GetDevConsole`, `GetDevFrameDump`, `GetDebugDraw`, `GetProfilerOverlay`, `GetBudgetRunRecord` and `RegisterBudgetEmitter` are `MAIN_THREAD` and valid during `Start` alone, a budget emitter registration is journaled and undone with its module, and an overlay-panel or frame-dump-section registration made through these accessors is owned by a token the module instance holds and is undone by destroying the module instance at its undo position, which is how a token-owned registration satisfies the journaled rule; `IsRecordingReplay`, `RequestExitAfterStart`, `GetSaveSystem`, `IsReplaying`, `GetScriptAsyncCapacity` and `GetDeviceInteropProvider` are `MAIN_THREAD` and valid during `Start` alone, `ProvideEntitlementQuery` is a registration valid during `Start` alone, at most one per process, journaled and undone with its module, and `GetTestAssertionSink` returns a sink whose `RecordFailure` is `ANY_THREAD` and may be called from a module instance's destructor; `GetStringTable` and `GetCaptureTickGate` are `MAIN_THREAD` and callable during `Start`, and the table and the gate they return stay valid for the process's lifetime (`Zenith_CaptureTickGate::HoldNextTick` is `MAIN_THREAD`); `AddTickBoundaryHandler` is `MAIN_THREAD`, valid during `AttachWorld` alone, before the world's first tick, and journaled and undone with its module; `SetInputCommandBridge` is `MAIN_THREAD`, valid during `AttachWorld` alone, at most one per world, and journaled and undone; `GetPublicationSink`, `FindCommandKind` and `GetWorldControl` are `MAIN_THREAD` during `AttachWorld`, and each context's `GetFaultInjector` is `MAIN_THREAD` during its call; adding these members, `Zenith_ModuleFrameListener` (with `OnBeforePlatformPump`, which has an empty default), `Zenith_TestAssertionSink`, `Zenith_WorldPurpose`, `Zenith_WorldAttachContext::GetSimulationClock` and the descriptor's optional `m_pfnCreateDeviceInterop` changes no entry point or virtual of `Zenith_ModuleInstance`, so `uZENITH_MODULE_INTERFACE_VERSION` stays 1. |
| Z2-N-ARCH-880 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture/CompositionRoot.md`:738 | Architecture — Composition root and Player › The frame loop | The frame loop runs a frame with `bRunRender` false whenever the renderer's polled device-loss state (`Flux_Renderer::GetDeviceLossState()`, an atomic read) is not `FLUX_DEVICE_LOSS_STATE_NORMAL`, and the device-loss clean exit requests the exit autosave (`Zenith_RuntimeSave_RequestAutosaveForExit`) once the last `RunFrame` has returned, bounds its wait in shutdown step 4 to 6 s plus 1 s after a rollback cancel (frame-loop step 4), and returns `EXIT_CODE_DEVICE_LOST` (6) through the ordinary shutdown order, never `EXIT_CODE_DEVICE` (4), which means only that a required device could not be created. |
| Z2-N-ARCH-881 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture.md`:1385 | Zenith2 Architecture › Determinism guarantee and mechanisms › The guarantee | Every simulation result — every component value, entity set, event sequence, command sequence, behaviour checkpoint and the simulation-ID counter after every tick — is bit-identical across runs with the same build, ECS backend, physics backend, content and replay log, for any worker count, chunk capacity, compaction schedule and thread interleaving. |
| Z2-N-ARCH-882 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture.md`:1386 | Zenith2 Architecture › Determinism guarantee and mechanisms › The guarantee | Nothing promises equality across ECS backends, physics backends, platforms or builds, and no test or acceptance criterion compares simulation results across them. |
| Z2-N-ARCH-883 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture.md`:1411 | Zenith2 Architecture › Determinism guarantee and mechanisms › Mechanisms | Vectorised simulation kernels produce, for each entity, exactly the result the scalar path produces for that entity (the same operations in the same order per lane), so an entity's result never depends on its position in a chunk or batch. |
| Z2-N-ARCH-884 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture.md`:1412 | Zenith2 Architecture › Determinism guarantee and mechanisms › Mechanisms | Simulation code never orders, hashes or keys by a memory address, pointer value, slot index, chunk index or worker index; hashed containers used by simulation iterate in an order defined by their keys, never by their bucket layout. |
| Z2-N-ARCH-885 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture.md`:1413 | Zenith2 Architecture › Determinism guarantee and mechanisms › Mechanisms | Simulation code reads no clock, environment variable, file timestamp, thread identity or OS random source; time comes from the tick index and randomness from the counter-based streams (Zenith2/Docs/Contracts.md → "Tests and code hygiene gates", rule F14). |
| Z2-N-ARCH-886 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture.md`:1414 | Zenith2 Architecture › Determinism guarantee and mechanisms › Mechanisms | The determinism suites (`Tests/Determinism/`, the ECS conformance determinism tests and the script fault matrix) replay the same logs with 1, 2, 8 and all workers, with forced compaction and two chunk capacities, on each backend, and compare per-tick world-state hashes. |
| Z2-N-ARCH-887 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture.md`:1415 | Zenith2 Architecture › Determinism guarantee and mechanisms › Mechanisms | A determinism-suite hash difference fails the suite and reports the first differing tick, entity and property. |
| Z2-N-ARCH-888 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture.md`:1416 | Zenith2 Architecture › Determinism guarantee and mechanisms › Mechanisms | Simulation-affecting code, and every computation whose output is tracked in source control or baked (cook steps, authoring tools, generators and bakes), never calls a C runtime transcendental function (`<cmath>` `sin`, `cos`, `tan`, `asin`, `acos`, `atan`, `atan2`, `exp`, `exp2`, `log`, `log2`, `log10`, `pow`, `cbrt`); it calls CORE's `Zenith_Sin`, `Zenith_Cos`, `Zenith_SinCos`, `Zenith_Tan`, `Zenith_Asin`, `Zenith_Acos`, `Zenith_Atan`, `Zenith_Atan2`, `Zenith_Exp`, `Zenith_Exp2`, `Zenith_Ln`, `Zenith_Log2`, `Zenith_Log10`, `Zenith_Pow`, `Zenith_Cbrt` or `Zenith_Sqrt` (`ZenithCore/Zenith_Transcendental.h`, binary32 and binary64), whose result bits are a pure function of the argument bits on every CPU, compiler and platform; `std::sqrt` of a non-negative argument stays allowed, because IEEE 754 rounds it correctly everywhere, and so do `+ − × ÷` without contraction, `floor`, `ceil` and `trunc`, which are exact or correctly rounded and outside the rule; a cube root is `Zenith_Cbrt`, which CORE's set provides (orchestrator ruling X314-FMA3, human may override; Zenith2/Docs/Design/CORE/Maths.md → "Deterministic transcendental functions"). |
| Z2-N-ARCH-889 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture.md`:1417 | Zenith2 Architecture › Determinism guarantee and mechanisms › Mechanisms | Every `main` and `SDL_main` of Zenith2 (the Player's entries, every tool, test, benchmark and integration-suite entry) calls `Zenith_ApplyCrtMathMode()` as its first statement, before the root heap is installed and before any thread starts; on Windows x64 it calls `_set_FMA3_enable(0)`, so third-party code that calls a CRT transcendental takes the non-FMA path on every CPU, and elsewhere it does nothing; it is defence in depth, and bullet 888's guarantee never depends on it (Zenith2/Docs/Design/CORE/Maths.md → "The CRT maths mode"). |

| Z2-N-ARCH-912 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture/PlatformLayer.md`:38 | Architecture — Platform layer › Settled rules and ownership | The SDL event pump runs only on the main thread, in step 1 (`Platform.Pump`) of the frame prologue (Architecture/CPUFrameGraph.md → "Frame structure"), and translates SDL events into `Zenith_PlatformEvent` records queued for the Input phase; SDL events never reach other libraries directly. |
| Z2-N-ARCH-913 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture/PlatformLayer.md`:55 | Architecture — Platform layer › SDL3 responsibilities | Threads, mutexes, atomics and timers come from `Zenith_Multithreading` and `Zenith_Clock`, never from SDL3's thread or timer APIs, so the lock-ordering and concurrency contracts cover every thread (Zenith2/Docs/Contracts.md → "Threading and concurrency contract"). |
| Z2-N-ARCH-914 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture/PlatformLayer.md`:73 | Architecture — Platform layer › Android lifecycle and surface recovery › Lifecycle state machine | On entering the background the runtime pauses every world at a tick boundary (the simulation clock returns zero ticks), completes the in-flight render subgraph, destroys the swapchain and releases the surface before the watch callback returns; the RHI device, its resources and every world stay alive. |
| Z2-N-ARCH-915 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture/PlatformLayer.md`:75 | Architecture — Platform layer › Android lifecycle and surface recovery › Lifecycle state machine | On returning to the foreground the runtime recreates the surface and swapchain from the new `ANativeWindow`, rebuilds size-dependent render targets through the render graph, resumes audio and unpauses; a swapchain recreation failure follows the device recovery policy of Zenith2/Docs/Rendering.md → "Recovery". |
| Z2-N-ARCH-916 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture/PlatformLayer.md`:76 | Architecture — Platform layer › Android lifecycle and surface recovery › Lifecycle state machine | The surface-loss handshake waits at most 2,000 ms; when the main thread has not acknowledged in time, the watch returns, the platform marks the surface lost, and the RHI treats every subsequent present as `SWAPCHAIN_OUT_OF_DATE` until the foreground recreation, so no GPU call ever uses a destroyed window. |

| Z2-N-ARCH-921 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture/PlatformLayer.md`:123 | Architecture — Platform layer › Virtual filesystem › Mounts and precedence | Every write goes to the `user` mount and nowhere else, with one exception: before the virtual filesystem exists, in the closing action of startup step 4, PACK's update applier writes under `<install>/Patches/`, `<install>/DLC/` and `<install>/.zenith2-install/` (Windows) and the internal storage's `Patches/` and `.zenith2-install/` (Android), through ZenithPackage's install file system ([Z2-N-ARCH-923]), and no other PACK runtime code writes outside the `user` mount; `content:/` paths are read-only in every build. |

The one exception is the Tracy client inside ZenithProfiling: it listens on the loopback interface only, starts only when `devtools.tracy.enabled` is set on the command line, carries no session token (Tracy's protocol has none), and is absent from Shipping ([Z2-N-ARCH-124]). |

| Z2-N-ARCH-964 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture.md`:1590 | Zenith2 Architecture › Security architecture › No debugger or remote tools in shipping | The `Libgraph` step fails when any symbol listed in `shipping_forbidden_symbols.json` appears in a Shipping game executable; the symbol check is complemented by the forbidden-string scan and the listener-import scan, each with a positive control (`Zenith2/Docs/Design/SEC/ShippingExposure.md` → "Audit layers"). |
| Z2-N-ARCH-965 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture.md`:1592 | Zenith2 Architecture › Security architecture › No debugger or remote tools in shipping | The Windows crash helper (`--zenith-crash-helper`, Architecture/CompositionRoot.md bullet 860) trusts only inherited handles: the parent process, an unnamed section and three events, and no PID and no path argument. The parent-process handle carries exactly `PROCESS_QUERY_INFORMATION \| PROCESS_VM_READ \| SYNCHRONIZE`, never `PROCESS_DUP_HANDLE`; the section and the events are created non-inheritable and only inheritable duplicates exist, for the one `CreateProcessW` call (`bInheritHandles = TRUE` with a `PROC_THREAD_ATTRIBUTE_HANDLE_LIST` of exactly those five), and are closed as soon as it returns; any other Zenith2 code that starts a child with `bInheritHandles = TRUE` passes its own handle list. The helper validates the section (magic, version, a 128-bit nonce, and the parent PID against the inherited handle), derives the crash directory itself, bounds and escapes every section string, and exits with code 3 on any failed check (Zenith2/Docs/Design/DEVT/Crash.md → "Crash helper security contract"). |
| Z2-N-ARCH-966 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture.md`:1581 | Zenith2 Architecture › Security architecture › Authenticated update manifests | The stamp record is a cache inside the trust boundary "patches and downloaded content", not a new boundary, and the stamp skip trusts `.zenith2-install/verified.zinv` (and, for packs, the delivery state file) exactly as much as the files it covers: the records are unauthenticated user state, so anyone able to write the install root (Windows) or the app-private storage (Android) can forge a record matching a rewritten file's new stamp together with the genuine signed manifest's digest, and that party can already replace the executable, which the boundary does not defend against. The update manifest's Ed25519 signature stays checked at every start but does not bind a file's bytes once its record is trusted; the cooked files' own header checks remain, and `package.reverifyOnStartup` turns every skip off for a run. The Stage 1 security-architecture ticket confirms (a) the stamp set, (b) that the records are unauthenticated and protected only by the install root's write permission, and (c) the escalation, a keyed record (a MAC under a secret kept outside the install root), if a distribution layout ever makes `.zenith2-install/` more writable than the executable (`Zenith2/Docs/Design/PACK/Algorithms.md` → "Verified cache", `Zenith2/Docs/Design/PACK/Formats.md` → "Verified stamp file"). |
| Z2-N-ARCH-981 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture.md`:1621 | Zenith2 Architecture › Named selection decisions | Every consumer of a selection in the table above is `blocks`-linked from that selection's ruling ticket and cannot be implemented before the ruling; its design references the selection by name with "(open, Stage N)" until the ruling lands. |
| Z2-N-ARCH-982 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture.md`:1622 | Zenith2 Architecture › Named selection decisions | A selection ruling states the chosen candidate, the measurements against each criterion with their report path under `Zenith2/Docs/Research/`, and the acceptance rule's outcome, and it never silently changes a default: a changed default is itself the ruling's recorded consequence. |
| Z2-N-ARCH-983 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture.md`:1623 | Zenith2 Architecture › Named selection decisions | Research measurements for selections run under controlled conditions (locked clocks, fixed-performance mode where available, at least 10 repetitions, medians with 95% confidence intervals, raw data committed), kept separate from shipping qualification. |
| Z2-N-ARCH-984 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture/DataDefinedComponents.md`:129 | Architecture — Data-defined components › The schema file › Field type grammar | An `entity` field's `default` is `null` only: an entity persistent ID names an entity of one world while a type default is shared by every world of the process, so a non-null `entity` default is `SCHEMA_ERROR_BAD_DEFAULT`; `asset<K>` defaults keep the persistent-ID form of the "Default JSON form" column. |
| Z2-N-ARCH-985 | `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture.md`:807 | Zenith2 Architecture › Reflection and runtime type registration › Registry types | Every caller of a `Zenith_TypeLifecycleHooks` member passes that hooks value's `m_pUserData` as the hook's last parameter, `pUserData`; the reflection registry stores `m_pUserData` verbatim and never dereferences it. C++ types' hooks carry a null `m_pUserData`; a schema type's hooks are the four constant generic functions of ZenithReflect with `m_pUserData` naming the type's registry-owned record, so the schema hooks need no per-type thunk slot table and no `ForbiddenCheck` writable-data allowlist entry (Zenith2/Docs/Contracts.md → "No mutable global state"). |
