| Z2-N-REND-040 | `Zenith2/Docs/Rendering.md`:477 | Zenith2 rendering: Flux › GPU baseline and profiles › Format feature header | `Zenith_FormatFeatures.h` is the one compiled form of the format-feature table: the Vulkan and D3D12 device checks, the graph's format check and the bindless probe read `axZENITH_RHI_FORMAT_FEATURES` and nothing else. |
| Z2-N-REND-041 | `Zenith2/Docs/Rendering.md`:478 | Zenith2 rendering: Flux › GPU baseline and profiles › Format feature header | `Zenith_RHIFormat` is the one format enumeration of ZenithRHI and Flux: its enumerators are `ZENITH_RHI_FORMAT_<DXGI_FORMAT name without the DXGI_FORMAT_ prefix>`, or for a format D3D12 lacks `ZENITH_RHI_FORMAT_<VkFormat name without VK_FORMAT_ and _BLOCK, upper case>` (the ASTC formats), plus `ZENITH_RHI_FORMAT_UNDEFINED`, every Flux resource description, attachment-format set and PSO key stores a `Zenith_RHIFormat`, and no `Flux_Format` type exists. |
| Z2-N-REND-042 | `Zenith2/Docs/Rendering.md`:479 | Zenith2 rendering: Flux › GPU baseline and profiles › Format feature header | The format-feature tests are `Test_FormatFeatures_ProfileMatchesHeader` in `Zenith2/Engine/ZenithRHI_Vulkan/Tests/` (it reads the backend's `Internal/Profiles/VP_ZENITH2_baseline.json` and includes `Zenith_FormatFeatures.h`, and fails when the `formats` section and the header differ), `Test_FormatFeatures_DeviceRefusesMissingFeature` in `Zenith2/Engine/ZenithRHI_Vulkan/Tests/` and `Zenith2/Engine/ZenithRHI_D3D12/Tests/` (a mocked capability report lacking one required bit is refused), `Test_FormatFeatures_GraphRejectsUngrantedUse` in `Zenith2/Engine/ZenithFlux/Tests/Graph/`, and the probe case `Test_BindlessProbe_StorageFormats`. |
| Z2-N-REND-043 | `Zenith2/Docs/Rendering.md`:338 | Zenith2 rendering: Flux › GPU baseline and profiles › Capability negotiation | Every type ZenithRHI declares carries the `Zenith_RHI` prefix, the capability tier `Zenith_RHICapabilityTier` and the device-handle tag types `Zenith_RHITextureTag`, `Zenith_RHIBufferTag`, `Zenith_RHISamplerTag`, `Zenith_RHIPipelineTag`, `Zenith_RHIHeapTag` and `Zenith_RHIQueryPoolTag` included; ZenithFlux keeps the `Flux_CapabilityTier` spelling and its two value names only as the aliases of the block above, and the `Flux_*Handle` aliases of the device handles stay (see `Zenith2/Docs/Contracts.md → "Handle kinds"`). |
| Z2-N-REND-044 | `Zenith2/Docs/Rendering.md`:363 | Zenith2 rendering: Flux › GPU baseline and profiles › Headless and CI devices | A GPU test that needs a swapchain first creates a hidden window through `Zenith_Window` with `Zenith_WindowDesc::m_bHidden = true` (realised by `SDL_WINDOW_HIDDEN`), and every other GPU test creates its device with no window and no swapchain; a headless run in the sense of [Z2-N-ARCH-785] (the CI integration smoke) creates no window, no device and no render subgraph, and lavapipe's pinned build includes its Win32 WSI for the hidden-window case. |
| Z2-N-REND-050 | `Zenith2/Docs/Rendering.md`:501 | Zenith2 rendering: Flux › Binding model › Descriptor index spaces | Every shader-visible resource is reached through a heap index into one of the four descriptor index spaces or through the static sampler table; no pass declares a descriptor set, descriptor table or root descriptor of its own. |
| Z2-N-REND-051 | `Zenith2/Docs/Rendering.md`:502 | Zenith2 rendering: Flux › Binding model › Descriptor index spaces | A texture view in `FLUX_DESCRIPTOR_SPACE_TEXTURE_UAV` covers exactly one mip level (all array layers of that mip), so a UAV field names one mip and the graph tracks it at mip granularity. |
| Z2-N-REND-052 | `Zenith2/Docs/Rendering.md`:503 | Zenith2 rendering: Flux › Binding model › Descriptor index spaces | Descriptor writes into a heap slot happen on the `Flux.BuildGraph` node after compile, once the frame's `Flux_UploadRing::ExtendReservation` has returned `FITS` or `EXTENDED` (the transient views of the frame's graph, order 5 of `Zenith2/Docs/Rendering/GpuResources.md` "Build-node upload order"; a deferred or failed frame writes none), or on the creating thread at resource creation, never inside a pass lambda or a `Flux.Record` job, and a slot is written at most once between allocation and retirement; record jobs read the indices already written. |
| Z2-N-REND-053 | `Zenith2/Docs/Rendering.md`:515 | Zenith2 rendering: Flux › Binding model › Vulkan heap realisation | Whatever D-SLANG (open, Stage 1) rules, the Vulkan backend creates exactly one descriptor set layout with `VK_DESCRIPTOR_SET_LAYOUT_CREATE_UPDATE_AFTER_BIND_POOL_BIT`, every array binding flagged `PARTIALLY_BOUND \| UPDATE_AFTER_BIND \| UPDATE_UNUSED_WHILE_PENDING`, one descriptor set allocated from it at device creation, and one global `VkPipelineLayout` (that set plus one `VkPushConstantRange{VK_SHADER_STAGE_ALL, 0, 8}`) shared by every pipeline. |
| Z2-N-REND-054 | `Zenith2/Docs/Rendering.md`:516 | Zenith2 rendering: Flux › Binding model › Vulkan heap realisation | The global descriptor set is bound once per command buffer per bind point (graphics and compute) by the graph's command-list partition prologue; no pass lambda binds it. |
| Z2-N-REND-055 | `Zenith2/Docs/Rendering.md`:528 | Zenith2 rendering: Flux › Binding model › D3D12 heap realisation | The D3D12 global root signature carries the flags `D3D12_ROOT_SIGNATURE_FLAG_CBV_SRV_UAV_HEAP_DIRECTLY_INDEXED` and `D3D12_ROOT_SIGNATURE_FLAG_ALLOW_INPUT_ASSEMBLER_INPUT_LAYOUT`, and every PSO on D3D12 uses it; the space base offsets are compile-time constants the shader compiler emits into `Zenith2/Shaders/Common/Bindless.slang` per capability tier. |
| Z2-N-REND-056 | `Zenith2/Docs/Rendering.md`:529 | Zenith2 rendering: Flux › Binding model › D3D12 heap realisation | A heap index as seen by shader code is space-local on both APIs; the D3D12 space base offset is added inside the `Bindless.slang` accessors (`Flux_TextureSRV<T>(uIndex)`, `Flux_TextureUAV<T>(uIndex)`, `Flux_BufferSRV(uIndex)`, `Flux_BufferUAV(uIndex)`), so parameter structs are byte-identical across backends. |
| Z2-N-REND-057 | `Zenith2/Docs/Rendering.md`:550 | Zenith2 rendering: Flux › Binding model › Static sampler table | Samplers are the sixteen static entries of the table above, identical on both APIs (Vulkan immutable samplers in the global layout, D3D12 static samplers in the global root signature); shaders select one through its `uFLUX_SAMPLER_*` index, and no runtime-created sampler exists. |
| Z2-N-REND-058 | `Zenith2/Docs/Rendering.md`:551 | Zenith2 rendering: Flux › Binding model › Static sampler table | Changing the static sampler table is a change to the global pipeline layout and root signature, and therefore bumps the shader blob container version and invalidates every persistent pipeline cache (see "Pipeline state objects"). |
| Z2-N-REND-059 | `Zenith2/Docs/Rendering.md`:566 | Zenith2 rendering: Flux › Binding model › Descriptor slot allocation | A freed descriptor slot is placed on its space's pending list with the per-queue last-use values of its resource and is returned to the free list after every queue's timeline has passed those values (the retirement rule of "GPU lifetime and synchronisation"), and not before. |
| Z2-N-REND-060 | `Zenith2/Docs/Rendering.md`:567 | Zenith2 rendering: Flux › Binding model › Descriptor slot allocation | `Allocate` on a full index space returns `FLUX_DESCRIPTOR_ERROR_SPACE_FULL`, increments the `Flux.Descriptors.Exhausted` counter and never evicts or reuses a live slot. |
| Z2-N-REND-061 | `Zenith2/Docs/Rendering.md`:608 | Zenith2 rendering: Flux › Binding model › The push constant | Every draw and every dispatch receives exactly one `Flux_PushConstants` (8 bytes), declared as the one `uniform` parameter of every entry point (an entry point declares no other ordinary-data `uniform` parameter); Slang lays it out as the entry point's push-constant block on Vulkan (`VK_SHADER_STAGE_ALL`, offset 0) and as the entry point's constant buffer on D3D12, which the global root signature binds as the 32-bit root constants of root parameter 0, and no other push or root constant exists except the D3D12 draw ID of root parameter 1 (Z2-D-054). |
| Z2-N-REND-062 | `Zenith2/Docs/Rendering.md`:609 | Zenith2 rendering: Flux › Binding model › The push constant | A shader reads its parameter struct exclusively through `Flux_LoadParams<T>(xPush)` at entry-point start, where `xPush` is the entry point's `uniform Flux_PushConstants` parameter and `T` is the generated params type named by the program's reflection. |
| Z2-N-REND-063 | `Zenith2/Docs/Rendering.md`:614 | Zenith2 rendering: Flux › Binding model › Parameter structs in the upload ring | A parameter struct is written into the current frame's upload ring at a 16-byte-aligned offset, with a maximum size of `uFLUX_MAX_PARAMS_BYTES` = 2,048 bytes; the shader compiler fails a program whose parameter struct exceeds it with `ZSC_ERROR_PARAMS_TOO_LARGE`. |
| Z2-N-REND-064 | `Zenith2/Docs/Rendering.md`:615 | Zenith2 rendering: Flux › Binding model › Parameter structs in the upload ring | Parameter structs use a scalar layout identical on both APIs (Slang scalar layout on Vulkan, byte-address loads on D3D12, C++ natural alignment in the generated struct), and each generated header asserts every field offset with `static_assert(offsetof(...))`. |
| Z2-N-REND-065 | `Zenith2/Docs/Rendering.md`:616 | Zenith2 rendering: Flux › Binding model › Parameter structs in the upload ring | A parameter struct is written once per pass at record time (compute and fullscreen passes) or once per `BeginDrawParams` call in a raster lambda, and is immutable after its push constant has been recorded. |
| Z2-N-REND-066 | `Zenith2/Docs/Rendering.md`:655 | Zenith2 rendering: Flux › Binding model › DescriptorHandle usage | A `Zenith_RHISRV<T>` or `Zenith_RHIUAV<T>` field holds a virtual graph handle from setup until record and a real descriptor from record onward; the graph patches every resource field listed in the struct's field table before the struct is copied into the upload ring, and a field left unset at setup is a graph compile error (`FLUX_RG_ERROR_FIELD_UNSET`). |
| Z2-N-REND-067 | `Zenith2/Docs/Rendering.md`:656 | Zenith2 rendering: Flux › Binding model › DescriptorHandle usage | The tag type `T` of `Zenith_RHISRV<T>`/`Zenith_RHIUAV<T>` names the Slang resource type (`Zenith_RHITex2D`, `Zenith_RHITex2DArray`, `Zenith_RHITex3D`, `Zenith_RHITexCube`, `Zenith_RHITexCubeArray`, `Zenith_RHIByteBuffer`, `Zenith_RHIStructBuffer<TElem>`), and the graph rejects a view whose dimension or element stride does not match the tag with `FLUX_RG_ERROR_VIEW_TYPE_MISMATCH`. |
| Z2-N-REND-068 | `Zenith2/Docs/Rendering.md`:657 | Zenith2 rendering: Flux › Binding model › DescriptorHandle usage | Descriptor handles that are not graph-tracked (persistent read-only resources reached through GPU scene tables or material constants) are written as `Zenith_RHIDescriptorHandle` values holding real heap indices, and exclusively resources registered `Flux_PersistentReadOnly` are referenced that way (see "GPU scene tables and pools"). A material-constants handle may carry the virtual-texture tag: word 0 (`m_uIndex`) stays a real `TEXTURE_SRV` heap index, the VT's tail texture, so a tag-blind reader samples the tail, and word 1 (`m_uReserved`) carries the VT's slot in bits 0–11, zeros in bits 12–27 and 0xA in bits 28–31 (`Zenith2/Docs/Design/ADVGFX/VirtualTexturing.md` → "Material handle encoding"); a shader reads both words through `uint2 Flux_HandleWords<T>(DescriptorHandle<T>)` of `Zenith2/Shaders/Common/Bindless.slang`, whose lowering the D-SLANG (open, Stage 1) ruling fixes. |
| Z2-N-REND-069 | `Zenith2/Docs/Rendering.md`:699 | Zenith2 rendering: Flux › Binding model › Draw ID on Vulkan and D3D12 | Every indirect draw command uses the 24-byte `Flux_IndirectDrawIndexed` layout on both APIs: Vulkan issues `vkCmdDrawIndexedIndirectCount` (or `vkCmdDrawIndexedIndirect` for the non-compacted transparent commands) with the argument offset advanced by 4 bytes and a stride of 24; D3D12 uses one global command signature for every indirect indexed draw, a `CONSTANT` argument (root parameter 1, one 32-bit value) followed by a `DRAW_INDEXED` argument with stride 24, plus one global signature for indirect dispatch and one for indirect mesh-task dispatch (stride 12 each). |
| Z2-N-REND-070 | `Zenith2/Docs/Rendering.md`:700 | Zenith2 rendering: Flux › Binding model › Draw ID on Vulkan and D3D12 | `m_uDrawIndex` equals the command's index within its bucket, so `Flux_DrawIndex()` returns the same value on both APIs, and `m_uFirstInstance` is 0 in every command so that `SV_InstanceID` is the same zero-based index on both APIs. |
| Z2-N-REND-071 | `Zenith2/Docs/Rendering.md`:701 | Zenith2 rendering: Flux › Binding model › Draw ID on Vulkan and D3D12 | A shader locates its draw record as `Flux_DrawRecordIndex(m_uBucketRecordBase)`, where `m_uBucketRecordBase` is a parameter-struct field the pass lambda sets per bucket through `BeginDrawParams`, and locates each instance through the visible-instance list; no per-draw data travels in a vertex stream. |
| Z2-N-REND-072 | `Zenith2/Docs/Rendering.md`:702 | Zenith2 rendering: Flux › Binding model › Draw ID on Vulkan and D3D12 | Direct (non-indirect) draws see `Flux_DrawIndex()` equal to 0: on Vulkan by definition, on D3D12 because `Flux_RasterContext` sets root parameter 1 to 0 before every direct draw. |
| Z2-N-REND-073 | `Zenith2/Docs/Rendering.md`:706 | Zenith2 rendering: Flux › Binding model › The raster vertex-stream exception | Raster passes fetch vertex data through fixed-function vertex streams whose layout is declared in Slang (see "Vertex data format"), bound exclusively through the `Flux_VertexStreamRef`/`Flux_IndexBufferRef` entries the pass declared in its `Flux_RasterTargets`; this is the one exception to bindless access, and it exists so that the fetch unit decodes the compressed formats. |
| Z2-N-REND-074 | `Zenith2/Docs/Rendering.md`:707 | Zenith2 rendering: Flux › Binding model › The raster vertex-stream exception | Compute passes that read or write mesh data (skinning, the visibility-buffer resolve, GPU scene work) reach the same buffers through `FLUX_DESCRIPTOR_SPACE_BUFFER_SRV`/`BUFFER_UAV` views and decode them with `Zenith2/Shaders/Common/VertexFormats.slang`, under GEOM's decode-route consistency rule (`Zenith2/Docs/Design/GEOM/VertexFormat.md → "Decode-route consistency"`): passes that must agree bit for bit on a vertex use one route, all fixed-function or all manual, because no API specifies the fetch unit's conversion bit for bit, and the fetch unit's agreement with the manual route is measured per format and per device by `Test_VertexFormat_FetchUnitParity`. |
| Z2-N-REND-075 | `Zenith2/Docs/Rendering.md`:711 | Zenith2 rendering: Flux › Binding model › What the binding model excludes | Zenith2 has no per-pass descriptor sets, no per-pass pipeline layouts or root signatures, no layout objects to keep in sync with shaders, and no "bind bindless" call in any public or pass-context API. |
| Z2-N-REND-076 | `Zenith2/Docs/Rendering.md`:712 | Zenith2 rendering: Flux › Binding model › What the binding model excludes | The binding-model tests `Test_Binding_PushConstantSize`, `Test_Binding_DrawIndexParity` (the same indirect bucket yields identical draw-record lookups on Vulkan and D3D12), `Test_Binding_NullSlotZero`, `Test_Binding_SlotRetirementAcrossFrames` and `Test_Binding_ViewTypeMismatchRejected` exist and pass on WARP and lavapipe. |
| Z2-N-REND-100 | `Zenith2/Docs/Rendering.md`:718 | Zenith2 rendering: Flux › Shader system and offline compilation | Slang is the source of truth for every shader program, parameter struct, vertex layout and permutation, and every C++ parameter struct is generated from it by `ZenithShaderCompiler`; no hand-written C++ mirror of a shader struct exists (Z2-D-015). |
| Z2-N-REND-101 | `Zenith2/Docs/Rendering.md`:719 | Zenith2 rendering: Flux › Shader system and offline compilation | Shaders are compiled offline exclusively, by the separate `ZenithShaderCompiler` process, at build time through a CMake custom command or in its file-watch mode during editor iteration; no game, editor or Player process compiles Slang, HLSL or any shader source (Z2-D-025). |
| Z2-N-REND-102 | `Zenith2/Docs/Rendering.md`:721 | Zenith2 rendering: Flux › Shader system and offline compilation | The runtime loads precompiled SPIR-V and DXIL blobs from the shader blob container, and driver ISA compilation happens at PSO creation through PSO precaching, never on a first draw. |
| Z2-N-REND-103 | `Zenith2/Docs/Rendering.md`:722 | Zenith2 rendering: Flux › Shader system and offline compilation | Every permutation of every program is enumerated and compiled offline; a permutation that content references and the cooked set lacks is a cook-time error, never a runtime compile. |
| Z2-N-REND-104 | `Zenith2/Docs/Rendering.md`:720 | Zenith2 rendering: Flux › Shader system and offline compilation | The one exemption from [Z2-N-REND-101] is the Stage 1 harness `Zenith_Bench_BindlessProbe` (`Zenith2/Benchmarks/BindlessProbe/`), which compiles its probe shaders under `Zenith2/Benchmarks/BindlessProbe/Shaders/` at build time with the pinned `slangc` host tool, because it runs before `ZenithShaderCompiler` exists; it is never a runtime or shipped target, every other test shader is compiled from its Slang source by `ZenithShaderCompiler` as a build step, and the ticket that lands `ZenithShaderCompiler` routes the probe's shaders through it and removes this exemption. |
| Z2-N-REND-105 | `Zenith2/Docs/Rendering/ShaderSystem.md`:43 | Zenith2 rendering: shader system and pipeline state objects › Shader source layout | Every `.slang` file under `Zenith2/Shaders/Passes/` and `Zenith2/Shaders/Tests/` is exactly one program and every file under `Zenith2/Shaders/Common/`, `Zenith2/Shaders/Lighting/`, `Zenith2/Shaders/Particles/` and `Zenith2/Shaders/Particles/Types/` is a module imported by programs; a module under those directories declares no entry point, and a program imports no other program. |
| Z2-N-REND-106 | `Zenith2/Docs/Rendering/ShaderSystem.md`:44 | Zenith2 rendering: shader system and pipeline state objects › Shader source layout | Games contribute shader code exclusively as material modules under `Zenith2/Games/<Name>/Assets/Materials/` and as code-authored particle update and shading types (implementing `Flux_ParticleUpdateType` or `Flux_ParticleShadingType`) under `Zenith2/Games/<Name>/Assets/VFX/`; a game declares no pass program, because passes are added by C++ feature functions and games contain no C++ (Z2-D-002). |
| Z2-N-REND-107 | `Zenith2/Docs/Rendering/ShaderSystem.md`:45 | Zenith2 rendering: shader system and pipeline state objects › Shader source layout | A program's stable ID is the 64-bit hash (selected by D-SEL-HASH (open, Stage 1)) of its shader-root-relative path without extension (`Passes/GTAO/GTAO`, or `Games/<Name>/Materials/<Material>` for a game material module), so renaming or moving a program changes its ID and invalidates the persistent pipeline caches' entries for it. |
| Z2-N-REND-108 | `Zenith2/Docs/Rendering/ShaderSystem.md`:109 | Zenith2 rendering: shader system and pipeline state objects › The ZenithShaderCompiler tool › Inputs and outputs | The shader-compile custom commands belong to `ZenithShadersGenerated`: `Zenith2/Engine/ZenithShadersGenerated/CMakeLists.txt` is the one helper call `zenith_add_library(ZenithShadersGenerated KIND GENERATED DEPENDS ZenithCore ZenithVertexFormat ZenithRHI SHADERS Zenith2/Shaders/)`, which adds one command per program running `ZenithShaderCompiler` with `--program`, `--depfile`, `--out-dir ${ZENITH_OUT}/generated/ZenithShadersGenerated/`, `--header-dir ${ZENITH_OUT}/generated/ZenithShadersGenerated/` and the preset's `--config`, `--target` and `--tier`, plus the one shared-types command of "Shared GPU structs"; each program command's outputs are the program's blob container, reflection, lint file and generated header (and its development container when it has a `[PermutationBuild("development")]` constant, [Z2-N-REND-993]), and no other `CMakeLists.txt` (in particular not `ZenithFlux`'s) declares a shader command. The build tree's container root is therefore `${ZENITH_OUT}/generated/ZenithShadersGenerated/Blobs/`, beside the generated headers: a program's container is `Blobs/<ProgramPath>.zshader` below the directory every test, conformance, benchmark and integration-suite target receives as the compile definition `ZENITH_SHADERS_GENERATED_DIR` (`Zenith2/Docs/Architecture/BuildSystem.md` → "Test, conformance and fuzz targets", [Z2-N-ARCH-254]). A test reads a built container only through that definition (`Zenith_ReadRepoFileAt(ZENITH_SHADERS_GENERATED_DIR, …)` with the relative path `Blobs/<ProgramPath>.zshader`), never through a path of its own, and a Player reads containers only from the cooked package ([Z2-N-REND-123]). |
| Z2-N-REND-109 | `Zenith2/Docs/Rendering/ShaderSystem.md`:110 | Zenith2 rendering: shader system and pipeline state objects › The ZenithShaderCompiler tool › Inputs and outputs | Android presets run the host `ZenithShaderCompiler` found through `ZENITH_HOST_TOOLS_DIR` and emit SPIR-V and the mobile tier; no Android target builds the compiler. |
| Z2-N-REND-110 | `Zenith2/Docs/Rendering/ShaderSystem.md`:111 | Zenith2 rendering: shader system and pipeline state objects › The ZenithShaderCompiler tool › Inputs and outputs | The compiler is deterministic: identical inputs, flags and tool versions produce byte-identical outputs, and generated headers are written only when their content changes, so an unchanged shader never triggers a C++ rebuild. |
| Z2-N-REND-111 | `Zenith2/Docs/Rendering/ShaderSystem.md`:112 | Zenith2 rendering: shader system and pipeline state objects › The ZenithShaderCompiler tool › Inputs and outputs | The compiler invokes Slang with SPIR-V 1.6 (Vulkan 1.3) and DXIL Shader Model 6.6 through DXC, row-major matrix layout, scalar layout on Vulkan, `-enable-16bit-types` for DXIL, and warnings as errors. |
| Z2-N-REND-112 | `Zenith2/Docs/Rendering/ShaderSystem.md`:113 | Zenith2 rendering: shader system and pipeline state objects › The ZenithShaderCompiler tool › Inputs and outputs | `--check` exits 3 and lists every stale output whenever any output is older than an input it depends on or was produced by a different tool version; the ShaderChecks gate step runs it after the Build step to prove the committed build graph produced every blob. |
| Z2-N-REND-113 | `Zenith2/Docs/Rendering/ShaderSystem.md`:136 | Zenith2 rendering: shader system and pipeline state objects › Slang authoring vocabulary | Every program declares exactly one `[FluxParams]` struct, and every resource field of it is a `DescriptorHandle<T>`; a program with zero or two parameter structs fails with `ZSC_ERROR_PARAMS_COUNT`. |
| Z2-N-REND-114 | `Zenith2/Docs/Rendering/ShaderSystem.md`:137 | Zenith2 rendering: shader system and pipeline state objects › Slang authoring vocabulary | A resource field's access kind is derived from its type and attributes: SRV types are `READ`, UAV types are `READ_WRITE`, and `[WriteOnly]` UAV fields are `WRITE`; the compiler emits `FULL_COVERAGE` only for fields attributed `[FullCoverage]`, and the pass author is responsible for the shader actually covering the view, which poison-on-first-use detects when it does not. |
| Z2-N-REND-115 | `Zenith2/Docs/Rendering/ShaderSystem.md`:138 | Zenith2 rendering: shader system and pipeline state objects › Slang authoring vocabulary | An attribute outside the table above, or one applied to the wrong kind of declaration, is a compile error (`ZSC_ERROR_UNKNOWN_ATTRIBUTE`, `ZSC_ERROR_ATTRIBUTE_TARGET`). |
| Z2-N-REND-116 | `Zenith2/Docs/Rendering/ShaderSystem.md`:231 | Zenith2 rendering: shader system and pipeline state objects › Generated parameter headers | Every generated header contains exactly the elements of the table above and includes nothing but `Zenith_RHIShaderInterface.h`, `Zenith_VertexFormat.h`, `Flux_SharedTypes.generated.h` and `Flux_StreamSets.generated.h`, so it uses no ZenithFlux type (Z2-N-ARCH-083) and compiles on its own in the HeaderSelfContained gate step; the C++ declarations of the shared GPU structs exist only as generated code, Slang being their source of truth (Z2-D-015). |
| Z2-N-REND-117 | `Zenith2/Docs/Rendering/ShaderSystem.md`:232 | Zenith2 rendering: shader system and pipeline state objects › Generated parameter headers | Every field of every generated parameter struct has a `static_assert(offsetof(...))` with the offset Slang reflection reports, and the struct has a `static_assert(sizeof(...))`, so a disagreement between the C++ and Slang layouts is a C++ compile error. |
| Z2-N-REND-118 | `Zenith2/Docs/Rendering/ShaderSystem.md`:233 | Zenith2 rendering: shader system and pipeline state objects › Generated parameter headers | The field table lists every resource field of the parameter struct, with offset, kind, access, stage mask, flags, dimension and element stride, and is the render graph's sole source of access information for parameter-struct fields. |

| Z2-N-REND-436 | `Zenith2/Docs/Rendering/RenderGraph.md`:493 | Zenith2 rendering: render graph, barrier synthesis and transient aliasing › Aliasing placement | Two transients share memory only when `may_share` holds, so memory reuse across queues is always ordered by a timeline wait, never by queue timing. |

| Z2-N-REND-438 | `Zenith2/Docs/Rendering/RenderGraph.md`:495 | Zenith2 rendering: render graph, barrier synthesis and transient aliasing › Aliasing placement | Each resource's first use in the frame is preceded by a discard (`UNDEFINED` transition on Vulkan, `D3D12_TEXTURE_BARRIER_FLAG_DISCARD` with `LAYOUT_UNDEFINED` on D3D12), synthesised automatically, whose source scope covers the last use of every earlier occupant of any overlapping memory. |
| Z2-N-REND-439 | `Zenith2/Docs/Rendering/RenderGraph.md`:501 | Zenith2 rendering: render graph, barrier synthesis and transient aliasing › Aliasing toggle and first-write rule › The toggle | `Flux_GraphSettings::m_bAliasTransients` switches placement at the next graph compile: off gives each transient its own dedicated allocation from the dedicated-resource cache (keyed by description hash and usage union, so the allocation is reused frame to frame), on resumes aliased placement; nothing else in the graph changes, and no restart is needed. |
| Z2-N-REND-440 | `Zenith2/Docs/Rendering/RenderGraph.md`:502 | Zenith2 rendering: render graph, barrier synthesis and transient aliasing › Aliasing toggle and first-write rule › The toggle | With aliasing off, each transient still receives the same first-use discard as with aliasing on, so the two modes present identical contents to every pass. |
| Z2-N-REND-441 | `Zenith2/Docs/Rendering/RenderGraph.md`:516 | Zenith2 rendering: render graph, barrier synthesis and transient aliasing › Aliasing toggle and first-write rule › The first-write rule | A transient's first access in the frame is a clear or a declared full-coverage write as the table above defines; a first access that is a write without full coverage is `FLUX_RG_ERROR_PARTIAL_FIRST_WRITE`, and a read (including a `LOAD` load op or a read-write UAV) of any subresource region not yet written is `FLUX_RG_ERROR_READ_BEFORE_WRITE`, both graph compile errors naming the pass, the resource and the subresource. |
| Z2-N-REND-442 | `Zenith2/Docs/Rendering/RenderGraph.md`:517 | Zenith2 rendering: render graph, barrier synthesis and transient aliasing › Aliasing toggle and first-write rule › The first-write rule | In development builds with `m_bPoisonTransients` set, the graph inserts, after each transient's discard and before its first write, a fill of every subresource with the poison value: quiet NaN for float, half and packed-float formats (`0x7FC00000`, `0x7E00`, and each R11G11B10 channel's NaN encoding), `(1, 0, 1, 0.5)` for UNORM and SNORM formats, all-ones for integer formats and 0xFFFFFFFF words for buffers; a shader that claims full coverage without achieving it, or any undefined read the compile could not see, then shows up as NaN or magenta. |
| Z2-N-REND-443 | `Zenith2/Docs/Rendering/RenderGraph.md`:518 | Zenith2 rendering: render graph, barrier synthesis and transient aliasing › Aliasing toggle and first-write rule › The first-write rule | Imported resources are exempt from the first-write rule, because version 0 of an imported resource is its previous frame's content; an imported resource whose previous content is invalid (a history after a camera cut) is read by consumers that treat the history as invalid through `m_bHistoryValid`, never by reading undefined memory. |

| Z2-N-REND-445 | `Zenith2/Docs/Rendering/RenderGraph.md`:523 | Zenith2 rendering: render graph, barrier synthesis and transient aliasing › Aliasing toggle and first-write rule › Both modes are first-class | The on-screen profiler shows transient memory with aliasing (the summed group heap sizes), without aliasing (the summed transient sizes), the savings in bytes and percent, per group and in total, and the peak over the last 300 frames; the same values appear in the frame dump's `memory` object. |

| Z2-N-REND-480 | `Zenith2/Docs/Rendering.md`:962 | Zenith2 rendering: Flux › GPU lifetime and synchronisation | Every queue has a timeline semaphore, and a resource or descriptor slot is retired once every queue's last-use value for it has completed, and not earlier; descriptor slots are reused after that retirement. |

| Z2-N-REND-485 | `Zenith2/Docs/Rendering/GpuResources.md`:37 | Zenith2 rendering: GPU lifetime, GPU scene tables and pools, recovery › Queues and timelines | Every queue has exactly one timeline (`Zenith_RHITimeline`), every submission batch signals that queue's timeline with a strictly increasing value, and the value a batch signals is recorded as the last use, on that queue, of every resource and descriptor slot the batch accessed. |
| Z2-N-REND-486 | `Zenith2/Docs/Rendering/GpuResources.md`:38 | Zenith2 rendering: GPU lifetime, GPU scene tables and pools, recovery › Queues and timelines | The render-submit thread is the one thread that waits on a GPU timeline from the CPU during a frame, and the main thread waits exclusively at a render-idle point for the five waits of the table above, none of them a frame wait; every such wait carries a timeout of 2 s, after which the device status is checked for loss (see "Device loss"); no job and no pass lambda waits on the GPU (see `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture.md → "CPU frame graph and scheduler"`). |
| Z2-N-REND-487 | `Zenith2/Docs/Rendering/GpuResources.md`:39 | Zenith2 rendering: GPU lifetime, GPU scene tables and pools, recovery › Queues and timelines | Swapchain acquire and present use the binary semaphores (Vulkan) or the swapchain's own fence (D3D12) that presentation requires; every other GPU-GPU and GPU-CPU dependency uses the queue timelines. |
| Z2-N-REND-488 | `Zenith2/Docs/Rendering/GpuResources.md`:61 | Zenith2 rendering: GPU lifetime, GPU scene tables and pools, recovery › Retirement | A resource, descriptor slot, table slot, pool slot or upload chunk is retired (freed or returned to its free list) only once every queue's completed timeline value is at or past that object's last-use value on that queue; no object is reused before its retirement. |
| Z2-N-REND-489 | `Zenith2/Docs/Rendering/GpuResources.md`:62 | Zenith2 rendering: GPU lifetime, GPU scene tables and pools, recovery › Retirement | `RetireCompleted` runs at the start of every frame on the render-submit thread, reads each queue's completed value once, and retires every satisfied entry, not just the oldest. |
| Z2-N-REND-490 | `Zenith2/Docs/Rendering/GpuResources.md`:63 | Zenith2 rendering: GPU lifetime, GPU scene tables and pools, recovery › Retirement | The deferred-deletion queue holds at most `uFLUX_DEFERRED_DELETE_CAPACITY` = 65,536 entries, a constant realised as the ZenithRHI retirement ring's `uZENITH_RHI_RETIREMENT_CAPACITY` with `uFLUX_DEFERRED_DELETE_CAPACITY` its ZenithFlux alias (`inline constexpr uint32 uFLUX_DEFERRED_DELETE_CAPACITY = uZENITH_RHI_RETIREMENT_CAPACITY;`); when it is full, `Enqueue` on the render-submit thread waits (through `WaitCPU`) for the oldest entry's last-use values and retires it first, which is the queue's full policy (see `Zenith2/Docs/Contracts.md → "Threading and concurrency contract"`), and every such wait increments `Flux.Lifetime.DeletionQueueFullWaits`. |
| Z2-N-REND-491 | `Zenith2/Docs/Rendering/GpuResources.md`:64 | Zenith2 rendering: GPU lifetime, GPU scene tables and pools, recovery › Retirement | An object that was never used on the GPU (all last-use values 0) is retired at the next `RetireCompleted`. |
| Z2-N-REND-492 | `Zenith2/Docs/Rendering/GpuResources.md`:68 | Zenith2 rendering: GPU lifetime, GPU scene tables and pools, recovery › Frames in flight | The number of GPU frames in flight is `r.framesInFlight`, 2 or 3 (default 2), within the adopted `Z2-B-RHI-008` Frames in flight row, fixed at device creation and changeable only through swapchain recreation by the procedure of the table "Changing the frames in flight" below; frame N's graph build starts only after the graphics timeline has reached the value signalled by the final graphics batch of the frame last submitted in its frame slot. |
| Z2-N-REND-493 | `Zenith2/Docs/Rendering/GpuResources.md`:82 | Zenith2 rendering: GPU lifetime, GPU scene tables and pools, recovery › Frames in flight › Changing the frames in flight | Per-frame-in-flight resources are the upload-ring segment of that frame, one timestamp query pool per queue, the command pools or allocators of every recording partition, and the frame's readback staging; each is reused only after its frame's final batch on every queue has completed. |
| Z2-N-REND-494 | `Zenith2/Docs/Rendering/GpuResources.md`:84 | Zenith2 rendering: GPU lifetime, GPU scene tables and pools, recovery › Frames in flight › Changing the frames in flight | GPU frames in flight are independent of the one frame of CPU render lag of frame pipelining (Z2-D-050): simulation N+1 overlaps the build and record of render frame N, and the GPU may additionally be executing frames N−1 and N−2. |
| Z2-N-REND-495 | `Zenith2/Docs/Rendering/GpuResources.md`:183 | Zenith2 rendering: GPU lifetime, GPU scene tables and pools, recovery › Upload ring › Upload paths | The upload ring grows only up to `Z2-B-RHI-006` Upload ring hard cap; at the cap it stalls until space frees, a single request larger than the cap is split into pieces when the request is splittable and is otherwise refused with `FLUX_UPLOAD_ERROR_TOO_LARGE`, and every exhaustion increments the `Flux.Upload.Exhausted` telemetry counter, as the tables above specify. |
| Z2-N-REND-496 | `Zenith2/Docs/Rendering/GpuResources.md`:184 | Zenith2 rendering: GPU lifetime, GPU scene tables and pools, recovery › Upload ring › Upload paths | Stalls happen exclusively on the render-submit thread, inside `ReserveFrame`; no `Allocate` and no ring write of a frame precedes that frame's `ExtendReservation` (an early one asserts); `Allocate` never stalls, allocating lock-free inside the frame's reservation, and returns `FLUX_UPLOAD_ERROR_RESERVATION_EXHAUSTED` (a programmer error, because the reservation is extended to the frame's compiled exact need) instead of growing; a raster lambda that issues more `BeginDrawParams` calls than its `ExpectedDraws` fails the frame in development builds with `FLUX_RECORD_CHECK_DRAW_PARAMS_EXCEEDED` and has the excess draws dropped in shipping builds. |
| Z2-N-REND-497 | `Zenith2/Docs/Rendering/GpuResources.md`:185 | Zenith2 rendering: GPU lifetime, GPU scene tables and pools, recovery › Upload ring › Upload paths | Worker jobs that upload data use `RequestUpload`, which enqueues into a bounded multi-producer request queue of `uFLUX_UPLOAD_REQUEST_CAPACITY` = 4,096 entries whose full policy is to reject with `FLUX_UPLOAD_ERROR_QUEUE_FULL` (the requester retries at its next tick), returns an operation handle, and never blocks the job; the requester keeps its source memory valid until the handle reports `STAGED`, and the upload-ring tests `Test_UploadRing_ReserveStallsOnSubmitThreadOnly`, `Test_UploadRing_AllocateNeverStalls`, `Test_UploadRing_RequestQueueFullRejects`, `Test_UploadRing_SplittableRequestCrossesFrames`, `Test_UploadRing_UnsplittableOverCapRefused` and `Test_UploadRing_DrawParamsBoundEnforced` exist and pass. |
| Z2-N-REND-498 | `Zenith2/Docs/Rendering/GpuResources.md`:244 | Zenith2 rendering: GPU lifetime, GPU scene tables and pools, recovery › Readbacks | A readback is an operation handle owned by the requester: it becomes `READY` after the timeline value of the frame that recorded its copy has completed, its data stays valid from `READY` until the requester calls `Release()`, and the graph never frees readback memory the requester still holds. |
| Z2-N-REND-499 | `Zenith2/Docs/Rendering/GpuResources.md`:245 | Zenith2 rendering: GPU lifetime, GPU scene tables and pools, recovery › Readbacks | Readback memory comes from a bounded readback pool sized by `Z2-B-RHI-011` GPU readback pool size (pending); `AddReadback` returns `FLUX_READBACK_ERROR_POOL_FULL` when the pool cannot hold the request (`FLUX_READBACK_ERROR_TOO_LARGE` for a range above the pool size), a readback declared in a deferred or failed frame fails with `FLUX_READBACK_ERROR_FRAME_DEFERRED` or `FLUX_READBACK_ERROR_FRAME_FAILED` and frees its range at once, and unreleased handles at renderer shutdown are reported as leaks with their request names. |
| Z2-N-REND-500 | `Zenith2/Docs/Rendering/GpuResources.md`:246 | Zenith2 rendering: GPU lifetime, GPU scene tables and pools, recovery › Readbacks | A readback whose result feeds simulation (for example editor picking) is published to simulation at a tick boundary in completion-ID order (Z2-D-053), never mid-tick. |
| Z2-N-REND-501 | `Zenith2/Docs/Rendering/GpuResources.md`:247 | Zenith2 rendering: GPU lifetime, GPU scene tables and pools, recovery › Readbacks | Device loss moves every pending readback to `FAILED`, a deferred or failed declaring frame moves its readbacks to `FAILED` with the two codes of [Z2-N-REND-499], and `Cancel()` moves a pending readback to `CANCELLED` and releases its pool space once its copy retires. |

| Z2-N-REND-504 | `Zenith2/Docs/Rendering/GpuResources.md`:275 | Zenith2 rendering: GPU lifetime, GPU scene tables and pools, recovery › GPU memory rules › Residency budgets | Flux reads the per-heap memory budget and usage every frame (`vmaGetHeapBudgets` over `VK_EXT_memory_budget`, `IDXGIAdapter3::QueryVideoMemoryInfo` for local and non-local segments) and reports GPU memory to the memory governor per category: render targets and transients, GPU scene tables, pools, streamed textures, meshes, upload ring, PSOs and other (see `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture.md → "Memory governor"`). |
| Z2-N-REND-505 | `Zenith2/Docs/Rendering/GpuResources.md`:276 | Zenith2 rendering: GPU lifetime, GPU scene tables and pools, recovery › GPU memory rules › Residency budgets | Each category's limit is its memory row: persistent render targets and view histories `Z2-B-RHI-001`, transient peak `Z2-B-RHI-010`, per-heap residency `Z2-B-RHI-003`, descriptor heaps `Z2-B-RHI-002`, GPU scene tables `Z2-B-RPROD-001`, unified vertex and index pools `Z2-B-GEOM-001`, streamed textures `Z2-B-GEOM-002`, PSOs `Z2-B-RPROD-002`, upload ring `Z2-B-RHI-006`, VT `Z2-B-ADVGFX-001`, particle pools `Z2-B-VFX-001`, all pending, within the adopted totals `Z2-B-CORE-003` Windows VRAM and `Z2-B-CORE-001` S24 process RSS (see `Zenith2/Docs/Budgets.md → "Memory budgets"`). |

| Z2-N-REND-507 | `Zenith2/Docs/Rendering/GpuResources.md`:200 | Zenith2 rendering: GPU lifetime, GPU scene tables and pools, recovery › Upload ring › Build-node upload order | The `Flux.BuildGraph` node performs the five orders of the table above in that order, every build-node producer of per-frame upload data declares its bytes at setup and writes them exclusively through `GetFrameData` at order 5, and a frame that leaves at order 4 by deferral or failure has written no ring byte; `Test_UploadRing_AllocateBeforeExtendAsserts` and `Test_UploadRing_DeferredFrameReservesExactNext` exist and pass. |
| Z2-N-REND-508 | `Zenith2/Docs/Rendering/GpuResources.md`:215 | Zenith2 rendering: GPU lifetime, GPU scene tables and pools, recovery › Upload ring › Frame deferral | A deferred frame has exactly the effects of the table above, the next frame preparation reserves the deferred frame's recorded exact need, and the GPU-scene tables after any sequence of submitted and deferred frames equal those of the same render-world sequence with no deferral (`Test_GPUScene_DeferredFrameKeepsDeltas`). |
| Z2-N-REND-509 | `Zenith2/Docs/Rendering/GpuResources.md`:227 | Zenith2 rendering: GPU lifetime, GPU scene tables and pools, recovery › Upload ring › Frame failures | A failed frame records, submits and presents nothing, keeps the GPU-scene pending store and the view histories as a deferred frame does, fails its readbacks with `FLUX_READBACK_ERROR_FRAME_FAILED`, is never retried as a deferral, and the 30th consecutive failed frame ends the run through the frame-failure exit of the table above with `EXIT_CODE_DEVICE_LOST` (6) (`Test_UploadRing_NeedAboveCapFailsFrame`). |
| Z2-N-REND-530 | `Zenith2/Docs/Rendering.md`:971 | Zenith2 rendering: Flux › GPU scene tables and pools | Indirectly reachable resources follow the UE5 GPU Scene model: a small fixed set of persistent table buffers (instance, primitive, material-slot, light) is imported into the graph every frame as external buffers, and passes read them through ordinary parameter-struct fields (Z2-D-058). |
| Z2-N-REND-531 | `Zenith2/Docs/Rendering.md`:972 | Zenith2 rendering: Flux › GPU scene tables and pools | The CPU never writes table memory directly: updates are queued as records in the frame's upload ring and applied by a scatter-upload graph pass that declares a write on the table and runs before any consumer. |

| Z2-N-REND-533 | `Zenith2/Docs/Rendering.md`:974 | Zenith2 rendering: Flux › GPU scene tables and pools | Dynamic content reaches its consumers through pools (skinned-vertex, particle, procedural-geometry, VT physical/page-table), which are ordinary graph resources written by a declared producer pass and read by declared consumer passes; tables stay read-only. |
| Z2-N-REND-535 | `Zenith2/Docs/Rendering/GpuResources.md`:373 | Zenith2 rendering: GPU lifetime, GPU scene tables and pools, recovery › GPU scene tables › Light record (64 bytes) | The four table buffers hold records with exactly the byte layouts of the four tables above, identical on both APIs; the records are declared `[FluxShared]` in `Zenith2/Shaders/Common/GPUScene.slang`, and their C++ declarations are generated into `Flux_SharedTypes.generated.h` with a size and offset assert per field, so the CPU and GPU layouts cannot diverge (Z2-D-058, Z2-D-015). |
| Z2-N-REND-536 | `Zenith2/Docs/Rendering/GpuResources.md`:374 | Zenith2 rendering: GPU lifetime, GPU scene tables and pools, recovery › GPU scene tables › Light record (64 bytes) | The tables are imported into the render graph every frame as write-restricted external buffers through `Flux_GPUScene::ImportTables`, passes read them through ordinary parameter-struct fields so the graph tracks them like any other buffer, and a write declared on them by any pass other than the scatter-upload and growth passes is `FLUX_RG_ERROR_TABLE_WRITE`. |
| Z2-N-REND-537 | `Zenith2/Docs/Rendering/GpuResources.md`:375 | Zenith2 rendering: GPU lifetime, GPU scene tables and pools, recovery › GPU scene tables › Light record (64 bytes) | The initial capacities are 262,144 instances, 65,536 primitives, 65,536 material slots and 4,096 lights on `FLUX_CAPABILITY_TIER_DESKTOP`, and a quarter of each on `FLUX_CAPABILITY_TIER_MOBILE`; the maximum capacities follow from the memory row `Z2-B-RPROD-001` GPU scene tables (pending) divided by the record sizes above. |
| Z2-N-REND-538 | `Zenith2/Docs/Rendering/GpuResources.md`:376 | Zenith2 rendering: GPU lifetime, GPU scene tables and pools, recovery › GPU scene tables › Light record (64 bytes) | Table slots are allocated from per-table free lists on the CPU; a freed slot is recycled only after the retirement fence of every frame that could have read it, as UE5 does with deferred instance-range frees. |
| Z2-N-REND-539 | `Zenith2/Docs/Rendering/GpuResources.md`:377 | Zenith2 rendering: GPU lifetime, GPU scene tables and pools, recovery › GPU scene tables › Light record (64 bytes) | Transparent instances are sorted on the CPU per view by the view-space depth of their bounds centre plus the material's `sortBias` and receive one non-compacted indirect command each in that order, emitted by the transparent step of `Zenith2/Docs/Rendering/Culling.md` "Transparent commands", whose instance count the GPU culling writes (0 when culled); the CPU learns nothing of which transparent instances were culled. |
| Z2-N-REND-540 | `Zenith2/Docs/Rendering/GpuResources.md`:398 | Zenith2 rendering: GPU lifetime, GPU scene tables and pools, recovery › Scatter upload | The CPU never writes table memory directly: every change reaches a table through a scatter record in the frame's upload ring and the scatter-upload graph pass, which declares its writes on the tables and is ordered before every reader by the graph. |
| Z2-N-REND-541 | `Zenith2/Docs/Rendering/GpuResources.md`:399 | Zenith2 rendering: GPU lifetime, GPU scene tables and pools, recovery › Scatter upload | Records of one frame never target the same slot twice (step 1 coalesces them), so the scatter pass has no write race and its result is independent of GPU thread order. |
| Z2-N-REND-542 | `Zenith2/Docs/Rendering/GpuResources.md`:400 | Zenith2 rendering: GPU lifetime, GPU scene tables and pools, recovery › Scatter upload | Reads of a table by earlier frames are ordered before a subsequent frame's scatter write by the read-to-write barrier the graph emits for the tracked external buffer; queue order alone is not relied on, and consumers on other queues are covered by the per-queue timelines and ownership rules, so no CPU-side table versioning exists. |
| Z2-N-REND-543 | `Zenith2/Docs/Rendering/GpuResources.md`:401 | Zenith2 rendering: GPU lifetime, GPU scene tables and pools, recovery › Scatter upload | A frame with no changed records adds no scatter pass. |
| Z2-N-REND-544 | `Zenith2/Docs/Rendering/GpuResources.md`:405 | Zenith2 rendering: GPU lifetime, GPU scene tables and pools, recovery › Table growth | When a slot allocation would exceed a table's capacity, the GPU scene creates a table buffer of twice the capacity (clamped to the maximum capacity row), adds a resize-and-copy graph pass (`GPUSceneGrow`, a buffer copy of the old contents) before that frame's scatter pass, switches every subsequent import to the new buffer, and retires the old buffer through deferred deletion. |
| Z2-N-REND-545 | `Zenith2/Docs/Rendering/GpuResources.md`:406 | Zenith2 rendering: GPU lifetime, GPU scene tables and pools, recovery › Table growth | An allocation beyond a table's maximum capacity fails with `FLUX_GPU_SCENE_ERROR_TABLE_FULL`, the instance or light is not rendered, and `Flux.GPUScene.TableFull` increments; no existing slot is evicted. |
| Z2-N-REND-546 | `Zenith2/Docs/Rendering/GpuResources.md`:429 | Zenith2 rendering: GPU lifetime, GPU scene tables and pools, recovery › Persistent resources | Everything a table references (textures, the unified mesh and vertex pools, the index pools, material constants) is a `Flux_PersistentReadOnly` resource: it stays in the steady `PERSISTENT_READ` state after its initial upload, and the graph does not track it per frame. |
| Z2-N-REND-547 | `Zenith2/Docs/Rendering/GpuResources.md`:430 | Zenith2 rendering: GPU lifetime, GPU scene tables and pools, recovery › Persistent resources | A `Flux_PersistentReadOnly` resource cannot hand out a UAV, render-target or depth-stencil view to any pass: the graph API has no such overload (the `UAV` overloads for these types are deleted), and the graph additionally rejects, with `FLUX_RG_ERROR_PERSISTENT_WRITE`, any frame in which the same RHI resource is imported writable; so two tables, or a table and a direct field, may reference the same resource, because every such use is a read. |
| Z2-N-REND-548 | `Zenith2/Docs/Rendering/GpuResources.md`:431 | Zenith2 rendering: GPU lifetime, GPU scene tables and pools, recovery › Persistent resources | A persistent resource's initial contents, and any subsequent sub-allocation into a persistent pool (a new mesh into the unified stream pools, a new material-constants block), are written on the copy queue into regions no table entry references; the resource or region becomes visible through the graph's one-time acquire list in the first frame after the copy timeline passes the upload, and is referenced by a table only after that acquire, through the scatter upload. |
| Z2-N-REND-549 | `Zenith2/Docs/Rendering/GpuResources.md`:432 | Zenith2 rendering: GPU lifetime, GPU scene tables and pools, recovery › Persistent resources | Referenced resources are reference-counted by the asset system, and a released persistent resource is destroyed through deferred deletion with the last-use values of the frame in which it was released on every queue. |
| Z2-N-REND-550 | `Zenith2/Docs/Rendering/GpuResources.md`:433 | Zenith2 rendering: GPU lifetime, GPU scene tables and pools, recovery › Persistent resources | Resources that are written during the frame (VT physical and page-table updates, VT feedback, GPU simulation state, streaming uploads in flight) are never reached through tables; they are ordinary graph resources in the passes that write and read them. |
| Z2-N-REND-551 | `Zenith2/Docs/Rendering/GpuResources.md`:445 | Zenith2 rendering: GPU lifetime, GPU scene tables and pools, recovery › Pools | Dynamic content reaches its consumers exclusively through the four pools of the table above: a table entry references either a `Flux_PersistentReadOnly` resource directly or a slot within a pool resource, the producer pass declares a write on the pool, every consumer binds the pool as a parameter-struct field (or vertex stream) declaring a read, and the table supplies only the slot index, so the graph always sees producer-to-consumer ordering and tables stay read-only. |
| Z2-N-REND-552 | `Zenith2/Docs/Rendering/GpuResources.md`:446 | Zenith2 rendering: GPU lifetime, GPU scene tables and pools, recovery › Pools | The skinned-vertex pool is double-buffered: each frame the halves swap, skinning writes the current half, and velocity for skinned and morphed geometry reads the previous half; an instance with `FLUX_INSTANCE_FLAG_HISTORY_RESET` uses its current positions as its previous positions for that frame. |
| Z2-N-REND-553 | `Zenith2/Docs/Rendering/GpuResources.md`:447 | Zenith2 rendering: GPU lifetime, GPU scene tables and pools, recovery › Pools | Pool slots are allocated on the CPU and retired through deferred deletion like table slots, and a pool whose capacity is exhausted grows by the resize-and-copy pattern of table growth, bounded by its memory row (`Z2-B-VFX-001` for the particle pool; the skinned-vertex and procedural-geometry pools within `Z2-B-GEOM-001`; VT within `Z2-B-ADVGFX-001`). |
| Z2-N-REND-554 | `Zenith2/Docs/Rendering/GpuResources.md`:460 | Zenith2 rendering: GPU lifetime, GPU scene tables and pools, recovery › Texture streaming swap | Texture streaming uses reallocate-and-swap as the table above specifies: the new resource is filled on the copy queue, swapped into the table by a scatter upload after the copy fence completes, and the old one retires through deferred deletion; a texture in use is never written in place. |
| Z2-N-REND-555 | `Zenith2/Docs/Rendering/GpuResources.md`:464 | Zenith2 rendering: GPU lifetime, GPU scene tables and pools, recovery › GPU scene tests | The GPU scene tests below exist in `Zenith2/Engine/ZenithFlux/Tests/GPUScene/`, run on WARP and lavapipe with synchronization validation and GPU-based validation clean throughout, and pass. |
| Z2-N-REND-590 | `Zenith2/Docs/Rendering.md`:1048 | Zenith2 rendering: Flux › Render views › Flux_View | A `Flux_View` is built by `Flux_BuildViewList` each frame and is immutable for the rest of that frame's graph build; features read it through `Flux_ViewContext` and never modify it. |
| Z2-N-REND-591 | `Zenith2/Docs/Rendering.md`:1049 | Zenith2 rendering: Flux › Render views › Flux_View | The render extent is `ceil(output × m_fDynamicResolutionScale)` per axis, the scale never goes below the dynamic-resolution floor of `Zenith2/Docs/Budgets.md → "Frame-rate definitions and measurement procedure"`, and every pass before the TAA/upscaler runs at render extent while every pass from the upscaler on runs at output extent. |
| Z2-N-REND-592 | `Zenith2/Docs/Rendering.md`:1078 | Zenith2 rendering: Flux › Render views › Depth mode | A perspective view that is not a shadow view uses standard Z when its far plane is finite and its worst-case D24 depth step at the far plane, `Δz(f) = 2⁻²⁴ · f · (f − n) / n`, is at most `Z2-B-RPROD-029` D24 depth-precision tolerance (pending; proposed 0.25 m), and otherwise uses reversed Z with D32F_S8 (Z2-D-061); the row's adopted value is strictly below 0.4767 m (the far-plane step of a 2 km view at a 0.5 m near plane), so that a 2 km outdoor main view takes reversed Z for every near plane from 0.05 m to 0.5 m. |
| Z2-N-REND-593 | `Zenith2/Docs/Rendering.md`:1079 | Zenith2 rendering: Flux › Render views › Depth mode | Orthographic views (every directional shadow cascade and every other orthographic view) and every shadow view always use standard Z with D24S8, or D32F_S8 under the capability fallback, and never reversed Z; the precision check is not evaluated for them. |
| Z2-N-REND-594 | `Zenith2/Docs/Rendering.md`:1080 | Zenith2 rendering: Flux › Render views › Depth mode | Depth format follows capability, independently of Z direction: D24S8 where `m_bDepthD24S8` is true, else D32F_S8; reversed-Z views use D32F_S8 in every case; stencil (S8) is present in every depth target. |
| Z2-N-REND-595 | `Zenith2/Docs/Rendering.md`:1081 | Zenith2 rendering: Flux › Render views › Depth mode | Every depth-mode combination runs through the same code: projection matrices, clear values, comparison operators and depth linearisation come from `Flux_DepthModeOps` (`GetClearDepth`, `GetOpaqueCompare`, `GetColourCompare`, `GetInvertedCompare` for back-face volume draws such as deferred decal boxes (`GREATER_OR_EQUAL` for standard Z, `LESS_OR_EQUAL` for reversed Z), `BuildProjection`, `LineariseDepth` on the CPU, and the matching `Zenith2/Shaders/Common/DepthMode.slang` functions), and no other shader or C++ code branches on the depth format or Z direction or names a depth compare operator. |
| Z2-N-REND-596 | `Zenith2/Docs/Rendering.md`:1082 | Zenith2 rendering: Flux › Render views › Depth mode | A D32F_S8 depth target on a view whose `m_eReason` is neither `FLUX_DEPTH_MODE_REASON_PRECISION` nor `FLUX_DEPTH_MODE_REASON_CAPABILITY` is a graph compile error (`FLUX_RG_ERROR_D32_UNJUSTIFIED`), which is the enforcement of the depth-format rule of "GPU performance rules and lints". |
| Z2-N-REND-597 | `Zenith2/Docs/Rendering.md`:1118 | Zenith2 rendering: Flux › Render views › View identity and persistent state | `Flux_MakeViewID` is a 64-bit hash, selected by D-SEL-HASH (open, Stage 1), of the kind, the owner ID and the sub-index in the table above, so a view's identity is stable across frames, sessions and view-list order. |
| Z2-N-REND-598 | `Zenith2/Docs/Rendering.md`:1119 | Zenith2 rendering: Flux › Render views › View identity and persistent state | `Flux_ViewRegistry` owns every `Flux_ViewPersistentState`, keyed by `Flux_ViewID`; each view's histories are independent, so an editor viewport never reads or writes the main view's TAA history, and no two views share a history resource. |
| Z2-N-REND-599 | `Zenith2/Docs/Rendering.md`:1120 | Zenith2 rendering: Flux › Render views › View identity and persistent state | A view's histories are invalid (`m_bHistoryValid == false`) on the first frame the view exists, on a camera cut, on an output-extent change or a change of a history resource's own extent, and on a shading-path change; every temporal consumer treats an invalid history as "no history" (TAA takes the current frame, HiZ phase 1 treats every instance as visible-last-frame-unknown, exposure starts from the view's configured exposure). |
| Z2-N-REND-600 | `Zenith2/Docs/Rendering.md`:1122 | Zenith2 rendering: Flux › Render views › View identity and persistent state | Persistent state of a view absent from the view list for `uFLUX_VIEW_STATE_RETIRE_FRAMES` = 60 consecutive frames is released through deferred deletion (the retirement rule of "GPU lifetime and synchronisation"). |
| Z2-N-REND-601 | `Zenith2/Docs/Rendering.md`:1185 | Zenith2 rendering: Flux › Render views › View kinds | A view's feature mask is the default of its kind in the table above, intersected with the quality tier's enabled features and the capability matrix of "Shading paths and capability matrix", then adjusted by per-view overrides; a feature whose bit is clear adds no passes for that view. |
| Z2-N-REND-602 | `Zenith2/Docs/Rendering.md`:1187 | Zenith2 rendering: Flux › Render views › View kinds | `VIEW_KIND_STEREO` is declared now and rejected by `Flux_BuildViewList` with `FLUX_VIEW_ERROR_KIND_UNAVAILABLE` until Stage 7, so the view identity, per-view state and feature-function signatures stay XR-ready without shipping stereo (Z2-D-072). |
| Z2-N-REND-603 | `Zenith2/Docs/Rendering.md`:1211 | Zenith2 rendering: Flux › Render views › View list | The view list is built in the order of the table above, with the stated tie-breaks, so that view-list order, pass declaration order and every derived ordering are deterministic for the same render world. |
| Z2-N-REND-604 | `Zenith2/Docs/Rendering.md`:1212 | Zenith2 rendering: Flux › Render views › View list | Shadowed-light selection and shadow-atlas tile allocation happen on the CPU during view-list construction, and the resulting atlas rectangles are written into the light records through the frame's GPU-scene scatter upload before any shadow view renders. |
| Z2-N-REND-605 | `Zenith2/Docs/Rendering.md`:1216 | Zenith2 rendering: Flux › Render views › Shared producers | The shared producers run once per frame, not once per view: GPU-scene scatter upload, streaming uploads, skinning, particle simulation, procedural geometry, the light and decal list, the shadow-atlas allocation and shadow-view records, the sky LUTs (transmittance, multiple scattering, sky view), and with the Environment module ENV's cloud layer (Stage 4) and its Stage 5 prologue passes; their outputs reach per-view passes through `Flux_FrameShared`. |
| Z2-N-REND-606 | `Zenith2/Docs/Rendering.md`:1307 | Zenith2 rendering: Flux › Render views › Per-view feature functions and Flux_ViewContext | Per-view feature functions are called once per view, with that view's `Flux_ViewContext`, and add that view's passes to the same frame graph as every other view, so the graph schedules across all views together and aliases their transients against each other. |
| Z2-N-REND-607 | `Zenith2/Docs/Rendering.md`:1308 | Zenith2 rendering: Flux › Render views › Per-view feature functions and Flux_ViewContext | Dependencies between views are explicit graph edges carried by resource handles: a texture view's output handle is read by the main view's material pass, and the shadow atlas handle is read by the lighting passes; no view reads another view's resource except through a handle the graph can see. |
| Z2-N-REND-608 | `Zenith2/Docs/Rendering.md`:1312 | Zenith2 rendering: Flux › Render views › View budgets | The number of views per frame is capped per quality tier by `Z2-B-RPROD-022` View count per frame per quality tier (pending), and texture views and captures by `Z2-B-RPROD-023` Texture views and captures per frame (pending); views beyond a cap are not rendered that frame and are counted in `Flux.Views.Deferred`. |
| Z2-N-REND-609 | `Zenith2/Docs/Rendering.md`:1313 | Zenith2 rendering: Flux › Render views › View budgets | Texture views and captures are round-robined across frames: each has a requested update interval in frames, and the scheduler picks, each frame, the requests whose interval has elapsed in ascending order of (frames overdue descending, view ID ascending) up to the tier cap. |
| Z2-N-REND-610 | `Zenith2/Docs/Rendering.md`:1317 | Zenith2 rendering: Flux › Render views › View delivery staging | Stage 2 delivers view identity, per-view resources and independent per-view histories (needed by shadow views and editor previews from Stage 3); Stage 5 adds advanced multi-view (many simultaneous captures and probes, stereo-ready paths). |
| Z2-N-REND-611 | `Zenith2/Docs/Rendering.md`:1321 | Zenith2 rendering: Flux › Render views › Render view tests | The view tests `Test_View_IDStableAcrossListOrder`, `Test_View_EditorHistoryIndependentOfMain` (an editor viewport's TAA leaves the main view's history bit-identical), `Test_View_CameraCutResetsHistory`, `Test_View_OutputExtentChangeResetsHistory` (an output-extent change invalidates every history; a dynamic-resolution step from 1.0 to 0.9 keeps `m_bHistoryValid`), `Test_View_PersistentStateRetired`, `Test_View_DepthModePrecisionCheck` (analytic cases either side of the tolerance; a 2 km view with near planes 0.05 m, 0.2 m and 0.5 m takes reversed Z with D32F_S8; an orthographic 2 km cascade and a perspective spot-light shadow view take standard Z with D24S8), `Test_View_D32UnjustifiedRejected`, `Test_View_ListOrderDeterministic` and `Test_View_TextureViewEdgeToMain` exist and pass. |
| Z2-N-REND-612 | `Zenith2/Docs/Rendering.md`:1121 | Zenith2 rendering: Flux › Render views › View identity and persistent state | A render-extent change alone (a dynamic-resolution step) keeps `m_bHistoryValid`: the output-extent histories (TAA colour, exposure), the froxel history (a fixed grid) and the SSR previous colour (sampled by normalised UV) stay valid as they are, each render-extent history (velocity, the SSGI history) is recreated at the new extent when its half next becomes current and its previous half is resampled by normalised UV with the extent recorded in `m_uRenderWidth`/`m_uRenderHeight`, and the previous HiZ pyramid is projected with its own extent. |
| Z2-N-REND-630 | `Zenith2/Docs/Rendering.md`:1327 | Zenith2 rendering: Flux › Hierarchical-Z occlusion culling | Two-phase HiZ occlusion culling is present in every shading path and runs for every main, editor and texture view, inside the shared GPU-scene pipeline after frustum culling (Z2-D-060). |
| Z2-N-REND-631 | `Zenith2/Docs/Rendering.md`:1328 | Zenith2 rendering: Flux › Hierarchical-Z occlusion culling | The HiZ pyramid is RG16F, holding linear view-space depth: the max (farthest) channel, rounded up, drives occlusion culling; the min (nearest) channel, rounded down, drives SSR's hierarchical tracing. |
| Z2-N-REND-632 | `Zenith2/Docs/Rendering.md`:1329 | Zenith2 rendering: Flux › Hierarchical-Z occlusion culling | Occlusion never lags a frame: phase 1 tests last frame's visible instances against last frame's unmodified pyramid with last frame's view-projection, and phase 2 tests every instance not drawn in phase 1 against this frame's pyramid. |
| Z2-N-REND-633 | `Zenith2/Docs/Rendering.md`:1330 | Zenith2 rendering: Flux › Hierarchical-Z occlusion culling | Culling writes compacted indirect draw arguments with a count buffer, per material bucket and pipeline, so a culled object never reaches the vertex or pixel stage and the CPU never sees per-object visibility. |
| Z2-N-REND-635 | `Zenith2/Docs/Rendering/Culling.md`:23 | Zenith2 rendering: hierarchical-Z occlusion culling › Pyramid format | The HiZ pyramid stores linear view-space depth in RG16F, never raw device depth: the max channel drives occlusion culling and the min channel drives SSR's hierarchical tracing, and both stay within the 16-bit render-target rule. |
| Z2-N-REND-636 | `Zenith2/Docs/Rendering/Culling.md`:24 | Zenith2 rendering: hierarchical-Z occlusion culling › Pyramid format | Linear depth increases away from the camera in every depth mode, so the pyramid and the occlusion test contain no min/max inversion and no branch on depth mode anywhere outside mip-0 conversion. |
| Z2-N-REND-637 | `Zenith2/Docs/Rendering/Culling.md`:25 | Zenith2 rendering: hierarchical-Z occlusion culling › Pyramid format | Far-plane or cleared texels, texels whose device or linear depth is NaN, and any linear depth at or above 65,504 m store +∞ in both channels, so a NaN never enters the max/min reduction; +∞ culls nothing, which is conservative and covers infinite-far reversed-Z projections. |
| Z2-N-REND-638 | `Zenith2/Docs/Rendering/Culling.md`:59 | Zenith2 rendering: hierarchical-Z occlusion culling › Pyramid construction › Conservative rounding | Mip 0 converts device depth to linear depth once, per the view's depth mode, with the formulas of the conversion table, and rounds the max channel up and the min channel down to half precision with the functions above; every coarser mip is a max (and min) of already-rounded values, which is exact, so nothing ever rounds the max channel towards nearer. |
| Z2-N-REND-639 | `Zenith2/Docs/Rendering/Culling.md`:60 | Zenith2 rendering: hierarchical-Z occlusion culling › Pyramid construction › Conservative rounding | Each texel of mip n+1 stores the max of the max channel and the min of the min channel over its footprint in mip n; the footprint is 2×2, except that when mip n has an odd width (height) the last column (row) of mip n+1 also folds in the extra column (row) of mip n, giving 3×2, 2×3 or 3×3 footprints at the edges, so the pyramid stays conservative. |
| Z2-N-REND-640 | `Zenith2/Docs/Rendering/Culling.md`:75 | Zenith2 rendering: hierarchical-Z occlusion culling › Pyramid construction › Single-pass downsampler | The pyramid is built by one compute dispatch of a single-pass downsampler (SPD style) as the table above specifies, declared with a `[FullCoverage]` UAV per mip and a cleared counter, and no per-mip pass exists. |
| Z2-N-REND-641 | `Zenith2/Docs/Rendering/Culling.md`:87 | Zenith2 rendering: hierarchical-Z occlusion culling › Occlusion test | The occlusion test is exactly the five steps above: a candidate is culled when its nearest linear depth is farther than the pyramid's farthest depth over its footprint, which guarantees it is hidden behind geometry already drawn, and candidates crossing the near plane or with a degenerate projection are always kept. |
| Z2-N-REND-642 | `Zenith2/Docs/Rendering/Culling.md`:88 | Zenith2 rendering: hierarchical-Z occlusion culling › Occlusion test | The projection and mip-selection mathematics exist once, in the module `Zenith2/Shaders/Common/HiZTest.slang` (a module, because the instance-cull, meshlet-cull, transparent-cull and HiZ debug overlay programs all import it and [Z2-N-REND-105] forbids a program importing another), and a bit-matching CPU reference in `Zenith2/Engine/ZenithFlux/Tests/Culling/` drives the analytic unit tests. |
| Z2-N-REND-643 | `Zenith2/Docs/Rendering/Culling.md`:99 | Zenith2 rendering: hierarchical-Z occlusion culling › Two-phase culling | Culling runs in two phases so occlusion never lags a frame: phase 1 tests last frame's visible instances with current bounds and the previous view-projection against last frame's unmodified pyramid (the conservative formulation of Nanite and Haar/Aaltonen; the pyramid is never reprojected), and phase 2 tests every instance phase 1 did not draw against this frame's pyramid with the current view-projection. |
| Z2-N-REND-644 | `Zenith2/Docs/Rendering/Culling.md`:100 | Zenith2 rendering: hierarchical-Z occlusion culling › Two-phase culling | The next frame's visibility bits are the current half of the view's ping-pong visibility-bits buffer, cleared to zero by an `AddClearBuffer` pass before phase 1; the emit steps of both phases set the bit of every instance they draw, so after phase 2 the buffer holds the union of the phase-1 and phase-2 draws, and no emit field claims full coverage of it. |
| Z2-N-REND-645 | `Zenith2/Docs/Rendering/Culling.md`:101 | Zenith2 rendering: hierarchical-Z occlusion culling › Two-phase culling | The final pyramid of the frame is kept as the view's persistent HiZ: its max channel serves next frame's phase 1, and its min channel serves SSR's hierarchical tracing this frame. |
| Z2-N-REND-646 | `Zenith2/Docs/Rendering/Culling.md`:102 | Zenith2 rendering: hierarchical-Z occlusion culling › Two-phase culling | When a view's history is invalid (first frame, camera cut, output-extent change, shading-path change), phase 1 has no candidates and phase 2 tests every instance against the pyramid built from an empty depth (all +∞), so every frustum-visible instance is drawn that frame. |
| Z2-N-REND-647 | `Zenith2/Docs/Rendering/Culling.md`:103 | Zenith2 rendering: hierarchical-Z occlusion culling › Two-phase culling | On tile-based GPUs, two-phase culling forces a mid-frame depth store and reload between the prepass phases; that bandwidth is budgeted by `Z2-B-RPROD-026` HiZ mid-frame depth store and reload bandwidth (pending) and is part of the D-TBDR measurement (open, Stage 5). |
| Z2-N-REND-648 | `Zenith2/Docs/Rendering/Culling.md`:124 | Zenith2 rendering: hierarchical-Z occlusion culling › Indirect draw compaction | Culling runs as compute over the GPU-scene instance buffer and writes compacted indirect draw arguments (`Flux_IndirectDrawIndexed`, 24 bytes) plus one count per bucket, per material bucket and pipeline, consumed by `vkCmdDrawIndexedIndirectCount` or `ExecuteIndirect` with a count buffer, so a culled object never reaches the vertex or pixel stage and the CPU never sees per-object visibility. |
| Z2-N-REND-649 | `Zenith2/Docs/Rendering/Culling.md`:125 | Zenith2 rendering: hierarchical-Z occlusion culling › Indirect draw compaction | Indirect buckets are keyed by (pipeline, raster stream-set code), taken from the material-slot record's bucket fields for the pass kind, and compaction emits one command slot per draw key (material slot, LOD) per bucket kind, each bucket's command region being sized on the CPU from its registered slots as the tables above specify, so the GPU never writes past a region. |
| Z2-N-REND-650 | `Zenith2/Docs/Rendering/Culling.md`:126 | Zenith2 rendering: hierarchical-Z occlusion culling › Indirect draw compaction | Compaction is deterministic: survivors are counted per command, the prefix step writes each non-zero command, and the separate `CullSort` dispatch orders each command's visible-instance range by instance index (a range bounded by the instance count), so the same scene and camera produce byte-identical indirect arguments and visible-instance lists on every run and on both APIs. |
| Z2-N-REND-651 | `Zenith2/Docs/Rendering/Culling.md`:134 | Zenith2 rendering: hierarchical-Z occlusion culling › Indirect draw compaction › Per-instance cull distance | Shadow views, depth-only opaque buckets and every position-only draw use the primitive's position-only index range when `m_uPositionOnlyFirstIndex` is valid. |
| Z2-N-REND-652 | `Zenith2/Docs/Rendering/Culling.md`:138 | Zenith2 rendering: hierarchical-Z occlusion culling › Meshlet culling | The visibility-buffer path runs the same two-phase test per meshlet after the instance test: each surviving instance's meshlets are tested for frustum, for backfacing with the meshlet's normal cone, and against the HiZ pyramid (phase 1 with the previous view-projection and last frame's pyramid, phase 2 with the current ones), and survivors are appended to the visible-meshlet list. |
| Z2-N-REND-653 | `Zenith2/Docs/Rendering/Culling.md`:139 | Zenith2 rendering: hierarchical-Z occlusion culling › Meshlet culling | The visible-meshlet list feeds `DrawMeshTasksIndirectCount` when `m_bMeshShaders` is set and otherwise a compute-expanded indirect index draw (the fallback), both producing identical visibility IDs. |
| Z2-N-REND-654 | `Zenith2/Docs/Rendering/Culling.md`:140 | Zenith2 rendering: hierarchical-Z occlusion culling › Meshlet culling | The visible-meshlet list holds at most 2²⁵ entries (the visibility ID's 25-bit meshlet field); a frame whose survivors would exceed it keeps the first 2²⁵ in deterministic order, draws the rest as ordinary instance-level draws of the visibility raster fallback, and increments `Flux.Culling.MeshletOverflow`. |
| Z2-N-REND-655 | `Zenith2/Docs/Rendering/Culling.md`:144 | Zenith2 rendering: hierarchical-Z occlusion culling › Shadow view culling | Shadow views use frustum and distance culling (the light's range, the cascade's far split), plus HiZ against the shadow view's own cascade-depth pyramid wherever the capability tier enables shadow HiZ; the shadow pyramid uses orthographic or perspective linear depth along the light view and the same two-phase scheme keyed by the shadow view's ID. |
| Z2-N-REND-656 | `Zenith2/Docs/Rendering/Culling.md`:145 | Zenith2 rendering: hierarchical-Z occlusion culling › Shadow view culling | A shadow view whose projection moved by more than its texel-snapping step since last frame, or whose history is invalid, skips the phase-1 HiZ test (frustum and distance only) for that frame. |
| Z2-N-REND-657 | `Zenith2/Docs/Rendering/Culling.md`:157 | Zenith2 rendering: hierarchical-Z occlusion culling › Culling diagnostics | The culling passes write the counters of the table above into a small readback buffer every frame, and the on-screen profiler shows them per view and per phase with at most `r.framesInFlight` frames of latency (the counters travel through a readback resolved when the frame slot is reused), each tagged with the frame index it describes. |
| Z2-N-REND-658 | `Zenith2/Docs/Rendering/Culling.md`:158 | Zenith2 rendering: hierarchical-Z occlusion culling › Culling diagnostics | A debug view (`r.debug.hiz`) overlays the chosen pyramid mip and channel, and the screen rectangles of culled bounds coloured by the phase that culled them. |
| Z2-N-REND-659 | `Zenith2/Docs/Rendering/Culling.md`:176 | Zenith2 rendering: hierarchical-Z occlusion culling › Culling tests | The culling tests of the table above exist in `Zenith2/Engine/ZenithFlux/Tests/Culling/`, run on WARP and lavapipe with validation clean, and pass. |
| Z2-N-REND-660 | `Zenith2/Docs/Rendering/Culling.md`:207 | Zenith2 rendering: hierarchical-Z occlusion culling › Transparent commands | The transparent pass departs from compacted indirect arguments on purpose: its commands are fixed, non-compacted slots in CPU sort order, one per transparent entry (one per instance-section, two for a `backThenFront` section), whose instance count the GPU culling writes (1 visible, 0 culled), and no count buffer exists for them. |
| Z2-N-REND-661 | `Zenith2/Docs/Rendering/Culling.md`:208 | Zenith2 rendering: hierarchical-Z occlusion culling › Transparent commands | Transparent commands are collected, sorted, grouped into runs, emitted and drawn exactly as the table above specifies, so draw order equals CPU sort order on both APIs and every culled entry draws nothing. |
| Z2-N-REND-662 | `Zenith2/Docs/Rendering/Culling.md`:133 | Zenith2 rendering: hierarchical-Z occlusion culling › Indirect draw compaction › Per-instance cull distance | The per-instance cull distance applies in every view that culls instances, shadow views included, with the frame's LOD reference view, so an instance culled by distance in the main view casts no shadow, and a primitive without the flag is never distance-culled by it. |
| Z2-N-REND-690 | `Zenith2/Docs/Rendering.md`:1356 | Zenith2 rendering: Flux › Shading paths and capability matrix › The four shading paths | Zenith2 provides exactly four shading paths behind `Flux_ShadingPath`, with clustered Forward+ as the default; Forward, Deferred and visibility buffer are supported behind the capability matrix below (Z2-D-035). |
| Z2-N-REND-691 | `Zenith2/Docs/Rendering.md`:1357 | Zenith2 rendering: Flux › Shading paths and capability matrix › The four shading paths | `Flux_SelectShadingPath` returns `FLUX_SHADING_PATH_FORWARD_PLUS` for every view on `ZENITH_RHI_CAPABILITY_TIER_MOBILE`; on desktop it returns `FLUX_SHADING_PATH_FORWARD` for `VIEW_KIND_PREVIEW` and `VIEW_KIND_CAPTURE`, and `Flux_RendererSettings::m_eShadingPath` for main, editor and texture views. Until the Forward path exists (Stage 5) it returns `FLUX_SHADING_PATH_FORWARD_PLUS` for desktop `VIEW_KIND_CAPTURE` and `VIEW_KIND_PREVIEW` views, and from the Stage 5 Forward-path implementation on it returns `FLUX_SHADING_PATH_FORWARD` for them. |
| Z2-N-REND-692 | `Zenith2/Docs/Rendering.md`:1358 | Zenith2 rendering: Flux › Shading paths and capability matrix › The four shading paths | The active path is switchable at runtime on desktop through the `r.shadingPath` console value and the on-screen debug UI; the switch takes effect at the next frame's graph build and invalidates the temporal histories of every affected view (see "Render views"). |
| Z2-N-REND-693 | `Zenith2/Docs/Rendering.md`:1359 | Zenith2 rendering: Flux › Shading paths and capability matrix › The four shading paths | Every path shares the single-source `EvaluateMaterial` and `EvaluateLighting` with explicit gradients; the paths differ in where `Flux_SurfaceData` lives between those two calls and in how light lists are built, never in the material or BRDF mathematics. |

| Z2-N-REND-698 | `Zenith2/Docs/Rendering.md`:1424 | Zenith2 rendering: Flux › Shading paths and capability matrix › Single-source lighting | `EvaluateLighting` keeps its three-argument signature and returns `EvaluateLightingEx(…).m_xRadiance`, and `EvaluateLightingEx` is a pure function of its three arguments (no UAV, no groupshared, no discard, no derivative instruction), so pixel and compute stages call the same code. |
| Z2-N-REND-720 | `Zenith2/Docs/Rendering.md`:1486 | Zenith2 rendering: Flux › Frame anatomy common prologue | The common prologue runs once per frame, not per view, in the order given by `Zenith2/Docs/Rendering/FrameAnatomy.md` "Common prologue": GPU-scene growth and scatter upload, the draw-key bucket scatter, geometry pool growth, streaming uploads, compute skinning and morph targets, particle simulation, procedural geometry, ENV's Stage 5 prologue passes, the light and decal list build, the shared sky LUTs and ENV's cloud layer, then the shadow views. |

| Z2-N-REND-722 | `Zenith2/Docs/Rendering.md`:1488 | Zenith2 rendering: Flux › Frame anatomy common prologue | Every depth-writing pass draws the opaque bucket first and the alpha-tested bucket second, the alpha-tested pipelines are the only pipelines allowed to `discard`, and every subsequent colour pass uses an equal depth test with no discard, so early-Z stays enabled for all shading. |

Step 2's copies complete on the copy queue and become visible to subsequent frames through the one-time acquire list of `Zenith2/Docs/Rendering/GpuResources.md` "Persistent resources"; no pass of the same frame reads a region that step 2 writes. |
