| Z2-N-REND-729 | `Zenith2/Docs/Rendering/FrameAnatomy.md`:56 | Zenith2 rendering: frame anatomy for each shading path › Early-Z ordering | Every colour pass after the depth-writing passes of a view uses an `EQUAL` depth test with depth writes off and no discard, so early-Z stays enabled for all shading; the visibility-buffer path's resolve reads the visibility ID and has no depth test at all. |
| Z2-N-REND-735 | `Zenith2/Docs/Rendering.md`:1494 | Zenith2 rendering: Flux › Frame anatomy Forward+ | The Forward+ frame for one main view is, in order: prepass phase 1, HiZ build, instance culling phase 2, prepass phase 2, final HiZ, light clustering, volumetric fog, GTAO, contact shadows, SSGI (Stage 5, desktop tiers), forward opaque, sky and atmosphere, SSR, transparent forward, particles and ribbons, ENV's underwater composite for a submerged view, then the common epilogue. |
| Z2-N-REND-736 | `Zenith2/Docs/Rendering.md`:1495 | Zenith2 rendering: Flux › Frame anatomy Forward+ | Forward+ is the default path and the one path on Android; volumetric fog integrates before any shading so the opaque, transparent and particle passes all sample the integrated volume in-shader. |

| Z2-N-REND-738 | `Zenith2/Docs/Rendering/FrameAnatomy.md`:83 | Zenith2 rendering: frame anatomy for each shading path › Forward+ frame | The Forward+ prepass writes depth, velocity, and thin normals plus roughness (RG16_UNORM octahedral normal and R8_UNORM roughness) for the screen-space passes, drawing the opaque bucket and then the alpha-tested bucket in each phase. |
| Z2-N-REND-739 | `Zenith2/Docs/Rendering/FrameAnatomy.md`:84 | Zenith2 rendering: frame anatomy for each shading path › Forward+ frame | Volumetric fog integrates into its froxel volume before any shading, and the opaque, transparent and particle passes all sample the integrated volume in-shader; no separate fog composite pass exists. |
| Z2-N-REND-740 | `Zenith2/Docs/Rendering/FrameAnatomy.md`:85 | Zenith2 rendering: frame anatomy for each shading path › Forward+ frame | Forward opaque uses the equal-depth test (no overdraw), runs `EvaluateMaterial` with clustered decals applied to `Flux_SurfaceData`, runs `EvaluateLighting` over the cluster lights, shadows, IBL and AO, applies froxel fog in-shader, and writes HDR colour. |
| Z2-N-REND-741 | `Zenith2/Docs/Rendering/FrameAnatomy.md`:86 | Zenith2 rendering: frame anatomy for each shading path › Forward+ frame | Sky and atmosphere render from the sky-view LUT with aerial perspective sampled from the froxel volume; SSR traces hierarchically against the final pyramid's min channel, samples the previous frame's colour, denoises, and composites with IBL specular. |
| Z2-N-REND-742 | `Zenith2/Docs/Rendering/FrameAnatomy.md`:87 | Zenith2 rendering: frame anatomy for each shading path › Forward+ frame | Transparent forward draws the CPU-sorted transparent instances back to front with clustered lighting and per-pixel froxel fog, with refraction reading a copy of the opaque colour made by `OpaqueColourCopy`; particles and ribbons follow, as soft particles against depth, with froxel fog. |
| Z2-N-REND-743 | `Zenith2/Docs/Rendering/FrameAnatomy.md`:88 | Zenith2 rendering: frame anatomy for each shading path › Forward+ frame | No pass of any path uses MSAA: antialiasing is TAA or the temporal upscaler, consistent with the non-MSAA prepass, HiZ and history. |
| Z2-N-REND-750 | `Zenith2/Docs/Rendering.md`:1501 | Zenith2 rendering: Flux › Frame anatomy Forward | The Forward frame is, in order: the Forward+ prepass, HiZ and culling passes; per-object light lists; volumetric fog, GTAO and contact shadows; SSGI (Stage 5); forward opaque with per-object lights, per-object decals and in-shader fog; sky, SSR, transparent, particles and the underwater composite as in Forward+; then the common epilogue. |
| Z2-N-REND-751 | `Zenith2/Docs/Rendering.md`:1502 | Zenith2 rendering: Flux › Frame anatomy Forward | Forward is the non-clustered reference path: it is used for the parity reference, previews and captures, and it is not selectable on mobile. |
| Z2-N-REND-752 | `Zenith2/Docs/Rendering/FrameAnatomy.md`:104 | Zenith2 rendering: frame anatomy for each shading path › Forward frame | The Forward frame declares its passes in the order of the table above, reusing the Forward+ prepass, HiZ and culling passes unchanged. |
| Z2-N-REND-753 | `Zenith2/Docs/Rendering/FrameAnatomy.md`:105 | Zenith2 rendering: frame anatomy for each shading path › Forward frame | Per-object light lists hold at most the per-object light cap `Z2-B-RPROD-013` Per-object light cap (pending; proposed 8) per object, chosen on the CPU and the GPU from the light list: the CPU part is the frame light list itself (the lights the CPU keeps after its view-frustum test and shadowed-light selection, which `LightDecalListBuild` compacts), and the GPU part selects each visible object's lights from it by descending contribution at the object's bounds, with the directional light always included; transparent forward in the Forward path uses the same per-object lists. |
| Z2-N-REND-754 | `Zenith2/Docs/Rendering/FrameAnatomy.md`:106 | Zenith2 rendering: frame anatomy for each shading path › Forward frame | Volumetric fog integration runs before the opaque pass, then GTAO and contact shadows, as in Forward+; the opaque pass applies per-object lights, per-object decals and in-shader fog. |
| Z2-N-REND-760 | `Zenith2/Docs/Rendering.md`:1508 | Zenith2 rendering: Flux › Frame anatomy Deferred | The Deferred frame is, in order: depth prepass phase 1, HiZ build, culling phase 2, prepass phase 2, final HiZ; the G-buffer pass with an equal depth test and no discard; deferred decals; GTAO and contact shadows; SSGI (Stage 5); light clustering and volumetric fog; compute deferred lighting into HDR colour with froxel fog; sky, SSR, transparent forward, particles and the underwater composite; then the common epilogue. |
| Z2-N-REND-761 | `Zenith2/Docs/Rendering.md`:1509 | Zenith2 rendering: Flux › Frame anatomy Deferred | Deferred is desktop-only, every G-buffer target (including the advanced-surface target GB4 of the Stage 5 advanced-surfaces permutation) is at most 16 bits per channel with the formats fixed in `Zenith2/Docs/Rendering/PerPass/Deferred.md`, and there is no MSAA. |
| Z2-N-REND-762 | `Zenith2/Docs/Rendering/FrameAnatomy.md`:124 | Zenith2 rendering: frame anatomy for each shading path › Deferred frame | The Deferred frame declares its passes in the order of the table above; the G-buffer pass uses an equal depth test and no discard, and `EvaluateMaterial` writes `Flux_SurfaceData` into the G-buffer. |
| Z2-N-REND-763 | `Zenith2/Docs/Rendering/FrameAnatomy.md`:125 | Zenith2 rendering: frame anatomy for each shading path › Deferred frame | Deferred decals modify the G-buffer after the G-buffer pass and before any pass that reads it; deferred lighting is one compute pass over tiles and clusters that runs `EvaluateLighting` into HDR colour with froxel fog applied. |
| Z2-N-REND-764 | `Zenith2/Docs/Rendering/FrameAnatomy.md`:126 | Zenith2 rendering: frame anatomy for each shading path › Deferred frame | Every G-buffer target is at most 16 bits per channel, with the formats fixed in `Zenith2/Docs/Rendering/PerPass/Deferred.md` "GBuffer". |
| Z2-N-REND-770 | `Zenith2/Docs/Rendering.md`:1515 | Zenith2 rendering: Flux › Frame anatomy Visibility buffer | The visibility-buffer frame is, in order: meshlet culling phase 1; visibility raster phase 1; HiZ build, meshlet culling phase 2, visibility raster phase 2, final HiZ; material classification; light and decal clustering; material resolve (one compute dispatch per material bin, applying clustered decals and writing `SurfaceData`); velocity reconstruction; GTAO and volumetric fog; SSGI (Stage 5); lighting as in Deferred; sky, SSR, transparent forward, particles and the underwater composite; then the common epilogue. |
| Z2-N-REND-771 | `Zenith2/Docs/Rendering.md`:1516 | Zenith2 rendering: Flux › Frame anatomy Visibility buffer | The visibility ID is 32 bits, (visible-meshlet index : 25 bits, triangle-in-meshlet : 7 bits), stored in an R32_UINT target cleared to the reserved value `0xFFFFFFFF` meaning "no geometry"; per-instance data is reached through the visible-meshlet list. |
| Z2-N-REND-772 | `Zenith2/Docs/Rendering.md`:1517 | Zenith2 rendering: Flux › Frame anatomy Visibility buffer | The visibility buffer is desktop-only, and transparent geometry never enters it. |
| Z2-N-REND-773 | `Zenith2/Docs/Rendering/FrameAnatomy.md`:147 | Zenith2 rendering: frame anatomy for each shading path › Visibility buffer frame | The visibility-buffer frame declares its passes in the order of the table above. |
| Z2-N-REND-774 | `Zenith2/Docs/Rendering/FrameAnatomy.md`:148 | Zenith2 rendering: frame anatomy for each shading path › Visibility buffer frame | Meshlet culling phase 1 tests instances, then meshlets, for frustum, cone and HiZ against last frame's pyramid with the previous view-projection; the visibility raster draws opaque meshlets first and alpha-tested meshlets second, through mesh shaders or the compute/indirect fallback. |
| Z2-N-REND-775 | `Zenith2/Docs/Rendering/FrameAnatomy.md`:149 | Zenith2 rendering: frame anatomy for each shading path › Visibility buffer frame | The visibility ID is `(visibleMeshletIndex << 7) \| triangleInMeshlet` in an R32_UINT target cleared to `0xFFFFFFFF` ("no geometry"), which allows up to 2²⁵ (33,554,432) visible meshlets and 128 triangles per meshlet; per-instance data is reached through the visible-meshlet list, because (instance, triangle) cannot fit 32 bits at 100k instances. |
| Z2-N-REND-776 | `Zenith2/Docs/Rendering/FrameAnatomy.md`:150 | Zenith2 rendering: frame anatomy for each shading path › Visibility buffer frame | Material classification bins every 8×8 pixel tile's material variants and emits one indirect dispatch argument set per material variant; the material resolve runs one compute dispatch per material bin, reconstructs attributes from barycentrics with analytic gradients, runs `EvaluateMaterial`, applies clustered decals to `Flux_SurfaceData`, and writes `Flux_SurfaceData` like a G-buffer. |
| Z2-N-REND-777 | `Zenith2/Docs/Rendering/FrameAnatomy.md`:151 | Zenith2 rendering: frame anatomy for each shading path › Visibility buffer frame | Velocity is reconstructed from the visibility ID and the previous transforms, including the skinned pool's previous positions; lighting is the deferred lighting pass, with froxel fog. |
| Z2-N-REND-778 | `Zenith2/Docs/Rendering/FrameAnatomy.md`:152 | Zenith2 rendering: frame anatomy for each shading path › Visibility buffer frame | Transparent geometry never enters the visibility buffer; it draws in the shared transparent forward pass with fog. |
| Z2-N-REND-785 | `Zenith2/Docs/Rendering.md`:1523 | Zenith2 rendering: Flux › Frame anatomy common epilogue | The common epilogue runs per view, in order: TAA or the temporal upscaler, auto-exposure, depth of field (optional, per camera), motion blur (optional), bloom, tonemap and colour grading, UI composite, debug overlays and the on-screen profiler, then present (the main view's frame-pacing-controlled swapchain present) or a copy into the view's output texture. |
| Z2-N-REND-786 | `Zenith2/Docs/Rendering.md`:1524 | Zenith2 rendering: Flux › Frame anatomy common epilogue | The TAA/upscaler is the resolution boundary: every pass before it runs at render extent and every pass from it on runs at output extent, and the upscaler choice is a quality-tier setting. |
| Z2-N-REND-787 | `Zenith2/Docs/Rendering/FrameAnatomy.md`:173 | Zenith2 rendering: frame anatomy for each shading path › Common epilogue | The common epilogue declares its passes per view in the order of the table above: TAA or temporal upscaler with jittered history, velocity reprojection, variance clipping and the dynamic-resolution upscale to output; auto-exposure (luminance histogram, then adaptation); optional depth of field (circle of confusion, gather, composite); optional motion blur (tile max velocity, then gather); bloom (downsample/upsample chain); tonemap and colour grading; the video composite and then the view extensions at `FLUX_EXTENSION_POINT_BEFORE_UI`, so 2D canvases draw over cinematic video and under the UI (`Zenith2/Docs/Design/RPROD/Passes.md` → "Common epilogue rows 6b and 6c"); UI composite; debug overlays and the on-screen profiler; present. |
| Z2-N-REND-788 | `Zenith2/Docs/Rendering/FrameAnatomy.md`:174 | Zenith2 rendering: frame anatomy for each shading path › Common epilogue | Tonemap and grading applies an ACES-style operator, a 3D grading LUT, the output-transform selection (SDR, HDR10 or scRGB) and the colour-space conversion, with vignette and film grain optional. |
| Z2-N-REND-789 | `Zenith2/Docs/Rendering/FrameAnatomy.md`:175 | Zenith2 rendering: frame anatomy for each shading path › Common epilogue | The UI composite blends the game UI over the output; world-space UI is drawn earlier, in the transparent pass, and is not drawn again. |
| Z2-N-REND-790 | `Zenith2/Docs/Rendering/FrameAnatomy.md`:176 | Zenith2 rendering: frame anatomy for each shading path › Common epilogue | Present is the frame-pacing-controlled swapchain present for the main view, or a copy into the view's output texture for every other view, the editor's game view among them, which outputs a tool texture; a main view under a development capture presents a letterboxed copy of its capture output; frame pacing is specified in `Zenith2/Docs/Design/RPROD.md`. |
| Z2-N-REND-791 | `Zenith2/Docs/Rendering/FrameAnatomy.md`:180 | Zenith2 rendering: frame anatomy for each shading path › Async compute placement | Exactly the passes marked A in the tables above (compute skinning and morph targets, the particle simulation passes, procedural geometry, ENV's Stage 5 prologue passes, light clustering, volumetric fog, GTAO and contact shadows, as the frame anatomy of every path fixes them) are declared with `FLUX_PASS_FLAG_ASYNC_COMPUTE`, and each runs on the async-compute queue exactly when the gap rule of `Zenith2/Docs/Rendering/RenderGraph.md` "Graph compile" (step 3) places it there; results are identical whichever queue runs it, which the parity suite checks by running every parity scene with `m_bAsyncCompute` on and off. |
| Z2-N-REND-792 | `Zenith2/Docs/Rendering/FrameAnatomy.md`:181 | Zenith2 rendering: frame anatomy for each shading path › Async compute placement | Every pass marked G runs on the graphics queue and carries no `FLUX_PASS_FLAG_ASYNC_COMPUTE`, and a pass marked C runs on the copy queue when one exists. |
| Z2-N-REND-793 | `Zenith2/Docs/Rendering/FrameAnatomy.md`:200 | Zenith2 rendering: frame anatomy for each shading path › Environment frame slots | ENV's passes run exactly in the slots of the table above, each adds no pass when its feature is absent from the view or the world, and a frame of a world without the Environment module declares none of them. |
| Z2-N-REND-794 | `Zenith2/Docs/Rendering/FrameAnatomy.md`:201 | Zenith2 rendering: frame anatomy for each shading path › Environment frame slots | `Flux_AddSSGI` (Stage 5, LIGHT) runs after `Flux_AddContactShadows` (after `Flux_AddSSAO` on the visibility-buffer path, which has no contact-shadow pass) in every desktop path, on the queue G, and adds no pass on `FLUX_CAPABILITY_TIER_MOBILE` or when the view's tier leaves `render.quality.ssgiEnabled` unset. |
| Z2-N-REND-795 | `Zenith2/Docs/Rendering.md`:1542 | Zenith2 rendering: Flux › Per-pass specifications | Every pass of every path is specified in exactly one per-pass section file with its parameter struct fields, its targets and their formats, its queue, its capability requirements, its budget row (by `Z2-B-` ID and name) and its permutations, and a pass implementation matches its specification table field for field. |
| Z2-N-REND-796 | `Zenith2/Docs/Rendering.md`:1543 | Zenith2 rendering: Flux › Per-pass specifications | A pass's budget row is a GPU-time row of `Zenith2/Docs/Budgets.md → "Rendering rows"` cited by ID; every such row is pending until the Stage 1 per-system budget ticket for its area (RPROD, LIGHT, GEOM, ENV, VFX, UI or DEVT) allocates it, and a ticket that cites a pending row is blocked by that budget ticket. |
| Z2-N-REND-797 | `Zenith2/Docs/Rendering.md`:1544 | Zenith2 rendering: Flux › Per-pass specifications | The parity scenes of "Validation roles" exercise every pass that appears in more than one path. |
| Z2-N-REND-800 | `Zenith2/Docs/Rendering/PerPass/Common.md`:17 | Zenith2 rendering: per-pass specifications, shared passes › Conventions | Every pass below is implemented with exactly the program, parameter fields (names, C++ types, access and stages), targets and formats, queue, capability requirements and permutations its tables give; a change to any of them edits this file in the same diff (Z2-D-085). |
| Z2-N-REND-801 | `Zenith2/Docs/Rendering/PerPass/Common.md`:18 | Zenith2 rendering: per-pass specifications, shared passes › Conventions | Every render target and storage texture listed in this file and the other per-pass files uses at most 16 bits per channel, except the visibility ID (R32_UINT) and justified D32F depth, which is the enforcement surface of `FLUX_RG_ERROR_32BIT_TARGET_UNJUSTIFIED`. |

| Z2-N-REND-804 | `Zenith2/Docs/Rendering/PerPass/Common.md`:1187 | Zenith2 rendering: per-pass specifications, shared passes › Epilogue passes › Present | The TAA history and every other per-view history above is written into the current half of the view's ping-pong resource, marked with `MarkOutput`, and read from the previous half the next frame. |

| Z2-N-REND-806 | `Zenith2/Docs/Rendering/PerPass/Common.md`:32 | Zenith2 rendering: per-pass specifications, shared passes › Conventions › Stage marks frame data and the frame environment | A pass that reads build-node frame data, the frame environment or the cluster mapping declares exactly the fields of the conventions table above, and a field marked with a stage is absent from the pass before that stage. |

| Z2-N-REND-822 | `Zenith2/Docs/Rendering/PerPass/ForwardPlus.md`:81 | Zenith2 rendering: per-pass specifications, Forward+ › Forward+ passes › ForwardPlusOpaque | Forward+ opaque binds the depth target read-only with an `EQUAL` test, contains no `discard`, and shades each visible pixel exactly once (no overdraw), for opaque and alpha-tested buckets alike. |
| Z2-N-REND-835 | `Zenith2/Docs/Rendering/PerPass/Forward.md`:9 | Zenith2 rendering: per-pass specifications, Forward › Conventions | The Forward passes below are implemented exactly as their tables specify, run on `FLUX_CAPABILITY_TIER_DESKTOP` alone, and produce, on parity scenes with no more lights affecting any object than the per-object light cap `Z2-B-RPROD-013`, the same surface-data channels as Forward+ within the matrix tolerances. |
| Z2-N-REND-836 | `Zenith2/Docs/Rendering/PerPass/Forward.md`:34 | Zenith2 rendering: per-pass specifications, Forward › Forward passes › PerObjectLightLists | Per-object light selection follows the scoring and tie-break rule above, so the Forward path's light set per object is deterministic. |
| Z2-N-REND-837 | `Zenith2/Docs/Rendering/PerPass/Forward.md`:60 | Zenith2 rendering: per-pass specifications, Forward › Forward passes › ForwardOpaquePerObject | Forward opaque with per-object lights uses the equal-depth test with no discard, applies per-object decals and in-shader froxel fog, and differs from Forward+ opaque only in its light and decal source. |
| Z2-N-REND-840 | `Zenith2/Docs/Rendering/PerPass/Deferred.md`:9 | Zenith2 rendering: per-pass specifications, Deferred › Conventions | The Deferred passes below are implemented exactly as their tables specify and run on `FLUX_CAPABILITY_TIER_DESKTOP` alone, with every G-buffer target at most 16 bits per channel and no MSAA. |
| Z2-N-REND-841 | `Zenith2/Docs/Rendering/PerPass/Deferred.md`:61 | Zenith2 rendering: per-pass specifications, Deferred › Deferred passes › GBuffer | The G-buffer pass uses an equal depth test with depth writes off and no discard, writes all five base colour targets (GB0–GB3 and velocity) in every permutation and GB4 in exactly the advanced-surfaces permutation, which is the one that attaches it, and clears them to zero at their first use each frame (pixels without geometry are not written, so `DONT_CARE` would break the first-write rule); consumers read the G-buffer only where depth is not the clear value. |
| Z2-N-REND-842 | `Zenith2/Docs/Rendering/PerPass/Deferred.md`:62 | Zenith2 rendering: per-pass specifications, Deferred › Deferred passes › GBuffer | The G-buffer formats are exactly GB0 mutable-format RGBA8 (`R8G8B8A8_SRGB` attachment and sampled view, `R8G8B8A8_UNORM` storage view), GB1 RG16_UNORM, GB2 RGBA8_UNORM, GB3 R11G11B10F, velocity RG16F and, in the advanced-surfaces permutation of the desktop-only Deferred and visibility-buffer paths, GB4 RGBA8_UNORM, and the visibility-buffer material resolve writes the same formats; a frame with no resident material of an advanced surface model allocates no GB4. |
| Z2-N-REND-843 | `Zenith2/Docs/Rendering/PerPass/Deferred.md`:86 | Zenith2 rendering: per-pass specifications, Deferred › Deferred passes › DeferredDecals | Deferred decals modify the G-buffer after the G-buffer pass and before any reader of it, blend only the channels their permutation writes (write mask 0 elsewhere), and read depth through a read-only attachment. |
| Z2-N-REND-844 | `Zenith2/Docs/Rendering/PerPass/Deferred.md`:88 | Zenith2 rendering: per-pass specifications, Deferred › Deferred passes › DeferredDecals | GB0 is a mutable-format image (see "Required format features" in the entry file): the G-buffer pass, deferred decals and every reader use its `R8G8B8A8_SRGB` view, so hardware encodes and decodes sRGB and decal blending happens in linear space; the visibility-buffer material resolve, which cannot store through an sRGB view, writes the `R8G8B8A8_UNORM` storage view with the exact IEC 61966-2-1 encode `Flux_EncodeSRGB8` from `Zenith2/Shaders/Common/Sampling.slang`, and the graph selects the view format from the use (`Flux_RGTextureDesc::m_eStorageViewFormat` for storage views). |
| Z2-N-REND-850 | `Zenith2/Docs/Rendering/PerPass/VisibilityBuffer.md`:9 | Zenith2 rendering: per-pass specifications, visibility buffer › Conventions | The visibility-buffer passes below are implemented exactly as their tables specify and run on `FLUX_CAPABILITY_TIER_DESKTOP` alone, with mesh shaders when `m_bMeshShaders` is set and the compute/indirect fallback otherwise. |
| Z2-N-REND-851 | `Zenith2/Docs/Rendering/PerPass/VisibilityBuffer.md`:65 | Zenith2 rendering: per-pass specifications, visibility buffer › Visibility buffer passes › VisibilityRaster | The visibility ID target is R32_UINT, one of the justified 32-bit targets, cleared to the reserved value `0xFFFFFFFF` meaning "no geometry", and meshlets hold at most 128 triangles so the 7-bit triangle field suffices. |
| Z2-N-REND-852 | `Zenith2/Docs/Rendering/PerPass/VisibilityBuffer.md`:120 | Zenith2 rendering: per-pass specifications, visibility buffer › Visibility buffer passes › MaterialResolve | The material resolve reconstructs attributes from barycentrics with analytic gradients (never hardware derivatives, which a compute shader does not have across triangles), and writes `Flux_SurfaceData` in the G-buffer formats of `Zenith2/Docs/Rendering/PerPass/Deferred.md` "GBuffer". |
| Z2-N-REND-853 | `Zenith2/Docs/Rendering/PerPass/VisibilityBuffer.md`:121 | Zenith2 rendering: per-pass specifications, visibility buffer › Visibility buffer passes › MaterialResolve | The surface-data targets are cleared to zero at their first use each frame (the first-write rule), because resolve dispatches cover only tiles with geometry; consumers read them only where the visibility ID is not `0xFFFFFFFF`. |
| Z2-N-REND-854 | `Zenith2/Docs/Rendering/PerPass/VisibilityBuffer.md`:148 | Zenith2 rendering: per-pass specifications, visibility buffer › Visibility buffer passes › VelocityReconstruct | Velocity reconstruction uses the instance's previous transform and, for skinned instances, the skinned pool's previous half, so skinned and morphed geometry gets correct motion vectors, and a history-reset instance produces zero object motion. |
| Z2-N-REND-865 | `Zenith2/Docs/Rendering.md`:1550 | Zenith2 rendering: Flux › Vertex data format | Vertex layouts are declared in Slang and nowhere else; the offline compiler bakes each layout into a generated table that runtime PSO creation and the cook's packers both consume, so the vertex writer and the vertex reader cannot disagree (Z2-D-062). |
| Z2-N-REND-866 | `Zenith2/Docs/Rendering.md`:1551 | Zenith2 rendering: Flux › Vertex data format | The default mesh family stores position in a dedicated SNORM16x4 stream dequantised by a per-mesh scale and bias, the tangent frame as an SNORM16x4 QTangent, UVs as UNORM16x2 with a per-mesh scale and bias, and indices as 16 bits with mesh splitting; a 32-bit vertex or index format appears exclusively where a documented precision check requires it, and the cook reports every fallback. |
| Z2-N-REND-867 | `Zenith2/Docs/Rendering.md`:1552 | Zenith2 rendering: Flux › Vertex data format | One codec, in the leaf library `ZenithVertexFormat` (owned by GEOM, depending on ZenithCore alone), serves Flux, ZenithCook and ZenithEditor, and its GPU half `Zenith2/Shaders/Common/VertexFormats.slang` matches it bit for bit, proven by round-trip unit tests of every format across both. |
| Z2-N-REND-868 | `Zenith2/Docs/Rendering/VertexData.md`:60 | Zenith2 rendering: vertex data format › Vertex declaration in Slang | `[VtxFmt("…")]` gives the storage format wherever it differs from the declared type, and the fetch unit widens and converts, so a compressed field is still declared `float4`; a field without `[VtxFmt]` stores its declared 32-bit type. |
| Z2-N-REND-869 | `Zenith2/Docs/Rendering/VertexData.md`:61 | Zenith2 rendering: vertex data format › Vertex declaration in Slang | `[Stream(n)]` places a field in stream n (0–7) and `[PerInstance]` puts it on an instance-rate stream; every field of one stream shares its rate, and fields of a stream are packed in declaration order at 4-byte alignment. |
| Z2-N-REND-870 | `Zenith2/Docs/Rendering/VertexData.md`:62 | Zenith2 rendering: vertex data format › Vertex declaration in Slang | The `[VtxFmt]` strings and the semantics are closed vocabularies, exactly the two tables above; any other string, or a lane-count mismatch between the storage format and the declared type, is a shader compile error (`ZSC_ERROR_VERTEX_FORMAT`), reported by the `ShaderChecks.VertexFormatVocabulary` lint. |
| Z2-N-REND-871 | `Zenith2/Docs/Rendering/VertexData.md`:134 | Zenith2 rendering: vertex data format › Generated layout tables | Runtime PSO creation builds the Vulkan `VkPipelineVertexInputStateCreateInfo` and the D3D12 input layout exclusively from the program's generated layout table, because the runtime has no Slang. |
| Z2-N-REND-872 | `Zenith2/Docs/Rendering/VertexData.md`:135 | Zenith2 rendering: vertex data format › Generated layout tables | The cook's vertex packers are driven by the same generated tables (`axFLUX_STREAMSET_LAYOUTS` of `Flux_StreamSets.generated.h`, so ZenithCook links `ZenithShadersGenerated` and the build orders the `--stream-sets` command before ZenithCook's compile), so the writer and the reader cannot disagree; a cooked mesh records the layout hash it was packed for, and the runtime refuses to bind a mesh whose recorded layout hash differs from the program's (`FLUX_VERTEX_ERROR_LAYOUT_MISMATCH`). |
| Z2-N-REND-873 | `Zenith2/Docs/Rendering/VertexData.md`:136 | Zenith2 rendering: vertex data format › Generated layout tables | `ZenithVertexFormat` exports the CPU codec (quantise and pack, unpack, QTangent encode and decode, octahedral, half) and the generated layout-table types through its public root header `Zenith_VertexFormat.h`; Flux (runtime), ZenithCook and ZenithEditor all link it, none of them reaches into another library's `Internal/`, and libgraph enforces that. |
| Z2-N-REND-874 | `Zenith2/Docs/Rendering/VertexData.md`:137 | Zenith2 rendering: vertex data format › Generated layout tables | The GPU half `Zenith2/Shaders/Common/VertexFormats.slang` holds the manual decoders and encoders for passes with no fetch unit (compute skinning, the visibility-buffer resolve), and matches the CPU codec bit for bit for every format. |
| Z2-N-REND-875 | `Zenith2/Docs/Rendering/VertexData.md`:159 | Zenith2 rendering: vertex data format › Default stream layout | Every mesh of the default family uses the streams and formats of the table above, with position alone in stream 0, and a 32-bit vertex format appears exclusively where a documented precision check of "Precision checks and fallbacks" requires it; the cook reports every fallback. |
| Z2-N-REND-876 | `Zenith2/Docs/Rendering/VertexData.md`:189 | Zenith2 rendering: vertex data format › QTangent codec | QTangents are encoded and decoded by the pseudocode above as realised in kernel operations by `Zenith2/Docs/Design/GEOM/VertexFormat.md → "The exact arithmetic kernel"` (reciprocal constants, fixed Newton sequences, results cleaned below 2⁻⁴⁰, `precise`) in both codec halves: the normal is `cross(t, b)` and is never sign-flipped, the handedness sign applies to the bitangent alone through the sign of `w`, and `abs(w)` is biased to at least 1/32767 with xyz renormalised before quantisation. |
| Z2-N-REND-877 | `Zenith2/Docs/Rendering/VertexData.md`:207 | Zenith2 rendering: vertex data format › Octahedral codec | Octahedral normals use the encoding above, as realised in kernel operations by `Zenith2/Docs/Design/GEOM/VertexFormat.md → "The exact arithmetic kernel"`, in vertex streams (SNORM16x2), the Forward+ and Forward prepass normal target and the G-buffer normal (RG16_UNORM, remapped by `× 0.5 + 0.5`), in both codec halves. |
| Z2-N-REND-878 | `Zenith2/Docs/Rendering/VertexData.md`:248 | Zenith2 rendering: vertex data format › Half-float codec | The half-float codec is the integer-only algorithm above in both halves (`Zenith_F32ToF16`/`Zenith_F16ToF32` on the CPU, `Flux_F32ToF16Bits`/`Flux_F16BitsToF32` in `Half.slang`), and the vertex codec's integer-only half conversion is kept regardless of `shaderFloat16`, so compute codecs never need `shaderInt16`. |
| Z2-N-REND-879 | `Zenith2/Docs/Rendering/VertexData.md`:266 | Zenith2 rendering: vertex data format › Precision checks and fallbacks | The cook runs every precision check of the table above per mesh, applies exactly the listed fallback when a check fails, and writes every fallback (mesh, attribute, metric value, tolerance, chosen format) to the game's cook report under `vertexFallbacks`. |
| Z2-N-REND-880 | `Zenith2/Docs/Rendering/VertexData.md`:267 | Zenith2 rendering: vertex data format › Precision checks and fallbacks | Every precision fallback (FLOAT32x3 position, FLOAT16 UV, UINT16x4 joints, UNORM16x4 weights, FLOAT32 skinned positions) is a distinct Slang layout, and so a distinct stream-set pool and PSO permutation; the cook reports the permutation count per game, and `Z2-B-RPROD-024` Shader permutation limit per game (pending) caps it. |
| Z2-N-REND-881 | `Zenith2/Docs/Rendering/VertexData.md`:280 | Zenith2 rendering: vertex data format › Index buffers and mesh splitting | Index buffers are 16-bit: the cook splits any mesh with more than 65,535 vertices into sub-meshes by step 4 above, primitive restart is disabled, and there are no 32-bit index buffers anywhere. |
| Z2-N-REND-882 | `Zenith2/Docs/Rendering/VertexData.md`:281 | Zenith2 rendering: vertex data format › Index buffers and mesh splitting | Every sub-mesh shares its parent's position quantisation box and UV scale and bias, so shared boundary vertices decode identically and never crack. |
| Z2-N-REND-883 | `Zenith2/Docs/Rendering/VertexData.md`:282 | Zenith2 rendering: vertex data format › Index buffers and mesh splitting | Meshlets use 8-bit local indices, with at most 64 vertices and 128 triangles per meshlet. |
| Z2-N-REND-884 | `Zenith2/Docs/Rendering/VertexData.md`:283 | Zenith2 rendering: vertex data format › Index buffers and mesh splitting | Position-only draws (shadow views, and the opaque bucket of pure depth-only passes) use the position-only index buffer, which re-indexes each triangle to a canonical vertex per unique position for better post-transform cache hits; it is not used by the Forward+ prepass, which reads stream 1 for normals and velocity, nor by alpha-tested buckets, which need UVs. |
| Z2-N-REND-885 | `Zenith2/Docs/Rendering/VertexData.md`:284 | Zenith2 rendering: vertex data format › Index buffers and mesh splitting | Triangles are wound counter-clockwise when viewed from their front face; the importer keeps glTF's counter-clockwise winding and reverses the index order of any node whose world transform has a negative determinant. |
| Z2-N-REND-886 | `Zenith2/Docs/Rendering/VertexData.md`:288 | Zenith2 rendering: vertex data format › Unified stream pools | Every mesh is suballocated into a per-stream unified pool and drawn through `vertexOffset` / `BaseVertexLocation` (int32, so 16-bit indices suffice), which is what GPU-driven indirect draws need; the 16-bit indices of every mesh live in one unified index pool addressed by `m_uFirstIndex`; the pools of every fixed-function-bound stream are keyed by the raster stream-set code, and a skinned mesh's bind-position and skin streams live in a compute-only skin-source pool keyed by its cooked code (SKIN included), addressed by the skinning dispatch record and never bound to a raster draw. |
| Z2-N-REND-887 | `Zenith2/Docs/Rendering/VertexData.md`:289 | Zenith2 rendering: vertex data format › Unified stream pools | Indirect buckets are keyed by (pipeline, raster stream-set code) — the code of the PSO's stream-set, `POOL16`/`POOL32` for a skinned mesh — so every draw in one bucket binds the same fixed-function stream pools, and two skinned meshes whose cooked codes differ only in their joint or weight format share a bucket and its raster pools. |
| Z2-N-REND-888 | `Zenith2/Docs/Rendering/VertexData.md`:290 | Zenith2 rendering: vertex data format › Unified stream pools | The unified pools are `Flux_PersistentReadOnly`: a new mesh is uploaded on the copy queue into a region no table entry references, and a freed region is reused only after retirement (see `Zenith2/Docs/Rendering/GpuResources.md` "Persistent resources"). |
| Z2-N-REND-889 | `Zenith2/Docs/Rendering/VertexData.md`:294 | Zenith2 rendering: vertex data format › Buffer usage unions | Mesh and pool buffers are created with the union of their declared uses (for example vertex + index + storage for buffers that the visibility-buffer resolve or skinning also reads or writes), and the union is part of the physical-resource cache key. |
| Z2-N-REND-890 | `Zenith2/Docs/Rendering/VertexData.md`:295 | Zenith2 rendering: vertex data format › Buffer usage unions | Raster passes fetch through the fixed-function unit; compute passes read the same buffers through bindless storage views, and read 16-bit indices as u32 words with a halfword select (`(word >> ((i & 1) × 16)) & 0xFFFF`). |
| Z2-N-REND-891 | `Zenith2/Docs/Rendering/VertexData.md`:299 | Zenith2 rendering: vertex data format › Skinned vertex pool | The skinned-vertex pool is written by compute, and because posed vertices leave the bind-pose box, pool positions are FLOAT16x4 in object space, not the SNORM16 bind-pose quantisation. |
| Z2-N-REND-892 | `Zenith2/Docs/Rendering/VertexData.md`:300 | Zenith2 rendering: vertex data format › Skinned vertex pool | The cook's precision check for the pool uses a conservative posed extent, because the posed extent is unknown at cook time: `E = \|bind position of the root joint\| + R + D`, where R is the skeleton's maximum reach (the largest root-relative sum of bone-chain lengths over every joint) and D is the largest bind-pose distance from a vertex to its most-weighted joint; the half-float error at magnitude E is `2^(floor(log2(E)) − 11)`, and a mesh whose error exceeds 1 mm gets a FLOAT32x4 pool layout, reported like any other fallback. |
| Z2-N-REND-893 | `Zenith2/Docs/Rendering/VertexData.md`:301 | Zenith2 rendering: vertex data format › Skinned vertex pool | Skinning re-encodes the tangent frame as a QTangent with the bias rule, so the GPU codec has encoders as well as decoders (the QTangent encode with its bias, and half-float packing), both bit-matched to the CPU codec by tests. |
| Z2-N-REND-894 | `Zenith2/Docs/Rendering/VertexData.md`:302 | Zenith2 rendering: vertex data format › Skinned vertex pool | The pool is double-buffered for velocity (see `Zenith2/Docs/Rendering/GpuResources.md` "Pools"). |
| Z2-N-REND-895 | `Zenith2/Docs/Rendering/VertexData.md`:318 | Zenith2 rendering: vertex data format › Codec tests | The codec tests of the table above exist (CPU tests in `Zenith2/Engine/ZenithVertexFormat/Tests/`, cook tests in `Zenith2/Tools/ZenithCook/Tests/`, Flux tests in `Zenith2/Engine/ZenithFlux/Tests/`) and pass; every format of the vocabulary is round-tripped across both codec halves. |
| Z2-N-REND-896 | `Zenith2/Docs/Rendering/VertexData.md`:337 | Zenith2 rendering: vertex data format › D-VTX-TANGENTLESS harness | The D-VTX-TANGENTLESS (open, Stage 3) harness implements every element of the table above, measures the three risks on the stated asset set on the S24 and the RTX 3070, and changes no default: QTangent stays the default until the ruling is recorded as a decision. |
| Z2-N-REND-915 | `Zenith2/Docs/Rendering.md`:1569 | Zenith2 rendering: Flux › GPU performance rules and lints | The graph compiler culls every raster pass that declares no draws and carries no side effect, folds every clear-only pass into the next user's load op, and merges consecutive raster passes into one rendering scope exactly under the merge conditions of `Zenith2/Docs/Rendering/RenderGraph.md` "Pass merging" (Z2-D-014). |
| Z2-N-REND-916 | `Zenith2/Docs/Rendering.md`:1570 | Zenith2 rendering: Flux › GPU performance rules and lints | Opaque pipelines contain no `discard` or `clip` and write no `SV_Depth`, `SV_DepthGreaterEqual` or `SV_DepthLessEqual`; `discard`/`clip` appear exclusively in code reachable from an entry point attributed `[AlphaTest]`, and a depth export is allowed only for a program listed with a `Z2-D-*` decision ID in `Zenith2/Shaders/ShaderChecks_allowlist.json`. |
| Z2-N-REND-917 | `Zenith2/Docs/Rendering.md`:1571 | Zenith2 rendering: Flux › GPU performance rules and lints | Every colour pass that follows a depth-writing pass in the same view uses an `EQUAL` depth test with depth writes off, which `PSO.ColourPassEqualDepth` verifies at PSO creation in development builds and at cook time for every precached PSO. |
| Z2-N-REND-918 | `Zenith2/Docs/Rendering.md`:1572 | Zenith2 rendering: Flux › GPU performance rules and lints | A render-target texture with 32 bits per channel is a graph compile error unless its format and use are on the justified list (R32_UINT for the visibility ID, D32F for a justified view), and extending the list takes a decision ID recorded beside the list in `Zenith2/Engine/ZenithFlux/Internal/Graph/Justified32BitFormats.json`. |
| Z2-N-REND-919 | `Zenith2/Docs/Rendering.md`:1573 | Zenith2 rendering: Flux › GPU performance rules and lints | A pass binds exactly the attachments in its `Flux_RasterTargets`, binds depth read-only whenever none of its declared programs writes depth, and every declared attachment is written by every declared program's reflected outputs or else carries a colour write mask of 0 in that program's PSOs. |
| Z2-N-REND-920 | `Zenith2/Docs/Rendering.md`:1575 | Zenith2 rendering: Flux › GPU performance rules and lints | Every raster pass declares its shader set (`Flux_RasterTargets::m_xPrograms`, the programs its lambda may bind); the graph compile checks the set's reflected outputs against the targets, and in development builds the record-time check `FLUX_RECORD_CHECK_UNWRITTEN_ATTACHMENT` fails the frame when an indirect multi-PSO pass binds a program outside its declared set or whose outputs leave a bound attachment unwritten. |
| Z2-N-REND-921 | `Zenith2/Docs/Rendering.md`:1576 | Zenith2 rendering: Flux › GPU performance rules and lints | Within one permutation, an MRT output is written on every control path or on none; `ShaderChecks.ConditionalOutputWrite` fails a program whose reflection reports a conditionally written output, and the PSO builder sets a write mask of 0 on every output a permutation never writes. |
| Z2-N-REND-922 | `Zenith2/Docs/Rendering.md`:1577 | Zenith2 rendering: Flux › GPU performance rules and lints | Colour, normal, BRDF-term and post-process arithmetic uses `half` with native 16-bit types; positions, depth, UV derivatives and accumulations stay `float`; `ShaderChecks.NoMin16Float` fails any use of `min16float`/`min16half`, and `ShaderChecks.HalfRatioReport` reports the float/half instruction ratio per program as a review signal. |
| Z2-N-REND-923 | `Zenith2/Docs/Rendering.md`:1578 | Zenith2 rendering: Flux › GPU performance rules and lints | The default raster state culls back faces, and `PSO.OpaqueCullBack` fails an opaque or alpha-tested PSO with culling off unless its material document sets `twoSided`; the number of two-sided PSOs per game is reported in the cook report. |
| Z2-N-REND-924 | `Zenith2/Docs/Rendering.md`:1579 | Zenith2 rendering: Flux › GPU performance rules and lints | Every graphics ticket's `## Validation` section names the checks of the table above that the change must pass, and the WorkerGuide carries these rules in short form. |
| Z2-N-REND-925 | `Zenith2/Docs/Rendering.md`:1574 | Zenith2 rendering: Flux › GPU performance rules and lints | In development builds and in every graph and render test, a raster pass that declared depth with `FLUX_DEPTH_ACCESS_WRITE`, issued at least one draw and bound no PSO with depth write enabled appends `FLUX_RG_ERROR_DEPTH_WRITABLE_UNUSED` to the frame's record-check report at the end of the pass's recording, which fails the frame; this is the one graph error code evaluated at record time rather than at compile. |
| Z2-N-REND-940 | `Zenith2/Docs/Rendering.md`:1592 | Zenith2 rendering: Flux › Validation roles › Cross-path consistency | The raster paths are compared with each other and between Vulkan and D3D12, surface-data channels first and final images second, as the table above specifies; the PathParity gate step runs the path comparisons (from Stage 5) and the BackendParity gate step runs the Vulkan-against-D3D12 comparisons (from Stage 3). |
| Z2-N-REND-941 | `Zenith2/Docs/Rendering.md`:1593 | Zenith2 rendering: Flux › Validation roles › Cross-path consistency | Only features that behave identically across paths are compared; a path-specific approximation is either disabled in the parity configuration or compared with its own per-feature tolerance from the capability matrix. |
| Z2-N-REND-942 | `Zenith2/Docs/Rendering.md`:1594 | Zenith2 rendering: Flux › Validation roles › Cross-path consistency | Parity scenes live under `Zenith2/Engine/ZenithFlux/Tests/Parity/Scenes/`, render with a fixed camera path, a fixed jitter sequence, fixed noise seeds per frame index and a fixed exposure, and capture at fixed frame indices, so a parity run is reproducible for the same build, backend and device. |
| Z2-N-REND-943 | `Zenith2/Docs/Rendering.md`:1595 | Zenith2 rendering: Flux › Validation roles › Cross-path consistency | A parity failure retains its artefacts (both images, the FLIP heat map, the per-channel difference images and the graph dump of both runs) under `Zenith2/out/<checkout-hash>/Parity/<scene>/`. |
| Z2-N-REND-944 | `Zenith2/Docs/Rendering.md`:1599 | Zenith2 rendering: Flux › Validation roles › Physical accuracy | The compute path tracer (a Slang compute program under `Zenith2/Shaders/Tests/PathTracer/` over a software BVH built on the CPU with a binned SAH builder, both in the tool-side library `ZenithPathTracer`, placed as `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture.md → "Repository layout"` lists it) is compared with the raster paths on controlled reference scenes (analytic lights, the IBL furnace, area-light shadows, VT-backed terrain materials) with every screen-space approximation disabled (Z2-D-037). |
| Z2-N-REND-945 | `Zenith2/Docs/Rendering.md`:1600 | Zenith2 rendering: Flux › Validation roles › Physical accuracy | The tracer runs to a convergence criterion: at least `Z2-B-ADVGFX-006` Path tracer minimum samples per pixel (pending; proposed 256), then until the per-pixel variance estimate is below `Z2-B-ADVGFX-007` Path tracer convergence variance threshold (pending; proposed: the standard error of the luminance mean below 1% of the mean for 99.5% of pixels), or until the scene's wall-clock cap; the error tolerance between the converged reference and each raster path is `Z2-B-ADVGFX-008` Physical-accuracy error tolerance per reference scene. |
| Z2-N-REND-946 | `Zenith2/Docs/Rendering.md`:1601 | Zenith2 rendering: Flux › Validation roles › Physical accuracy | WARP and lavapipe runs of the tracer are capped per scene by `Z2-B-ADVGFX-004` Path tracer resolution cap per reference scene and `Z2-B-ADVGFX-005` Path tracer wall-clock cap per reference scene, and a run that hits its wall-clock cap before convergence reports "not converged" rather than passing. |
| Z2-N-REND-947 | `Zenith2/Docs/Rendering.md`:1602 | Zenith2 rendering: Flux › Validation roles › Physical accuracy | Screen-space and approximate techniques (SSAO, SSR, contact shadows, approximate GI) are validated against their own documented error budgets on dedicated scenes, not against path-tracer convergence. |

| Z2-N-REND-992 | `Zenith2/Docs/Rendering/ShaderSystem.md`:341 | Zenith2 rendering: shader system and pipeline state objects › Shader blob container | A blob reference's `blobHash` is `Zenith_Hash64` (`Zenith2/Docs/Design/CORE/Maths.md` → "Zenith_Hash64", the algorithm selected by D-SEL-HASH (open, Stage 1)) of the blob's `blobSize` bytes exactly as stored at `blobOffset`, alignment padding excluded, with seed 0; the compiler writes it into the container and the same value as the blob hash of `--manifest`, and the loader's check of [Z2-N-REND-122] recomputes it the same way. It is a corruption check and never an identity: no cache key, file name or PSO key derives from it. The compiler and the loader depend on D-SEL-HASH through it as they already do through the program ID ([Z2-N-REND-107]); no SHA-256 digest or truncation of one is used for it. A change of the algorithm behind `Zenith_Hash64` changes every blob hash and is therefore a change to this format: it bumps `uFLUX_SHADER_CONTAINER_VERSION`, so an older container is refused by [Z2-N-REND-121] and recompiled, never checked against the new algorithm. |
| Z2-N-REND-993 | `Zenith2/Docs/Rendering/ShaderSystem.md`:371 | Zenith2 rendering: shader system and pipeline state objects › Permutations › Enumeration rule | A `[PermutationBuild("development")]` constant keeps its non-default values out of every cooked package, in every configuration including `Shipping`: the compiler writes the permutations in which it is not at its default value into the development container `DevelopmentBlobs/<ProgramPath>.zshader` alone, the cook ([Z2-N-REND-123]) never packages a development container and compiles no development permutation in cook mode, and the PSO precache predictor never reads one, so a request for such a permutation from a cooked package is a missing permutation (`FLUX_SHADER_ERROR_MISSING_PERMUTATION`, "Missing-PSO fallback"). A development test or benchmark loads the development container through `ZENITH_SHADERS_GENERATED_DIR` and adds it to `Flux_ShaderLibrary` after the program's own container; the library then resolves a key of that program from either, and refuses a development container whose program ID, layout hash or tool hash differs from the program container's with `FLUX_SHADER_ERROR_CONTAINER_VERSION`. The attribute on a material-level constant, or with an argument other than `development`, is `ZSC_ERROR_ATTRIBUTE_TARGET`. |
| Z2-N-REND-994 | `Zenith2/Docs/Rendering/ShaderSystem.md`:338 | Zenith2 rendering: shader system and pipeline state objects › Shader blob container | The container magic and version are declared once, in the generated header `Flux/Shaders/Flux_ShaderContainerFormat.generated.h` under `${ZENITH_OUT}/generated/ZenithShadersGenerated/` (so `#include "Flux/Shaders/Flux_ShaderContainerFormat.generated.h"` through `ZenithShadersGenerated`): `inline constexpr uint32 uFLUX_SHADER_CONTAINER_MAGIC = 0x4448535Au;` and `inline constexpr uint32 uFLUX_SHADER_CONTAINER_VERSION = <n>u;`. `zenith_add_shaders` (`CMake/ZenithShaders.cmake`) writes it at configure time, rewriting it only when its content changes, from the two CMake values `ZENITH_SHADER_CONTAINER_MAGIC` and `ZENITH_SHADER_CONTAINER_VERSION` set once in that file, and passes the same two values to every compiler invocation as `--container-magic` and `--container-version`, which the compiler writes into every container it produces and without which it exits 2; a version bump is an edit of that one CMake value. Its readers include it and name no literal of either value: the compiler's other callers (the cook's material compiles and watch mode) pass the header's constants, ZenithFlux's `Flux_ShaderLibrary` and `Flux_PipelineCacheStore` (the persistent cache key) compare with them, and RHI's header-only test reader in `ZenithRHI/Tests/Support/` reaches the header through its library's `TEST_DEPENDS ZenithShadersGenerated`, never through a ZenithFlux header. |
| Z2-N-REND-995 | `Zenith2/Docs/Rendering.md`:1263 | Zenith2 rendering: Flux › Render views › Shared producers | `Flux_FrameShared::m_pxEnvironment`, `Flux_FrameShared::m_pxUIFrame`, `Flux_FrameShared::m_px2DFrame` and `Flux_FrameShared::m_pxVideo` are the only routes from a path or feature function to ENV's, UI's, TWOD's and CINE's GPU sides (a process-wide pointer is forbidden, [Z2-N-CONTRACT-149], Z2-D-046): `Flux_Renderer` sets each before any feature function of the frame runs, each to null when its module did not enable it (`Zenith2/Docs/Design/RPROD/Renderer.md` → "Feature systems enabled after Create"), and every ENV entry point (`Env_AddCloudLayer`, `Env_AddUnderwater`, `Env_AddVolumetricClouds`, `Env_AddCloudShadowMap`, `Env_AddOceanFFT`) and UI pass function (`Flux_AddUIComposite`, `Flux_AddUIWorldSpace`) adds no pass when its pointer is null. |
| Z2-N-REND-996 | `Zenith2/Docs/Rendering.md`:826 | Zenith2 rendering: Flux › Materials › Surface function contract | `Flux_SampleMaterial` branches to `Flux_SampleVirtual` when a handle carries the virtual-texture tag and the pass's pools-bound flag (bit 1 of `m_uFeedbackFlags`) is set, and otherwise samples word 0 as an ordinary texture. Every pass whose programs evaluate materials of the `opaque` or `alphaTest` domains (every pass with an alpha-test permutation or material variants: `Prepass`, `DeferredDepthPrepass`, `ShadowDepth`, `VisibilityRaster`, `ForwardPlusOpaque`, `ForwardOpaquePerObject`, `GBuffer`, `MaterialResolve`) declares the three `[Optional]` VT pool fields; the four colour passes (`ForwardPlusOpaque`, `ForwardOpaquePerObject`, `GBuffer`, `MaterialResolve`) add the feedback fields `m_xVTFeedback`, `m_uVTFeedbackStride` and `m_uVTFeedbackJitter`, and the three pixel passes among them carry `[earlydepthstencil]`; the transparent and decal passes declare none. `Flux_ViewTargets::m_xVTFeedback` carries a view's feedback buffer, and every path function calls `Flux_AddVTFeedbackClear` before a feedback view's first writer and `Flux_AddVTFeedbackReadback` after its last (`Zenith2/Docs/Design/ADVGFX/VirtualTexturing.md` → "Pass tables"). |

| Z2-N-REND-998 | `Zenith2/Docs/Rendering.md`:610 | Zenith2 rendering: Flux › Binding model › The push constant | No `vk::` attribute or intrinsic appears in any Slang source or Slang snippet (Z2-D-141): push and root constants are entry-point `uniform` parameters, never a global `[vk::push_constant]` declaration, and resources are reached through the bindless accessors of `Common/Bindless.slang` and, where a program declares one, `ParameterBlock` or `ConstantBuffer` with Slang's reflection-driven layout, never an explicit `vk::binding`. The shader compiler reads the push constant's placement from the program's reflection (`EntryPointReflection::getVarLayout`: offset 0 of the push-constant range on Vulkan, `b0, space0` on D3D12) and fails a program whose placement differs from the global layout's; `ShaderChecks.NoVulkanAttributes` (`Zenith2/Docs/Rendering/ShaderSystem.md`) fails any `vk::` token. |

| Z2-N-SCRIPT-033 | `Zenith2/Docs/Scripting.md`:165 | Zenith2 Scripting › Luau integration and the exception firewall › Luau API linkage check | The CTest `Test_Script_Firewall_LuauApiLinkageRecorded` repeats the measurement on every run of the `Test` step and fails when the measured linkage differs from the recorded value, so a Luau pin bump that changes linkage cannot land unnoticed. |
