| Z2-N-SCRIPT-035 | `Zenith2/Docs/Scripting.md`:243 | Zenith2 Scripting › Luau integration and the exception firewall › The protected entry | `lua_pcall`, `lua_resume` and `luau_load` (and any further protected-call entry point the pinned `lua.h` declares, confirmed by the Stage 1 linkage ticket) appear in exactly one translation unit, `Internal/Zenith_ScriptProtectedCall.cpp`; `ForbiddenCheck` fails on any other occurrence. |
| Z2-N-SCRIPT-036 | `Zenith2/Docs/Scripting.md`:244 | Zenith2 Scripting › Luau integration and the exception firewall › The protected entry | Every Luau API call that can allocate or raise runs inside a protected region established by `Zenith_ScriptProtectedCall`; the exceptions are `lua_newstate`, `lua_close` and the non-raising stack-inspection calls listed in `Scripting/Bindings.md` → "Binding registration and the thunk ABI". |
| Z2-N-SCRIPT-037 | `Zenith2/Docs/Scripting.md`:245 | Zenith2 Scripting › Luau integration and the exception firewall › The protected entry | `Zenith_ScriptVM` keeps a protected-region depth counter `m_uProtectedDepth`; in Debug builds the VM allocator hook asserts that the counter is non-zero for every allocation after VM construction completes, which catches an unprotected allocating call on its first execution. |
| Z2-N-SCRIPT-038 | `Zenith2/Docs/Scripting.md`:246 | Zenith2 Scripting › Luau integration and the exception firewall › The protected entry | `Zenith_ScriptProtectedCall` never lets any exception escape (it carries no exception specification, `Zenith2/Docs/Contracts.md` [Z2-N-CONTRACT-582]; its own catch clauses are the guarantee); its `catch (const std::exception&)` and `catch (...)` clauses, and the compiler guard's in "The ZenithScriptCompiler library", are the only `catch` clauses in Zenith2, and they convert a non-Luau exception into a `SCRIPT_ERROR_KIND_INTERNAL` (runtime) or `SCRIPT_ERROR_KIND_COMPILE` (compiler) error. |
| Z2-N-SCRIPT-039 | `Zenith2/Docs/Scripting.md`:247 | Zenith2 Scripting › Luau integration and the exception firewall › The protected entry | `Zenith_ScriptProtectedCall` returns every Luau error as a `Zenith_ScriptError` value and never asserts on a script error: script misuse always raises a controlled Luau error and never relies on an assert (Z2-D-045). |
| Z2-N-SCRIPT-040 | `Zenith2/Docs/Scripting.md`:248 | Zenith2 Scripting › Luau integration and the exception firewall › The protected entry | Restated (Z2-D-141): `Zenith_ScriptCallResult` is a `Zenith_Result` with no `[[nodiscard]]` (`Zenith2/Docs/Contracts.md` [Z2-N-CONTRACT-586]); every caller handles the script failure it reports or discards it explicitly with a reason ([Z2-N-CONTRACT-402]), which review confirms. |
| Z2-N-SCRIPT-041 | `Zenith2/Docs/Scripting.md`:268 | Zenith2 Scripting › Luau integration and the exception firewall › Protected entry algorithm | A nested protected call consumes the enclosing entity transaction's safepoint budget: its limit is the enclosing call's remaining budget and its safepoints are added to the enclosing count, so re-entry through a callback never resets a budget. |
| Z2-N-SCRIPT-042 | `Zenith2/Docs/Scripting.md`:269 | Zenith2 Scripting › Luau integration and the exception firewall › Protected entry algorithm | Protected calls nest to a depth of at most `uSCRIPT_MAX_PROTECTED_DEPTH` (8); a re-entry beyond it is refused with the catalogued misuse error before any Luau code runs. |
| Z2-N-SCRIPT-043 | `Zenith2/Docs/Scripting.md`:275 | Zenith2 Scripting › Luau integration and the exception firewall › noexcept rule for engine functions called from bindings | Restated (Z2-D-141; the `noexcept` rule this heading names is withdrawn): an engine function reachable from a binding thunk carries no exception specification (`Zenith2/Docs/Contracts.md` [Z2-N-CONTRACT-582]) and cannot throw, because it and the thunk that `Zenith_ScriptBindingTable::AddFunction<auto pfnFunction>` instantiates are compiled in the owning engine library with exceptions off; `AddFunction` performs no nothrow check, and no Luau error unwinds through the thunk or the engine function, because the dispatcher raises only after the thunk has returned ([Z2-N-SCRIPT-044]) and a re-entry into script goes through the protected entry ([Z2-N-SCRIPT-046]). |
| Z2-N-SCRIPT-044 | `Zenith2/Docs/Scripting.md`:276 | Zenith2 Scripting › Luau integration and the exception firewall › noexcept rule for engine functions called from bindings | Every thunk has the signature `Zenith_ScriptThunkStatus (*)(Zenith_ScriptCallFrame& xFrame)` and reports failure by returning `SCRIPT_THUNK_STATUS_ERROR` with `xFrame.m_xError` filled; the dispatcher raises the Luau error after the thunk has returned, from inside the exception-enabled translation unit. |
| Z2-N-SCRIPT-045 | `Zenith2/Docs/Scripting.md`:277 | Zenith2 Scripting › Luau integration and the exception firewall › noexcept rule for engine functions called from bindings | Luau headers (`lua.h`, `lualib.h`, `luacode.h`, `luacodegen.h`, and anything under `Luau/`) are included only by `ZenithScript/Internal` and `ZenithScriptCompiler/Internal` translation units; `ForbiddenCheck` fails on an include anywhere else, and libgraph's leakage check fails if one reaches a public header. |
| Z2-N-SCRIPT-046 | `Zenith2/Docs/Scripting.md`:283 | Zenith2 Scripting › Luau integration and the exception firewall › Engine callbacks that re-enter script | An engine callback that runs script calls `Zenith_ScriptInvocationContext::InvokeCallback` on the context its thunk received; the interface is declared in ZenithReflect (`Scripting/Bindings.md` → "Binding registration and the thunk ABI") and implemented in `ZenithScript/Internal`, which enters Luau through `Zenith_ScriptProtectedCall`; no engine library holds a `lua_State`, calls a Luau function directly, or depends on `ZenithScript`. |
| Z2-N-SCRIPT-047 | `Zenith2/Docs/Scripting.md`:284 | Zenith2 Scripting › Luau integration and the exception firewall › Engine callbacks that re-enter script | A Luau error raised inside a re-entered callback is caught at the inner protected entry and returned to the calling engine frame as a `Zenith_ScriptThunkError` value (its 8-byte code-led record: `m_eCode` of `Zenith_ScriptThunkErrorCode`, the kind plus one; the ZS code; the VM's error-scratch sequence), whose text the engine frame reads through `Zenith_ScriptInvocationContext::GetErrorMessage`; no engine frame compiled with exceptions off is ever unwound. |
| Z2-N-SCRIPT-048 | `Zenith2/Docs/Scripting.md`:285 | Zenith2 Scripting › Luau integration and the exception firewall › Engine callbacks that re-enter script | An abort-class or fatal error returned from a re-entered callback is re-raised by the dispatcher of the outer binding after the engine function returns, with the same kind, so the outer invocation fails with it and budget exhaustion cannot be laundered through a callback. |
| Z2-N-SCRIPT-049 | `Zenith2/Docs/Scripting.md`:309 | Zenith2 Scripting › Luau integration and the exception firewall › ForbiddenCheck rules | `ForbiddenCheck` enforces every row of the table "ForbiddenCheck rules"; a `lua_call` or `lua_callk` outside `ZenithScript/Internal` is a gate failure, whatever the file (Z2-D-017). |
| Z2-N-SCRIPT-050 | `Zenith2/Docs/Scripting.md`:310 | Zenith2 Scripting › Luau integration and the exception firewall › ForbiddenCheck rules | Luau is always built with its default C++-exception error model; defining `LUA_USE_LONGJMP` would make `lua_error` skip C++ destructors and is a gate failure. |
| Z2-N-SCRIPT-051 | `Zenith2/Docs/Scripting.md`:336 | Zenith2 Scripting › Luau integration and the exception firewall › The two RAII tests | `Test_Script_Firewall_RaiiUnwindsThroughBinding` and `Test_Script_Firewall_ReentrantCallbackErrorCaught` are part of the ZenithScript test target and pass on `win-debug`, `win-release`, `win-asan` and `linux-tsan`, and in the S24 HWASan run of each stage acceptance. |
| Z2-N-SCRIPT-052 | `Zenith2/Docs/Scripting.md`:148 | Zenith2 Scripting › Luau integration and the exception firewall › Exception-enabled target list | `ZenithScriptCompiler` is the compile-time half of the firewall: a separate library declared `SUPPORTED ZENITH_HOST` in the build system's matrix, linked by the host tools ZenithCook and ZenithEditor and by test targets, and by no Player in any configuration, so no runtime target links the Luau compiler; its absence from every game's Shipping link closure is checked by the build system's Libgraph rule (`Zenith2/Docs/Architecture.md` → "Build system and presets"), not by dead-stripping, and `ZenithScript` has no dependency on it (the dependency runs the other way, for shared public types). |
| Z2-N-SCRIPT-053 | `Zenith2/Docs/Scripting.md`:324 | Zenith2 Scripting › Luau integration and the exception firewall › The ZenithScriptCompiler library | Every call from `ZenithScriptCompiler` into `Luau.Ast` or `Luau.Compiler` runs inside `Zenith_ScriptCompilerGuarded`, whose public callers (`Zenith_ScriptCompileModule`, `Zenith_ScriptCheckStateless`) let no exception escape and return `Zenith_Result`; no compiler exception reaches ZenithCook, ZenithEditor or a Player frame. |
| Z2-N-SCRIPT-054 | `Zenith2/Docs/Scripting.md`:325 | Zenith2 Scripting › Luau integration and the exception firewall › The ZenithScriptCompiler library | The Shipping link closure of every game (the `win-shipping` and `android-arm64-shipping` build presets, as the build system defines it) contains neither `ZenithScriptCompiler`, `Luau.Compiler` nor `Luau.Ast`, which the `Libgraph` step checks (`Zenith2/Docs/Architecture.md` → "Build system and presets"). |
| Z2-N-SCRIPT-055 | `Zenith2/Docs/Scripting.md`:239 | Zenith2 Scripting › Luau integration and the exception firewall › The protected entry | `Zenith_ScriptError` is the `Zenith_Result` error of every ZenithScript C++ API in the struct form of `Zenith2/Docs/Contracts.md` → "Errors and results": a trivially copyable record of at most 32 bytes whose first member is `Zenith_ScriptErrorCode m_eCode` (numbered from 1: codes 1–12 are the twelve `Zenith_ScriptErrorKind` values plus one, codes 13–21 the C++-API failures), followed by the kind, the ZS catalogue code, the module, the line, the diagnostic sequence and the failing transaction's stable simulation ID; it carries no text. |
| Z2-N-SCRIPT-056 | `Zenith2/Docs/Scripting.md`:240 | Zenith2 Scripting › Luau integration and the exception firewall › The protected entry | The message and traceback of every script failure are recorded, before the protected call returns, as a `Zenith_ScriptDiagnostic` in the diagnostic store of the failing world (one 64-slot store per `Zenith_ScriptWorld`, one more in `Zenith_ScriptRuntime` for failures outside a world), named by the error's `m_uDiagnosticSequence` and read through `Zenith_ScriptWorld::GetDiagnostic` or `Zenith_ScriptRuntime::GetDiagnostic`, out of tick or on the world's tick-boundary node; a slot overwritten by 64 newer diagnostics reads `false`, and sequence numbers are diagnostic only: no simulation value, merge decision or replayed quantity reads them. |
| Z2-N-SCRIPT-071 | `Zenith2/Docs/Scripting.md`:348 | Zenith2 Scripting › VM model and threading › VM ownership table | Each world owns exactly one main VM, and every behaviour of that world runs on it on the main thread, in the Early scripts or Late scripts phase the behaviour declares (Z2-D-018, Z2-D-080). |
| Z2-N-SCRIPT-072 | `Zenith2/Docs/Scripting.md`:349 | Zenith2 Scripting › VM model and threading › VM ownership table | Chunk systems run on per-worker, per-world VMs; a chunk-system VM is used by its owning worker thread alone and is never shared across worlds (Z2-D-018). |
| Z2-N-SCRIPT-073 | `Zenith2/Docs/Scripting.md`:350 | Zenith2 Scripting › VM model and threading › VM ownership table | Worlds never share a VM, a Luau value or a registry reference; world isolation (`Zenith2/Docs/Architecture.md` → "Worlds and isolation") holds for scripts as for ECS and physics. |
| Z2-N-SCRIPT-074 | `Zenith2/Docs/Scripting.md`:351 | Zenith2 Scripting › VM model and threading › VM ownership table | The VMs are owned by `Zenith_ScriptWorld` and passed nothing global: there is no process-wide VM, no static `lua_State*` and no service lookup of a VM (Z2-D-046). |
| Z2-N-SCRIPT-075 | `Zenith2/Docs/Scripting.md`:404 | Zenith2 Scripting › VM model and threading › Lazy worker VM creation | A worker VM slot is written only by its owning worker during a sub-job, and read or destroyed by the main thread only while no sub-job of the world is running (world teardown or a quiescent tick boundary); no lock guards the slot. |
| Z2-N-SCRIPT-076 | `Zenith2/Docs/Scripting.md`:405 | Zenith2 Scripting › VM model and threading › Lazy worker VM creation | Lazy creation and module loading have no observable effect on simulation results: which worker created a VM, and when, never changes any committed value. |
| Z2-N-SCRIPT-077 | `Zenith2/Docs/Scripting.md`:406 | Zenith2 Scripting › VM model and threading › Lazy worker VM creation | A failure to create a worker VM (the allocator refusing the initial state) is fatal for the world, reported as `SCRIPT_ERROR_KIND_VM_MEMORY`. |
| Z2-N-SCRIPT-078 | `Zenith2/Docs/Scripting.md`:423 | Zenith2 Scripting › VM model and threading › Memory cap and allocator hook | Every Luau allocation of every VM goes through `Zenith_ScriptAlloc` and the general heap; no Luau memory comes from a frame arena, a pool or a raw `malloc` (`Zenith2/Docs/Contracts.md` → "Ownership and allocation"). |
| Z2-N-SCRIPT-079 | `Zenith2/Docs/Scripting.md`:424 | Zenith2 Scripting › VM model and threading › Memory cap and allocator hook | The main VM's cap comes from the main VM memory cap row and every worker VM's cap from the worker VM memory cap row; the product of the worker cap and the worker count is bounded by the worker VM memory total row, and the memory governor's script budget (`Zenith2/Docs/Architecture.md` → "Memory governor") is the sum of the main and worker totals. |
| Z2-N-SCRIPT-080 | `Zenith2/Docs/Scripting.md`:426 | Zenith2 Scripting › VM model and threading › Memory cap and allocator hook | Hitting a VM memory cap is fatal and not replayable, like the watchdog: the error is `SCRIPT_ERROR_KIND_VM_MEMORY`, the world halts, and the event is reported through the configurable OOM handler, because a VM's heap occupancy depends on which invocations shared it and on GC progress, both of which vary with worker count, so treating it as a recoverable rollback would break the determinism guarantee. The reading of Z2-D-019 for the VM memory cap is confirmed by D-SCRIPT-TXN refinement (open, Stage 1). |
| Z2-N-SCRIPT-081 | `Zenith2/Docs/Scripting.md`:427 | Zenith2 Scripting › VM model and threading › Memory cap and allocator hook | The memory caps are sized from the Scripting rows with headroom so that content within its per-entity snapshot and state budgets never reaches a VM cap; a workload run that reaches one fails its acceptance. |
| Z2-N-SCRIPT-082 | `Zenith2/Docs/Scripting.md`:435 | Zenith2 Scripting › VM model and threading › Garbage collection | Luau's incremental collector stays enabled; each VM is configured at creation with `lua_gc(L, LUA_GCSETGOAL, uGoalPercent)`, `LUA_GCSETSTEPMUL` and `LUA_GCSETSTEPSIZE` from the script GC tuning row. |
| Z2-N-SCRIPT-083 | `Zenith2/Docs/Scripting.md`:436 | Zenith2 Scripting › VM model and threading › Garbage collection | Each world has a per-tick GC step budget (the script GC step row, in kilobytes of collector work per VM per tick): the main VM runs `Zenith_ScriptVM::StepGC` once after each behaviour node completes, and each worker VM runs it at the end of each chunk-system sub-job it executed, never inside an invocation. |
| Z2-N-SCRIPT-084 | `Zenith2/Docs/Scripting.md`:440 | Zenith2 Scripting › VM model and threading › Garbage collection | Collection timing never affects simulation results: weak tables (`__mode`), `__gc` metamethods on script tables, `collectgarbage` and `gcinfo` are unavailable to scripts (see "Modules, bindings and generated types"), so no script can observe when collection happens. |
| Z2-N-SCRIPT-085 | `Zenith2/Docs/Scripting.md`:441 | Zenith2 Scripting › VM model and threading › Garbage collection | GC work is reported per VM to the on-screen profiler's Luau GC row (see "Debugging and profiling") and is never counted as safepoints. |
| Z2-N-SCRIPT-086 | `Zenith2/Docs/Scripting.md`:445 | Zenith2 Scripting › VM model and threading › Floating-point environment | Every thread that runs simulation or job-graph work has the fixed floating-point environment of `Zenith2/Docs/Contracts.md` → "Threading and concurrency contract" (its "Floating-point environment" table: round to nearest, flush-to-zero and denormals-are-zero set on x86-64, FZ and DN set on arm64, every exception masked or trapless), applied by `Zenith_ApplyFloatEnvironment()` at thread start and read back by `Zenith_FloatEnvironmentState Zenith_ReadFloatEnvironment()` (both declared by ZenithJobs in `Zenith_FloatEnvironment.h`; the state struct holds the raw MXCSR or FPCR bits); the per-toolchain call sequences below implement that state, and the job system asserts it at the start of every frame-graph node, so a chunk system computes identical results on any worker. |
| Z2-N-SCRIPT-087 | `Zenith2/Docs/Scripting.md`:464 | Zenith2 Scripting › VM model and threading › DAP fan-out | Every VM of a world, main and worker, receives every breakpoint change before its next invocation starts, and a VM created after the change applies the current set when it loads each module. |
| Z2-N-SCRIPT-088 | `Zenith2/Docs/Scripting.md`:468 | Zenith2 Scripting › VM model and threading › VM teardown | World shutdown drains the tick (`Zenith2/Docs/Architecture.md` → "Lifecycle commands"), then destroys the worker VMs, then the main VM, on the main thread; no VM is destroyed while an invocation, a GC step or a DAP request is running on it. |
| Z2-N-SCRIPT-089 | `Zenith2/Docs/Scripting.md`:437 | Zenith2 Scripting › VM model and threading › Garbage collection | `StepGC`, invocation-thread creation, DAP breakpoint application and module loading each run as a `SCRIPT_CALL_KIND_NATIVE_BODY` protected call with `m_ulSafepointLimit = 0`, so every allocation they make happens inside a protected region; a `LUA_ERRMEM` raised by a GC step is classified `SCRIPT_ERROR_KIND_VM_MEMORY` like any other. |
| Z2-N-SCRIPT-090 | `Zenith2/Docs/Scripting.md`:439 | Zenith2 Scripting › VM model and threading › Garbage collection | Each worker VM owns one pooled invocation thread, created once with `lua_newthread` and `luaL_sandboxthread` and reset with `lua_resetthread` after every chunk-system invocation; each behaviour instance owns one coroutine thread, created when its `Run` coroutine starts (`OnCreate` or `OnRecover`) and released at quarantine or destruction; no thread is allocated per entity or per invocation. |
| Z2-N-SCRIPT-091 | `Zenith2/Docs/Scripting.md`:453 | Zenith2 Scripting › VM model and threading › Floating-point environment | `Test_Script_Determinism_WorkerFloatEnvironment` calls `Zenith_ReadFloatEnvironment()` on every worker and on the main thread, on every preset, and asserts the column "Read back by the test", which is the fixed state ZenithJobs' `Test_FloatEnvironment_WorkerThreads_MatchMain` asserts; it also asserts, as `Test_FloatEnvironment_Denormal_FlushesToZero` does, that a denormal product (`1e-30f * 1e-10f`) is zero on every worker. |
| Z2-N-SCRIPT-092 | `Zenith2/Docs/Scripting.md`:352 | Zenith2 Scripting › VM model and threading › VM ownership table | A world holds at most `uSCRIPT_MAX_WORKER_VM_SLOTS` (254) worker VM slots, so a VM index is at most 254; the index `0xFFFF` (`uSCRIPT_RESERVED_VM_INDEX`) is never issued to a VM and never appears in the top 16 bits of a `Zenith_ScriptInvocationID`, because ECS uses it for the invocation scopes of its own playbacks (`Zenith2/Docs/Design/ECS/WorldAPI.md` → "Zenith_ChunkView header"). |
| Z2-N-SCRIPT-093 | `Zenith2/Docs/Scripting.md`:353 | Zenith2 Scripting › VM model and threading › VM ownership table | The members `m_bDebuggerStopped` and `m_ulWatchdogRebaseTicks` exist in every configuration (no layout-changing conditional, `Zenith2/Docs/Contracts.md` → "No layout-changing conditionals") and are written only in development builds, by the VM's own thread, as "Wall-clock watchdog" step 4 states. |
| Z2-N-SCRIPT-101 | `Zenith2/Docs/Scripting.md`:482 | Zenith2 Scripting › Modules, bindings and generated types › Script sources and module kinds | A module's kind is decided by its return value at load time; a `Zenith.ChunkSystem` or `Zenith.Behaviour` declaration nested inside a library module's table, or created anywhere but as the module's return value, is a `SCRIPT_ERROR_KIND_LOAD` error. |
| Z2-N-SCRIPT-102 | `Zenith2/Docs/Scripting.md`:483 | Zenith2 Scripting › Modules, bindings and generated types › Script sources and module kinds | Every simulation module (chunk system, behaviour, and every library they require) starts with `--!strict`; ZenithCook refuses a non-strict simulation module. |
| Z2-N-SCRIPT-103 | `Zenith2/Docs/Scripting.md`:489 | Zenith2 Scripting › Modules, bindings and generated types › Module loading and sandboxing | `require` accepts a constant string literal only, resolved at cook time against the game's module manifest (aliases `@game/…`, `@zenith/…`, `@types`, `@<module>/…`, and `./…` relative paths); a non-literal argument is a compile-time error reported by ZenithCook and a `SCRIPT_ERROR_KIND_LOAD` error when hot-reloaded source is loaded. |
| Z2-N-SCRIPT-104 | `Zenith2/Docs/Scripting.md`:490 | Zenith2 Scripting › Modules, bindings and generated types › Module loading and sandboxing | `require` is callable at module scope only, while the module is being loaded; a call during an invocation raises the catalogued misuse error. |
| Z2-N-SCRIPT-105 | `Zenith2/Docs/Scripting.md`:491 | Zenith2 Scripting › Modules, bindings and generated types › Module loading and sandboxing | Module dependency cycles are a load error naming the cycle (`a -> b -> a`). |
| Z2-N-SCRIPT-106 | `Zenith2/Docs/Scripting.md`:492 | Zenith2 Scripting › Modules, bindings and generated types › Module loading and sandboxing | Each VM loads each module at most once per module generation; the cached module table is deep-frozen with `table.freeze` applied recursively to every table reachable from it at the end of its load. |
| Z2-N-SCRIPT-107 | `Zenith2/Docs/Scripting.md`:493 | Zenith2 Scripting › Modules, bindings and generated types › Module loading and sandboxing | Each VM is sandboxed at creation: `luaL_openlibs`, then the reduction of the globals to the environment of its role, then `luaL_sandbox(L)`, which makes the built-in library tables and the global table read-only and enables safe-environment optimisations; every thread that runs an invocation (the pooled worker thread, each behaviour coroutine thread) is sandboxed with `luaL_sandboxthread` when it is created, so a global write lands in a per-thread proxy that the load-time check has already rejected. |
| Z2-N-SCRIPT-108 | `Zenith2/Docs/Scripting.md`:494 | Zenith2 Scripting › Modules, bindings and generated types › Module loading and sandboxing | The environments are those of `Scripting/Bindings.md` → "Environments and library subsets"; a function absent from an environment is replaced by a stub that raises its catalogued misuse error, so a script that calls it gets a specific message rather than "attempt to call a nil value". |
| Z2-N-SCRIPT-109 | `Zenith2/Docs/Scripting.md`:537 | Zenith2 Scripting › Modules, bindings and generated types › Bindings in brief | Engine functionality reaches Luau through bindings registered with ZenithReflect by the composition root for the engine and for every static module the game selects in `game.json` (Z2-D-024); a module that is not selected registers nothing and its bindings are absent from the game's environment and `.d.luau`. |
| Z2-N-SCRIPT-110 | `Zenith2/Docs/Scripting.md`:540 | Zenith2 Scripting › Modules, bindings and generated types › Bindings in brief | Binding thunks are generated by the C++ registration template from the reflected function's signature; there is no hand-written marshalling code per function and no source-to-source binding generator. |
| Z2-N-SCRIPT-111 | `Zenith2/Docs/Scripting.md`:541 | Zenith2 Scripting › Modules, bindings and generated types › Bindings in brief | Components, including data-defined components, reach Luau through reflection-driven views (field ID, offset, kind), with no per-component generated code (`Scripting/Bindings.md` → "Component views through reflection"). |
| Z2-N-SCRIPT-112 | `Zenith2/Docs/Scripting.md`:542 | Zenith2 Scripting › Modules, bindings and generated types › Bindings in brief | Every binding declares its allowed contexts (`SCRIPT_FUNCTION_CONTEXT_SYSTEM`, `SCRIPT_FUNCTION_CONTEXT_BEHAVIOUR`, `SCRIPT_FUNCTION_CONTEXT_LOAD`, `SCRIPT_FUNCTION_CONTEXT_TOOLS`) and its effect class (`SCRIPT_FUNCTION_EFFECT_PURE`, `SCRIPT_FUNCTION_EFFECT_READ_WORLD`, `SCRIPT_FUNCTION_EFFECT_BUFFERED`); a call from a disallowed context raises the catalogued misuse error. |
| Z2-N-SCRIPT-113 | `Zenith2/Docs/Scripting.md`:543 | Zenith2 Scripting › Modules, bindings and generated types › Bindings in brief | A binding with a side effect is `SCRIPT_FUNCTION_EFFECT_BUFFERED` and writes the effect into the invocation's side-effect sub-buffer through `Zenith_ScriptCallFrame::m_pxContext` (`Zenith_ScriptInvocationContext::AppendSideEffect`); no binding performs a world-visible side effect directly, and review confirms that every new side-effect kind is routed through the private buffer. |
| Z2-N-SCRIPT-114 | `Zenith2/Docs/Scripting.md`:547 | Zenith2 Scripting › Modules, bindings and generated types › Generated types in brief | ZenithCook's `luau-types` step writes the game's Luau definition files from the live reflection registry (engine types, the selected modules' types, the game's data-defined schemas and every registered binding) to `Zenith2/out/<checkout-hash>/Generated/Luau/<Game>/`: `zenith.d.luau`, `components.d.luau`, the prelude module `Zenith.luau`, the descriptor module `Types.luau` and a `.luaurc` whose aliases match the runtime module resolver (`Scripting/Bindings.md` → "Luau type generation"); none of them is hand-edited or tracked. ZenithCook links every optional module and, before emitting, registers exactly the set the game's `game.json` selects (`Zenith2/Docs/Architecture.md` → "Engine modules and game projects"). |
| Z2-N-SCRIPT-115 | `Zenith2/Docs/Scripting.md`:548 | Zenith2 Scripting › Modules, bindings and generated types › Generated types in brief | ZenithCook type-checks every script of the game with `luau-analyze --definitions=<each .d.luau>` out of process, and a type error in a simulation module fails the cook. |

| Z2-N-SCRIPT-125 | `Zenith2/Docs/Scripting.md`:539 | Zenith2 Scripting › Modules, bindings and generated types › Bindings in brief | A library at or above ZenithReflect registers its own bindings from an `Internal/` translation unit; the bindings of libraries below ZenithReflect (ZenithCore, ZenithGraph, ZenithJobs, ZenithProfiling, ZenithPlatform, ZenithVertexFormat, ZenithCompression) are registered by the composition root in `Zenith2/Engine/ZenithRuntime/Internal/ScriptBindings/`. |

| Z2-N-SCRIPT-152 | `Zenith2/Docs/Scripting.md`:774 | Zenith2 Scripting › Behaviours and chunk systems › Schedule compilation of script nodes | System order, the first key of every merge order in this document, is the position of the script node in the compiled schedule's deterministic topological order for its phase, with ties broken by the stable source ID; it depends on the module set and declarations, never on timing. |
| Z2-N-SCRIPT-153 | `Zenith2/Docs/Scripting.md`:778 | Zenith2 Scripting › Behaviours and chunk systems › Schedule compilation of script nodes | Adding, removing or hot-reloading a script module with a changed declaration recompiles the schedule in the frame prologue (the loop steps before `RunFrame` that precede the frame's first tick boundary, where the hot-reload window and world swaps happen); a reload whose declaration is unchanged reuses the compiled schedule. |
| Z2-N-SCRIPT-154 | `Zenith2/Docs/Scripting.md`:782 | Zenith2 Scripting › Behaviours and chunk systems › Commands and cross-entity writes | Every structural change a script requests (spawn, destroy, add or remove component, reparent) and every write to another entity or to a field outside the current phase's authority is a command recorded in the entity transaction's side-effect sub-buffer and played back at the next sync point; nothing a script does changes structure immediately. |
| Z2-N-SCRIPT-155 | `Zenith2/Docs/Scripting.md`:783 | Zenith2 Scripting › Behaviours and chunk systems › Commands and cross-entity writes | Commands that write the same field of the same entity are applied at playback in merge order, so the last in merge order wins, independent of worker count. |

| Z2-N-SCRIPT-159 | `Zenith2/Docs/Scripting.md`:776 | Zenith2 Scripting › Behaviours and chunk systems › Schedule compilation of script nodes | The hazard edges from a script node to every node that depends on it originate at the script node's merge continuation, not at the join of its sub-jobs: a node (script or engine) that reads or writes a field column a script node writes starts only after that node's merge point has accepted or rejected every entity and committed, so no dependent ever observes an in-place write that a merge-time rejection subsequently restores; nodes whose access is disjoint from the script node still overlap with it. |

| Z2-N-SCRIPT-161 | `Zenith2/Docs/Scripting.md`:775 | Zenith2 Scripting › Behaviours and chunk systems › Schedule compilation of script nodes | Behaviour-module order, which sequences every main-thread behaviour node of a phase, is ascending module ID, that is, the sorted manifest path order of the modules (behaviour declarations carry no `Before`/`After`); it is the system order of those nodes. |
| Z2-N-SCRIPT-171 | `Zenith2/Docs/Scripting.md`:791 | Zenith2 Scripting › Transaction scope and the entity boundary › Invocations and entity transactions | Every script invocation is a transaction: a chunk-system call over one chunk, a behaviour callback, or a behaviour coroutine resume segment (Z2-D-016). |
| Z2-N-SCRIPT-172 | `Zenith2/Docs/Scripting.md`:792 | Zenith2 Scripting › Transaction scope and the entity boundary › Invocations and entity transactions | A chunk-system call iterates entities, and each entity's processing is its own logical transaction; a behaviour callback or segment is one entity's transaction. |
| Z2-N-SCRIPT-173 | `Zenith2/Docs/Scripting.md`:793 | Zenith2 Scripting › Transaction scope and the entity boundary › Invocations and entity transactions | A failure rolls back only the failing entity's writes and side effects; the other entities of the same call and of the same tick commit normally. |
| Z2-N-SCRIPT-174 | `Zenith2/Docs/Scripting.md`:794 | Zenith2 Scripting › Transaction scope and the entity boundary › Invocations and entity transactions | A behaviour coroutine commits at each yield: a yield ends the segment's entity transaction, and the next resume starts a new one. |

| Z2-N-SCRIPT-176 | `Zenith2/Docs/Scripting.md`:813 | Zenith2 Scripting › Transaction scope and the entity boundary › What is transactional | Script randomness is counter-based, derived per invocation (see "Counter-based random numbers"); there is no shared mutable random stream, so rollback has nothing to restore and parallel workers cannot race on one. |
| Z2-N-SCRIPT-177 | `Zenith2/Docs/Scripting.md`:817 | Zenith2 Scripting › Transaction scope and the entity boundary › What is not transactional | The Luau heap (script tables, globals, upvalues, coroutine stacks) is not transactional; a failing behaviour instance is quarantined, its heap state discarded, and it is restored from its last successfully committed serialized state (see "Quarantine and hot reload"). |
| Z2-N-SCRIPT-178 | `Zenith2/Docs/Scripting.md`:818 | Zenith2 Scripting › Transaction scope and the entity boundary › What is not transactional | Log output (`print`, diagnostics) is not transactional and is emitted immediately; it never feeds simulation state. |
