| Z2-N-SCRIPT-180 | `Zenith2/Docs/Scripting.md`:825 | Zenith2 Scripting › Transaction scope and the entity boundary › Cross-entity read rule | Other entities' rows are readable only in field columns that no node of the current phase declares as written; per-field authority makes this checkable when the schedule compiles, and a declared cross-entity read of such a column is a schedule-compile error (`SCRIPT_SCHEDULE_CROSS_ENTITY_READ_OF_WRITTEN_COLUMN`). |
| Z2-N-SCRIPT-181 | `Zenith2/Docs/Scripting.md`:826 | Zenith2 Scripting › Transaction scope and the entity boundary › Cross-entity read rule | At runtime, a read through a view or `ctx:Read(entity, field)` of another row in a column written in this phase raises the catalogued misuse error, in all builds. |
| Z2-N-SCRIPT-182 | `Zenith2/Docs/Scripting.md`:827 | Zenith2 Scripting › Transaction scope and the entity boundary › Cross-entity read rule | The same two rules bind behaviours: a behaviour reads another entity's field only when no node of the current phase writes it. |
| Z2-N-SCRIPT-183 | `Zenith2/Docs/Scripting.md`:833 | Zenith2 Scripting › Transaction scope and the entity boundary › Reductions and shared structures | Sums, counters, minima, maxima, spatial grids and every other structure shared across entities are never written in place across entities; each entity emits a contribution into its private sub-buffer (`ctx:Contribute(name, value)`), and contributions are folded at the merge point, in merge order, with the fixed-order reduction tree (see "Fixed-order reduction tree"). |
| Z2-N-SCRIPT-184 | `Zenith2/Docs/Scripting.md`:834 | Zenith2 Scripting › Transaction scope and the entity boundary › Reductions and shared structures | A reduction's `Target` field is written at commit by the merge step and by nothing else in that phase; the schedule compiler treats the reducing system as the target field's direct writer. |
| Z2-N-SCRIPT-185 | `Zenith2/Docs/Scripting.md`:838 | Zenith2 Scripting › Transaction scope and the entity boundary › Snapshot lifetime | Chunk snapshots live until the system's merge point, not merely until the call returns, because merge-time capacity rejection can still roll back an entity's rows afterwards. |
| Z2-N-SCRIPT-186 | `Zenith2/Docs/Scripting.md`:839 | Zenith2 Scripting › Transaction scope and the entity boundary › Snapshot lifetime | Change-tracking versions advance at merge, never at write. |
| Z2-N-SCRIPT-187 | `Zenith2/Docs/Scripting.md`:840 | Zenith2 Scripting › Transaction scope and the entity boundary › Snapshot lifetime | The per-tick total of snapshot memory is a budget row (the per-tick snapshot total row), measured and reported every tick; exceeding it raises a performance diagnostic and never a rollback, because the per-chunk snapshot total depends on chunk layout. Z2-B-SCRIPT-013 is `BR-REPORT` in `Zenith2/Docs/Budgets.md` → "Scripting rows", in agreement with this bullet. |
| Z2-N-SCRIPT-188 | `Zenith2/Docs/Scripting.md`:841 | Zenith2 Scripting › Transaction scope and the entity boundary › Snapshot lifetime | "Commit cannot fail" holds because acceptance and rejection are both decided at the merge point, before commit. |
| Z2-N-SCRIPT-189 | `Zenith2/Docs/Scripting.md`:845 | Zenith2 Scripting › Transaction scope and the entity boundary › Disable from the next tick boundary | A failing chunk system or behaviour module is disabled for its world from the next tick boundary, never mid-tick, so every other invocation of the current tick runs regardless of which worker failed first. |
| Z2-N-SCRIPT-190 | `Zenith2/Docs/Scripting.md`:846 | Zenith2 Scripting › Transaction scope and the entity boundary › Disable from the next tick boundary | The failure is reported with the module, the entity's stable simulation ID, the tick, the error kind and code, the message and the traceback, and the module stays disabled for that world until it is reloaded (see "Quarantine and hot reload"). |
| Z2-N-SCRIPT-191 | `Zenith2/Docs/Scripting.md`:870 | Zenith2 Scripting › Transaction scope and the entity boundary › Worked example three entities second fails | `Test_Script_Txn_EntityBoundaryMiddleFailure` reproduces this worked example with the three rows placed in one chunk, then in three chunks, then on three workers, and asserts the final column values 100 / 40 / 70, one delivered `StaminaFull` event (from A), `TotalStamina == 100`, `FullCount == 1`, and the module disabled from the next tick in every placement. |
| Z2-N-SCRIPT-211 | `Zenith2/Docs/Scripting.md`:889 | Zenith2 Scripting › Component writes shadows and side-effect buffers › Data structures | Transaction memory (snapshots, records, payloads, shadows) is `Zenith_Arena` memory partitioned per (script system, frame-thread slot), because one worker interleaves sub-jobs of several script nodes of a phase: one scratch arena per frame-thread slot plus the main slot (the behaviour nodes' slot), holding the structures that grow while the slot's one running invocation executes and reset to that invocation's marker when it ends, and, per slot, retention arenas, one claimed by each script system at its first invocation on the slot in a tick, holding that system's transactions until its merge point and `Reset()` by that system's merge; its captures are trivially destructible, as every arena capture is (`Zenith2/Docs/Contracts.md` → "Ownership and allocation"), and ZenithScript has no allocator of its own. |
| Z2-N-SCRIPT-212 | `Zenith2/Docs/Scripting.md`:910 | Zenith2 Scripting › Component writes shadows and side-effect buffers › Write path algorithm | Component writes go in place, so reads within the same entity transaction see the script's own writes. |
| Z2-N-SCRIPT-213 | `Zenith2/Docs/Scripting.md`:911 | Zenith2 Scripting › Component writes shadows and side-effect buffers › Write path algorithm | A component field with out-of-line storage (containers, strings) is never mutated in place from script; behaviours mutate it through its copy-on-write shadow, and chunk systems change strings and handles through commands. |
| Z2-N-SCRIPT-214 | `Zenith2/Docs/Scripting.md`:920 | Zenith2 Scripting › Component writes shadows and side-effect buffers › First-write snapshot | On the first write to a chunk, only that chunk's declared-writable field columns are snapshotted, over the invocation's row range; their change-tracking bits need no copy, because writes set only pending bits in the invocation's private set, which a rollback clears for the failing row; a column the job graph granted to another job is never snapshotted or restored, so jobs with disjoint access cannot clobber each other. |
| Z2-N-SCRIPT-215 | `Zenith2/Docs/Scripting.md`:922 | Zenith2 Scripting › Component writes shadows and side-effect buffers › First-write snapshot | A behaviour invocation snapshots only its own entity's row (`m_uRowCount = 1`) of the declared-writable columns, on its first write in that invocation, because a behaviour transaction touches one row. |
| Z2-N-SCRIPT-216 | `Zenith2/Docs/Scripting.md`:923 | Zenith2 Scripting › Component writes shadows and side-effect buffers › First-write snapshot | Pending change bits are per (invocation, column) (`Zenith_RawColumn::m_pulRowChangeBits`), so the bits a job sets and clears belong to that job alone, and they are folded into the column's change-tracking state at the merge point for committed entities only (`Zenith2/Docs/Architecture.md` → "ECS model"). |
| Z2-N-SCRIPT-217 | `Zenith2/Docs/Scripting.md`:933 | Zenith2 Scripting › Component writes shadows and side-effect buffers › Copy-on-write shadows | A mutated container or string field gets a per-invocation copy-on-write shadow which the script reads back; the commit swaps it in and a failure drops it. |
| Z2-N-SCRIPT-218 | `Zenith2/Docs/Scripting.md`:934 | Zenith2 Scripting › Component writes shadows and side-effect buffers › Copy-on-write shadows | Container fields are not exposed in chunk views; they are reached through the behaviour API alone. |
| Z2-N-SCRIPT-219 | `Zenith2/Docs/Scripting.md`:943 | Zenith2 Scripting › Component writes shadows and side-effect buffers › Side-effect sub-buffers | Structural commands, events, spawned entities, audio and VFX triggers, save requests, asynchronous requests and reduction contributions all go to the private per-entity sub-buffer of the running invocation (its ECS command buffer for the ECS kinds, its SCRIPT record list for the SCRIPT-held kinds), and nothing of it reaches a world queue or a consumer unless the entity transaction is accepted at the merge point: the ECS buffer through `Zenith_MergeContext::AcceptBuffer`, each SCRIPT record through its kind's `Zenith_ScriptSideEffectConsumer` at commit. |
| Z2-N-SCRIPT-220 | `Zenith2/Docs/Scripting.md`:948 | Zenith2 Scripting › Component writes shadows and side-effect buffers › Provisional spawn handles | `ctx:Spawn(prefabOrArchetype)` returns a provisional `Zenith.Entity` (a handle with the provisional bit set and an invocation-local index) that the same entity transaction can pass to further commands; commands naming it are remapped to the real entity at playback. |
| Z2-N-SCRIPT-221 | `Zenith2/Docs/Scripting.md`:949 | Zenith2 Scripting › Component writes shadows and side-effect buffers › Provisional spawn handles | On rollback or rejection, the provisional handles issued by the entity transaction are released with its sub-buffer: no entity is created, no entity capacity is consumed, and the next accepted spawn in merge order receives the stable simulation ID it would have received had the failed spawn never been requested. |
| Z2-N-SCRIPT-222 | `Zenith2/Docs/Scripting.md`:950 | Zenith2 Scripting › Component writes shadows and side-effect buffers › Provisional spawn handles | A provisional handle stored into behaviour state is recorded in the checkpoint's fixup list and rewritten to the real handle when the spawn is played back; reading a provisional handle after playback resolves it through the world's one-tick remap table, and after that it is stale. |
| Z2-N-SCRIPT-223 | `Zenith2/Docs/Scripting.md`:963 | Zenith2 Scripting › Component writes shadows and side-effect buffers › Per-entity memory accounting | The snapshot-memory cap is per entity, not per call, so it does not vary with chunk size; exceeding it raises a `SCRIPT_ERROR_KIND_SNAPSHOT_CAP` Luau error for that entity, which triggers that entity's rollback. |
| Z2-N-SCRIPT-224 | `Zenith2/Docs/Scripting.md`:891 | Zenith2 Scripting › Component writes shadows and side-effect buffers › Data structures | At world creation each slot's scratch arena is pre-reserved to `round_up(2 × W, 64 KiB) + 64 KiB`, and entry 0 of each slot's retention set to W, where W is the worst case of one invocation: the per-entity snapshot cap (Z2-B-SCRIPT-012) times `uSCRIPT_MAX_ROWS_PER_INVOCATION` (256; a chunk with more rows is processed as several invocations of at most that many rows, which changes no result because the transaction boundary is the entity) plus the 32,896 fixed bytes of the transaction header and its entity, error and snapshot tables, plus, for the main slot, the largest declared checkpoint slot (Z2-B-SCRIPT-015); so every private reservation of an entity within its cap is served without touching the general heap, and every layout-independent overflow surfaces as `SNAPSHOT_CAP` (D-SCRIPT-TXN refinement, open, Stage 1, confirms this capacity model; `Zenith2/Docs/Design/SCRIPT/Transactions.md` → "Transaction arena partition"). |
| Z2-N-SCRIPT-225 | `Zenith2/Docs/Scripting.md`:892 | Zenith2 Scripting › Component writes shadows and side-effect buffers › Data structures | When transactions retained until the merge point push a retention arena beyond its retained block, it grows in blocks from the general heap; a growth failure is fatal (`SCRIPT_ERROR_KIND_RESERVATION`, world halted, not replayable), because general-heap state depends on scheduling and on every other subsystem, exactly as a VM cap trip does. A scratch arena never grows, and a retention arena's cap is derived from existing rows (Z2-B-SCRIPT-012, the world's entity capacity, Z2-B-GAME-003) so that no world within its rows reaches it: reaching that cap is a `ZENITH_CHECK` failure (an engine defect), never a script error, and adds no budget row (`Zenith2/Docs/Design/SCRIPT/Transactions.md` → "Retention ceiling derivation"). |
| Z2-N-SCRIPT-226 | `Zenith2/Docs/Scripting.md`:944 | Zenith2 Scripting › Component writes shadows and side-effect buffers › Side-effect sub-buffers | Each SCRIPT-held side-effect kind has exactly one `Zenith_ScriptSideEffectConsumer` (the Luau-free ABI interface of `ZenithReflect/Zenith_ScriptBinding.h`, `Scripting/Bindings.md` → "Binding registration and the thunk ABI"), registered on the binding table with `SetSideEffectConsumer` before `Seal` by the library that owns the kind (audio and VFX: those modules; save requests: ZenithRuntime over ZenithSave; asynchronous requests: one ZenithRuntime multiplexer over the owning submitters); `Seal` refuses a buffered binding whose mask names a SCRIPT-held kind with no consumer (`SCRIPT_BINDING_ERROR_MISSING_CONSUMER`). |
| Z2-N-SCRIPT-241 | `Zenith2/Docs/Scripting.md`:983 | Zenith2 Scripting › Commit and failure semantics › Failure classes | A merge-time capacity rejection rolls back the entity and reports a diagnostic, but does not disable the module, because it reflects shared world capacity rather than a fault of the script. (Confirmed by D-SCRIPT-TXN refinement, open, Stage 1.) |
| Z2-N-SCRIPT-242 | `Zenith2/Docs/Scripting.md`:984 | Zenith2 Scripting › Commit and failure semantics › Failure classes | A fatal event abandons the tick: no merge point of that tick commits, the world enters `SCRIPT_WORLD_STATE_HALTED`, the diagnostic is delivered to the game's fatal handler, and the tick's `REPLAY_RECORD_TICK` record carries `REPLAY_TICK_FLAG_NON_REPLAYABLE`, so a replay stops before it; this holds for every fatal script event (VM memory cap, transaction-arena growth failure, watchdog, internal exception). |
| Z2-N-SCRIPT-243 | `Zenith2/Docs/Scripting.md`:997 | Zenith2 Scripting › Commit and failure semantics › Execution-time failure algorithm | On failure, the failing entity's snapshotted rows are restored, its private buffers are discarded so no queued destroy or event survives, its shadows are dropped, and the error is reported (Z2-D-051). |
| Z2-N-SCRIPT-244 | `Zenith2/Docs/Scripting.md`:1012 | Zenith2 Scripting › Commit and failure semantics › Merge point algorithm | The merge point decides acceptance and rejection of every entity transaction of a node before anything is committed, in merge order, so a rejection never depends on completion order. |
| Z2-N-SCRIPT-245 | `Zenith2/Docs/Scripting.md`:1030 | Zenith2 Scripting › Commit and failure semantics › Commit splice algorithm | On success, the snapshots are dropped, the private buffers and shadows are committed, and the written columns' change-tracking versions advance through `Zenith_ChunkView::CommitRowChanges` at the merge point; component writes need no copy because they are already in place. |
| Z2-N-SCRIPT-246 | `Zenith2/Docs/Scripting.md`:1031 | Zenith2 Scripting › Commit and failure semantics › Commit splice algorithm | A commit never invalidates another invocation's view: structural epochs change only at sync-point playback, and a commit advances change-tracking versions alone. |
| Z2-N-SCRIPT-247 | `Zenith2/Docs/Scripting.md`:1035 | Zenith2 Scripting › Commit and failure semantics › Commit cannot fail | All capacity an invocation needs is reserved during the invocation (private arena memory for records, payloads, shadows and snapshots, served from the pre-reserved arena; the checkpoint slot at instance creation) or at the merge point (shared capacity); an entity that would exceed its private share raises the `SNAPSHOT_CAP` Luau error inside its transaction. |
| Z2-N-SCRIPT-248 | `Zenith2/Docs/Scripting.md`:1036 | Zenith2 Scripting › Commit and failure semantics › Commit cannot fail | Commit only splices memory that is already reserved and flips indices; it performs no allocation, no capacity check and no fallible operation, and it returns `void`. |
| Z2-N-SCRIPT-249 | `Zenith2/Docs/Scripting.md`:1041 | Zenith2 Scripting › Commit and failure semantics › Merge chain across systems | The merge points of the script nodes of one phase run as a chain, each after the previous one completes (the frame graph adds a merge-chain edge between consecutive merge continuations), in the stage's cost-independent topological order: Kahn's algorithm over the stage's declared hazard and ordering edges, the chain itself excluded, ties broken by registration index, which for script systems is the system order of [Z2-N-SCRIPT-152] (`Zenith2/Docs/Architecture/ECSModel.md` [Z2-N-ARCH-692]); so a script system registered before a writer of a property it reads still schedules, the nodes' sub-jobs themselves still run in parallel, and shared capacity is consumed in chain order and, within one merge, in (logical invocation ID, sequence) order, independent of which node finished first and identical on every backend. |
| Z2-N-SCRIPT-250 | `Zenith2/Docs/Scripting.md`:1042 | Zenith2 Scripting › Commit and failure semantics › Merge chain across systems | Playback at the sync point applies the accepted records in merge-key order (system order, logical ID, sequence), whatever the chain order in which their merges accepted them, so the queue contents at the sync point are the concatenation, in system order, of each node's accepted records in (logical ID, sequence) order. |
| Z2-N-SCRIPT-251 | `Zenith2/Docs/Scripting.md`:1046 | Zenith2 Scripting › Commit and failure semantics › Behaviour invocations of one instance | When one instance has several invocations in one node (several event handlers, then its `Run` segment), each invocation is its own entity transaction with its own row snapshot; a subsequent invocation reads the in-place results of the earlier ones. |
| Z2-N-SCRIPT-252 | `Zenith2/Docs/Scripting.md`:1047 | Zenith2 Scripting › Commit and failure semantics › Behaviour invocations of one instance | If an earlier invocation of an instance fails or is rejected, every subsequent invocation of that instance in the same node is skipped or rejected with it, and the instance's row is restored to the snapshot of the earliest failed or rejected invocation, so no committed invocation ever builds on an uncommitted one. |
| Z2-N-SCRIPT-271 | `Zenith2/Docs/Scripting.md`:1065 | Zenith2 Scripting › Determinism rules and randomness › Ordering keys | Command buffers from parallel invocations are merged in (system order, logical invocation ID, sequence) order, never in completion order. |
| Z2-N-SCRIPT-272 | `Zenith2/Docs/Scripting.md`:1066 | Zenith2 Scripting › Determinism rules and randomness › Ordering keys | The logical invocation ID of an entity transaction is (the entity's stable simulation ID, the invocation ordinal), never a chunk index, storage index, worker index or VM-local counter. |
| Z2-N-SCRIPT-273 | `Zenith2/Docs/Scripting.md`:1067 | Zenith2 Scripting › Determinism rules and randomness › Ordering keys | Behaviours of one module and phase execute in ascending (stable simulation ID, invocation ordinal) order, so main-thread execution order is itself the merge order. |
| Z2-N-SCRIPT-274 | `Zenith2/Docs/Scripting.md`:1068 | Zenith2 Scripting › Determinism rules and randomness › Ordering keys | The invocation ordinal of a behaviour invocation is its index among that instance's invocations in the current tick, in execution order (`OnCreate`, then per phase in phase order the event handlers in delivery order followed by `OnUpdate` or the `Run` segment, then a checkpoint-only transaction); a recovery invocation takes no ordinal of this sequence; a chunk-system invocation's ordinal is 0. |
| Z2-N-SCRIPT-275 | `Zenith2/Docs/Scripting.md`:1093 | Zenith2 Scripting › Determinism rules and randomness › Counter-based random numbers | Each 64-bit draw is `Hash64` of the 40-byte `Zenith_ScriptRandomKey` block (world seed, source ID, tick, stable simulation ID, invocation ordinal, draw counter); `Hash64` is the 64-bit hash function selected by D-SEL-HASH (open, Stage 1; the candidate is xxHash XXH3-64 with seed 0), and the same function computes `m_ulSourceID`. |
| Z2-N-SCRIPT-276 | `Zenith2/Docs/Scripting.md`:1094 | Zenith2 Scripting › Determinism rules and randomness › Counter-based random numbers | Randomness is never keyed by a chunk index, storage index, worker index or VM, all of which change with compaction and layout. |
| Z2-N-SCRIPT-277 | `Zenith2/Docs/Scripting.md`:1095 | Zenith2 Scripting › Determinism rules and randomness › Counter-based random numbers | The draw counter belongs to the entity transaction and starts at 0 for each entity transaction; re-running a failed invocation (in a replay) therefore produces the identical sequence, and two chunk systems running in parallel never share a counter. |
| Z2-N-SCRIPT-278 | `Zenith2/Docs/Scripting.md`:1096 | Zenith2 Scripting › Determinism rules and randomness › Counter-based random numbers | A change to the key layout, the hash function, or the derivation functions below changes every script's random sequence and therefore requires a replay-format version bump. |
| Z2-N-SCRIPT-279 | `Zenith2/Docs/Scripting.md`:1111 | Zenith2 Scripting › Determinism rules and randomness › Random API | `ctx.Random` is the only source of randomness available to simulation scripts, in both environments. |
| Z2-N-SCRIPT-280 | `Zenith2/Docs/Scripting.md`:1122 | Zenith2 Scripting › Determinism rules and randomness › Fixed-order reduction tree | Parallel reductions use the fixed-order tree above, independent of worker count; the tree shape depends only on the number of accepted contributions and their merge order. |
| Z2-N-SCRIPT-281 | `Zenith2/Docs/Scripting.md`:1126 | Zenith2 Scripting › Determinism rules and randomness › Spawn ID assignment at playback | Stable simulation IDs of spawned entities are assigned at sync-point playback, in merged order, from the world's monotonic simulation-ID counter; the counter's value is part of the world state that saves and replays record. |
| Z2-N-SCRIPT-282 | `Zenith2/Docs/Scripting.md`:1127 | Zenith2 Scripting › Determinism rules and randomness › Spawn ID assignment at playback | Spawns are created at a sync point and are visible to the next phase (`Zenith2/Docs/Architecture.md` → "Lifecycle commands"); a behaviour's `OnCreate` runs at the first invocation of its instance in or after that phase. |
| Z2-N-SCRIPT-283 | `Zenith2/Docs/Scripting.md`:1131 | Zenith2 Scripting › Determinism rules and randomness › Event order | Events are delivered in phase 9 in the merged order of their emitting records; within each subscribed instance, its handlers for them run in phase 10 in that same order, and instances are ordered as the behaviour-ordering bullet of "Ordering keys" states. |
| Z2-N-SCRIPT-284 | `Zenith2/Docs/Scripting.md`:1132 | Zenith2 Scripting › Determinism rules and randomness › Event order | An event emitted during phase 10 is delivered in phase 9 of the next tick. |
| Z2-N-SCRIPT-285 | `Zenith2/Docs/Scripting.md`:1139 | Zenith2 Scripting › Determinism rules and randomness › Iteration order and address-dependent values | ZenithCook's script lint rejects, in every simulation module, a `pairs`/`next`/generalized `for … in t` iteration over a table whose inferred key type (from `luau-analyze` in strict mode) is not `number`, `string`, `boolean` or a string-literal union (`SCRIPT_LINT_ADDRESS_KEYED_ITERATION`); entity-keyed maps use `entity:Id()` (the stable simulation ID as a number) as the key. |
| Z2-N-SCRIPT-286 | `Zenith2/Docs/Scripting.md`:1140 | Zenith2 Scripting › Determinism rules and randomness › Iteration order and address-dependent values | Behaviour state `Map` fields accept string, enum and integer keys alone, so no serialized or restored state depends on address order. |
| Z2-N-SCRIPT-287 | `Zenith2/Docs/Scripting.md`:1141 | Zenith2 Scripting › Determinism rules and randomness › Iteration order and address-dependent values | No script-visible value exposes an address: the Zenith `tostring` hides addresses, and `Zenith.Entity` exposes its stable simulation ID and never its storage location. |
| Z2-N-SCRIPT-288 | `Zenith2/Docs/Scripting.md`:1156 | Zenith2 Scripting › Determinism rules and randomness › Determinism test matrix | The same replay run with 1, 2, 8 and all workers, with and without forced compaction and with the default and the alternate chunk capacity, gives bit-identical per-tick world-state hashes, on each backend separately (`Test_Script_Determinism_ReplayMatrix`). |
| Z2-N-SCRIPT-301 | `Zenith2/Docs/Scripting.md`:1175 | Zenith2 Scripting › Budgets safepoints and the watchdog › Safepoint counting | The script CPU budget is a safepoint count: Luau's interrupt fires at safepoints (loop back-edges, calls and returns) with `gc == -1`, and the count is deterministic for the same bytecode, so it is replayable. |
| Z2-N-SCRIPT-302 | `Zenith2/Docs/Scripting.md`:1176 | Zenith2 Scripting › Budgets safepoints and the watchdog › Safepoint counting | Interrupt calls with `gc >= 0` (GC step notifications) are never counted, because GC timing differs between VMs. |
| Z2-N-SCRIPT-303 | `Zenith2/Docs/Scripting.md`:1177 | Zenith2 Scripting › Budgets safepoints and the watchdog › Safepoint counting | Time spent inside C++ bindings is not counted; the wall-clock watchdog remains the backstop there. |
| Z2-N-SCRIPT-304 | `Zenith2/Docs/Scripting.md`:1189 | Zenith2 Scripting › Budgets safepoints and the watchdog › Budget scopes | Safepoint budgets are enforced per entity transaction (chunk-system entity or behaviour invocation), never per call and never per tick, so whether a budget is exceeded depends only on the entity's own processing. |
| Z2-N-SCRIPT-305 | `Zenith2/Docs/Scripting.md`:1190 | Zenith2 Scripting › Budgets safepoints and the watchdog › Budget scopes | A declaration's `Budget` field can lower its limit below the budget row and never raise it. |
| Z2-N-SCRIPT-306 | `Zenith2/Docs/Scripting.md`:1194 | Zenith2 Scripting › Budgets safepoints and the watchdog › Abort-class errors and script pcall | The budget error, the snapshot-cap error, the yield-in-system error and the checkpoint error are raised as a Zenith abort value (a tagged light userdata carrying the kind), which the Zenith `pcall` and `xpcall` re-raise, so they always reach the protected entry and roll back the entity transaction. |
| Z2-N-SCRIPT-307 | `Zenith2/Docs/Scripting.md`:1195 | Zenith2 Scripting › Budgets safepoints and the watchdog › Abort-class errors and script pcall | After a budget abort has been raised, the interrupt callback raises it again at every further safepoint of the same entity transaction, so a script that somehow resumes execution cannot run past its budget. |
| Z2-N-SCRIPT-308 | `Zenith2/Docs/Scripting.md`:1201 | Zenith2 Scripting › Budgets safepoints and the watchdog › Native codegen conformance | D-SCRIPT-TXN refinement (open, Stage 1) includes the conformance test `Test_Script_Budget_NativeSafepointParity`, which runs every module of the budget adversarial corpus (`Zenith2/Engine/ZenithScript/Tests/Corpus/Budget/`: tight numeric loops, nested and generic `for` loops, `while` and `repeat` with `break`, deep recursion, vararg calls, fastcall built-ins, string and table library heavy code, closures created per call, metamethod-driven code) and of the representative corpus (`Zenith2/Engine/ZenithScript/Tests/Corpus/Representative/`) interpreted and native, over the same inputs, and asserts that each entity transaction's safepoint count and committed results match exactly. The test is staged: the Stage 1 D-SCRIPT-TXN ruling runs the budget corpus at VM level (each module's invocations as the transaction unit, with no world) on Windows x64 and the S24 and fixes `Zenith_ScriptNativePolicy` per platform from that run; the representative corpus, run per entity transaction, joins in Stage 3 as the extended test of the budget-scope ticket, once the world runtime exists; and no platform's `script.nativePolicy` is recorded `all` until that Stage 3 run passes on the platform (a Stage 1 policy is `disabled` or `toolsOnly`). |
| Z2-N-SCRIPT-309 | `Zenith2/Docs/Scripting.md`:1202 | Zenith2 Scripting › Budgets safepoints and the watchdog › Native codegen conformance | If the parity test fails on a platform, native codegen is disabled for budget-limited simulation invocations on that platform (`Zenith_ScriptNativePolicy` = `SCRIPT_NATIVE_POLICY_TOOLS_ONLY`), and the policy is recorded in the Luau entry of `ThirdParty/manifest.json`; editor and tool invocations without a budget may still use native code. |
| Z2-N-SCRIPT-310 | `Zenith2/Docs/Scripting.md`:1203 | Zenith2 Scripting › Budgets safepoints and the watchdog › Native codegen conformance | The parity test runs on Windows x64 and on the S24 (arm64) for every Luau pin change; a pin change that turns it red lands only together with the policy change. |
| Z2-N-SCRIPT-311 | `Zenith2/Docs/Scripting.md`:1226 | Zenith2 Scripting › Budgets safepoints and the watchdog › Wall-clock watchdog | Wall-clock time is only a watchdog: tripping it is a fatal, non-replayable diagnostic event, never a recoverable rollback, and it never changes a safepoint count. |
| Z2-N-SCRIPT-312 | `Zenith2/Docs/Scripting.md`:1227 | Zenith2 Scripting › Budgets safepoints and the watchdog › Wall-clock watchdog | The watchdog's limit is the script watchdog row, set well above the time the largest per-entity safepoint budget can take on the S24, so a watchdog trip indicates a stuck binding or an engine defect, not a slow script. |
| Z2-N-SCRIPT-313 | `Zenith2/Docs/Scripting.md`:1231 | Zenith2 Scripting › Budgets safepoints and the watchdog › Snapshot caps | Snapshot memory has a hard per-entity cap (the per-entity snapshot cap row), enforced as a `SNAPSHOT_CAP` Luau error, and a per-tick total (the per-tick snapshot total row), measured and reported. |
| Z2-N-SCRIPT-314 | `Zenith2/Docs/Scripting.md`:1235 | Zenith2 Scripting › Budgets safepoints and the watchdog › Checkpoint cost | Behaviour checkpoint serialization cost is a budget row (the checkpoint cost row: bytes serialized per tick and main-thread time per tick for checkpoints), measured in every workload run; the reserved checkpoint slot size per instance is bounded by the checkpoint slot size row. |
| Z2-N-SCRIPT-315 | `Zenith2/Docs/Scripting.md`:1224 | Zenith2 Scripting › Budgets safepoints and the watchdog › Wall-clock watchdog | A debugger stop exempts the stopped VM alone from the watchdog: the VM's own thread sets `m_bDebuggerStopped` (release) before it blocks, and on resuming adds the stop's duration to its published start time (when an invocation is running) and to `m_ulWatchdogRebaseTicks` before it clears the flag (release); the watchdog has no world-level suspension, and the poll decides a trip on a start time re-read after it saw the flag clear, so every elapsed time a trip or a hang report acts on is running time (`Zenith2/Docs/Design/SCRIPT/Runtime.md` → "Watchdog internals"). |
| Z2-N-SCRIPT-331 | `Zenith2/Docs/Scripting.md`:1280 | Zenith2 Scripting › Merge-time capacity and asynchronous results › Capacity classes | Shared capacity (entity IDs, command and event queues, container storage, the intern table, triggers, save and async requests) is checked at the merge point in merge order, never reserved first-come during parallel execution. |
| Z2-N-SCRIPT-332 | `Zenith2/Docs/Scripting.md`:1281 | Zenith2 Scripting › Merge-time capacity and asynchronous results › Capacity classes | An entity whose side effects exceed the remaining capacity of any class at its merge is rolled back deterministically as an entity-level failure with a `SCRIPT_MERGE_REJECTED_CAPACITY` diagnostic naming the class; commit still cannot fail, because acceptance happens before commit. |
| Z2-N-SCRIPT-333 | `Zenith2/Docs/Scripting.md`:1282 | Zenith2 Scripting › Merge-time capacity and asynchronous results › Capacity classes | A rejected entity's demand is not subtracted, so a subsequent entity in merge order with a smaller demand can still be accepted; acceptance is a pure function of the merge-ordered demands and the phase's starting capacity. (First-fit acceptance is confirmed by D-SCRIPT-TXN refinement, open, Stage 1.) |
| Z2-N-SCRIPT-334 | `Zenith2/Docs/Scripting.md`:1298 | Zenith2 Scripting › Merge-time capacity and asynchronous results › Asynchronous requests and completion IDs | Completion IDs are assigned at commit in merge order from a per-world monotonic counter, so the completion ID of every request is independent of worker count and completion timing. |
| Z2-N-SCRIPT-335 | `Zenith2/Docs/Scripting.md`:1339 | Zenith2 Scripting › Merge-time capacity and asynchronous results › Tick-boundary publication | Asynchronous results (pathfinding, streaming loads, physics queries, online calls) become visible only at a tick boundary of a world that is not paused, in completion-ID order (Z2-D-053); a result completing while its world is paused publishes at the first tick boundary after the world resumes. |
| Z2-N-SCRIPT-336 | `Zenith2/Docs/Scripting.md`:1384 | Zenith2 Scripting › Merge-time capacity and asynchronous results › Replay-log recording | The tick at which each asynchronous result was published is written to the replay log, and replay publishes it at the same tick; recomputable results (pathfinding, physics queries, successful loads of cooked content) are recomputed and held until their recorded tick, and non-reproducible results (online calls, I/O failures) are replayed from the recorded payload. |
| Z2-N-SCRIPT-337 | `Zenith2/Docs/Scripting.md`:1385 | Zenith2 Scripting › Merge-time capacity and asynchronous results › Replay-log recording | In a replay, a recomputed result that is not ready by its recorded publication tick holds the tick until it is ready, so the replayed simulation never observes a different publication tick. |
| Z2-N-SCRIPT-338 | `Zenith2/Docs/Scripting.md`:1386 | Zenith2 Scripting › Merge-time capacity and asynchronous results › Replay-log recording | Publication ticks are simulation inputs: two live runs whose asynchronous work completes at different wall-clock moments may diverge, and replaying either run's log reproduces that run exactly. |
| Z2-N-SCRIPT-339 | `Zenith2/Docs/Scripting.md`:1299 | Zenith2 Scripting › Merge-time capacity and asynchronous results › Asynchronous requests and completion IDs | A `Zenith.AsyncTicket` returned during an invocation holds a provisional ticket index; at commit it is remapped to the request's completion ID through the same one-tick remap table as provisional spawn handles, and a ticket still provisional after that window raises ZS1105. |
| Z2-N-SCRIPT-340 | `Zenith2/Docs/Scripting.md`:1300 | Zenith2 Scripting › Merge-time capacity and asynchronous results › Asynchronous requests and completion IDs | `Zenith.AsyncTicket` is not a serializable state kind: a coroutine that held a ticket across a yield and is restored by `OnRecover` re-issues its request. |
| Z2-N-SCRIPT-341 | `Zenith2/Docs/Scripting.md`:1340 | Zenith2 Scripting › Merge-time capacity and asynchronous results › Tick-boundary publication | The world's pending-result set and its monotonic completion-ID counter are owned by `Zenith_AsyncResultTable` (ZenithScript, public header `Zenith_ScriptAsync.h`, `Scripting/PublicTypes.md` → "Public types"), one per `Zenith_ScriptWorld`, which exists for every world, including one without script modules: producers declare their source kinds (`DeclareSource`), handlers (`SetHandler`) and resolvers (`SetPublicationResolver`) before the first tick, `AllocateCompletionID` runs on the world's merge chain or on the main thread in the frame prologue, an allocation outside a tick (the frame prologue) is made only for a RECORDED publication kind, and under `--replay` a prologue producer of a recorded kind that needs the recorded completion ID (the world host's transition intake) reads it from the replay log and allocates nothing while the table adopts, at each tick boundary, the next completion ID recorded in the `REPLAY_RECORD_TICK` record (a live counter above it being a replay divergence that halts the world, [Z2-N-ARCH-794]), `Complete` on any thread, and `GetNextCompletionID` / `RestoreNextCompletionID` save and restore the counter with the world's state; the replay recorder observes publications through `Zenith_ScriptPublicationObserver`, the replay reader supplies them through `Zenith_ScriptPublicationSource`, and under `--replay` `Zenith_ScriptRuntime::CanRunTick` holds a tick until every recomputed result recorded for it has completed. |
| Z2-N-SCRIPT-342 | `Zenith2/Docs/Scripting.md`:1342 | Zenith2 Scripting › Merge-time capacity and asynchronous results › Tick-boundary publication | A published script result stays in the table until an invocation that read it (`ticket:Result()` or a `Zenith.WaitAsync` resume) commits, after which it is released at the next tick boundary, or until its owning instance is destroyed or quarantined; its in-flight unit of Z2-B-SCRIPT-026 returns at that release, not at publication, and a result no behaviour reads is traced by the cumulative counter `script.async.published_unconsumed.<kind>` and, when it blocks a request, by `SCRIPT_ASYNC_CAPACITY_HELD` (`Scripting/MisuseCatalogue.md` → "Catalogue"). |
| Z2-N-SCRIPT-343 | `Zenith2/Docs/Scripting.md`:1382 | Zenith2 Scripting › Merge-time capacity and asynchronous results › Replay-log recording | A source kind whose results C++ consumes (`ASYNC_SOURCE_PLATFORM`, `ASYNC_SOURCE_SCENE_LOAD`, `ASYNC_SOURCE_UI`, `ASYNC_SOURCE_ONLINE`) registers exactly one `Zenith_AsyncPublicationHandler` per world with `Zenith_AsyncResultTable::SetHandler` before the first tick; the handler runs at tick-boundary step 1, writes world state only through the tick boundary's `Zenith_TickBoundaryAccess` (property reads and writes, never structure), and records nothing a script sees before the tick's phases run. |
| Z2-N-SCRIPT-351 | `Zenith2/Docs/Scripting.md`:1392 | Zenith2 Scripting › Stateless system enforcement › The rule | Chunk-system modules, and every library module they require, hold no state: nothing a chunk-system invocation does can be observed by a subsequent invocation except through committed component data, events and commands. |
| Z2-N-SCRIPT-352 | `Zenith2/Docs/Scripting.md`:1393 | Zenith2 Scripting › Stateless system enforcement › The rule | The rule is enforced at load time by the mechanism D-LUAU-STATELESS selects; a module that violates it is rejected when it is loaded, never merely caught by a test or at runtime. |
| Z2-N-SCRIPT-353 | `Zenith2/Docs/Scripting.md`:1406 | Zenith2 Scripting › Stateless system enforcement › Decision D-LUAU-STATELESS | The D-LUAU-STATELESS ruling is appended to `Zenith2/Docs/DecisionLog.md` with an explicit `Z2-D-NNN` in the ruling ticket's own diff, together with the measurements of `Scripting/StatelessCorpus.md` → "Alternative evaluation procedure". |
| Z2-N-SCRIPT-354 | `Zenith2/Docs/Scripting.md`:1407 | Zenith2 Scripting › Stateless system enforcement › Decision D-LUAU-STATELESS | Whichever alternative D-LUAU-STATELESS selects, the enforcement covers captured locals, nested tables, imported modules, mutable userdata and stateful library functions, and each of those five has at least one adversarial corpus module. |
| Z2-N-SCRIPT-355 | `Zenith2/Docs/Scripting.md`:1408 | Zenith2 Scripting › Stateless system enforcement › Decision D-LUAU-STATELESS | Stateful library functions (`math.random`, `math.randomseed`, the `os` library and similar) are removed from the system environment regardless of the alternative selected. |
| Z2-N-SCRIPT-356 | `Zenith2/Docs/Scripting.md`:1449 | Zenith2 Scripting › Stateless system enforcement › Load-time interface | ZenithCook runs `Zenith_ScriptCheckStateless` on every chunk-system module, every behaviour module and every library module they require, and a rejection fails the cook with the diagnostic, line and detail; every hot-reload producer (ZenithEditor's in-process compile, ZenithCook's file-watch re-cook) runs it again on changed source before any bytecode reaches `Zenith_ScriptWorld::RequestHotReload`, which refuses bytecode whose `.zlbc` flags lack the stateless-verified bit. |
| Z2-N-SCRIPT-357 | `Zenith2/Docs/Scripting.md`:1450 | Zenith2 Scripting › Stateless system enforcement › Load-time interface | A library module is accepted for use by systems only if it passes the check as a library; a system that requires a rejected library is rejected with `SCRIPT_STATELESS_IMPORTED_STATE` naming the library. |
| Z2-N-SCRIPT-358 | `Zenith2/Docs/Scripting.md`:1454 | Zenith2 Scripting › Stateless system enforcement › Acceptance | D-LUAU-STATELESS is accepted when every module of the adversarial corpus (`Scripting/StatelessCorpus.md` → "Adversarial corpus") is rejected at load time with its expected diagnostic, and every module of the accepted corpus (`Scripting/StatelessCorpus.md` → "Accepted corpus") loads; `Test_Script_Stateless_AdversarialCorpusRejectedAtLoad` and `Test_Script_Stateless_AcceptedCorpusLoads` assert both. |
| Z2-N-SCRIPT-359 | `Zenith2/Docs/Scripting.md`:1484 | Zenith2 Scripting › Stateless system enforcement › Corpus structure | The adversarial corpus holds at least one module for each of the 21 patterns in the table "Corpus structure", and the corpus grows (never shrinks) when a new bypass is found. |
| Z2-N-SCRIPT-360 | `Zenith2/Docs/Scripting.md`:1488 | Zenith2 Scripting › Stateless system enforcement › Behaviour module extension | Behaviours cannot share mutable tables: behaviour modules are checked by the same mechanism for module-scope state, and cross-instance data goes through components, events or entity handles. |
| Z2-N-SCRIPT-361 | `Zenith2/Docs/Scripting.md`:1489 | Zenith2 Scripting › Stateless system enforcement › Behaviour module extension | Behaviour per-instance data lives in the declared `State` table alone; module-scope values of a behaviour module are constants (literals, deep-frozen tables, functions and accepted `require` results). |
| Z2-N-SCRIPT-381 | `Zenith2/Docs/Scripting.md`:1517 | Zenith2 Scripting › Behaviour state model and recovery › Declared state schema | Serializable behaviour state is only the behaviour's declared state table: reflected fields of the kinds in the table "Declared state schema" (primitives, strings, persistent IDs, entity and asset handles, and nested tables and bounded arrays and maps of the same). |
| Z2-N-SCRIPT-382 | `Zenith2/Docs/Scripting.md`:1518 | Zenith2 Scripting › Behaviour state model and recovery › Declared state schema | The state schema is registered with ZenithReflect as a runtime type `Behaviour.<Name>.State` of kind `TYPE_KIND_BEHAVIOUR_STATE` (`Origin(TYPE_ORIGIN_SCHEMA)`) with stable field IDs and the declared `StateVersion`, at startup step 10, before the registry freezes, from the state-schema records of the cooked script manifest `content:/Scripts/scripts.zlmf` (`Zenith_ScriptRegisterBehaviourStateTypes(Zenith_TypeRegistry&, const Zenith_ScriptModuleManifest&)`, in module-ID order, after the game's `schemas.zreg`; the live declaration's shape is compared with the record at world load, ZS1806 on a mismatch), and afterwards only inside the hot-reload window, so it is serialized, migrated and shown in the inspector like any reflected type (`Zenith2/Docs/Architecture.md` → "Reflection and runtime type registration"). |
| Z2-N-SCRIPT-383 | `Zenith2/Docs/Scripting.md`:1519 | Zenith2 Scripting › Behaviour state model and recovery › Declared state schema | A field ID is never reused within a behaviour's schema history; reusing one is a load error. |
| Z2-N-SCRIPT-384 | `Zenith2/Docs/Scripting.md`:1523 | Zenith2 Scripting › Behaviour state model and recovery › Load errors | Declaring a state field whose default or spec is a closure, a coroutine, a userdata other than the Zenith handle and value types, or a table without a declared schema is a `SCRIPT_ERROR_KIND_LOAD` error naming the field; closures, coroutines and userdata other than handles are not serializable. |
| Z2-N-SCRIPT-385 | `Zenith2/Docs/Scripting.md`:1524 | Zenith2 Scripting › Behaviour state model and recovery › Load errors | A schema whose maximum serialized size (computed from the declared maxima) exceeds the checkpoint slot size row is a load error; the maximum is computed at load, so serialization never needs an unbounded buffer. |
| Z2-N-SCRIPT-386 | `Zenith2/Docs/Scripting.md`:1528 | Zenith2 Scripting › Behaviour state model and recovery › State proxy and dirty tracking | The `state` value passed to callbacks is a proxy whose `__newindex` validates every write against the declared schema (kind, range, maximum length or count) and raises the catalogued misuse error on violation, so an unserializable value can never be stored. |
| Z2-N-SCRIPT-387 | `Zenith2/Docs/Scripting.md`:1529 | Zenith2 Scripting › Behaviour state model and recovery › State proxy and dirty tracking | Every accepted write sets the instance's dirty flag; the flag is cleared when a checkpoint commits. |
| Z2-N-SCRIPT-388 | `Zenith2/Docs/Scripting.md`:1530 | Zenith2 Scripting › Behaviour state model and recovery › State proxy and dirty tracking | Assigning a table to a `Table`, `Array` or `Map` field copies its contents into the proxy's storage (value semantics), so no two instances ever alias one Luau table through state. |
| Z2-N-SCRIPT-389 | `Zenith2/Docs/Scripting.md`:1541 | Zenith2 Scripting › Behaviour state model and recovery › Checkpoint rule | The checkpoint is serialized into reserved memory inside the transaction, before commit, only when the declared state is dirty, and at most once per tick per instance: at the instance's last invocation in the tick, which ends in a return or a yield. |
| Z2-N-SCRIPT-390 | `Zenith2/Docs/Scripting.md`:1542 | Zenith2 Scripting › Behaviour state model and recovery › Checkpoint rule | A checkpoint serialization failure is a Luau error, so the entity's transaction rolls back; commit then swaps the checkpoint in, which cannot fail. |
| Z2-N-SCRIPT-391 | `Zenith2/Docs/Scripting.md`:1543 | Zenith2 Scripting › Behaviour state model and recovery › Checkpoint rule | Each behaviour instance owns two checkpoint slots reserved at instance creation, each of the schema's maximum serialized size; the active slot always holds the last successfully committed state. |
| Z2-N-SCRIPT-392 | `Zenith2/Docs/Scripting.md`:1561 | Zenith2 Scripting › Behaviour state model and recovery › Checkpoint format | A checkpoint records at most `uSCRIPT_MAX_CHECKPOINT_FIXUPS` (16) provisional handles; a state holding more provisional handles at the checkpoint point fails serialization with `SCRIPT_ERROR_KIND_CHECKPOINT`, as does a payload (declared fields plus preserved unknown fields) larger than the slot. |
| Z2-N-SCRIPT-393 | `Zenith2/Docs/Scripting.md`:1572 | Zenith2 Scripting › Behaviour state model and recovery › Restore algorithm | Restore never deserializes a Luau function, coroutine or execution position; the instance's coroutine, if any, is created fresh by `OnRecover`. |
| Z2-N-SCRIPT-394 | `Zenith2/Docs/Scripting.md`:1576 | Zenith2 Scripting › Behaviour state model and recovery › OnRecover | `OnRecover(self, state, ctx)` runs only for an instance whose behaviour declares it (exactly the behaviours that declare `Run`, [Z2-N-SCRIPT-141]), on the main VM in the instance's declared phase of the first tick after recovery, before any other callback of the instance in that tick, as its own entity transaction under the recovery safepoint row and the recovery ordinal of [Z2-N-SCRIPT-928]. |
| Z2-N-SCRIPT-395 | `Zenith2/Docs/Scripting.md`:1578 | Zenith2 Scripting › Behaviour state model and recovery › OnRecover | `OnRecover` returns the function to start as the instance's new `Run` coroutine, or `nil` to start `Run` itself; the coroutine restarts its logic from the phase recorded in the declared state (Z2-D-023). |
| Z2-N-SCRIPT-396 | `Zenith2/Docs/Scripting.md`:1579 | Zenith2 Scripting › Behaviour state model and recovery › OnRecover | A failure inside `OnRecover` rolls back its transaction and leaves the instance quarantined with its restored checkpoint intact. |
| Z2-N-SCRIPT-397 | `Zenith2/Docs/Scripting.md`:1584 | Zenith2 Scripting › Behaviour state model and recovery › Stale references | On restore, entity and asset references are resolved through persistent IDs (and, for entities without one, stable simulation IDs within the same world session); anything that cannot be resolved becomes `nil`, and a `SCRIPT_STATE_REFERENCE_UNRESOLVED` diagnostic naming the instance, the field and the unresolved ID is logged. |
| Z2-N-SCRIPT-398 | `Zenith2/Docs/Scripting.md`:1588 | Zenith2 Scripting › Behaviour state model and recovery › No shared mutable tables | No Luau table is ever shared mutably between behaviour instances: state has value semantics, `self` is not extensible, and module scope is constant (see "Behaviour module extension"). |
| Z2-N-SCRIPT-399 | `Zenith2/Docs/Scripting.md`:1592 | Zenith2 Scripting › Behaviour state model and recovery › State migration chain | `Migrate[n]` receives a plain Luau table decoded from a version-n checkpoint and returns a table valid for version n+1; the chain runs every step from the checkpoint's version to the module's `StateVersion`, each under the recovery safepoint row, and the result is validated against the new schema before it is accepted. |
| Z2-N-SCRIPT-400 | `Zenith2/Docs/Scripting.md`:1593 | Zenith2 Scripting › Behaviour state model and recovery › State migration chain | Every `Migrate` step has a migration fixture per version under the game's `Scripts/Tests/Migration/<Behaviour>/v<n>.zbst`, and the fixture test loads each fixture with the current module; the content cook skips everything below the `Tests/` directory of the `Scripts` content root, so the fixtures are never cooked or packaged (`Zenith2/Docs/Architecture/BuildSystem.md` [Z2-N-ARCH-246]). |
| Z2-N-SCRIPT-421 | `Zenith2/Docs/Scripting.md`:1606 | Zenith2 Scripting › Quarantine and hot reload › Quarantine semantics | A failing behaviour instance is quarantined: its Luau heap state is discarded, and it is restored on reload from its last successfully committed serialized state, using the same mechanism as hot-reload migration. |
| Z2-N-SCRIPT-422 | `Zenith2/Docs/Scripting.md`:1607 | Zenith2 Scripting › Quarantine and hot reload › Quarantine semantics | A failing chunk system has no heap state to quarantine; it is disabled for the world from the next tick boundary until it is reloaded. |
| Z2-N-SCRIPT-423 | `Zenith2/Docs/Scripting.md`:1620 | Zenith2 Scripting › Quarantine and hot reload › Recovery policy | The recovery policy is set per game in `game.json` (`"scriptRecovery": { "policy": "automatic", "delayTicks": 60, "maxRecoveriesPerModule": 3 }`; the `game.json` schema is `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture.md` → "Engine modules and game projects"); the automatic policy reloads a disabled module at a tick boundary with the last-good restore below, and after `maxRecoveriesPerModule` recoveries of one module in one session the module stays disabled for the session with a diagnostic. |
| Z2-N-SCRIPT-424 | `Zenith2/Docs/Scripting.md`:1628 | Zenith2 Scripting › Quarantine and hot reload › Last-good restore | Last-good restore uses the instance's active checkpoint slot, which by the checkpoint rule is its last successfully committed state; state from a failed or rejected transaction never reaches it. |
| Z2-N-SCRIPT-425 | `Zenith2/Docs/Scripting.md`:1639 | Zenith2 Scripting › Quarantine and hot reload › Hot reload of behaviour modules | Luau hot reload migrates state, with rollback on error: a reload either succeeds for every instance of the module or leaves the old module and every instance exactly as it was. |
| Z2-N-SCRIPT-426 | `Zenith2/Docs/Scripting.md`:1640 | Zenith2 Scripting › Quarantine and hot reload › Hot reload of behaviour modules | A hot reload is applied at a tick boundary and never mid-tick. |
| Z2-N-SCRIPT-427 | `Zenith2/Docs/Scripting.md`:1642 | Zenith2 Scripting › Quarantine and hot reload › Hot reload of behaviour modules | Hot-reload latency from save to running code is measured against the script hot-reload row of the W-Editor targets. |
| Z2-N-SCRIPT-428 | `Zenith2/Docs/Scripting.md`:1651 | Zenith2 Scripting › Quarantine and hot reload › Hot reload of chunk-system modules | A chunk-system reload that fails leaves the previous module generation in use on every VM. |
| Z2-N-SCRIPT-429 | `Zenith2/Docs/Scripting.md`:1655 | Zenith2 Scripting › Quarantine and hot reload › Disable-from-next-tick rule | Every failure reported during a tick (execution-time failure, checkpoint failure, `OnRecover` failure) disables its module from the next tick boundary; a reload in the same tick boundary re-enables it only if the reload succeeds. |
| Z2-N-SCRIPT-430 | `Zenith2/Docs/Scripting.md`:1641 | Zenith2 Scripting › Quarantine and hot reload › Hot reload of behaviour modules | During play, a hot reload changes only `TYPE_KIND_BEHAVIOUR_STATE` types, inside the registry's hot-reload window in the frame prologue; a data-defined component schema changes only in the editor between play sessions, never in a running play world (`Zenith2/Docs/Architecture.md` → "Reflection and runtime type registration"). |
| Z2-N-SCRIPT-441 | `Zenith2/Docs/Scripting.md`:1681 | Zenith2 Scripting › Views epochs and misuse errors › View identity | A view (row proxy, component view, container object, invocation context) is valid only during the invocation that created it; each view is scoped to its invocation ID and becomes invalid when that invocation ends. |
| Z2-N-SCRIPT-442 | `Zenith2/Docs/Scripting.md`:1682 | Zenith2 Scripting › Views epochs and misuse errors › View identity | Views never survive a yield: a behaviour view records its coroutine segment and is invalid in every subsequent segment. |
| Z2-N-SCRIPT-443 | `Zenith2/Docs/Scripting.md`:1683 | Zenith2 Scripting › Views epochs and misuse errors › View identity | Retained or stale views raise a Luau error in all builds, Debug, Release and shipping alike, detected by comparing the view's invocation ID and segment with the VM's current ones and its structural epoch with the chunk's. |
| Z2-N-SCRIPT-444 | `Zenith2/Docs/Scripting.md`:1684 | Zenith2 Scripting › Views epochs and misuse errors › View identity | Strings (interned IDs), entity handles and asset handles are read-only in chunk views and changed through commands; container fields are not exposed in chunk views. |
| Z2-N-SCRIPT-445 | `Zenith2/Docs/Scripting.md`:1697 | Zenith2 Scripting › Views epochs and misuse errors › Misuse error format | Every script misuse raises a controlled Luau error of kind `SCRIPT_ERROR_KIND_MISUSE` (or the abort kind named in the catalogue) and never an assert, a crash or undefined behaviour (Z2-D-045). |
| Z2-N-SCRIPT-446 | `Zenith2/Docs/Scripting.md`:1698 | Zenith2 Scripting › Views epochs and misuse errors › Misuse error format | A misuse error's message is exactly `ZS<code>: <text>` with the text from `Scripting/MisuseCatalogue.md` → "Catalogue", after Luau's usual `<chunkname>:<line>: ` position prefix; placeholders in angle brackets are substituted and nothing else varies. |
| Z2-N-SCRIPT-447 | `Zenith2/Docs/Scripting.md`:1699 | Zenith2 Scripting › Views epochs and misuse errors › Misuse error format | Catalogue codes are stable: a code is never reused for a different misuse, and a changed message text keeps its code. |
| Z2-N-SCRIPT-461 | `Zenith2/Docs/Scripting.md`:1732 | Zenith2 Scripting › Debugging and profiling › DAP server design | The DAP server is constructed only in configurations other than `Shipping` and `ShippingTest` (`if constexpr (!Zenith_IsShippingBuild())` in the composition root) and binds the loopback interface alone; libgraph's link-surface check asserts that no `Zenith_ScriptDap` symbol is present in a game's Shipping link closure. |
| Z2-N-SCRIPT-462 | `Zenith2/Docs/Scripting.md`:1735 | Zenith2 Scripting › Debugging and profiling › DAP server design | A debugger stop suspends the world's script invocations (the stopped VM, and every other script node body at the world's debugger gate) and exempts the stopped VM from the watchdog, and never changes a safepoint count or any committed value, so a replay debugged step by step produces the same world-state hashes as an undebugged run. |
| Z2-N-SCRIPT-463 | `Zenith2/Docs/Scripting.md`:1747 | Zenith2 Scripting › Debugging and profiling › Decision D-LUAUDBG | The D-LUAUDBG ruling is appended to `Zenith2/Docs/DecisionLog.md` with an explicit `Z2-D-NNN` in the ruling ticket's own diff and records the selected client, the supported DAP requests (at minimum `setBreakpoints`, `configurationDone`, `threads`, `stackTrace`, `scopes`, `variables`, `continue`, `next`, `stepIn`, `stepOut`, `pause`, `evaluate` restricted to reading locals), and the attach workflow on Windows and the S24 (over `adb forward tcp:27851 tcp:27851`). |
| Z2-N-SCRIPT-464 | `Zenith2/Docs/Scripting.md`:1751 | Zenith2 Scripting › Debugging and profiling › Profiling zones | Every script node emits a CPU frame-graph zone, and every invocation emits a nested zone named `<Module>` with the entity count (chunk systems) or the stable simulation ID (behaviours), feeding the on-screen profiler and Tracy (Z2-D-005). |
| Z2-N-SCRIPT-465 | `Zenith2/Docs/Scripting.md`:1752 | Zenith2 Scripting › Debugging and profiling › Profiling zones | Merge points, checkpoint serialization, restores, hot reloads and GC steps emit their own zones (`Script.Merge`, `Script.Checkpoint`, `Script.Restore`, `Script.HotReload`, `Script.GC`). |
| Z2-N-SCRIPT-466 | `Zenith2/Docs/Scripting.md`:1762 | Zenith2 Scripting › Debugging and profiling › On-screen profiler rows | The on-screen profiler shows the Luau time, Luau GC and script counter rows in the game and the editor on Windows and the S24, behind the runtime toggle, as `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture.md` → "CPU frame graph and scheduler" specifies for every node. |
| Z2-N-SCRIPT-467 | `Zenith2/Docs/Scripting.md`:1766 | Zenith2 Scripting › Debugging and profiling › Sampling profiler | In development builds, a script sampling profiler can be enabled at runtime: every 997th safepoint of a VM records the Luau call stack (`lua_getinfo` up to 32 frames) into a per-VM ring of 65,536 samples, dumped as a flame-graph JSON on request; sampling never changes safepoint counts or results. |
| Z2-N-SCRIPT-468 | `Zenith2/Docs/Scripting.md`:1734 | Zenith2 Scripting › Debugging and profiling › DAP server design | The configuration test is `Zenith_IsShippingBuild()` from `ZenithCore/Zenith_BuildInfo.h`, the build system's `constexpr` reader of the configuration defines: `true` in the `Shipping` configuration (build presets `win-shipping`, `android-arm64-shipping`) and in the `ShippingTest` configuration that builds test targets with Shipping's code (build and test preset `win-shipping-test`, `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture/BuildSystem.md` [Z2-N-ARCH-231]), `false` in every other configuration and preset (`Zenith2/Docs/Architecture.md` → "Build system and presets"); it changes no layout (`Zenith2/Docs/Contracts.md` → "No layout-changing conditionals"). |
| Z2-N-SCRIPT-469 | `Zenith2/Docs/Scripting.md`:1730 | Zenith2 Scripting › Debugging and profiling › DAP server design | A debugger stop blocks a thread inside a job body only under the development-build exemption of `Zenith2/Docs/Contracts.md` → "Threading and concurrency contract" ([Z2-N-CONTRACT-365]): the stopping or parking thread reads `Zenith_IsJobWorkerThread()`, calls `Zenith_MarkThreadAsJobWorker(false)`, acquires `uLOCK_LEVEL_SCRIPT_DAP` only inside that bracket, and once released reports its wait once as `"debugger-stop"` through `Zenith_DispatchBlockingWait`, before it restores the flag it read; the protocol, its deadlock-freedom argument and its wake-up rules are `Zenith2/Docs/Design/SCRIPT/Debugger.md` → "Stop protocol", and no Shipping build contains the bracket ([Z2-N-SCRIPT-461]). |
| Z2-N-SCRIPT-481 | `Zenith2/Docs/Scripting.md`:1776 | Zenith2 Scripting › Sandboxing and security › Trust boundary | The script trust boundary is recorded in `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture.md` → "Security architecture" alongside the asset, remote-tool and update boundaries; this section is the script side of it. |
| Z2-N-SCRIPT-482 | `Zenith2/Docs/Scripting.md`:1780 | Zenith2 Scripting › Sandboxing and security › What a script can never reach | A script can never reach the filesystem, processes, environment variables, sockets, dynamic libraries, raw memory addresses, another world's data, another VM's heap, the engine's `Internal/` state, or the debugger; no binding exposes any of them, and the environments contain no `io`, `os`, `package`, `load`, `loadstring`, `dofile`, `loadfile`, `getfenv`, `setfenv` or `debug` beyond `debug.traceback` and `debug.info`. |
| Z2-N-SCRIPT-483 | `Zenith2/Docs/Scripting.md`:1781 | Zenith2 Scripting › Sandboxing and security › What a script can never reach | A script can never load code: bytecode reaches a VM only through the module loader from the cooked package (shipping) or through `Zenith_ScriptCompileModule` from the game's `Scripts/` tree (development), and there is no binding that compiles or loads a string. |
| Z2-N-SCRIPT-484 | `Zenith2/Docs/Scripting.md`:1782 | Zenith2 Scripting › Sandboxing and security › What a script can never reach | A script can never exhaust the process: safepoint budgets, the per-entity snapshot cap, the VM memory caps and the watchdog bound its CPU and memory, and the protected-call depth limit bounds its C stack. |
| Z2-N-SCRIPT-485 | `Zenith2/Docs/Scripting.md`:1786 | Zenith2 Scripting › Sandboxing and security › Untrusted inputs | Behaviour checkpoints read from saves and replays are untrusted input: the decoder bounds-checks every length and count against the declared schema before reading, rejects a mismatched magic, version or source ID, and never allocates more than the checkpoint slot size; it is fuzzed by `Fuzz_Script_CheckpointDecode` under `win-asan` with libFuzzer. |
| Z2-N-SCRIPT-486 | `Zenith2/Docs/Scripting.md`:1787 | Zenith2 Scripting › Sandboxing and security › Untrusted inputs | Luau bytecode is not verified by Luau's loader, so bytecode is accepted only from package content whose integrity the asset system has verified (the cooked-content hashes and, for updates, the authenticated update manifest); saves and replays never contain bytecode. |
| Z2-N-SCRIPT-487 | `Zenith2/Docs/Scripting.md`:1788 | Zenith2 Scripting › Sandboxing and security › Untrusted inputs | Hot-reload and cook-time compilation of source enforce parser limits: at most 1 MiB of source per module, 200 levels of syntactic nesting (Luau's `LUAI_MAXCCALLS`-equivalent recursion limit), and 8,192 modules per game; exceeding one is a `SCRIPT_ERROR_KIND_COMPILE` error. |
| Z2-N-SCRIPT-488 | `Zenith2/Docs/Scripting.md`:1792 | Zenith2 Scripting › Sandboxing and security › Shipping exposure | Shipping builds contain no DAP server, no script console, no sampling profiler endpoint and no source compiler: no Player links `ZenithScriptCompiler`, and the `Libgraph` step asserts that a game's Shipping link closure contains neither that target, `Luau.Compiler`, `Luau.Ast` nor any `Zenith_ScriptDap` symbol. |
| Z2-N-SCRIPT-501 | `Zenith2/Docs/Scripting.md`:1798 | Zenith2 Scripting › Shipping bytecode compatibility › Pinning | The Luau revision is pinned in `ThirdParty/manifest.json` by commit hash and release tag, together with its recorded API linkage, its native policy and the bytecode version it emits (`LBC_VERSION_TARGET`) and accepts (`LBC_VERSION_MIN`..`LBC_VERSION_MAX`). |
| Z2-N-SCRIPT-502 | `Zenith2/Docs/Scripting.md`:1799 | Zenith2 Scripting › Shipping bytecode compatibility › Pinning | A Luau pin change is a dependency upgrade (Z2-D-082) that re-runs the linkage test, the native parity test, the D-LUAU-STATELESS corpus and the determinism matrix before it lands. |
| Z2-N-SCRIPT-503 | `Zenith2/Docs/Scripting.md`:1819 | Zenith2 Scripting › Shipping bytecode compatibility › Cooked bytecode format | Cooked scripts are versioned binary data independent of C++ layout (Z2-D-038), compiled with the options in the table "Cooked bytecode format" for every configuration, so the safepoint counts measured in development equal those in shipping. |
| Z2-N-SCRIPT-504 | `Zenith2/Docs/Scripting.md`:1823 | Zenith2 Scripting › Shipping bytecode compatibility › Load-time verification | The runtime refuses a `.zlbc` whose magic, container version, Luau pin hash, engine version hash or binding-set hash differs from the running build's, or whose Luau bytecode version lies outside the pinned accepted range, with a `SCRIPT_ERROR_KIND_LOAD` error naming the module and the mismatched field; it never attempts to run mismatched bytecode. |
| Z2-N-SCRIPT-505 | `Zenith2/Docs/Scripting.md`:1827 | Zenith2 Scripting › Shipping bytecode compatibility › Cook-time recompile rule | Script bytecode is re-cooked on any engine version change and on any Luau pin change, as every cooked asset is (Z2-D-048); there is no runtime translation of older bytecode and no compatibility shim. |
| Z2-N-SCRIPT-506 | `Zenith2/Docs/Scripting.md`:1828 | Zenith2 Scripting › Shipping bytecode compatibility › Cook-time recompile rule | Saves never contain bytecode or execution positions, so save migration depends on behaviour state schemas and their `Migrate` chains alone, never on bytecode compatibility. |
| Z2-N-SCRIPT-507 | `Zenith2/Docs/Scripting.md`:1829 | Zenith2 Scripting › Shipping bytecode compatibility › Cook-time recompile rule | A content patch ships re-cooked bytecode for every changed module within one release line; a patch whose bytecode's engine version hash differs from the installed engine is refused by the loader. |
| Z2-N-SCRIPT-508 | `Zenith2/Docs/Scripting.md`:1833 | Zenith2 Scripting › Shipping bytecode compatibility › Development compilation | Every hot-reload producer (ZenithEditor in process, ZenithCook's file-watch mode) compiles with the same `Zenith_ScriptCompileOptions` as the cook, including the mutable-globals list, so a hot-reloaded module and its cooked form produce identical bytecode. |
| Z2-N-SCRIPT-521 | `Zenith2/Docs/Scripting.md`:1889 | Zenith2 Scripting › Required conformance tests | Every test in the tables of this section, which live in `Scripting/ConformanceTests.md` → "Conformance test tables" under the five headings below, exists under exactly the name shown, asserts what its row states, and is added in the same ticket as the code it tests. |
| Z2-N-SCRIPT-522 | `Zenith2/Docs/Scripting.md`:1890 | Zenith2 Scripting › Required conformance tests | Misuse tests assert the controlled Luau error and its exact catalogue text, and fail if any assert, crash or sanitizer report occurs. |
| Z2-N-SCRIPT-523 | `Zenith2/Docs/Scripting.md`:1887 | Zenith2 Scripting › Required conformance tests | Every test of this section is a doctest case under `Zenith2/Engine/ZenithScript/Tests/` (or, where marked, in the script conformance executable `ZenithScript_Conformance`, which links all three ECS backends; tests of `ZenithScriptCompiler` under `Zenith2/Engine/ZenithScriptCompiler/Tests/`), registered with CTest under the label `script-conformance` and run by the `Test` step, with its scenario scripts in `Zenith2/Engine/ZenithScript/Tests/Scenarios/<TestName>/`. |

When a module loads, the loader resolves every string-constant argument the module passes to a marked parameter against the kind's table and records the result in the module's load context; a name the table lacks raises ZS1807 (`unknown input <Kind> '<Name>' in <Module>`, `Zenith2/Docs/Scripting/MisuseCatalogue.md`) at load, never an assert. At a call, the dispatcher passes the thunk the name's index in the table given to `AddNameTable` as a `SCRIPT_VALUE_KIND_NUMBER`; a non-constant argument is looked up in the same table at the call, and a name it lacks is the catalogued bad-argument misuse error at the call. `Seal` refuses a marked parameter whose kind has no table (`SCRIPT_BINDING_ERROR_UNKNOWN_NAME_KIND`) and a second table for one kind or a name repeated within a table (`SCRIPT_BINDING_ERROR_DUPLICATE_NAME_TABLE`), and every name table is part of the binding-set hash. |

| Z2-N-SCRIPT-927 | `Zenith2/Docs/Scripting.md`:1643 | Zenith2 Scripting › Quarantine and hot reload › Hot reload of behaviour modules | Every hot-reload request (`Zenith_ScriptWorld::RequestHotReload`, `Zenith_ScriptRuntime::RequestCookedReload`) carries the reloaded module's load-time name references as `Zenith_Span<const Zenith_ScriptNameRefRecord>` (`Scripting/PublicTypes.md` → "ZenithScript public types"; default empty, for a module with no marked argument), supplied by the editor from the `Zenith_ScriptExtractNameRefs` run it already makes and, for `RequestCookedReload`, by the development Player's file watcher from `Zenith_ScriptModuleManifest::GetNameRefs` of the re-cooked `content:/Scripts/scripts.zlmf`, which the cook's watch mode writes after the changed `.zlbc` files; the staging resolves them as the loader does at world load ([Z2-N-SCRIPT-922]) before go-live, and an unresolved reference fails that module's reload with ZS1807 and the rollback of [Z2-N-SCRIPT-425] or [Z2-N-SCRIPT-428], never degrading to the call-time ZS1511. |
| Z2-N-SCRIPT-928 | `Zenith2/Docs/Scripting.md`:1069 | Zenith2 Scripting › Determinism rules and randomness › Ordering keys | A recovery invocation (`OnRecover`) is an entity transaction whose logical invocation ID is (stable simulation ID, `uSCRIPT_RECOVERY_ORDINAL` = 0xFFFFFFFF), a value no ordinary invocation reaches; its command buffer, SCRIPT records and random draws use that ordinal, so the instance's other invocations of the tick keep the ordinals, merge keys and draws of a tick without the recovery; within the merge an instance's recovery transaction is accepted or rejected before its ordinary invocations (the earliest-failure rule of [Z2-N-SCRIPT-252] treats it as the earliest), while playback stays in merge-key order. |
| Z2-N-SCRIPT-929 | `Zenith2/Docs/Scripting.md`:1577 | Zenith2 Scripting › Behaviour state model and recovery › OnRecover | A last-good restore, a hot-reload go-live or a save restore returns an instance of a behaviour that does not declare `OnRecover` to ACTIVE (DORMANT while disabled) with no recovery invocation, so its first invocation after the restore is the one a run without the restore would make. |
| Z2-N-SCRIPT-930 | `Zenith2/Docs/Scripting.md`:1580 | Zenith2 Scripting › Behaviour state model and recovery › OnRecover | The save-state snapshot holds one entry per instance past NEW (an empty checkpoint for an instance with no committed checkpoint, restoring its declared defaults) with the flags `m_bRunSuspended`, `m_bRecoveryPending` and `m_bQuarantinedOrDisabled`; a save restore gives an entry a recovery invocation only when its behaviour declares `OnRecover` and one flag is set, and a restore of a snapshot with no suspended `Run` and no quarantined or disabled entry continues bit-identically to the uninterrupted run (`Zenith2/Docs/Design/SCRIPT/HotReload.md` → "Recovery invocations and exact restore"). |
| Z2-N-SCRIPT-931 | `Zenith2/Docs/Scripting.md`:1341 | Zenith2 Scripting › Merge-time capacity and asynchronous results › Tick-boundary publication | Every completion ID allocated from a world's `Zenith_AsyncResultTable` is completed exactly once by the producer that allocated it (or took ownership of it), including work its `DetachWorld` cancels, which it completes with its failure or cancellation payload (`Complete` being `ANY_THREAD`); a producer never counts a discarded result itself, because the table, live or retired, discards and counts it and the world host is the sole publisher of `world.async_discarded` ([Z2-N-ARCH-558]); and a table is destroyed only when no producer of its world can still complete into it, a world destroyed with results in flight retiring its table instead ([Z2-N-ARCH-558], [Z2-N-ARCH-560]). |

## CONTRACT

| ID | File | Heading | Statement |
|---|---|---|---|
| Z2-N-CONTRACT-001 | `Zenith2/Docs/Contracts.md`:11 | Zenith2 engineering contracts › Purpose and scope › What this document binds | This document binds every C and C++ source file under `Zenith2/Engine/`, `Zenith2/Tools/`, `Zenith2/Player/` and `Zenith2/Benchmarks/`, and every generated C++ file emitted into those libraries' build outputs. |
| Z2-N-CONTRACT-002 | `Zenith2/Docs/Contracts.md`:12 | Zenith2 engineering contracts › Purpose and scope › What this document binds | Third-party source pinned in `Zenith2/ThirdParty/manifest.json` is exempt from every rule in this document except confinement (see "Third-party confinement" under "API boundary"); Zenith2 code that wraps it is not exempt. |

| Z2-N-CONTRACT-099 | `Zenith2/Docs/Contracts.md`:253 | Zenith2 engineering contracts › Handle kinds › Zenith_EntityHandle | Reissuing a tag leaves a residual aliasing window: a stale handle (tag T, slot S, generation G) of a destroyed world would resolve in a subsequently created world holding tag T if that world's slot S were at generation G. It is closed by the initial generation `Zenith_WorldRegistry` issues with each tag, in two cases: a tag issued before, whose destroyed world reported its highest generation h > 0 through `ReleaseTag`, starts at h + 1, wrapping past 2^28 − 1 to 1 (`Zenith2/Docs/Architecture/ECSModel.md` [Z2-N-ARCH-725]); a tag never issued starts at the offset `1 + ((uWorldCreationCounter × 65,537) mod 2^27)`, computed from the registry's own count of worlds created so far. Aliasing then needs the generation counter of one tag to wrap; the remaining risk is accepted and documented in the handle's contract block. |

The six profiling hooks are invoked only through the dispatch functions declared in the same header ([Z2-N-CONTRACT-590]); the lock-acquire hook receives the wait the acquiring thread measured, `ulWaitTicks`, in ticks of `Zenith_GetMonotonicTimeNs()` (one tick is one nanosecond), which is 0 for an uncontended acquire (one that did not block) and for a successful `TryLock`, and at least 1 for an acquire that blocked. `pLock` of the lock hooks is an identity token that no hook dereferences, because the lock's owner may be destroyed as soon as the lock is released. `Zenith_InstallProfilerHooks` refuses, with `ZENITH_CHECK` in every build, a table in which exactly one of `m_pfnOnZoneBegin` and `m_pfnOnZoneEnd` is non-null, or exactly one of `m_pfnOnLockAcquire` and `m_pfnOnLockRelease` is non-null, so a dispatched begin always has a dispatchable end and a caller never stores hook pointers to pair them (Z2-D-142). |

The anchor is written only by `Zenith_CrashHandler::Create` (CAS null → the handler), by its destructor (CAS the handler → null, retried every 1 ms while a claim holds it), by the crash path's one claim (CAS the handler → a reserved capturing value, after which the process terminates) and, when the claimed handler is not armed, by the same trampoline's unclaim (capturing value → the handler), and is read only by `Create` and the OS-callback trampolines; a trampoline owns the handler through its own CAS before it dereferences it, because a flag inside the object could be read after a concurrent destructor freed it (`Zenith2/Docs/Design/DEVT/Crash.md` → "Crash handler anchor"). |
| Z2-N-CONTRACT-153 | `Zenith2/Docs/Contracts.md`:733 | Zenith2 engineering contracts › No mutable global state › Writable-data symbol scan | `writable_data_allowlist.json` is one JSON object `{ "comment": "<text>", "entries": [ … ] }`. JSON carries no comments, so its `comment` members are the file's comments: the top-level one states that every entry is an exemption of Z2-D-046 and names `Zenith2/Docs/Contracts.md → "Writable-data allowlist entries" [Z2-N-CONTRACT-152]`. Each entry has the form `{ "symbol": "<demangled name>", "source": "<repo-relative path>", "decision": "Z2-D-NNN", "extensions": [ "<YYYY-MM-DD> <ruling>", … ], "comment": "<text>" }`: `extensions` lists every dated extension of the decision that the variable relies on (empty when the decision's own text covers it), and `comment` states in one sentence why the variable exists, then names the decision ID, each extension and the signpost `Zenith2/Docs/Contracts.md → "Writable-data allowlist entries" [Z2-N-CONTRACT-152]`. An entry without a decision ID, without an `extensions` array, or whose `comment` omits the decision ID, an extension or the signpost fails the step. |

| Z2-N-CONTRACT-217 | `Zenith2/Docs/Contracts/Memory.md`:173 | Zenith2 engineering contracts: memory mechanisms › OOM handler | An allocation failure on a thread that is already inside the OOM handler terminates at once through `ZENITH_FATAL(FATAL_CODE_OUT_OF_MEMORY, …)`. |
| Z2-N-CONTRACT-218 | `Zenith2/Docs/Contracts/Memory.md`:175 | Zenith2 engineering contracts: memory mechanisms › OOM handler | The default handler releases the emergency reserve on the first failure and returns `OOM_RESPONSE_RETRY`, then returns `OOM_RESPONSE_ABORT`; it raises the memory governor's critical pressure flag through the user-data pointer installed in `main`, so shedding runs at the next sync point, never inside the handler (see `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture.md → "Memory governor"`). |

| Z2-N-CONTRACT-224 | `Zenith2/Docs/Contracts/Memory.md`:187 | Zenith2 engineering contracts: memory mechanisms › Fallible construction | A destructor tolerates every partially initialised state its factory can leave behind. |

| Z2-N-CONTRACT-226 | `Zenith2/Docs/Contracts/Memory.md`:269 | Zenith2 engineering contracts: memory mechanisms › Zenith_Arena | `Zenith_Arena` implements the allocation steps listed above; allocation is O(1) amortised and a failed `TryAllocate` leaves the arena unchanged. |

A `DROP_OLDEST` or `DROP_NEWEST` overflow is a drop and never a full event: it increments `.dropped` alone (`Zenith2/Docs/Design/CORE/Containers.md` → "Zenith_RingBuffer", policy table). A refused push whose element the queue's owner then discards, because it is queued nowhere else, counts in both (`Zenith2/Docs/Design/PLAT/Platform.md` → "Event queue and overflow"), and a `FATAL` queue counts neither, because its full event ends the process. |

| Z2-N-CONTRACT-430 | `Zenith2/Docs/Contracts.md`:1350 | Zenith2 engineering contracts › Tests and code hygiene gates › Checked narrowing | `Zenith_NarrowCast` and `Zenith_TryNarrowCast` check before they cast: first NaN and the range of `TTo` (compared in the wider common type, so no out-of-range floating-to-integer cast ever executes), giving `NARROW_ERROR_OUT_OF_RANGE`; then the sign, giving `NARROW_ERROR_SIGN_CHANGE`; then the exact round trip, giving `NARROW_ERROR_PRECISION_LOSS`; the cast to `TTo` runs only after every check passes, so UBSan's `float-cast-overflow` never fires inside them. |

| Z2-N-CONTRACT-441 | `Zenith2/Docs/Contracts.md`:1502 | Zenith2 engineering contracts › No layout-changing conditionals | No preprocessor conditional changes the layout of any type: its members, base classes, virtual functions, `sizeof`, `alignof` or member offsets are identical in every configuration, preset and platform. |
