| Z2-N-CONTRACT-467 | `Zenith2/Docs/Contracts.md`:1593 | Zenith2 engineering contracts › Errors and results › Error enumerations | An enumerator once shipped in a fix version keeps its meaning; a new failure gets a new enumerator. |
| Z2-N-CONTRACT-468 | `Zenith2/Docs/Contracts.md`:1606 | Zenith2 engineering contracts › Errors and results › Assertions | Assertion macros are hygienic: each is a single statement, evaluates its condition exactly once when enabled, and when disabled expands to `static_cast<void>(sizeof(!(Condition)))`, which type-checks the condition without evaluating it. |

| Z2-N-CONTRACT-474 | `Zenith2/Docs/Contracts.md`:1618 | Zenith2 engineering contracts › Errors and results › Exceptions and the Luau firewall | No C++ exception is thrown, caught or propagated in Zenith2 code outside Luau, `ZenithScript/Internal` and `ZenithScriptCompiler/Internal` (Z2-D-045, Z2-D-017). |
| Z2-N-CONTRACT-475 | `Zenith2/Docs/Contracts.md`:1619 | Zenith2 engineering contracts › Errors and results › Exceptions and the Luau firewall | Every entry into Luau, from anywhere, is a protected call inside `ZenithScript/Internal`; an engine callback that re-enters script goes through that same protected entry, so no Luau error unwinds through an exceptions-disabled engine frame. |
| Z2-N-CONTRACT-476 | `Zenith2/Docs/Contracts.md`:1620 | Zenith2 engineering contracts › Errors and results › Exceptions and the Luau firewall | Restated (Z2-D-141): an engine function a Luau binding calls carries no exception specification ([Z2-N-CONTRACT-582]); it cannot throw, because every engine target outside the firewall compile groups is compiled with exceptions off ([Z2-N-CONTRACT-394]), and it re-enters script only through the protected entry of [Z2-N-CONTRACT-475], so no Luau error unwinds through it. |
| Z2-N-CONTRACT-477 | `Zenith2/Docs/Contracts.md`:1621 | Zenith2 engineering contracts › Errors and results › Exceptions and the Luau firewall | Whether the pinned Luau's API has C or C++ linkage is verified by a Stage 1 ticket, and the firewall design in `Zenith2/Docs/Scripting.md → "Luau integration and the exception firewall"` records the result. |
| Z2-N-CONTRACT-478 | `Zenith2/Docs/Contracts.md`:1638 | Zenith2 engineering contracts › Errors and results › Fatal errors | A fatal error terminates through `ZENITH_FATAL` with one `Zenith_FatalCode` from the table "Fatal errors"; a new fatal condition adds a code to that table no later than the diff that raises it, landed through the core-doc batch that applies the raising area's design as [Z2-N-CONTRACT-320] states for lock rows. |
| Z2-N-CONTRACT-479 | `Zenith2/Docs/Contracts.md`:1605 | Zenith2 engineering contracts › Errors and results › Assertions | Each assertion macro is enabled exactly as the table "Assertions" states, and `ZENITH_ASSERT` is controlled per configuration, never per target: every target of a configuration (engine libraries, the Player, game executables and the host tools `ZenithCook`, `ZenithShaderCompiler`, `ZenithHub` and every `zenith_add_tool` target alike) compiles with `ZENITH_ASSERTS_ENABLED=1` in Debug builds and sanitizer builds and `ZENITH_ASSERTS_ENABLED=0` in Release and Shipping, so every link sees one value and an asserting inline or template member of an engine header has one definition per link (a per-target value would give it two in a Release tool, an ODR violation); `ZENITH_CHECK`, `ZENITH_FATAL` and `ZENITH_UNREACHABLE` are enabled in every target and configuration, and host-tool code that needs an invariant checked in the Release tools the `win-tools` preset builds uses `ZENITH_CHECK`. The `win-tools` preset therefore needs no configuration of its own (`Zenith2/Docs/Architecture/BuildSystem.md` → "Configurations"). |
| Z2-N-CONTRACT-480 | `Zenith2/Docs/Contracts.md`:1581 | Zenith2 engineering contracts › Errors and results › Zenith_Result | `E` takes one of two forms, enforced by the `Zenith_ErrorType` concept through `static_assert(Zenith_ErrorType<E>)` inside `Zenith_Result`: an error enumeration (an `enum class`, or the prefixed `enum Zenith_<Subsystem>Error : uint8` form of "Error enumerations"), trivially copyable; or a trivially copyable struct of at most 32 bytes whose public `m_eCode` member is such an enumeration, carrying fixed-size context beside the code (a step index, an ID, a count), never a pointer to heap data or a string. |
| Z2-N-CONTRACT-481 | `Zenith2/Docs/Contracts.md`:1582 | Zenith2 engineering contracts › Errors and results › Zenith_Result | Generic code reads the code of either form through `Zenith_GetErrorCode(const E&)`, which returns the enumeration itself or `m_eCode`; a failure result's code is never 0, and `ZENITH_TRY` propagates an error unchanged within one `E`, whichever form it has. |
| Z2-N-CONTRACT-482 | `Zenith2/Docs/Contracts.md`:1591 | Zenith2 engineering contracts › Errors and results › Error enumerations | An error type takes the name `Zenith_<Subsystem>Error` in both forms of "Zenith_Result": in the enumeration form the enumeration itself is `Zenith_<Subsystem>Error`; in the struct form the struct is `Zenith_<Subsystem>Error` and its code enumeration, held in the member `m_eCode`, is `Zenith_<Subsystem>ErrorCode`, numbered from 1 with the same `<SUBSYSTEM>_ERROR_` enumerator prefix. |
| Z2-N-CONTRACT-483 | `Zenith2/Docs/Contracts.md`:1640 | Zenith2 engineering contracts › Errors and results › Fatal errors | When a fatal error is raised while a log-record hook is installed, the log router outputs every buffered record and then the fatal record to every registered sink synchronously, in sequence order, before the process terminates, so no record accepted before the fatal error is lost (see `Zenith2/Docs/Design/CORE/Diagnostics.md` → "Log router"). |
| Z2-N-CONTRACT-484 | `Zenith2/Docs/Contracts.md`:1639 | Zenith2 engineering contracts › Errors and results › Fatal errors | A device loss that cannot be recovered is a clean exit, never a fatal: the device-loss handler requests an autosave and shuts down with `EXIT_CODE_DEVICE_LOST` (6, "the graphics device was lost and could not be recovered; autosave requested", which also covers 30 consecutive frames that could not be rendered), and raises `FATAL_CODE_DEVICE_LOST` only when that exit has not finished within 10 s; `EXIT_CODE_DEVICE` (4) keeps the meaning "a required device could not be created", so CI tells a runtime loss from a missing driver (`Zenith2/Docs/Architecture.md → "Composition root and Player"`, table "Exit codes" of its CompositionRoot companion; `Zenith2/Docs/Design/RHI/Recovery.md` → "Device-loss state machine"). |
| Z2-N-CONTRACT-491 | `Zenith2/Docs/Contracts.md`:1659 | Zenith2 engineering contracts › Review and change management › Diff-derived API review | A diff that changes any path in the Z2 categories' `reviewPaths` requires an independent review before the ticket can finish Done, whatever the ticket's risk or labels. |
| Z2-N-CONTRACT-492 | `Zenith2/Docs/Contracts.md`:1660 | Zenith2 engineering contracts › Review and change management › Diff-derived API review | The Z2 categories list as `reviewPaths` every public root header and public inline file (`Zenith2/Engine/*/*.h`, `Zenith2/Engine/*/*.inl`), every generated public API output (generated public headers and the generated `.d.luau` files), `Zenith2/Tools/Gates/libgraph_baseline.json`, and `Zenith2/Tools/Maintenance/*.json` (the engine changelog, the API surface listing and the ownership, rehearsal and sample-games files). |
| Z2-N-CONTRACT-493 | `Zenith2/Docs/Contracts.md`:1665 | Zenith2 engineering contracts › Review and change management › Review record hash | The review record hash is SHA-256 over the UTF-8 text formed by sorting the changed paths inside the `reviewPaths` scope by byte order and joining, with `\n`, one line per path of the form `<repo-relative path> <git blob SHA-1 of the working-tree content>`, where a deleted path uses the blob value `deleted`. |
| Z2-N-CONTRACT-494 | `Zenith2/Docs/Contracts.md`:1666 | Zenith2 engineering contracts › Review and change management › Review record hash | Any change to a reviewed path after the review record was written changes the hash and invalidates the record; a new review is then needed. |
| Z2-N-CONTRACT-495 | `Zenith2/Docs/Contracts.md`:1670 | Zenith2 engineering contracts › Review and change management › Finish refusal | `zagent finish --status Done` for a Z2 ticket whose `review.requiredByDiff` is true is refused unless a `zagent review record` entry exists whose hash matches the current blobs; the refusal names the paths whose blobs differ. |
| Z2-N-CONTRACT-496 | `Zenith2/Docs/Contracts.md`:1674 | Zenith2 engineering contracts › Review and change management › Reviewer conventions | The Z2 categories carry a `reviewerConventions` value, inlined into the reviewer prompt by the generic per-category field a definite Stage 0 PROC ticket adds, instructing the reviewer to challenge behaviour beyond the author's tests and to check each item of the ticket's "Review focus" list in `## Validation`. |
| Z2-N-CONTRACT-497 | `Zenith2/Docs/Contracts.md`:1675 | Zenith2 engineering contracts › Review and change management › Reviewer conventions | Every ticket's `## Validation` section carries a "Review focus" list of behaviours and missing cases a reviewer probes beyond the author's tests. |
| Z2-N-CONTRACT-498 | `Zenith2/Docs/Contracts.md`:1679 | Zenith2 engineering contracts › Review and change management › Independent review for risky changes | A ticket routed `risk HIGH` (a defect would be silent — memory, synchronisation, determinism — or the change is a destructive migration, a security boundary, a release or packaging path, or a save-format change) is reviewed independently of its author. |
| Z2-N-CONTRACT-499 | `Zenith2/Docs/Contracts.md`:1680 | Zenith2 engineering contracts › Review and change management › Independent review for risky changes | A Definition of Done asserts observable checks and avoids phrasing that forces review without need ("provably", "byte-identical", "no new allocations"), unless review is wanted. |
| Z2-N-CONTRACT-500 | `Zenith2/Docs/Contracts.md`:1687 | Zenith2 engineering contracts › Review and change management › Refactor owed | A change that leaves a known shortcut behind (a temporary limitation, a duplicated mechanism, a missing failure path) records it in its result, and a refactor ticket is filed in the same stage, owned by the area epic of the shortcut. |
| Z2-N-CONTRACT-501 | `Zenith2/Docs/Contracts.md`:1688 | Zenith2 engineering contracts › Review and change management › Refactor owed | A refactor ticket names the shortcut's exact location and the contract it restores; a shortcut without a filed ticket is rejected in review. |
| Z2-N-CONTRACT-502 | `Zenith2/Docs/Contracts.md`:1692 | Zenith2 engineering contracts › Review and change management › Living documents | A ticket that changes a contract, an API or a design edits the governing section of `Zenith2/Docs/Design/<CODE>.md`, this document or the core document concerned in the same diff, and its Definition of Done says so (Z2-D-085). |
| Z2-N-CONTRACT-503 | `Zenith2/Docs/Contracts.md`:1693 | Zenith2 engineering contracts › Review and change management › Living documents | A ruling is recorded by appending its entry to `Zenith2/Docs/DecisionLog.md` in the ticket's own diff with an explicit, previously unused `Z2-D-NNN` ID (Z2-D-097), following `Zenith2/Docs/DecisionLog.md → "How this log is written"`; `zagent decide --ID Z2-D-NNN` is at most an optional knowledge-row mirror and never the record. |
| Z2-N-CONTRACT-504 | `Zenith2/Docs/Contracts.md`:1695 | Zenith2 engineering contracts › Review and change management › Living documents | A section a ticket edits keeps its heading text, because tickets cite headings by exact text; a rename is a separate ticket that updates every citing ticket through the filer's reconcile mode. |
| Z2-N-CONTRACT-505 | `Zenith2/Docs/Contracts.md`:1696 | Zenith2 engineering contracts › Review and change management › Living documents | Every normative statement added to a scanned document is a tagged bullet with a fresh `[Z2-N-<DOC>-NNN]` ID, never reusing a retired ID. |
| Z2-N-CONTRACT-506 | `Zenith2/Docs/Contracts.md`:1700 | Zenith2 engineering contracts › Review and change management › Subsystem ownership | Every subsystem has a named owner, which is its area epic `Z2 <CODE> — <Area name>`; the owner is responsible for the subsystem's maintenance tickets, refactors owed and dependency upgrades. |
| Z2-N-CONTRACT-507 | `Zenith2/Docs/Contracts.md`:1701 | Zenith2 engineering contracts › Review and change management › Subsystem ownership | Anything spanning areas is owned by one area, which names the others in its tickets' `## Dependencies` section. |
| Z2-N-CONTRACT-508 | `Zenith2/Docs/Contracts.md`:1705 | Zenith2 engineering contracts › Review and change management › Benchmark regressions | A benchmark or workload regression beyond its budget row's tolerance blocks the stage acceptance until it is fixed or re-budgeted by a decision (Z2-D-012). |
| Z2-N-CONTRACT-509 | `Zenith2/Docs/Contracts.md`:1706 | Zenith2 engineering contracts › Review and change management › Benchmark regressions | A mid-sprint integration check that measures a regression files it as a ticket in the current sprint, owned by the regressing area. |
| Z2-N-CONTRACT-510 | `Zenith2/Docs/Contracts.md`:1710 | Zenith2 engineering contracts › Review and change management › Challenges to settled choices | A settled `Z2-D-` decision is challenged only as a flagged proposal with a concern, evidence, a proposal and a trade-off; nothing changes until the human approves it (Z2-D-022), and work proceeds under the settled decision meanwhile. |
| Z2-N-CONTRACT-511 | `Zenith2/Docs/Contracts.md`:1681 | Zenith2 engineering contracts › Review and change management › Independent review for risky changes | A ticket's Goal and Definition of Done contain no `##` or `###` heading, and no heading named `Gates` appears anywhere in a ticket body. |
| Z2-N-CONTRACT-512 | `Zenith2/Docs/Contracts.md`:1682 | Zenith2 engineering contracts › Review and change management › Independent review for risky changes | Governing rulings are cited by `Z2-D-NNN` inside the Goal or Definition of Done text, because the rulings matcher reads those two sections; the `Decisions:` line repeats them. |
| Z2-N-CONTRACT-513 | `Zenith2/Docs/Contracts.md`:1683 | Zenith2 engineering contracts › Review and change management › Independent review for risky changes | Outside `needs-human` tickets, the Goal and Definition of Done avoid board references (`the blocker`, `the blocking`, `linked` or `related` ticket, `the parent` ticket) and human-only phrases (human, manual or visual sign-off or approval; "no agent may", "no agent can"; "a human must", "a person must"; "human-executed", "human-authored", "human-only"; "needs a human"; "only a human can"; "must be done by a human", "must be run by a human", "must be authored by a human"), which raise prompt warnings. |
| Z2-N-CONTRACT-521 | `Zenith2/Docs/Contracts.md`:1725 | Zenith2 engineering contracts › Compatibility policy per kind › Compatibility table | Each kind of persistent or consumed interface follows its row of the table "Compatibility table" (Z2-D-048). |
| Z2-N-CONTRACT-522 | `Zenith2/Docs/Contracts.md`:1729 | Zenith2 engineering contracts › Compatibility policy per kind › Source API deprecation | A deprecated C++ declaration carries `[[deprecated("use <Replacement>; removed in Z2 Stage <N+1>")]]`, and its contract block's `Version` field records the deprecation. |
| Z2-N-CONTRACT-523 | `Zenith2/Docs/Contracts.md`:1730 | Zenith2 engineering contracts › Compatibility policy per kind › Source API deprecation | Because every C++ consumer is in this repository and deprecation warnings are errors, the diff that deprecates a declaration migrates every in-repo caller; the deprecated declaration then remains for one fix version and is removed by a ticket filed in that fix version. |
| Z2-N-CONTRACT-524 | `Zenith2/Docs/Contracts.md`:1736 | Zenith2 engineering contracts › Compatibility policy per kind › Save migration | A save carries a `(major, minor)` version; the runtime loads every save version shipped within the current major release by applying forward migration steps in sequence, and refuses a save of another major version with a recoverable error. |
| Z2-N-CONTRACT-525 | `Zenith2/Docs/Contracts.md`:1737 | Zenith2 engineering contracts › Compatibility policy per kind › Save migration | Each shipped save version has a committed migration fixture and a test that loads it, migrates it to the current version and asserts the migrated state. |
| Z2-N-CONTRACT-526 | `Zenith2/Docs/Contracts.md`:1738 | Zenith2 engineering contracts › Compatibility policy per kind › Save migration | Save writes are atomic and journaled like authoring documents (see "Journaled atomic write"); save-specific rules live in `Zenith2/Docs/Design/SAVE.md`. |
| Z2-N-CONTRACT-527 | `Zenith2/Docs/Contracts.md`:1743 | Zenith2 engineering contracts › Compatibility policy per kind › Cooked asset versioning | Every cooked file begins with a header recording a four-byte format magic, the format version, the engine cook version, the compression codec ID of each compressed section, and the content hash of the source inputs. |
| Z2-N-CONTRACT-528 | `Zenith2/Docs/Contracts.md`:1744 | Zenith2 engineering contracts › Compatibility policy per kind › Cooked asset versioning | The runtime refuses a cooked file whose format version or engine cook version differs from its own with a recoverable asset error, never an assert; a patch applies only within one release line. |
| Z2-N-CONTRACT-529 | `Zenith2/Docs/Contracts.md`:1745 | Zenith2 engineering contracts › Compatibility policy per kind › Cooked asset versioning | For cooked data the engine version is the engine cook version `uZENITH_ENGINE_COOK_VERSION` that every cooked header records (`Zenith2/Docs/Design/ASSET/RuntimeAPI.md` → "Constants and fault points"): any change of it triggers a re-cook, and cooked data is never migrated at runtime; MAINT's engine semantic version (`Zenith2/Docs/Design/MAINT/Versioning.md` → "Engine version") moves without forcing a re-cook, and the re-cook rule for Luau bytecode is [Z2-N-SCRIPT-505]'s, with its own key. |
| Z2-N-CONTRACT-530 | `Zenith2/Docs/Contracts.md`:1754 | Zenith2 engineering contracts › Compatibility policy per kind › Authoring-document migration | Each authoring-document type carries a per-type `uint32` schema version and a migration chain with exactly one step per version increment. |
| Z2-N-CONTRACT-531 | `Zenith2/Docs/Contracts.md`:1755 | Zenith2 engineering contracts › Compatibility policy per kind › Authoring-document migration | Before anything is written, an upgrade preview presents a dry-run diff of every document the migration would change. |
| Z2-N-CONTRACT-532 | `Zenith2/Docs/Contracts.md`:1756 | Zenith2 engineering contracts › Compatibility policy per kind › Authoring-document migration | An automatic backup of every document to be changed is written before the migration writes any document. |
| Z2-N-CONTRACT-533 | `Zenith2/Docs/Contracts.md`:1757 | Zenith2 engineering contracts › Compatibility policy per kind › Authoring-document migration | Every migration write is a journaled atomic write, so an interrupted migration resumes or rolls back cleanly (see "Journaled atomic write"). |
| Z2-N-CONTRACT-534 | `Zenith2/Docs/Contracts.md`:1758 | Zenith2 engineering contracts › Compatibility policy per kind › Authoring-document migration | Authored overrides are preserved, keyed by stable authoring IDs (`Zenith_PersistentID` plus property path), never by array index, name or position (see "Override preservation"). |
| Z2-N-CONTRACT-535 | `Zenith2/Docs/Contracts.md`:1759 | Zenith2 engineering contracts › Compatibility policy per kind › Authoring-document migration | Unknown fields are preserved on round-trip with a warning and never dropped, so newer tools' data survives (see "Unknown-field preservation"). |
| Z2-N-CONTRACT-536 | `Zenith2/Docs/Contracts.md`:1760 | Zenith2 engineering contracts › Compatibility policy per kind › Authoring-document migration | Each authoring-document type has a migration fixture per version (see "Migration fixtures"). |
| Z2-N-CONTRACT-537 | `Zenith2/Docs/Contracts.md`:1762 | Zenith2 engineering contracts › Compatibility policy per kind › Authoring-document migration | A document whose schema version is newer than the running tool's is opened read-only with a diagnostic and is never written by that tool. |
| Z2-N-CONTRACT-538 | `Zenith2/Docs/Contracts.md`:1785 | Zenith2 engineering contracts › Compatibility policy per kind › Migration step interface | A migration step is a pure function over the in-memory document tree: it performs no I/O, reads no global or engine state, and is deterministic, so the preview and the write produce identical output. |
| Z2-N-CONTRACT-539 | `Zenith2/Docs/Contracts.md`:1786 | Zenith2 engineering contracts › Compatibility policy per kind › Migration step interface | A chain with a missing step for any version between a document's version and the current version is a registration error reported at tool start, naming the type and the missing version. |
| Z2-N-CONTRACT-540 | `Zenith2/Docs/Contracts.md`:1805 | Zenith2 engineering contracts › Compatibility policy per kind › Journaled atomic write | Every authoring-document and save write follows the steps of the table "Journaled atomic write", and start-up recovery follows the recovery table; re-running recovery converges without duplicating or losing any entry. |
| Z2-N-CONTRACT-541 | `Zenith2/Docs/Contracts.md`:1806 | Zenith2 engineering contracts › Compatibility policy per kind › Journaled atomic write | Authoring-document backups are written under `Games/<Name>/.migration/backups/<UTC timestamp>/` with the document's game-relative path, and that directory is excluded from source control by the nested `Zenith2/.gitignore`. |
| Z2-N-CONTRACT-542 | `Zenith2/Docs/Contracts.md`:1808 | Zenith2 engineering contracts › Compatibility policy per kind › Journaled atomic write | The journal format and the recovery paths are tested by `Test_JournaledWrite_InterruptedAtEachStep_Recovers`, which interrupts after each step, and after each individual rename, and asserts every document is either fully old or fully new. |
| Z2-N-CONTRACT-543 | `Zenith2/Docs/Contracts.md`:1812 | Zenith2 engineering contracts › Compatibility policy per kind › Unknown-field preservation | The document reader keeps every JSON member it does not recognise, with its path and raw value, and the writer re-emits it at the same position in canonical order. |
| Z2-N-CONTRACT-544 | `Zenith2/Docs/Contracts.md`:1813 | Zenith2 engineering contracts › Compatibility policy per kind › Unknown-field preservation | Each unknown field produces one warning `MIGRATION_WARNING_UNKNOWN_FIELD` per document per field path per load, naming the document, the path and the schema version that wrote it. |
| Z2-N-CONTRACT-545 | `Zenith2/Docs/Contracts.md`:1814 | Zenith2 engineering contracts › Compatibility policy per kind › Unknown-field preservation | A migration step never deletes an unknown field; removing a field the schema knows is an explicit step operation recorded in the step's name. |
| Z2-N-CONTRACT-546 | `Zenith2/Docs/Contracts.md`:1818 | Zenith2 engineering contracts › Compatibility policy per kind › Override preservation | An override (a prefab-instance override, a variant override, an importer setting override) is keyed by the target object's `Zenith_PersistentID` and the property's `Zenith_PropertyID` path. |
| Z2-N-CONTRACT-547 | `Zenith2/Docs/Contracts.md`:1819 | Zenith2 engineering contracts › Compatibility policy per kind › Override preservation | A migration step that renames, moves or restructures a property rewrites every override keyed to it in the same step; an override whose target no longer exists is kept, flagged in the preview, and reported, never silently dropped. |
| Z2-N-CONTRACT-548 | `Zenith2/Docs/Contracts.md`:1823 | Zenith2 engineering contracts › Compatibility policy per kind › Migration fixtures | For every authoring-document type and every schema version from its first shipped version, a fixture document lives under the owning library's `Tests/Fixtures/Migration/<Type>/v<N>.json`, and a test migrates each fixture to the current version and compares the result against `v<N>.expected.json`. |
| Z2-N-CONTRACT-549 | `Zenith2/Docs/Contracts.md`:1824 | Zenith2 engineering contracts › Compatibility policy per kind › Migration fixtures | A schema version bump adds the new version's fixture and expected output in the same diff; a bump without them fails the owning library's tests. |
| Z2-N-CONTRACT-550 | `Zenith2/Docs/Contracts.md`:1828 | Zenith2 engineering contracts › Compatibility policy per kind › Delivery stages of the migration framework | The authoring-document migration framework (versioning, chain, preview, backup, journaled write, override and unknown-field preservation) is delivered in Stage 2 by SCENE and ASSET, and complete coverage of every authoring-document type, with the editor's migration UI, in Stage 4 by EDIT. |
| Z2-N-CONTRACT-551 | `Zenith2/Docs/Contracts.md`:1829 | Zenith2 engineering contracts › Compatibility policy per kind › Delivery stages of the migration framework | The per-kind compatibility policy is demonstrated with migration fixtures in Stage 6 shipping qualification. |
| Z2-N-CONTRACT-552 | `Zenith2/Docs/Contracts.md`:1753 | Zenith2 engineering contracts › Compatibility policy per kind › Authoring-document migration | The authoring-document types are scenes, prefabs, data-defined component schemas (`*.schema.json`), animation documents (`Zenith.AnimationGraphDocument`, `Zenith.AnimationMaskDocument`, `Zenith.AnimationRigDocument`, `Zenith.AnimationClipDocument`, `Zenith.AnimationRetargetDocument`), UI documents, font-family documents (`Zenith.UI.FontFamilyDocument`), localization-project documents (`Zenith.UI.LocalizationProjectDocument`), physics documents (`Zenith.PhysicsMaterialDocument`, `Zenith.RagdollDocument`, `Zenith.CollisionShapeDocument`), 2D documents (`Zenith.TwoD.SpriteAtlasDocument`, `Zenith.TwoD.SpriteAnimationDocument`, `Zenith.TwoD.TilesetDocument`, `Zenith.TwoD.TilemapDocument`, `Zenith.TwoD.RigDocument`), navigation documents (`Zenith.NavigationSettingsDocument`, `Zenith.NavigationDocument`), character documents (`Zenith.CharacterAssemblyDocument`, `Zenith.CharacterLipSyncDocument`, `Zenith.CharacterPoseBookDocument`, `Zenith.CharacterControlSetDocument`, `Zenith.CharacterProjectDocument`, registered by `Zenith_RegisterCharacterDocumentTypes`), world documents (`Zenith.World.WorldDocument`, `Zenith.World.GeneratorDocument`, `Zenith.World.LightBakeDocument`, `Zenith.World.LightPageDocument`), import presets (`Zenith.Content.ImportPresetDocument`, `*.importpreset.json`), packaging recipes (`Zenith.Package.RecipeDocument`, `package.recipe.json`), online catalogue documents (`Zenith.Online.CatalogueDocument`, schema tag `zenith2.online`, version 1, an empty migration chain, unknown fields preserved; registered by `Zenith_RegisterOnlineDocumentTypes` in `Zenith2/Tools/Authoring/Zenith_AuthoringOnline.h`; fixture `Zenith2/Tools/Authoring/Tests/Fixtures/Migration/Online/CatalogueDocument/v1.json` with `v1.expected.json` beside it), cinematic documents, material definitions, project settings and importer `.meta` settings; a new authoring-document type joins this list in the diff that introduces it. The per-product owner members of a `.meta` sub-asset entry (`exclude` and `materialRemap` of CONTENT, `annotation` and `rig` of ANIM, the ASSET-written `fingerprint`) are part of the `Zenith.AssetMeta` schema and change only through `.meta` version steps, while the top-level `.meta` member `provenance` carries its own `schema` and migrates through a member chain inside the `.meta` type's visitor. |
| Z2-N-CONTRACT-553 | `Zenith2/Docs/Contracts.md`:1807 | Zenith2 engineering contracts › Compatibility policy per kind › Journaled atomic write | A save write's step 2 backup is one `<save file>.bak` beside the save, replaced after each successful write, per the slot policy of `Zenith2/Docs/Design/SAVE.md`; saves never write under `Games/<Name>/`. |
| Z2-N-CONTRACT-571 | `Zenith2/Docs/Contracts.md`:1872 | Zenith2 engineering contracts › Enforceability matrix › Categories and step activation | The hard gates are `Libgraph` (cycles, encapsulation, undeclared edges, leakage, the Slang and DXC link surface), `Complexity` (the Zenith2 profile), `ForbiddenCheck` (unsafe allocation and forbidden constructs), `TestSeparationCheck` (shipping and test separation) and `ApiContractCheck` (type-level API contracts); a failure of any of them fails the phase and is never downgraded to a report (Z2-D-042). |
| Z2-N-CONTRACT-572 | `Zenith2/Docs/Contracts.md`:1873 | Zenith2 engineering contracts › Enforceability matrix › Categories and step activation | The gate list is decided by the diff: a ticket runs the union of the gates of every Z2 category whose `paths` its diff touches, and CI runs the union of every Z2 category's active gates except `Zenith2Acceptance` (Z2-D-031, Z2-D-032). |

| Z2-N-CONTRACT-583 | `Zenith2/Docs/Contracts.md`:105 | Zenith2 engineering contracts › API boundary › Library directory layout | One signposted exception to [Z2-N-CONTRACT-022], [Z2-N-CONTRACT-026], [Z2-N-CONTRACT-442] and `ForbiddenCheck` rule F18 exists, by the human's ruling of 2026-10-23 (Z2-D-141), and it follows the old Zenith pattern of one class per platform: ZenithCore's per-platform threading header `Zenith2/Engine/ZenithCore/Platform/<Platform>/Zenith_PlatformThreading.h`, one file each for `Windows`, `Android` and `Linux`, declares that platform's synchronisation class templates (`Zenith_WindowsMutex_T<bool bProfiled>`, `Zenith_AndroidMutex_T<bool bProfiled>`, `Zenith_LinuxMutex_T<bool bProfiled>`, and likewise `…ConditionVariable_T` and `…Semaphore_T`; Z2-D-142), each holding its native object as a typed member: on Android and Linux `pthread_mutex_t m_xMutex`, `pthread_cond_t m_xCondition`, and `sem_t m_xSemaphore` with `uint m_uMaxValue`; on Windows the pointer-sized `SRWLOCK`, `CONDITION_VARIABLE` and semaphore `HANDLE` as `void* m_pSRWLock`, `void* m_pConditionVariable` and `void* m_pHandle`, so `<windows.h>` reaches no public header. `zenith_add_library()` adds exactly `${CMAKE_CURRENT_SOURCE_DIR}/Platform/${CMAKE_SYSTEM_NAME}`, the target platform's directory, to ZenithCore's public include directories (the signposted carve-out of `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture/BuildSystem.md` [Z2-N-ARCH-161], [Z2-N-ARCH-162] and [Z2-N-ARCH-164]); each platform header ends with the six aliases of its templates (`using Zenith_Mutex = Zenith_<Platform>Mutex_T<true>;` and `using Zenith_MutexNoProfiling = Zenith_<Platform>Mutex_T<false>;`, and the same pair for `Zenith_ConditionVariable` with `Zenith_ConditionVariableNoProfiling` and for `Zenith_Semaphore` with `Zenith_SemaphoreNoProfiling`), and the public `Zenith_Mutex.h`, `Zenith_ConditionVariable.h` and `Zenith_Semaphore.h` only `#include "Zenith_PlatformThreading.h"` (the one bare include of an engine header, because a platform-neutral header cannot name the platform class without an `#if`), so the selection is platform code in platform files chosen by the build, never a preprocessor conditional (rule F11); `Zenith_LockDesc` has its own public header `ZenithCore/Zenith_LockDesc.h`, which `Zenith_Mutex.h` includes first and the platform header includes for the mutex's desc; the out-of-line definitions stay under `ZenithCore/Internal/<Platform>/`. The Android and Linux files may include `<pthread.h>` and `<semaphore.h>` and no other platform header; the Windows file includes none. No synchronisation type holds an opaque native byte array (`m_auNative` does not exist). These classes are the one case where a type's layout differs between platforms ([Z2-N-CONTRACT-441]); on one platform each template has one layout in every configuration and in both of its instantiations, which differ in behaviour alone ([Z2-N-CONTRACT-589]). |
| Z2-N-CONTRACT-584 | `Zenith2/Docs/Contracts.md`:106 | Zenith2 engineering contracts › API boundary › Library directory layout | The exception of [Z2-N-CONTRACT-583] is signposted in documentation and in code, as every exemption is. In documentation: this bullet and [Z2-N-CONTRACT-583] are its one statement, every document sentence that relies on it links `Zenith2/Docs/Contracts.md → "Library directory layout"` [Z2-N-CONTRACT-583], and `Zenith2/Tools/Gates/platform_headers.json` carries one `publicPlatformHeaders` element per POSIX platform file ([Z2-N-CONTRACT-432]), `{ "path": "Zenith2/Engine/ZenithCore/Platform/Android/Zenith_PlatformThreading.h", "platform": "Android", "headers": [ "pthread.h", "semaphore.h" ], "comment": "ZENITH-EXEMPTION: <text>" }` and its Linux twin, whose `comment` states why the include exists and names Z2-D-141 and the signpost `Zenith2/Docs/Contracts.md → "Library directory layout" [Z2-N-CONTRACT-583]`, in the manner of [Z2-N-CONTRACT-153]; the Windows file has no element, so `<windows.h>` there stays an F18 finding. In code: each of the three platform files carries the four-line comment block of [Z2-N-CONTRACT-578] with the file as its subject, the Android and Linux files directly above their platform includes, as below, and the Windows file directly after `#pragma once`. The `SourceLayout` step accepts these three files outside `Internal/<Platform>/` and no other platform source (rules SL3 and SL4 of `Zenith2/Docs/Design/PROC/BaseSteps.md` → "SourceLayout"), and `ForbiddenCheck` admits the two POSIX includes through the elements (`Zenith2/Docs/Design/PROC/StructuralGates.md` → "ForbiddenCheck allowlist files"). The template form of Z2-D-142 changes none of the three blocks, because each names its file and the exemption, not a class. |
| Z2-N-CONTRACT-585 | `Zenith2/Docs/Contracts.md`:453 | Zenith2 engineering contracts › Contract blocks per type › Block grammar | A `struct` or `class` whose block's `Thread affinity` field begins with `IMMUTABLE` declares its data members `const` wherever that is possible, so the values fixed at construction cannot be written afterwards (Z2-D-141). A member stays non-const only when `const` would break an operation the type needs (copy or move assignment by an API or a container, as by `Zenith_Result` or a sorted or ring-buffered array; default construction followed by filling; a builder or decoder that writes fields after construction), and then the `Thread affinity` field names that operation in one clause, for example `IMMUTABLE. … Members non-const: held as the error of a Zenith_Result, whose move assignment assigns it.` Every `const` data member carries a default member initializer (`const uint32 m_uMajor = 0;`), so the type stays default-constructible, as `Zenith_Result` ([Z2-N-CONTRACT-587]) and a fixed array of the type require, and stays aggregate-initialisable; such a type is not copy- or move-assignable, which is acceptable when no API assigns it (`Zenith_Result`'s move assignment is then never instantiated for it). A trivially copyable descriptor that callers aggregate-initialise and pass by `const&` takes `const` members; one that callers default-construct and then fill does not. Review confirms each judgement. |
| Z2-N-CONTRACT-586 | `Zenith2/Docs/Contracts.md`:1357 | Zenith2 engineering contracts › Tests and code hygiene gates › nodiscard | `[[nodiscard]]` is not written anywhere in Zenith2 code, `Zenith_Result` included (Z2-D-141), except on a line carrying the `// ZENITH-ALLOW: <reason>` signpost of [Z2-N-CONTRACT-582] where a mechanism requires it; `ForbiddenCheck` rule F25 refuses every other occurrence. `[[noreturn]]` is written only on a function that genuinely never returns (the fatal and unreachable handlers behind `ZENITH_FATAL`, `ZENITH_CHECK` and `ZENITH_UNREACHABLE`, the process-exit path), where warnings-as-errors needs it to accept the code after a call; on any other function it is rejected in review. |
| Z2-N-CONTRACT-587 | `Zenith2/Docs/Contracts.md`:1580 | Zenith2 engineering contracts › Errors and results › Zenith_Result | `Zenith_Result<T, E>` stores exactly `{ T m_xValue; E m_eError; }`, and `Zenith_Result<void, E>` exactly `{ E m_eError; }`, in every build: no union, no success flag and no debug-only member, so the layout is identical in every configuration ([Z2-N-CONTRACT-441]) and a non-debug build carries no state beyond the value and the error. `T` and `E` are default-constructible, each checked by a `static_assert(std::is_default_constructible_v<…>)` (a failure result holds a default-constructed `T`, a success result a value-initialised `E`), so every type used as a result value or error has a default constructor or default member initializers on every member, `const` members of `IMMUTABLE` types included ([Z2-N-CONTRACT-585]); a success result holds a value-initialised `E`, whose code is 0, and `IsOk()` is `Zenith_GetErrorCode(m_eError) == 0`, which [Z2-N-CONTRACT-465] makes exact because no error code is 0 (Z2-D-141). |
| Z2-N-CONTRACT-588 | `Zenith2/Docs/Contracts.md`:1262 | Zenith2 engineering contracts › Tests and code hygiene gates › Naming and layout | Zenith2 code names integers only through `Zenith_Types.h` (`uint8`, `uint16`, `uint32`, `uint64`, `sint8`, `sint16`, `sint32`, `sint64`, and `uint` and `sint` for the platform's default `unsigned int` and `int`), never `u_int*`, `intN_t` or `uintN_t`, except at a third-party API boundary inside `Internal/`; and `final` appears only on a class that inherits from a base, never on a class or struct with no base (Z2-D-141). `ForbiddenCheck` rule F25 refuses `u_int*`, `intN_t` and `uintN_t` outside `Zenith_Types.h` and the `f25Boundary` files of [Z2-N-CONTRACT-417], and `final` on a class with no base clause. |
| Z2-N-CONTRACT-589 | `Zenith2/Docs/Contracts.md`:948 | Zenith2 engineering contracts › Threading and concurrency contract › Zenith_Multithreading | Every synchronisation primitive exists in two variants that are the two instantiations of one class template per platform on `bool bProfiled`: the profiled `Zenith_Mutex`, `Zenith_ConditionVariable` and `Zenith_Semaphore`, and the unprofiled `Zenith_MutexNoProfiling`, `Zenith_ConditionVariableNoProfiling` and `Zenith_SemaphoreNoProfiling` (Z2-D-142). The variants are identical except that the unprofiled one never reads the hook table (no `Zenith_GetProfilerHooks`, no `Zenith_Dispatch…` call and no clock read appears in its instantiation) and the profiled one reports to the installed hooks. A profiled `Lock` tries the native lock first and reports the acquisition with a wait of 0, meaning it did not block; on contention it reads `Zenith_GetMonotonicTimeNs()` before and after blocking, only when an acquire hook is installed, and reports the measured wait, at least 1; `TryLock` reports a success with a wait of 0 and calls no hook on failure; `Unlock` reports the release, from a copy of the desc taken before the native unlock. A profiled condition-variable wait reports a release before the native wait and an acquire (wait 0) after it, and the blocking-wait hook reports the time asleep under the paired mutex's `m_szName` (`wait:<mutex name>`); a `Zenith_Semaphore` reports under its optional static-storage name, default `Zenith_Semaphore`. In a Shipping build the profiled lock operations skip the hook lookup altogether, under `if constexpr (bProfiled && !Zenith_IsShippingBuild())`. Lock-level registration, the lock-order checks and the held-lock stack apply to both variants. A condition variable waits only on a mutex of its own variant, and a `static_assert` refuses the mixed pairing. `Zenith_ScopedLock` is a class template on the mutex type. `Zenith_Thread::Join` and `Zenith_SleepUs` call the blocking-wait hook. The earlier rule of `Zenith2/Docs/Design/CORE/Threading.md` that there is no unprofiled variant is withdrawn. |
| Z2-N-CONTRACT-590 | `Zenith2/Docs/Contracts.md`:571 | Zenith2 engineering contracts › No mutable global state › The two installed hooks | Every invocation of the six profiling hooks `m_pfnOnZoneBegin`, `m_pfnOnZoneEnd`, `m_pfnOnCounter`, `m_pfnOnLockAcquire`, `m_pfnOnLockRelease` and `m_pfnOnBlockingWait` anywhere in the engine goes through the inline dispatch functions of `Zenith_ProfilerHooks.h` (below): `Zenith_DispatchZoneBegin`, `Zenith_DispatchZoneEnd`, `Zenith_DispatchCounter`, `Zenith_DispatchLockAcquire`, `Zenith_DispatchLockRelease` and `Zenith_DispatchBlockingWait(szPrimitive, ulWaitTicks)`, which a primitive calls after its wait returns. No code outside that header calls a member of the hook table; `Zenith_GetProfilerHooks()` stays public for tests and for the profiler's own install code. A dispatch does nothing when its hook is null. Otherwise it calls `Zenith_EnterHookDispatch()`, which raises the calling thread's `m_uHookDepth` (a member of the Debug thread state, present in every build), calls the hook, and calls `Zenith_LeaveHookDispatch()`. When the depth is already non-zero the dispatch does not call the hook: `Zenith_EnterHookDispatch()` increments the thread's `m_uHookReentryCount` and, wherever `ZENITH_ASSERTS_ENABLED=1`, fails `ZENITH_ASSERT(false, "profiler hook re-entered: a hook used a profiled primitive")`. So a hook that uses a profiled primitive recurses at most one level in any build, and a debug build reports it; in every build the first refusal on each thread also writes one line through `Zenith_WriteDiagnosticLine`, because the count itself is visible only to that thread. A table in which only one of a pair (zone begin and end, lock acquire and release) is set cannot be installed, so a dispatch needs no pairing logic and a caller keeps no hook pointer between a begin and its end; `Zenith_IsZoneHookInstalled()`, `Zenith_IsLockAcquireHookInstalled()` and `Zenith_IsBlockingWaitHookInstalled()` are the three sanctioned cost gates for work done only to feed a zone, a lock report or a wait duration. The log-record members `m_pfnOnLogRecord` and `m_pfnIsLogLevelEnabled` are not dispatched through this guard: the failure procedure emits its fatal log record from inside whatever hook asserted, and a refused dispatch would drop exactly that record; the log router's own locks are unprofiled instead ([Z2-N-CONTRACT-591]). Z2-D-142. |
| Z2-N-CONTRACT-591 | `Zenith2/Docs/Contracts.md`:572 | Zenith2 engineering contracts › No mutable global state › The two installed hooks | A profiled primitive cannot recurse through the profiler, for three independent reasons (Z2-D-142). Types: ZenithProfiling's code and everything ZenithCore calls from inside a hook dispatch (the clock, `Zenith_SpscRing`, the Debug thread state, the failure procedure, the log router with its sink fan-out and ring locks and its drain thread's semaphore) use only `Zenith_MutexNoProfiling`, `Zenith_ConditionVariableNoProfiling` and `Zenith_SemaphoreNoProfiling`; the lock at level 940 is an ordinary row of the lock ordering table and is not exempted from recording at run time. Gate: `ForbiddenCheck` rule F26 refuses a profiled `Zenith_Mutex`, `Zenith_ConditionVariable` or `Zenith_Semaphore` under `Zenith2/Engine/ZenithProfiling/**`, under `Zenith2/Engine/ZenithCore/Internal/Profiling/**` and in the files of the `profilerReachable` list of `forbidden_allowlist.json`, and refuses a direct use of the hook table outside `Zenith_ProfilerHooks.h` ([Z2-N-CONTRACT-416], [Z2-N-CONTRACT-417]). Run time: the guard of [Z2-N-CONTRACT-590], in every build. The ZenithCore sources a dispatch reaches are listed in `Zenith2/Docs/Design/CORE/Threading.md` → "Lock profiling and hook dispatch". |

## BUDGET

| ID | File | Heading | Statement |
|---|---|---|---|
| Z2-N-BUDGET-001 | `Zenith2/Docs/Budgets.md`:13 | Zenith2 Budgets › Purpose versioning and change rule › Purpose | Every numeric budget limit (a performance, memory, package, storage, capacity, iteration-time or gate-time limit) that Zenith2 code, content or tooling is held to lives in this document as a budget row with an ID of the form `Z2-B-<AREA>-NNN`; a limit stated anywhere else (a design doc, a ticket, a test) cites the row ID instead of restating the number. |
| Z2-N-BUDGET-002 | `Zenith2/Docs/Budgets.md`:14 | Zenith2 Budgets › Purpose versioning and change rule › Purpose | `<AREA>` in a row ID is one of the 41 area codes (CORE, JOBS, PLAT, ECS, SCENE, STREAM, ASSET, PACK, RHI, GEOM, LIGHT, ENV, ANIM, PHYS, AI, GAME, SAVE, AUDIO, UI, VFX, CINE, EDIT, DEVT, SHIP, ADVGFX, NET, MOD, GRAPH, COMPRESS, SCRIPT, TWOD, CONTENT, WORLD, RPROD, CHAR, ONLINE, SEC, MAINT, RESEARCH, SCOPE, PROC), and that area is the row's owner. |
| Z2-N-BUDGET-003 | `Zenith2/Docs/Budgets.md`:15 | Zenith2 Budgets › Purpose versioning and change rule › Purpose | Procedure parameters (repetition counts, sampling rates, and the thresholds and constants inside a measurement procedure or a breach rule) are not budget limits: they are listed in the table "Procedure parameters" under "Measurement procedure catalogue", and a change to one follows the workload change rule (a decision plus a version bump of every workload whose procedures use it). |
| Z2-N-BUDGET-004 | `Zenith2/Docs/Budgets.md`:16 | Zenith2 Budgets › Purpose versioning and change rule › Purpose | Row IDs are assigned only in this document, are never renumbered, and are never reused for a different quantity, including after the row is removed by a decision. |
| Z2-N-BUDGET-005 | `Zenith2/Docs/Budgets.md`:27 | Zenith2 Budgets › Purpose versioning and change rule › Version and adoption | A change to an adopted row's value, unit, tolerance, platform, workload list or measurement procedure requires a new decision, appended to `Zenith2/Docs/DecisionLog.md` in that same diff with the next explicit `Z2-D-NNN` as `Zenith2/Docs/DecisionLog.md → "How this log is written"` defines (`zagent decide --ID Z2-D-NNN` is only an optional knowledge-row mirror and never writes the log), and, in the same diff, a version bump of every workload the row is measured in and a major version bump of this document. |
| Z2-N-BUDGET-006 | `Zenith2/Docs/Budgets.md`:28 | Zenith2 Budgets › Purpose versioning and change rule › Version and adoption | A change to a workload's content, scripted path, duration, graphics settings or metrics requires a decision and a version bump of that workload (`v1` to `v2`), and evidence recorded against the old workload version is invalid for the new one. |
| Z2-N-BUDGET-007 | `Zenith2/Docs/Budgets.md`:30 | Zenith2 Budgets › Purpose versioning and change rule › Version and adoption | Allocating a pending row (writing its first value and changing its status to `adopted`) is done by the row's named producer ticket (a `needs-human` ruling ticket, see "Producer ticket shape") in the same diff that writes the value and appends the allocating decision to `Zenith2/Docs/DecisionLog.md` with the next explicit `Z2-D-NNN`, and bumps this document's minor version (`v1` to `v1.1`, and so on) without a workload version bump, because no measured workload changes. |
| Z2-N-BUDGET-008 | `Zenith2/Docs/Budgets.md`:31 | Zenith2 Budgets › Purpose versioning and change rule › Version and adoption | A row is removed only by a decision; the removed row's ID, name and last value are recorded in that decision's DecisionLog entry and the ID is not reassigned. |
| Z2-N-BUDGET-009 | `Zenith2/Docs/Budgets.md`:35 | Zenith2 Budgets › Purpose versioning and change rule › Row fields | Every row table in this document has exactly the columns of the table below, in this order, and the `Row index` repeats the ID, Name, Status, Value, Unit and Producer columns of every row. |

| Z2-N-BUDGET-017 | `Zenith2/Docs/Budgets.md`:62 | Zenith2 Budgets › Purpose versioning and change rule › Row status and pending rows | In acceptance mode (`-Stage N`), the coverage gate step treats a cited row as existing only when it is present in this document with status `adopted`; a pending row cited by a stage ≤ N ticket fails stage N's acceptance. |
| Z2-N-BUDGET-018 | `Zenith2/Docs/Budgets.md`:63 | Zenith2 Budgets › Purpose versioning and change rule › Row status and pending rows | A ticket body never contains a budget number that is not also a row value here; it cites the row ID, and where the row is pending it says "pending, allocated by <producer>". |
| Z2-N-BUDGET-019 | `Zenith2/Docs/Budgets.md`:67 | Zenith2 Budgets › Purpose versioning and change rule › Producer ticket shape | Every producer that allocates a row is a `needs-human` TASK whose title begins with the row's exact producer name; it writes the values, the allocation table and the DecisionLog entry in one diff, and no agent claims it. |
| Z2-N-BUDGET-020 | `Zenith2/Docs/Budgets.md`:68 | Zenith2 Budgets › Purpose versioning and change rule › Producer ticket shape | A "Stage 1 per-system budget ticket for <AREA>" is split per Z2-D-084 only where Stage 1 has something to measure for that area (a probe on the Stage 0 scaffold, a pulled-forward Stage 2 library, or the reference engine at `C:/dev/Zenith/Zenith`): an ordinary harness TASK titled "Stage 1 per-system budget harness for <AREA>" builds the measurement harness under `Zenith2/Benchmarks/Budgets/<AREA>/`, with a short CTest smoke run inside the normal Test step, and the `needs-human` ruling ticket that carries the producer name is `blocks`-linked from it. |
| Z2-N-BUDGET-021 | `Zenith2/Docs/Budgets.md`:69 | Zenith2 Budgets › Purpose versioning and change rule › Producer ticket shape | Every other per-system producer is a single `needs-human` ruling ticket whose basis is reference-engine data or published data; the table "Per-system producer shape" says which areas have a harness half, and the "Producer tickets" table under "Row index" repeats it in its `Harness half` column. |
| Z2-N-BUDGET-022 | `Zenith2/Docs/Budgets.md`:70 | Zenith2 Budgets › Purpose versioning and change rule › Producer ticket shape | A harness whose measurement basis names a workload's content scale is `blocks`-linked from that workload's Stage 1 workload scale ticket: the ECS harness from "Stage 1 workload scale ticket for W-Dense", and the ANIM harness from "Stage 1 workload scale ticket for W-Characters". |
| Z2-N-BUDGET-023 | `Zenith2/Docs/Budgets.md`:71 | Zenith2 Budgets › Purpose versioning and change rule › Producer ticket shape | The per-system ruling tickets are ordered by `blocks` links: the CORE and JOBS rulings are blocked by no other allocation; the RPROD ruling is `blocks`-linked from the CORE and JOBS rulings; every other per-system ruling is `blocks`-linked from the CORE, JOBS and RPROD rulings, because MP-ALLOC checks every allocation against the reserve and headroom rows those three adopt; a ruling whose row is bounded by another area's row, such as Z2-B-STREAM-006 by Z2-B-ECS-003 ([Z2-N-BUDGET-473]), is also `blocks`-linked from that area's ruling; the "Producer tickets" table lists these links in its `Blocked by` column. |
| Z2-N-BUDGET-024 | `Zenith2/Docs/Budgets.md`:72 | Zenith2 Budgets › Purpose versioning and change rule › Producer ticket shape | Where a harness half exists, the human runs its full measurement on the reference desktop and the S24 and commits the report under `Zenith2/Docs/Research/Budgets/` in the ruling ticket's diff; the loop's worker never allocates a row. |
| Z2-N-BUDGET-025 | `Zenith2/Docs/Budgets.md`:73 | Zenith2 Budgets › Purpose versioning and change rule › Producer ticket shape | The W-Editor row, flagship acceptance, Stage 0 gate-row, gate-activation and Stage 6 Play-limit producers are single `needs-human` tickets with no harness half; the gate producers are PROC tickets that time the step's cold run in the same human session that activates it. |
| Z2-N-BUDGET-026 | `Zenith2/Docs/Budgets.md`:106 | Zenith2 Budgets › Purpose versioning and change rule › Allocation rule for pending rows | Memory rows are allocated against three bounds: on Windows, the rows of bound `MEM-WIN-RAM` sum to at most Z2-B-CORE-002 (8 GB) × (1 − Z2-B-CORE-011) and the rows of bound `MEM-WIN-VRAM` to at most Z2-B-CORE-003 (6 GB) × (1 − Z2-B-CORE-011); on the S24, the rows of bound `MEM-S24` sum to at most Z2-B-CORE-001 (3.0 GB) × (1 − Z2-B-CORE-011); the reserve covers fragmentation, driver overhead and measurement noise. |
| Z2-N-BUDGET-027 | `Zenith2/Docs/Budgets.md`:108 | Zenith2 Budgets › Purpose versioning and change rule › Allocation rule for pending rows | Per-system CPU rows are allocated per thread path of the compiled CPU frame schedule: the rows of bound `CPU-SIM` (the simulation critical path, phases 1 to 12 and the engine services scheduled with them) sum to at most Z2-B-SHIP-001 × (1 − Z2-B-JOBS-003), and the rows of bound `CPU-RENDER` (render-graph build, recording and submit) meet the same bound separately, per platform, because pipelining overlaps the two paths (Z2-D-050). |
| Z2-N-BUDGET-028 | `Zenith2/Docs/Budgets.md`:110 | Zenith2 Budgets › Purpose versioning and change rule › Allocation rule for pending rows | GPU per-pass rows are allocated per (platform, shading path): the rows of the bound `GPU-<PATH>` (common prologue, shadow views, the path's per-view passes for the main view, common epilogue) sum to at most Z2-B-SHIP-001 × (1 − Z2-B-RPROD-028) on each platform the path runs on (Forward+ and Forward on Windows and the S24; Deferred and Visibility buffer on Windows only). |
| Z2-N-BUDGET-029 | `Zenith2/Docs/Budgets.md`:122 | Zenith2 Budgets › Purpose versioning and change rule › Allocation rule for pending rows | The allocation check (MP-ALLOC) is run by every per-system ruling ticket before it lands, and a ruling whose allocation breaks any bound does not land. |
| Z2-N-BUDGET-030 | `Zenith2/Docs/Budgets.md`:123 | Zenith2 Budgets › Purpose versioning and change rule › Allocation rule for pending rows | The bounds and their exact member rows are the table "Allocation sums"; every row of unit `MB` under "Memory budgets", "Rendering rows" or "Scripting rows", and every row of unit `ms` whose name ends in "CPU" or "GPU" or contains "CPU per", is either a member of a bound or listed under "Excluded from every bound" with its reason, and `Check-Allocation.ps1` exits 2 when it finds such a row in neither list. |
| Z2-N-BUDGET-031 | `Zenith2/Docs/Budgets.md`:161 | Zenith2 Budgets › Purpose versioning and change rule › Allocation file and check | Every per-system ruling ticket commits its allocation as `Zenith2/Docs/Research/Budgets/Stage1/<AREA>/allocation.json` in the format of the block "Allocation file format", listing one entry per (row, platform, path) cell it allocates and one `sums` entry per bound any of its rows belongs to; a row of "Approximation error rows" (platform `Host`, a member of no bound, [Z2-N-BUDGET-281]) has no `rows` entry, and its ruled values live in its Value cell in this document alone. |
| Z2-N-BUDGET-032 | `Zenith2/Docs/Budgets.md`:162 | Zenith2 Budgets › Purpose versioning and change rule › Allocation file and check | `pwsh -NoProfile -File Zenith2/Benchmarks/Budgets/Check-Allocation.ps1 [-Area <AREA>] [-Final]` reads the "All rows" table and the "Allocation sums" table of `Zenith2/Docs/Budgets.md` and every `Zenith2/Docs/Research/Budgets/Stage1/*/allocation.json`, recomputes every bound, writes `Zenith2/out/<checkout-hash>/budgets/allocation-check.json`, and exits 0 when every bound holds, 1 when a bound is broken, and 2 when a file is malformed or an allocated value differs from the row's value in `Budgets.md`. |
| Z2-N-BUDGET-033 | `Zenith2/Docs/Budgets.md`:163 | Zenith2 Budgets › Purpose versioning and change rule › Allocation file and check | Without `-Final`, a pending member row contributes 0 and its bound reports `incomplete: true`; with `-Final`, which the Stage 1 acceptance runs, a pending member row fails its bound. |
| Z2-N-BUDGET-034 | `Zenith2/Docs/Budgets.md`:164 | Zenith2 Budgets › Purpose versioning and change rule › Allocation file and check | `Check-Allocation.ps1` and its fixtures under `Zenith2/Benchmarks/Budgets/Fixtures/` are built by the Stage 1 per-system budget harness for CORE, which registers the CTest test `Budgets_CheckAllocation_Fixtures`: one passing fixture, one fixture breaking each bound kind (memory, CPU, GPU), one with a value mismatch and one malformed file, each asserting the documented exit code. |
| Z2-N-BUDGET-035 | `Zenith2/Docs/Budgets.md`:165 | Zenith2 Budgets › Purpose versioning and change rule › Allocation file and check | Units are converted before summing: `GB` and `MB` to bytes by 10^9 and 10^6, `KB` by 10^3, `%` rows to fractions; a member row with two units (for example `entries, MB`) contributes its memory-unit value; a member row with no memory unit in a memory bound, or not `ms` in a CPU or GPU bound, makes the check exit 2. |
| Z2-N-BUDGET-036 | `Zenith2/Docs/Budgets.md`:185 | Zenith2 Budgets › Purpose versioning and change rule › Allocation file and check | A producer ticket that allocates rows commits, with the values, its `allocation.json` (see "Allocation file and check") showing each bound its rows belong to against its limit, and the basis of every split (a Stage 1 harness measurement, a measurement of the reference engine at `C:/dev/Zenith/Zenith`, or published reference-engine data, each cited), and the evidence records of any measurement it used. |
| Z2-N-BUDGET-037 | `Zenith2/Docs/Budgets.md`:186 | Zenith2 Budgets › Purpose versioning and change rule › Allocation file and check | Per-system rows are split from the adopted totals in Stage 1, before most systems exist; the first stage acceptance that measures a system confirms its rows, and a measured breach there follows the regression rule (fix, or re-budget by a decision). |
| Z2-N-BUDGET-038 | `Zenith2/Docs/Budgets.md`:209 | Zenith2 Budgets › Purpose versioning and change rule › Breach consequences | Every row carries exactly one breach code from the table "Breach consequences", and the engine, the tools and the gate steps implement that consequence exactly as the table defines it. |
| Z2-N-BUDGET-039 | `Zenith2/Docs/Budgets.md`:210 | Zenith2 Budgets › Purpose versioning and change rule › Breach consequences | A benchmark regression beyond its row's tolerance blocks the stage acceptance until it is fixed or re-budgeted by a decision; no stage acceptance ticket is completed while a BR-ACCEPT breach against a row it measures is open. |
| Z2-N-BUDGET-040 | `Zenith2/Docs/Budgets.md`:211 | Zenith2 Budgets › Purpose versioning and change rule › Breach consequences | A regression BUG ticket filed for a BR-ACCEPT breach carries `--severity S2_MAJOR` when the breach is within 10% of the row's value and `S1_CRITICAL` when it exceeds 10% or the row is a hard platform total (frame-rate, memory total, cold boot), cites the row ID in `## Budgets`, and names the evidence record path in `## Validation`. |
| Z2-N-BUDGET-041 | `Zenith2/Docs/Budgets.md`:212 | Zenith2 Budgets › Purpose versioning and change rule › Breach consequences | Runtime breach consequences (BR-LUAU, BR-MERGE, BR-FATAL, BR-STALL, BR-REFUSE, BR-GOVERNOR, BR-DEFER, BR-DROP, BR-COOK, BR-LOAD, BR-FALLBACK) are exercised by tests that force the breach, and those tests are listed in the owning area's design doc. |
| Z2-N-BUDGET-042 | `Zenith2/Docs/Budgets.md`:228 | Zenith2 Budgets › Purpose versioning and change rule › Tolerance defaults | A row whose Tolerance column reads `default` uses the default tolerance of its row kind from the table "Tolerance defaults"; a producer ticket may set a narrower per-row tolerance and records it in the row, and a wider one needs a decision. |
| Z2-N-BUDGET-061 | `Zenith2/Docs/Budgets.md`:234 | Zenith2 Budgets › Frame-rate definitions and measurement procedure › Frame-rate definitions | "60 fps" means that, over a run's measured interval, the p95 frame time is at most 16.7 ms and the p99 frame time is at most 20.0 ms (rows "Frame time p95" and "Frame time p99"). |
| Z2-N-BUDGET-062 | `Zenith2/Docs/Budgets.md`:236 | Zenith2 Budgets › Frame-rate definitions and measurement procedure › Frame-rate definitions | Frame time is the interval between the timestamps of two consecutive main-view presents, each taken on the render-submit thread immediately after the present call returns, with the monotonic high-resolution clock (`QueryPerformanceCounter` on Windows, `CLOCK_MONOTONIC` on Android), in nanoseconds; frame `i`'s time is `t(i) − t(i−1)`. |
| Z2-N-BUDGET-063 | `Zenith2/Docs/Budgets.md`:238 | Zenith2 Budgets › Frame-rate definitions and measurement procedure › Frame-rate definitions | The p95 and p99 of a run are computed by the nearest-rank method over the frame times of the measured interval: sort the `N` frame times ascending; p95 is the value at 1-based rank `ceil(0.95 × N)` and p99 the value at rank `ceil(0.99 × N)`; no interpolation, no outlier removal. |
| Z2-N-BUDGET-064 | `Zenith2/Docs/Budgets.md`:240 | Zenith2 Budgets › Frame-rate definitions and measurement procedure › Frame-rate definitions | Every frame-rate workload (W-Indoor, W-Outdoor, W-Characters, W-Dense, W-UI2D, W-OpenWorld, W-Cinematic, W-Game2D) targets "60 fps" on both production platforms, and the frame-time rows apply to every one of them. |
| Z2-N-BUDGET-065 | `Zenith2/Docs/Budgets.md`:241 | Zenith2 Budgets › Frame-rate definitions and measurement procedure › Frame-rate definitions | Background streaming, runtime asset uploads, and shader or PSO compilation and creation always count toward the frame-time statistics; the only frames excluded from a run are those of the initial-load exclusion window. |
| Z2-N-BUDGET-066 | `Zenith2/Docs/Budgets.md`:245 | Zenith2 Budgets › Frame-rate definitions and measurement procedure › Measured interval and the initial-load exclusion | Only the first 10 s after the initial load is excluded from a run's frame statistics, once per run; no other interval is excluded for any reason, including a subsequent load, a world transition, a teleport or a PSO creation. |
| Z2-N-BUDGET-067 | `Zenith2/Docs/Budgets.md`:247 | Zenith2 Budgets › Frame-rate definitions and measurement procedure › Measured interval and the initial-load exclusion | The initial load ends at the first present after the Player emits its world-ready telemetry marker `PLAYER_EVENT_WORLD_READY` for the workload's start scene (`Zenith2/Docs/Architecture.md → "Composition root and Player"`), and that present's timestamp is recorded as `tReadyNs`; the exclusion window is `[tReady, tReady + 10 s)` on the present-timestamp clock, and the measured interval is every frame whose present timestamp is at or after `tReady + 10 s` until the run's scripted path ends. |
| Z2-N-BUDGET-068 | `Zenith2/Docs/Budgets.md`:249 | Zenith2 Budgets › Frame-rate definitions and measurement procedure › Measured interval and the initial-load exclusion | Frames presented before `tReady` belong to the boot measurement of W-Load and are not part of any frame-rate statistic. |
| Z2-N-BUDGET-069 | `Zenith2/Docs/Budgets.md`:250 | Zenith2 Budgets › Frame-rate definitions and measurement procedure › Measured interval and the initial-load exclusion | Every run reports its excluded intervals in its evidence record (field `excludedIntervals`, a list of `{startNs, endNs, reason}`), and a run whose record lists any excluded interval other than the single initial-load window is invalid (BR-INVALID). |
| Z2-N-BUDGET-070 | `Zenith2/Docs/Budgets.md`:254 | Zenith2 Budgets › Frame-rate definitions and measurement procedure › Run length | A workload run lasts at least 120 s and at least 7,200 frames; a run shorter than either is invalid (rows "Minimum measured run duration" and "Minimum measured run frame count"). |
| Z2-N-BUDGET-071 | `Zenith2/Docs/Budgets.md`:256 | Zenith2 Budgets › Frame-rate definitions and measurement procedure › Run length | Both minimums apply to the measured interval, after the initial-load exclusion, so a run's total wall-clock length is at least 130 s plus its boot. |
| Z2-N-BUDGET-072 | `Zenith2/Docs/Budgets.md`:257 | Zenith2 Budgets › Frame-rate definitions and measurement procedure › Run length | A workload whose scripted path is shorter than the minimum run length loops its path from the start point until both minimums are met, and the evidence record states the loop count. |
| Z2-N-BUDGET-073 | `Zenith2/Docs/Budgets.md`:261 | Zenith2 Budgets › Frame-rate definitions and measurement procedure › Warm-up | The warm-up of every workload run is the initial-load exclusion window; qualification runs use no other warm-up. |
| Z2-N-BUDGET-074 | `Zenith2/Docs/Budgets.md`:263 | Zenith2 Budgets › Frame-rate definitions and measurement procedure › Warm-up | A research run additionally discards one complete warm-up repetition, run immediately before its measured repetitions on the same boot of the device, so the measured repetitions start with the OS page cache, driver caches and clocks in steady state. |
| Z2-N-BUDGET-075 | `Zenith2/Docs/Budgets.md`:264 | Zenith2 Budgets › Frame-rate definitions and measurement procedure › Warm-up | Cache state is a run parameter, never a warm-up side effect: a run is either cold-cache or warm-cache, as defined in "Cache state pairs", and the evidence record states which. |
| Z2-N-BUDGET-076 | `Zenith2/Docs/Budgets.md`:268 | Zenith2 Budgets › Frame-rate definitions and measurement procedure › Repetitions and statistics | Research runs use at least 10 repetitions per (workload, platform, backend, configuration) and report medians with 95% confidence intervals, plus the run-to-run variance. |
| Z2-N-BUDGET-077 | `Zenith2/Docs/Budgets.md`:270 | Zenith2 Budgets › Frame-rate definitions and measurement procedure › Repetitions and statistics | The 95% confidence interval of a research median is the distribution-free order-statistic interval: for `n` repetitions sorted ascending, the interval is `[x(j), x(k)]` with `j` and `k` the ranks given by the binomial(n, 0.5) quantiles at 0.025 and 0.975; for `n = 10` that is `[x(2), x(9)]`. |
| Z2-N-BUDGET-078 | `Zenith2/Docs/Budgets.md`:272 | Zenith2 Budgets › Frame-rate definitions and measurement procedure › Repetitions and statistics | Qualification runs use at least 3 repetitions per (workload, platform, backend, cache state), and every repetition individually meets every row it measures; a single failing repetition is a breach, and a median is never used to pass a qualification row. |
| Z2-N-BUDGET-079 | `Zenith2/Docs/Budgets.md`:273 | Zenith2 Budgets › Frame-rate definitions and measurement procedure › Repetitions and statistics | Raw data of research runs (per-frame capture files and evidence records) is committed under `Zenith2/Docs/Research/`, and the report states the hardware, driver, OS build, clock settings and repetition count. |
| Z2-N-BUDGET-080 | `Zenith2/Docs/Budgets.md`:277 | Zenith2 Budgets › Frame-rate definitions and measurement procedure › Controlled and ordinary conditions | Research measurements run under controlled conditions: fixed hardware, drivers and clocks; locked desktop GPU and CPU clocks; and Android fixed-performance mode where the device exposes it. |
| Z2-N-BUDGET-081 | `Zenith2/Docs/Budgets.md`:278 | Zenith2 Budgets › Frame-rate definitions and measurement procedure › Controlled and ordinary conditions | Research reports still state run-to-run variance, because Android fixed-performance mode can overheat the device and does not remove scheduling noise. |
| Z2-N-BUDGET-082 | `Zenith2/Docs/Budgets.md`:279 | Zenith2 Budgets › Frame-rate definitions and measurement procedure › Controlled and ordinary conditions | Shipping qualification runs devices in their ordinary state, including thermal throttling, with no clock locking and no fixed-performance mode. |
| Z2-N-BUDGET-083 | `Zenith2/Docs/Budgets.md`:281 | Zenith2 Budgets › Frame-rate definitions and measurement procedure › Controlled and ordinary conditions | On the reference desktop, controlled conditions lock the GPU core and memory clocks with `nvidia-smi --lock-gpu-clocks=<base>,<base>` and `--lock-memory-clocks=<rated>,<rated>`, and fix the CPU at its base frequency through a power plan with minimum and maximum processor state at the same value; ordinary conditions use the "Balanced" power plan and no locks. |
| Z2-N-BUDGET-084 | `Zenith2/Docs/Budgets.md`:283 | Zenith2 Budgets › Frame-rate definitions and measurement procedure › Controlled and ordinary conditions | On the S24, controlled conditions enable fixed-performance mode with `adb shell cmd power set-fixed-performance-mode-enabled true` before the run and disable it after; ordinary conditions leave it disabled, with adaptive brightness off and screen brightness fixed at 50% for both. |
| Z2-N-BUDGET-085 | `Zenith2/Docs/Budgets.md`:284 | Zenith2 Budgets › Frame-rate definitions and measurement procedure › Controlled and ordinary conditions | Every evidence record states its condition class in the field `conditions` (`controlled` or `ordinary`), and a qualification row is never met by a controlled-conditions record. |
| Z2-N-BUDGET-086 | `Zenith2/Docs/Budgets.md`:288 | Zenith2 Budgets › Frame-rate definitions and measurement procedure › Dynamic-resolution floor rule | The dynamic-resolution floor is 75% of the output resolution: the reference desktop outputs 2560×1440 and the S24 outputs 2340×1080 (row "Dynamic-resolution floor"). |
| Z2-N-BUDGET-087 | `Zenith2/Docs/Budgets.md`:290 | Zenith2 Budgets › Frame-rate definitions and measurement procedure › Dynamic-resolution floor rule | This document reads the 75% as applying per axis (its interpretation of the plan text of Z2-D-012), so the floor is 1920×1080 on the reference desktop and 1755×810 on the S24; the dynamic-resolution controller never renders the main view below the floor in a budget run. |
| Z2-N-BUDGET-088 | `Zenith2/Docs/Budgets.md`:291 | Zenith2 Budgets › Frame-rate definitions and measurement procedure › Dynamic-resolution floor rule | A run that hits the floor (renders at least one measured frame at the floor scale) and still misses its frame-rate target fails; dynamic resolution never excuses a missed target. |
| Z2-N-BUDGET-089 | `Zenith2/Docs/Budgets.md`:293 | Zenith2 Budgets › Frame-rate definitions and measurement procedure › Dynamic-resolution floor rule | Every run records a histogram of the main view's per-frame render scale in 1% buckets (field `dynresHistogram`) and the count of measured frames at the floor (field `framesAtFloor`). |
| Z2-N-BUDGET-090 | `Zenith2/Docs/Budgets.md`:297 | Zenith2 Budgets › Frame-rate definitions and measurement procedure › Sustained thermal run procedure | "Never below 45" means that the 1-second rolling average frame rate stays at or above 45 fps through a 30-minute run, on an S24 that is unplugged, in 25 °C ambient air, and starts the run at 80% battery (rows "Sustained floor" to "Sustained run power state"). |
| Z2-N-BUDGET-091 | `Zenith2/Docs/Budgets.md`:299 | Zenith2 Budgets › Frame-rate definitions and measurement procedure › Sustained thermal run procedure | The 1-second rolling average at present `i` is the number of presents with timestamps in `(t(i) − 1 s, t(i)]`; it is evaluated at every present of the measured interval, and a single evaluation below 45 is a breach. |
| Z2-N-BUDGET-092 | `Zenith2/Docs/Budgets.md`:301 | Zenith2 Budgets › Frame-rate definitions and measurement procedure › Sustained thermal run procedure | The sustained run procedure is, in order: charge the S24 to at least 85%, unplug it, let it idle with the screen off in the 25 °C room until the battery reads 80% and the device's skin temperature has been within 1 °C of ambient for 10 minutes; start the workload with ordinary conditions; run the looped scripted path for 30 minutes of measured interval; record `ambientC`, `batteryStartPct`, `batteryEndPct`, and the Android thermal status (`PowerManager.getCurrentThermalStatus`) sampled once per second. |
| Z2-N-BUDGET-093 | `Zenith2/Docs/Budgets.md`:303 | Zenith2 Budgets › Frame-rate definitions and measurement procedure › Sustained thermal run procedure | The sustained run is measured in every flagship slice and in W-Outdoor and W-Characters (workload set WS-SUSTAIN), and it runs on the S24 only; the reference desktop has no sustained-floor row. |
| Z2-N-BUDGET-094 | `Zenith2/Docs/Budgets.md`:304 | Zenith2 Budgets › Frame-rate definitions and measurement procedure › Sustained thermal run procedure | The initial-load exclusion applies to the sustained run like any other run, so the 30 minutes are counted from `tReady + 10 s`. |
| Z2-N-BUDGET-095 | `Zenith2/Docs/Budgets.md`:312 | Zenith2 Budgets › Frame-rate definitions and measurement procedure › Evidence records and the input fingerprint | Every measured run writes one evidence record as UTF-8 JSON, with exactly the fields of the block "Evidence record format" below; a run with no evidence record has measured nothing. |
| Z2-N-BUDGET-096 | `Zenith2/Docs/Budgets.md`:313 | Zenith2 Budgets › Frame-rate definitions and measurement procedure › Evidence records and the input fingerprint | Every field of the block is present in every record: a field the run does not measure (for example `gfxSamples` on Windows, or `sustained` outside a sustained run) carries its default (`null`, `[]` or `0` as the block shows), never absent, so one reader parses every record. |
| Z2-N-BUDGET-097 | `Zenith2/Docs/Budgets.md`:314 | Zenith2 Budgets › Frame-rate definitions and measurement procedure › Evidence records and the input fingerprint | An evidence record states the tested tree SHA and build configuration (CMake preset and configuration), the workload name and version, the platform and backend, the result per row, and the artifact location of the raw capture. |
| Z2-N-BUDGET-098 | `Zenith2/Docs/Budgets.md`:315 | Zenith2 Budgets › Frame-rate definitions and measurement procedure › Evidence records and the input fingerprint | An evidence record stores an input fingerprint composed of: blob hashes of every source in the transitive target-dependency closure of the code under test (from the CMake File API plus libgraph); the toolchain and `Zenith2/ThirdParty/manifest.json` hashes; the shader-blob set hash; the content and workload version hashes; and the test-harness version. |
| Z2-N-BUDGET-099 | `Zenith2/Docs/Budgets.md`:316 | Zenith2 Budgets › Frame-rate definitions and measurement procedure › Evidence records and the input fingerprint | Acceptance mode recomputes the fingerprint of every evidence record it reads and rejects any record whose fingerprint does not match the current inputs; recency never makes a record valid. |
| Z2-N-BUDGET-100 | `Zenith2/Docs/Budgets.md`:317 | Zenith2 Budgets › Frame-rate definitions and measurement procedure › Evidence records and the input fingerprint | Tests in the coverage gate step prove that a change only in a dependency, a toolchain bump, a middleware bump and a content bump each invalidate an evidence record. |
| Z2-N-BUDGET-101 | `Zenith2/Docs/Budgets.md`:319 | Zenith2 Budgets › Frame-rate definitions and measurement procedure › Evidence records and the input fingerprint | Evidence records of loop and CI runs are written under `Zenith2/out/<checkout-hash>/evidence/<workload>/<platform>-<backend>/<utc-timestamp>-r<repetition>.json` and are never tracked; evidence records of human-run reference-hardware and device measurements are committed under `Zenith2/Docs/Research/Budgets/Stage<N>/<workload>/` together with their raw capture files, compressed with zstd. |
| Z2-N-BUDGET-102 | `Zenith2/Docs/Budgets.md`:368 | Zenith2 Budgets › Frame-rate definitions and measurement procedure › Evidence records and the input fingerprint | The block's field defaults and sources: `framesInFlight` is the configured value (Z2-B-RHI-008); `hitchCount` and `maxFrameMs` are the MP-HITCH results of the measured interval, repeated as `rows[]` entries against the hitch rows; `gfxSource` is `"kgsl"`, `"dumpsys"` or `null` (Windows); `gfxSamples` and `gfxSampleCostNs` are `[]` on Windows; `sustained` is `null` outside an MP-SUSTAIN run, and inside one `thermalStatus` holds the 1 Hz `PowerManager.getCurrentThermalStatus` samples; `latencySamples` holds one entry per MP-LATENCY event, `[]` when not measured; `walkthroughSteps` holds MP-WALKTHROUGH per-step times, `[]` otherwise. |
| Z2-N-BUDGET-103 | `Zenith2/Docs/Budgets.md`:369 | Zenith2 Budgets › Frame-rate definitions and measurement procedure › Evidence records and the input fingerprint | A `rows[]` entry's `statistic` is one of `p95`, `p99`, `max`, `min`, `count`, `total`, `rollingMin`, `totalWorkP95`, `median`, `pass`; the CPU total-work figure of MP-CPUZONE is a `rows[]` entry with `statistic: "totalWorkP95"` against the node's CPU row and `result: "report"`. |
| Z2-N-BUDGET-104 | `Zenith2/Docs/Budgets.md`:371 | Zenith2 Budgets › Frame-rate definitions and measurement procedure › Evidence records and the input fingerprint | The raw per-frame capture of a run is a CSV with the header `frameIndex,presentNs,frameMs,cpuSimMs,cpuRenderMs,gpuMs,scaleX,scaleY,excluded,hitch`, one line per presented frame, written next to its evidence record. |
| Z2-N-BUDGET-105 | `Zenith2/Docs/Budgets.md`:376 | Zenith2 Budgets › Frame-rate definitions and measurement procedure › Measurement procedure catalogue | Every row's Procedure column names one of the procedures in the table below, and each procedure is run by the Player through the budget-run mode (`Zenith2/Docs/Architecture.md → "Composition root and Player"`) with the command-line switches `--budget-run <workload> --workload-version <N> --evidence-dir <path> --cache-state cold\|warm --conditions controlled\|ordinary --repetition <n>`, driven by the workload runner `Zenith2/Benchmarks/Run-Workload.ps1`. |
| Z2-N-BUDGET-106 | `Zenith2/Docs/Budgets.md`:438 | Zenith2 Budgets › Frame-rate definitions and measurement procedure › Procedure parameters | A row is measured only with the procedure its Procedure column names; a measurement taken any other way is not evidence for that row. |
| Z2-N-BUDGET-107 | `Zenith2/Docs/Budgets.md`:439 | Zenith2 Budgets › Frame-rate definitions and measurement procedure › Procedure parameters | The on-screen profiler and the Tracy client are disabled during every budget run except for rows whose procedure reads their data, and the profiler's own overhead rows are measured by comparing an enabled and a disabled run. |
| Z2-N-BUDGET-108 | `Zenith2/Docs/Budgets.md`:441 | Zenith2 Budgets › Frame-rate definitions and measurement procedure › Procedure parameters | Every budget run uses a Release build (`win-release` or `android-arm64` Release); Debug and sanitizer builds are never used for budget evidence. |
| Z2-N-BUDGET-121 | `Zenith2/Docs/Budgets.md`:477 | Zenith2 Budgets › Reference hardware › Reference desktop | Every `Win` row is measured on the reference desktop: a Ryzen 7 5800X-class CPU, 32 GB of RAM and an RTX 3070, at 1440p output. |
| Z2-N-BUDGET-122 | `Zenith2/Docs/Budgets.md`:478 | Zenith2 Budgets › Reference hardware › Reference desktop | Graphics settings are fixed per workload version: the quality tier, dynamic-resolution enablement, upscaler, shading path and every per-feature toggle of a budget run come from the workload definition, never from the user's settings file or command line. |
| Z2-N-BUDGET-123 | `Zenith2/Docs/Budgets.md`:480 | Zenith2 Budgets › Reference hardware › Reference desktop | The reference desktop's GPU driver and OS build are recorded in every evidence record; a driver change between the evidence records a stage acceptance compares is permitted only when the acceptance report states it, because the fingerprint does not cover the driver. |
