| Z2-N-BUDGET-124 | `Zenith2/Docs/Budgets.md`:491 | Zenith2 Budgets › Reference hardware › Reference Android device | Every `S24` row is measured on the Galaxy S24 Ultra, the only Android device Zenith2 is validated on; measurements from any other Android device are best-effort data and are never evidence for a row. |
| Z2-N-BUDGET-125 | `Zenith2/Docs/Budgets.md`:499 | Zenith2 Budgets › Reference hardware › Headless CI hosts | Timings measured on WARP, lavapipe, the `linux-tsan` host or any sanitizer build are never evidence for a frame-rate, CPU, GPU or memory row; the path tracer cap rows and the gate rows, whose platform is `Host`, are the only rows measured there. |
| Z2-N-BUDGET-141 | `Zenith2/Docs/Budgets.md`:506 | Zenith2 Budgets › Memory budgets › Unit conventions | In this document `GB` means 10^9 bytes and `MB` means 10^6 bytes; every memory value is compared against a measurement in bytes, so the S24 total of 3.0 GB is 3,000,000,000 bytes. |
| Z2-N-BUDGET-142 | `Zenith2/Docs/Budgets.md`:516 | Zenith2 Budgets › Memory budgets › Totals | The S24 process RSS stays at or below 3.0 GB, and on Windows the process stays at or below 8 GB of RAM and 6 GB of VRAM, for the whole of every workload run including boot. |
| Z2-N-BUDGET-143 | `Zenith2/Docs/Budgets.md`:517 | Zenith2 Budgets › Memory budgets › Totals | The memory totals apply to the runtime Player; the editor (`win-tools`) is budgeted by the editor rows and the "Editor caches" row, and is not measured against the Windows totals. |
| Z2-N-BUDGET-144 | `Zenith2/Docs/Budgets.md`:521 | Zenith2 Budgets › Memory budgets › Memory governor inputs | The memory governor reads its per-subsystem budgets (rendering, streaming, audio, scripts, editor caches and every other per-subsystem memory row) and its pressure thresholds from the rows of this section, through a generated table, never from constants written in engine code (`Zenith2/Docs/Architecture.md → "Memory governor"`). |
| Z2-N-BUDGET-145 | `Zenith2/Docs/Budgets.md`:523 | Zenith2 Budgets › Memory budgets › Memory governor inputs | The generated table is `Zenith2/Engine/ZenithCore/Internal/Generated/Zenith_MemoryBudgets.generated.h`, produced at configure time from the `Row index` of this document by the budget-table generator specified in `Zenith2/Docs/Design/CORE.md`; it holds, per platform, one `uint64` byte value per memory row, keyed by row ID, and a row still `pending` generates as the platform total with a `pending` flag set, so a pending row never sheds and is reported. |
| Z2-N-BUDGET-146 | `Zenith2/Docs/Budgets.md`:524 | Zenith2 Budgets › Memory budgets › Memory governor inputs | Every transition peak (streaming double residency, world transition, texture swap) is budgeted explicitly by its own row, and the governor's pressure evaluation includes the transition in progress. |
| Z2-N-BUDGET-147 | `Zenith2/Docs/Budgets.md`:525 | Zenith2 Budgets › Memory budgets › Memory governor inputs | Tests force each pressure level (normal, warning, critical) and assert each subsystem's defined shedding; the thresholds they use are the pressure-threshold rows. |
| Z2-N-BUDGET-148 | `Zenith2/Docs/Budgets.md`:573 | Zenith2 Budgets › Memory budgets › Per-subsystem memory rows | The row "String intern table capacity" bounds the process-wide Core string intern table (the configuration keys `strings.maxStrings` and `strings.maxTextBytes`, supplied by ZenithCore's platform configuration fragments); an intern request beyond either cap returns an error (BR-REFUSE); it is distinct from Z2-B-SCRIPT-022, the per-world-session capacity class the script merge point checks. |
| Z2-N-BUDGET-149 | `Zenith2/Docs/Budgets.md`:574 | Zenith2 Budgets › Memory budgets › Per-subsystem memory rows | The row "Log router ring bytes" bounds the runtime log ring size `log.ringBytes` that the composition root applies at startup; it is allocated at least as large as the 1 MiB bootstrap ring `uLOG_RING_BYTES_DEFAULT`, which is a build constant used from startup until the resize and is not this row; when the ring is full the oldest records are dropped and counted in `log.router.dropped` (BR-DROP). |
| Z2-N-BUDGET-150 | `Zenith2/Docs/Budgets.md`:575 | Zenith2 Budgets › Memory budgets › Per-subsystem memory rows | Every allocation the general heap, an arena or a pool makes is attributed to exactly one per-subsystem memory row (or to a scripting memory row) by the memory governor's per-subsystem accounting, and the unattributed residual row bounds whatever the accounting cannot attribute. |
| Z2-N-BUDGET-151 | `Zenith2/Docs/Budgets.md`:577 | Zenith2 Budgets › Memory budgets › Per-subsystem memory rows | S24 Graphics term: at boot the Player tests whether `/sys/class/kgsl/kgsl/proc/<pid>/gpumem_mapped` and `/sys/class/kgsl/kgsl/proc/<pid>/gpumem_unmapped` are readable; if they are, it reads both in-process at 10 Hz (each file holds one decimal byte count) and the Graphics term is their sum (`gfxSource: "kgsl"`); if they are not, the runner samples `adb shell dumpsys meminfo <pid>` every 10 s from the return of `am start -W` (process start) and the Graphics term is that sample (`gfxSource: "dumpsys"`). |
| Z2-N-BUDGET-152 | `Zenith2/Docs/Budgets.md`:578 | Zenith2 Budgets › Memory budgets › Per-subsystem memory rows | The `dumpsys meminfo <pid>` parse rule is: find the line matching `^\s*App Summary\s*$`, then the first following line matching `^\s*Graphics:\s+(\d+)\b`; the captured number is kB and is multiplied by 1,024 to give bytes; output without such a line is a failed sample and makes the run invalid (BR-INVALID). |
| Z2-N-BUDGET-153 | `Zenith2/Docs/Budgets.md`:579 | Zenith2 Budgets › Memory budgets › Per-subsystem memory rows | Whichever source is primary, the runner takes one `dumpsys meminfo <pid>` sample at the end of the run as a cross-check, which is also the run's final Graphics sample; with `gfxSource: "kgsl"`, a disagreement above 5% between the last kgsl sum and the cross-check makes the run invalid. |
| Z2-N-BUDGET-154 | `Zenith2/Docs/Budgets.md`:580 | Zenith2 Budgets › Memory budgets › Per-subsystem memory rows | Every Graphics sample is recorded in the evidence record as `gfxSamples[]: {ns, bytes, source}`, and every `dumpsys` call's duration as `gfxSampleCostNs`; the kgsl source is preferred because a `dumpsys` call binds into the measured process and can perturb the frame-time and hitch rows of the same run. |
| Z2-N-BUDGET-155 | `Zenith2/Docs/Budgets.md`:581 | Zenith2 Budgets › Memory budgets › Per-subsystem memory rows | The row "S24 process RSS" is judged against the MP-MEM S24 statistic, `VmRSS` plus the Graphics term, so its 3.0 GB covers CPU and GPU memory together and every GPU-resident row counts toward it; host-visible GPU memory the CPU has touched can appear in both terms, and the sum is taken as measured, which overcounts conservatively. |
| Z2-N-BUDGET-156 | `Zenith2/Docs/Budgets.md`:583 | Zenith2 Budgets › Memory budgets › Per-subsystem memory rows | The MP-MEM harness carries a device test: on the S24 it allocates a 256 MB device-local, never host-mapped Vulkan image, forces its physical commit by recording one `vkCmdClearColorImage` over the whole image and waiting for that submission's fence, and then asserts that the MP-MEM S24 statistic has risen by at least 230 MB while `VmRSS` alone has risen by less than 32 MB, and, where both sources are available, that the kgsl sum and a `dumpsys` sample agree within 5%; it is built by the Stage 1 per-system budget harness for PLAT, which builds the MP-MEM S24 sampler under `Zenith2/Benchmarks/Budgets/PLAT/`, and run by the human as a device step of the PLAT ruling ticket; the Player's budget-run mode adopts that sampler in Stage 2. |
| Z2-N-BUDGET-157 | `Zenith2/Docs/Budgets.md`:584 | Zenith2 Budgets › Memory budgets › Per-subsystem memory rows | On the S24 every GPU-resident row counts toward the S24 process RSS row; on Windows, GPU-resident rows count toward the VRAM total and CPU-resident rows toward the RAM total, and a row whose data lives in both is allocated one value per side. |
| Z2-N-BUDGET-158 | `Zenith2/Docs/Budgets.md`:603 | Zenith2 Budgets › Memory budgets › Transition peaks and pressure thresholds | The governor's pressure level is the higher of the OS-signalled level (Android `onTrimMemory` levels mapped per `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture.md → "Memory governor"`) and the budget-derived level from the two threshold rows, evaluated every frame. |
| Z2-N-BUDGET-159 | `Zenith2/Docs/Budgets.md`:585 | Zenith2 Budgets › Memory budgets › Per-subsystem memory rows | The row "GPU-scene CPU pending store" bounds the general-heap mirror `Flux_GPUScene` keeps of staged records and pending frees: its maximum is the sum over the four GPU-scene tables of capacity times record size (the whole table set staged on a full-state world) plus the staging hash map; it is a CPU-resident row of `MEMORY_SUBSYSTEM_RENDERING`, a member of `MEM-WIN-RAM` and `MEM-S24`, and is distinct from Z2-B-RPROD-001, the VRAM row of the tables themselves. |
| Z2-N-BUDGET-160 | `Zenith2/Docs/Budgets.md`:586 | Zenith2 Budgets › Memory budgets › Per-subsystem memory rows | The rows "Asset in-flight load bytes", "Asset I/O staging bytes" and "Asset keep-alive cache bytes" bound the configuration keys `asset.load.maxInFlightBytes`, `asset.io.stagingBytes` and `asset.cache.maxBytes`; each is a share of Z2-B-ASSET-001, and the three values plus the registry's resident data and decode buffers stay within Z2-B-ASSET-001's value. |
| Z2-N-BUDGET-161 | `Zenith2/Docs/Budgets.md`:587 | Zenith2 Budgets › Memory budgets › Per-subsystem memory rows | A load whose admission would exceed "Asset in-flight load bytes" waits in admission and is admitted when bytes return (BR-DEFER); a read that would exceed "Asset I/O staging bytes" is not issued until staging frees (BR-STALL); an insertion that would exceed "Asset keep-alive cache bytes" evicts least-recently-used cached assets until it fits, counted in the cache's eviction counter (BR-DROP). |
| Z2-N-BUDGET-162 | `Zenith2/Docs/Budgets.md`:588 | Zenith2 Budgets › Memory budgets › Per-subsystem memory rows | The row "Platform and input memory" bounds the platform layer's event ring, lifecycle transition log, input binding table, mount tables and I/O queues, attributed to `MEMORY_SUBSYSTEM_OTHER`, and is a member of `MEM-WIN-RAM` and `MEM-S24`. |
| Z2-N-BUDGET-163 | `Zenith2/Docs/Budgets.md`:589 | Zenith2 Budgets › Memory budgets › Per-subsystem memory rows | The row "Material constants memory" bounds the GPU material constants pool reported by `Flux.Materials.ConstantsBytes` (`MEMORY_SUBSYSTEM_RENDERING`); the pool grows by resize-and-copy only up to this row, and it is a member of `MEM-WIN-VRAM` and `MEM-S24`. |
| Z2-N-BUDGET-164 | `Zenith2/Docs/Budgets.md`:590 | Zenith2 Budgets › Memory budgets › Per-subsystem memory rows | The rows "Reflection probe and environment memory" (captured and filtered probe and environment textures) and "Baked GI resident memory" (resident irradiance volumes and lightmap pages) are GPU-resident LIGHT rows, members of `MEM-WIN-VRAM` and `MEM-S24`. |
| Z2-N-BUDGET-165 | `Zenith2/Docs/Budgets.md`:591 | Zenith2 Budgets › Memory budgets › Per-subsystem memory rows | The row "Environment resident CPU data" bounds the environment module's CPU-side terrain, foliage and atmosphere data, attributed to the `MEMORY_SUBSYSTEM_STREAMING` share; it is a share of Z2-B-STREAM-001 and is excluded from every bound, because Z2-B-STREAM-001 already counts it. |
| Z2-N-BUDGET-171 | `Zenith2/Docs/Budgets.md`:609 | Zenith2 Budgets › Package and storage budgets › Android package | The Android App Bundle's base module stays within Google Play's base-module download limit, and every other piece of content ships through Play Asset Delivery asset packs (row "AAB base module download size"). |
| Z2-N-BUDGET-172 | `Zenith2/Docs/Budgets.md`:610 | Zenith2 Budgets › Package and storage budgets › Android package | The exact base-module limit is pinned at Stage 6 from Google Play's current documentation by the Stage 6 PACK ticket that pins it, which records the documentation URL and the retrieval date in the row's allocation table. |
| Z2-N-BUDGET-173 | `Zenith2/Docs/Budgets.md`:612 | Zenith2 Budgets › Package and storage budgets › Android package | MP-PKG measures the base module's download size with `bundletool get-size total --apks=<apks> --modules=base --dimensions=ALL` (without `--modules`, bundletool sizes every module installed at install time, install-time asset packs included; the PACK ticket confirms the flag against the pinned bundletool's `help get-size`) over the split APKs generated for the S24 device spec, and reports the maximum; the check runs in the Stage 6 shipping acceptance for every flagship slice. |
| Z2-N-BUDGET-174 | `Zenith2/Docs/Budgets.md`:616 | Zenith2 Budgets › Package and storage budgets › Windows install size | Windows install size is a budget row per flagship slice (rows "Windows install size OpenWorldSlice", "Windows install size CinematicSlice" and "Windows install size Game2D"). |
| Z2-N-BUDGET-175 | `Zenith2/Docs/Budgets.md`:618 | Zenith2 Budgets › Package and storage budgets › Windows install size | MP-PKG measures Windows install size as the sum of file sizes of the directory produced by the packaging step for the slice's `win-release` build, excluding symbol files (`*.pdb`), which symbol retention stores separately. |
| Z2-N-BUDGET-176 | `Zenith2/Docs/Budgets.md`:622 | Zenith2 Budgets › Package and storage budgets › Repository asset storage | Source-asset storage is budgeted: the Git LFS policy and per-game quotas are rows of this section. |
| Z2-N-BUDGET-177 | `Zenith2/Docs/Budgets.md`:623 | Zenith2 Budgets › Package and storage budgets › Repository asset storage | Large binary content is LFS-tracked, because lane worktrees duplicate the working tree; a binary file at or above the LFS tracking threshold row committed outside LFS is a failure of the content validation that the CONTENT area delivers. |
| Z2-N-BUDGET-178 | `Zenith2/Docs/Budgets.md`:624 | Zenith2 Budgets › Package and storage budgets › Repository asset storage | The storage cost of a Z2 lane checkout is measured and recorded in the row "Lane checkout storage per Z2 lane". |
| Z2-N-BUDGET-179 | `Zenith2/Docs/Budgets.md`:626 | Zenith2 Budgets › Package and storage budgets › Repository asset storage | MP-STORAGE measures a game's source-asset storage as the sum of the LFS object sizes (`git lfs ls-files --size --long`) plus the tracked non-LFS blob sizes under `Zenith2/Games/<Name>/`, and a lane checkout's storage as the on-disk size of the lane worktree directory after `lane_init`, including ignored build outputs of one warm `win-debug` build. |
| Z2-N-BUDGET-180 | `Zenith2/Docs/Budgets.md`:656 | Zenith2 Budgets › Package and storage budgets › Package and storage rows | The row "Local DDC size" is the configuration key `cook.ddc.localMaxBytes` of a host's local derived-data cache; it is storage on the cook host, never summed into a memory total, and a store that would exceed it first evicts least-recently-used entries to 90% of the row (BR-DROP), as `Zenith2/Docs/Design/ASSET.md` specifies. |
| Z2-N-BUDGET-191 | `Zenith2/Docs/Budgets.md`:663 | Zenith2 Budgets › Workload definitions › Workload rules | Every workload is versioned (`v1` is the version in force for all ten), and its definition fixes its content, content scale, scripted path, duration, graphics settings and metrics; any change to one of them is a version bump governed by the change rule of this document. |
| Z2-N-BUDGET-192 | `Zenith2/Docs/Budgets.md`:665 | Zenith2 Budgets › Workload definitions › Workload rules | Each workload's machine-readable definition is `Zenith2/Games/<Game>/Workload/<WorkloadName>.workload.json`, with the fields `name`, `version`, `game`, `startScene`, `scriptedPath` (the path of the input-replay or camera-path file), `loopUntilMinimums` (bool), `graphics` (quality tier, shading path, dynamic resolution on or off, upscaler), `rows` (the row IDs measured), and `metrics` (the procedure IDs run); the workload-definition hash in the evidence fingerprint is the SHA-256 of this file. |
| Z2-N-BUDGET-193 | `Zenith2/Docs/Budgets.md`:666 | Zenith2 Budgets › Workload definitions › Workload rules | A workload's scripted path is deterministic: it is an input-replay or camera-path file played back on fixed simulation ticks, so two runs of the same build and workload version execute the same simulation. |
| Z2-N-BUDGET-194 | `Zenith2/Docs/Budgets.md`:667 | Zenith2 Budgets › Workload definitions › Workload rules | The content scale of W-Dense and W-UI2D, and the scripted paths and scene content of every workload, are set by their Stage 1 workload scale tickets, which are planned producers that block their consumers. |
| Z2-N-BUDGET-195 | `Zenith2/Docs/Budgets.md`:668 | Zenith2 Budgets › Workload definitions › Workload rules | A "Stage 1 workload scale ticket for <W>" is a `needs-human` ruling TASK, parented to the epic of the area that owns the workload's primary rows (W-Indoor and W-Outdoor: RPROD; W-Characters: ANIM; W-Dense: ECS; W-UI2D: UI), which commits `Zenith2/Docs/Research/Workloads/<W>.md` (the numeric content scale, the scripted-path specification, the start-scene description and the loop rule) and the `name`, `version`, `game`, `graphics`, `rows` and `metrics` fields of the workload's `*.workload.json`, leaving `startScene` and `scriptedPath` empty. |
| Z2-N-BUDGET-196 | `Zenith2/Docs/Budgets.md`:669 | Zenith2 Budgets › Workload definitions › Workload rules | The workload's scene and replay are authored in Stage 3 by the focused workload-game ticket, which the scale ticket `blocks`; that ticket fills `startScene` and `scriptedPath` to the committed specification and changes no number of it without a workload version bump. |
| Z2-N-BUDGET-197 | `Zenith2/Docs/Budgets.md`:670 | Zenith2 Budgets › Workload definitions › Workload rules | The focused workloads live in the focused workload games `Zenith2/Games/WorkloadIndoor`, `WorkloadOutdoor`, `WorkloadCharacters`, `WorkloadDense` and `WorkloadUI2D`, and the flagship workloads in `Zenith2/Games/OpenWorldSlice`, `CinematicSlice` and `Game2D`; like every game, they contain no C++. |
| Z2-N-BUDGET-198 | `Zenith2/Docs/Budgets.md`:695 | Zenith2 Budgets › Workload definitions › W-Indoor | W-Indoor v1 keeps at least 1M triangles visible (after culling, as counted by the drawn-instance counters) on at least 90% of measured frames, and at least 200 lights and 32 shadowed lights inside the view frustum's light list on every measured frame; the Stage 1 W-Indoor ticket authors the path to meet this, and a run whose counters miss it is invalid. |
| Z2-N-BUDGET-199 | `Zenith2/Docs/Budgets.md`:709 | Zenith2 Budgets › Workload definitions › W-Outdoor | W-Outdoor v1 traverses its terrain at 10 m/s, so 120 s of measured interval covers 1.2 km; the path never re-enters a cell it left during the same loop. |
| Z2-N-BUDGET-200 | `Zenith2/Docs/Budgets.md`:723 | Zenith2 Budgets › Workload definitions › W-Characters | Every one of the 100 skinned characters of W-Characters v1 is animated and skinned every frame at full update rate in the measured runs of the per-system rows, so the rows measure the unreduced cost; significance-based update-rate reduction (an ANIM implementation ticket) is measured separately by the animation budgeting rows it produces. |
| Z2-N-BUDGET-201 | `Zenith2/Docs/Budgets.md`:737 | Zenith2 Budgets › Workload definitions › W-Load | Cold boot takes at most 10 s on Windows and at most 15 s on the S24 (rows "Cold boot Windows" and "Cold boot S24"). |
| Z2-N-BUDGET-202 | `Zenith2/Docs/Budgets.md`:739 | Zenith2 Budgets › Workload definitions › W-Load | A cold boot is the first launch of the Player after a device reboot, with the OS file cache cold and the engine's PSO and driver caches in the state the installed build leaves them after one prior launch; the first launch after install is the cold-cache run of MP-CACHE and is reported against the cold-cache rows, not the cold-boot rows. |
| Z2-N-BUDGET-203 | `Zenith2/Docs/Budgets.md`:758 | Zenith2 Budgets › Workload definitions › W-Editor | W-Editor's iteration times are set by the Stage 1 W-Editor row tickets, and W-Editor rows are measured on Windows only. |
| Z2-N-BUDGET-204 | `Zenith2/Docs/Budgets.md`:772 | Zenith2 Budgets › Workload definitions › W-Dense | W-Dense is run once per ECS backend (custom, flecs, EnTT) for qualification, and every backend a game can select meets the W-Dense rows. |
| Z2-N-BUDGET-205 | `Zenith2/Docs/Budgets.md`:822 | Zenith2 Budgets › Workload definitions › W-Game2D | The three flagship workloads (W-OpenWorld, W-Cinematic, W-Game2D) are equally binding: no stage acceptance that measures flagship workloads is met while any one of them misses a row it measures. |
| Z2-N-BUDGET-206 | `Zenith2/Docs/Budgets.md`:824 | Zenith2 Budgets › Workload definitions › W-Game2D | A flagship playthrough is measured as consecutive segments, each segment meeting the minimum run length, with the initial-load exclusion applied once at the start of the playthrough only; the segment boundaries are the playthrough script's checkpoints. |
| Z2-N-BUDGET-221 | `Zenith2/Docs/Budgets.md`:831 | Zenith2 Budgets › Per-system rows › CPU row statistic | A per-system CPU row is measured with MP-CPUZONE: per frame, the wall-clock span of the named CPU frame-graph node from its start to its completion, including every dynamic sub-job it fans out and joins; the statistic is the p95 of that span over the measured interval. |
| Z2-N-BUDGET-222 | `Zenith2/Docs/Budgets.md`:833 | Zenith2 Budgets › Per-system rows › CPU row statistic | Every CPU row's evidence record also reports the node's total work (the sum of all its zones' durations across worker threads) per frame as a BR-REPORT figure, as a `rows[]` entry with `statistic: "totalWorkP95"`, so parallel efficiency is visible without being budgeted. |
| Z2-N-BUDGET-223 | `Zenith2/Docs/Budgets.md`:834 | Zenith2 Budgets › Per-system rows › CPU row statistic | Every CPU frame-graph node and every sub-job emits a profiler zone named after the node, so every per-system CPU row is measurable without additional instrumentation (`Zenith2/Docs/Architecture.md → "CPU frame graph and scheduler"`). |
| Z2-N-BUDGET-224 | `Zenith2/Docs/Budgets.md`:862 | Zenith2 Budgets › Per-system rows › Frame-phase CPU rows | The save-snapshot row is measured only on frames at which a save is requested, and the playthrough scripts of W-OpenWorld request at least one save per segment; a save-snapshot frame counts toward the hitch rows like any other frame. |
| Z2-N-BUDGET-225 | `Zenith2/Docs/Budgets.md`:899 | Zenith2 Budgets › Per-system rows › Engine service CPU rows | The on-screen profiler is available in game and in editor, on Windows and on the S24, as a runtime toggle with no build flag; it is available in development builds and disabled by default in shipping builds, and its enabled overhead is bounded by the on-screen profiler overhead rows. |
| Z2-N-BUDGET-226 | `Zenith2/Docs/Budgets.md`:900 | Zenith2 Budgets › Per-system rows › Engine service CPU rows | The on-screen profiler overlay shows the live values of the budget procedures: the CPU frame-graph timeline per worker (MP-CPUZONE), GPU time per render-graph pass from the graph's automatic timestamp queries (MP-GPUPASS), frame-time history with hitch markers (MP-FRAME, MP-HITCH), memory including heap, pools, GPU heaps and aliasing savings (MP-SUBMEM, MP-GPUMEM, MP-TRANSIENT), counters including draws, culled instances, streaming and uploads (MP-COUNTER), and Luau time and GC (MP-SCRIPT), each labelled with the row IDs it feeds; Tracy remains the deep-capture tool. |
| Z2-N-BUDGET-227 | `Zenith2/Docs/Budgets.md`:902 | Zenith2 Budgets › Per-system rows › Engine service CPU rows | Scheduler overhead (Z2-B-JOBS-001) is not a single profiler zone, so it is measured with MP-JOBOVERHEAD: the per-frame counter `jobs.overhead_ns` (the sum over threads of iteration wall time minus busy and idle time, i.e. scheduler bookkeeping) at its p95 over the measured interval of every WS-FRAME workload, plus the scheduler-only micro-workload `Budgets_JobsSchedulerOnly`, which isolates the scheduler from job bodies. |
| Z2-N-BUDGET-228 | `Zenith2/Docs/Budgets.md`:903 | Zenith2 Budgets › Per-system rows › Engine service CPU rows | `Budgets_JobsSchedulerOnly` runs, per frame for 7,200 frames, a fan-out of 1,024 empty jobs joined by one continuation and a chain of 64 dependent empty continuations, on 1, 2, 8 and all workers; with empty bodies the frame's wall time is scheduler overhead, reported at p95 per worker count; it is built by the Stage 1 per-system budget harness for JOBS, which runs it on the reference engine's `Zenith/TaskSystem` in Stage 1, and runs on `ZenithJobs` from Stage 2. |
| Z2-N-BUDGET-229 | `Zenith2/Docs/Budgets.md`:904 | Zenith2 Budgets › Per-system rows › Engine service CPU rows | A job queue at its capacity row follows its declared full policy (`Zenith2/Docs/Contracts.md → "Threading and concurrency contract"`); the breach code records the `REJECT` case, in which the submission is refused and the submitter runs the job inline, and the `<queue>.full` counter increments. |
| Z2-N-BUDGET-230 | `Zenith2/Docs/Budgets.md`:905 | Zenith2 Budgets › Per-system rows › Engine service CPU rows | The job system's resident memory (the per-thread deques, the job queues, the background operation table and its ticket array, and the per-slot arenas, reported as `jobs.memory_bytes`; a tool host's tools-host background queue and its table slots are counted there too but exist in no WS-FRAME workload) is bounded by the row "Job system resident memory", and the design's per-slot arena caps are defaults bound to that row. |
| Z2-N-BUDGET-231 | `Zenith2/Docs/Budgets.md`:906 | Zenith2 Budgets › Per-system rows › Engine service CPU rows | The audio mixer callback row is a hard real-time limit measured by AUDIO's callback-timing instrument read as counters (`audio.mixer.callback_share_run_max`, `audio.mixer.overruns`; `Zenith2/Docs/Design/AUDIO.md` → "Measuring the mixer callback row"), never by profiler zones on the device thread: the measured quantity per callback is share = callback duration ÷ duration of the audio the callback rendered (blocks × 256 / 48,000 s, or the delivered frames / 48,000 s when it rendered no block), the statistic is the MAXIMUM of that share over the run, and one threshold, `audio.mixer.overrunSharePercent`, equals the row's value, so a single callback above it is an overrun and a breach. |
| Z2-N-BUDGET-232 | `Zenith2/Docs/Budgets.md`:923 | Zenith2 Budgets › Per-system rows › Decompression throughput floors | The decompression throughput floors are lower limits: a codec whose measured one-shot decompress throughput falls below its row minus the tolerance breaches it. |
| Z2-N-BUDGET-233 | `Zenith2/Docs/Budgets.md`:924 | Zenith2 Budgets › Per-system rows › Decompression throughput floors | MP-DECOMP runs `Zenith_Bench_Compression` (`Zenith2/Benchmarks/Compression/`, built with nanobench) in a Release build under controlled conditions, with one discarded warm-up repetition and at least 10 repetitions of at least 11 nanobench epochs each, and the statistic is the median one-shot decompress throughput (decoded bytes ÷ median call time, MB/s of 10^6 bytes) at the codec's default level. |
| Z2-N-BUDGET-234 | `Zenith2/Docs/Budgets.md`:925 | Zenith2 Budgets › Per-system rows › Decompression throughput floors | The corpus is the benchmark corpus `Zenith_CompressionBenchCorpus` (classes `text`, `json`, `json-small`, `floats`, `runs`, `random`, `mixed`, generated at 16 MiB per class), and each floor row is judged on the `mixed` class, with every other class reported; the S24 run pushes the Android build and corpus to `/data/local/tmp/` and runs it through `adb shell`. |
| Z2-N-BUDGET-235 | `Zenith2/Docs/Budgets.md`:926 | Zenith2 Budgets › Per-system rows › Decompression throughput floors | The benchmark report (`schema: "zenith2.bench.compression.v1"`), committed under `Zenith2/Docs/Research/Compression/Stage<N>/<platform>/` with a `report.md` summary, is the raw artifact of the run; the run's evidence record (the block "Evidence record format", with `workload: null`) names it in `artifact` and carries the two floor results as `rows[]` entries with `statistic: "median"`. |
| Z2-N-BUDGET-236 | `Zenith2/Docs/Budgets.md`:928 | Zenith2 Budgets › Per-system rows › Decompression throughput floors | The Stage 1 per-system budget ticket for COMPRESS is the `needs-human` ruling that allocates both floors; its harness half, the "Stage 1 per-system budget harness for COMPRESS", is the ticket that delivers `Zenith_Bench_Compression` under `Zenith2/Benchmarks/Compression/` (the COMPRESS benchmark, not a harness under `Zenith2/Benchmarks/Budgets/COMPRESS/`) with its `--smoke` CTest run. |
| Z2-N-BUDGET-237 | `Zenith2/Docs/Budgets.md`:964 | Zenith2 Budgets › Per-system rows › Shared capacity rows | A shared-capacity row's value is identical on every worker count and chunk layout, and the merge consumes it in merge order, so the entity that is rejected when capacity runs out is the same on every run of the same replay. |
| Z2-N-BUDGET-238 | `Zenith2/Docs/Budgets.md`:965 | Zenith2 Budgets › Per-system rows › Shared capacity rows | Decompression back-pressure uses the decode queue depth row: I/O is throttled by decode capacity, and a full decode queue stalls the I/O issuer instead of growing. |
| Z2-N-BUDGET-239 | `Zenith2/Docs/Budgets.md`:864 | Zenith2 Budgets › Per-system rows › Frame-phase CPU rows | The row "Environment update CPU" is the summed MP-CPUZONE span of the four phase-1 environment nodes and is a member of `CPU-SIM`; the row "Environment extraction CPU" is the span of the two environment render extractors, which run inside the span of Z2-B-RPROD-003, so it is reported (BR-REPORT) and excluded from every bound. |
| Z2-N-BUDGET-240 | `Zenith2/Docs/Budgets.md`:907 | Zenith2 Budgets › Per-system rows › Engine service CPU rows | The row "Platform pump CPU" is the MP-CPUZONE span of the `Platform.Pump` zone (frame prologue step 1, main thread, counter `platform.pump_us`), measured on Windows and the S24, and is a member of `CPU-SIM`. |
| Z2-N-BUDGET-241 | `Zenith2/Docs/Budgets.md`:908 | Zenith2 Budgets › Per-system rows › Engine service CPU rows | The row "Asset service CPU per frame" is the MP-CPUZONE span of the epilogue engine node `Assets.Service`, which bounds its own work by the configuration key `asset.service.frameBudgetUs` allocated from this row; it is a member of `CPU-SIM`. |
| Z2-N-BUDGET-242 | `Zenith2/Docs/Budgets.md`:909 | Zenith2 Budgets › Per-system rows › Engine service CPU rows | The row "LIGHT CPU on Flux.BuildGraph" is the summed span of LIGHT's zones on the render subgraph's `Flux.BuildGraph` node and its sub-jobs; that work runs inside the span of Z2-B-RHI-004, so the row is excluded from every bound and is judged on its own. |
| Z2-N-BUDGET-243 | `Zenith2/Docs/Budgets.md`:966 | Zenith2 Budgets › Per-system rows › Shared capacity rows | The decode queue depth row counts decode units, each one reserved read or one undecoded section held between its read and its decode, which is the unit of the configuration key `asset.decode.queueDepth`. |
| Z2-N-BUDGET-244 | `Zenith2/Docs/Budgets.md`:967 | Zenith2 Budgets › Per-system rows › Shared capacity rows | The row "Asset registry slot capacity" is the configuration key `asset.registry.maxAssets`: the registry never grows, and a registration that finds no free slot fails with `REGISTRY_FULL` (BR-REFUSE); the row "Asset loads in flight" is `asset.load.maxInFlight`, and a load beyond it waits in admission holding no load record until one returns (BR-DEFER). |
| Z2-N-BUDGET-245 | `Zenith2/Docs/Budgets.md`:984 | Zenith2 Budgets › Per-system rows › Latency and duration rows | The row "Critical asset load latency" is the p95, over the W-Load repetitions, of the time from the request to the activation of a `CRITICAL`-priority load of the W-Load start scene's largest asset. |
| Z2-N-BUDGET-246 | `Zenith2/Docs/Budgets.md`:985 | Zenith2 Budgets › Per-system rows › Latency and duration rows | The row "Async I/O critical read latency" is the p95 of the gauge `io.critical_latency_us` (queue to completion of a critical read) measured while a saturating background load runs, on Windows and the S24; the PLAT harness under `Zenith2/Benchmarks/Platform/` supplies the saturating load. |
| Z2-N-BUDGET-247 | `Zenith2/Docs/Budgets.md`:989 | Zenith2 Budgets › Per-system rows › Latency and duration rows | The row "Resume-state write duration" is the S24 time of the resume-state write performed on backgrounding, from the start of the write to its durable completion; the row "Configuration and bindings build time at startup" is the MP-CPUZONE span of startup step 6, which builds the configuration store and the input bindings, on Windows and the S24. |
| Z2-N-BUDGET-251 | `Zenith2/Docs/Budgets.md`:995 | Zenith2 Budgets › Rendering rows › GPU row statistic | Every GPU pass row is measured with MP-GPUPASS from the timestamp queries the render graph inserts automatically around every pass; no pass adds its own timing code. |
| Z2-N-BUDGET-252 | `Zenith2/Docs/Budgets.md`:997 | Zenith2 Budgets › Rendering rows › GPU row statistic | Where the graph compiler merges consecutive raster passes into one rendering scope, the merged scope is timed as one unit and is judged against the sum of the merged passes' rows; the frame dump names the merge. |
| Z2-N-BUDGET-253 | `Zenith2/Docs/Budgets.md`:998 | Zenith2 Budgets › Rendering rows › GPU row statistic | A pass row is measured for the main view in every shading path that contains the pass; the allocation writes one value per (platform, shading path) cell, and the Deferred and Visibility-buffer cells exist on Windows only, because those paths are desktop only and Forward+ is the only path on Android. |
| Z2-N-BUDGET-254 | `Zenith2/Docs/Budgets.md`:999 | Zenith2 Budgets › Rendering rows › GPU row statistic | Every pass in `Zenith2/Docs/Rendering.md → "Per-pass specifications"` names exactly one GPU pass row of this section as its budget row. |
| Z2-N-BUDGET-255 | `Zenith2/Docs/Budgets.md`:1015 | Zenith2 Budgets › Rendering rows › Per-view Forward+ pass rows | The rows of this table also budget the same pass kinds in the other shading paths wherever a path contains them (Forward reuses the prepass, HiZ, culling, fog, SSAO, screen-space shadows, sky, SSR, transparent and particle rows; Deferred reuses those same rows plus clustering; Visibility buffer reuses the HiZ, clustering, fog, SSAO, sky, SSR, transparent and particle rows, and its culling and raster have their own rows); the table "Allocation sums" is the authoritative membership of each path. |
| Z2-N-BUDGET-256 | `Zenith2/Docs/Budgets.md`:1041 | Zenith2 Budgets › Rendering rows › Forward path pass rows | In the Forward path, lights beyond the per-object light cap are dropped from that object's list by the selection order `Zenith2/Docs/Rendering.md → "Frame anatomy Forward"` defines, and a per-frame counter reports the number of dropped (object, light) pairs. |
| Z2-N-BUDGET-257 | `Zenith2/Docs/Budgets.md`:1079 | Zenith2 Budgets › Rendering rows › Common epilogue pass rows | The frame pacing row's statistic is the p95 of `abs(frameMs(i) − targetIntervalMs)` over the measured interval, where the target interval is the display refresh interval divided into the frame-rate target (16.67 ms at 60 fps on a 60 Hz or 120 Hz display). |
| Z2-N-BUDGET-258 | `Zenith2/Docs/Budgets.md`:1092 | Zenith2 Budgets › Rendering rows › Upload ring and GPU memory rows | The upload ring stalls until space frees, and grows only up to the upload ring hard cap row; a single request larger than the cap is split, or else refused with an error, and ring exhaustion increments a telemetry counter. |
| Z2-N-BUDGET-259 | `Zenith2/Docs/Budgets.md`:1094 | Zenith2 Budgets › Rendering rows › Upload ring and GPU memory rows | Frames in flight are configurable between 2 and 3; every budget run records the configured value in its evidence record, and each workload definition fixes it. |
| Z2-N-BUDGET-260 | `Zenith2/Docs/Budgets.md`:1095 | Zenith2 Budgets › Rendering rows › Upload ring and GPU memory rows | The CPU side of the frame pipeline has exactly one frame of render lag: simulation of frame N+1 overlaps rendering of frame N and never runs further ahead (Z2-D-050), and GPU frames in flight are counted separately. |
| Z2-N-BUDGET-261 | `Zenith2/Docs/Budgets.md`:1096 | Zenith2 Budgets › Rendering rows › Upload ring and GPU memory rows | The bindless heap capacities are fixed per platform at device creation from the bindless heap capacity row, and a descriptor allocation beyond a heap's capacity returns an error instead of growing the heap. |
| Z2-N-BUDGET-262 | `Zenith2/Docs/Budgets.md`:1109 | Zenith2 Budgets › Rendering rows › Views permutations and PSO rows | The view count per frame is capped per quality tier by the view count row; texture views and captures beyond their per-frame row are round-robined across frames, and the main view and the shadow views of visible shadowed lights are never deferred. |
| Z2-N-BUDGET-263 | `Zenith2/Docs/Budgets.md`:1110 | Zenith2 Budgets › Rendering rows › Views permutations and PSO rows | The D24 precision check computes, for each view with standard Z and D24, the worst-case linear-depth step of a 24-bit depth buffer at the view's far distance from its near and far planes (MP-DEPTHPREC); a view whose step exceeds the "D24 depth-precision tolerance" row uses reversed Z with D32F_S8 instead (BR-FALLBACK), as `Zenith2/Docs/Rendering.md → "Render views"` defines. |
| Z2-N-BUDGET-264 | `Zenith2/Docs/Budgets.md`:1111 | Zenith2 Budgets › Rendering rows › Views permutations and PSO rows | The D24 depth-precision tolerance row is allocated below 0.477 m, the ceiling `Zenith2/Docs/Rendering.md → "Render views"` derives for its reference outdoor view; the allocation table states the value's basis. |
| Z2-N-BUDGET-265 | `Zenith2/Docs/Budgets.md`:1112 | Zenith2 Budgets › Rendering rows › Views permutations and PSO rows | The on-disk pipeline-cache size row bounds the persisted Vulkan pipeline cache and D3D12 PSO library files of one game per driver version, measured after a warm-cache run of every workload the game carries (MP-DISKCACHE). |
| Z2-N-BUDGET-266 | `Zenith2/Docs/Budgets.md`:1113 | Zenith2 Budgets › Rendering rows › Views permutations and PSO rows | The cook reports the shader permutation count per game, including every stream-set layout that a vertex precision fallback creates, and a game whose count exceeds the shader permutation limit row fails its cook. |
| Z2-N-BUDGET-267 | `Zenith2/Docs/Budgets.md`:1115 | Zenith2 Budgets › Rendering rows › Views permutations and PSO rows | A qualification run records zero missing-PSO events (the draw-skip and magenta-fallback counters of the missing-PSO fallback both read zero at the end of the run), because PSO precaching makes a miss a defect; development runs report the counters as BR-REPORT figures. |
| Z2-N-BUDGET-268 | `Zenith2/Docs/Budgets.md`:1127 | Zenith2 Budgets › Rendering rows › HiZ on tile-based GPUs | The HiZ mid-frame depth store and reload bandwidth row is measured on the S24 with Adreno counters over the prepass phase 1 store, the HiZ build read and the prepass phase 2 reload, and the D-TBDR (open, Stage 5) measurement reports it for every candidate configuration. |
| Z2-N-BUDGET-269 | `Zenith2/Docs/Budgets.md`:1142 | Zenith2 Budgets › Rendering rows › Virtual texturing and path tracer rows | The per-scene parity tolerances row holds one tolerance pair per parity scene for each comparison the BackendParity and PathParity steps make, and a comparison outside its pair fails the step (BR-TEST); features the capability matrix excludes from a comparison carry no pair. |
| Z2-N-BUDGET-270 | `Zenith2/Docs/Budgets.md`:1143 | Zenith2 Budgets › Rendering rows › Virtual texturing and path tracer rows | WARP and lavapipe path tracer runs are capped in resolution and wall-clock time per reference scene by the path tracer cap rows; the tracer runs to its convergence criterion (per-pixel variance estimate below the variance threshold row, with at least the minimum samples per pixel row), and a run that reaches its wall-clock cap first fails. |
| Z2-N-BUDGET-271 | `Zenith2/Docs/Budgets.md`:1093 | Zenith2 Budgets › Rendering rows › Upload ring and GPU memory rows | The row "Upload ring stall frames" counts the frames of the measured interval on which the upload ring stalled or the frame was deferred because the graph compile's `ExtendReservation` returned `DEFERRED` (counter `Flux.Upload.ReservationDeferredFrames`, `Zenith2/Docs/Rendering.md → "GPU lifetime and synchronisation"`); a frame with both counts once, and its breach consequence stays BR-ACCEPT. |
| Z2-N-BUDGET-272 | `Zenith2/Docs/Budgets.md`:1155 | Zenith2 Budgets › Rendering rows › Lighting environment and terrain pass rows | The row "SSGI GPU" times the `SSGI` pass (trace and denoise) of every desktop shading path on Windows; the S24 runs no SSGI, so the row has no S24 cell, and the pass's own Rendering per-pass table names it as its budget row. |
| Z2-N-BUDGET-273 | `Zenith2/Docs/Budgets.md`:1156 | Zenith2 Budgets › Rendering rows › Lighting environment and terrain pass rows | The row "Probe capture and filter GPU" times the common-prologue `SkyCapture` and `ProbeFilter` passes per frame, including the reflection-probe captures the per-frame capture count of Z2-B-RPROD-023 admits; the sky passes themselves stay under Z2-B-ENV-001. |
| Z2-N-BUDGET-274 | `Zenith2/Docs/Budgets.md`:1157 | Zenith2 Budgets › Rendering rows › Lighting environment and terrain pass rows | The row "Water surface and underwater GPU" times the water surface passes and the `Env_AddUnderwater` composite of the main view in every shading path, and from Stage 5 the prologue `Env_AddOceanFFT` dispatches of the FFT ocean tier; it is a per-view member of every `GPU-<PATH>` bound. |
| Z2-N-BUDGET-275 | `Zenith2/Docs/Budgets.md`:1159 | Zenith2 Budgets › Rendering rows › Lighting environment and terrain pass rows | The row "Detail foliage generation GPU" is the detail foliage share of the procedural geometry regeneration passes timed by Z2-B-GEOM-005; it is reported (BR-REPORT) and excluded from every bound. |
| Z2-N-BUDGET-276 | `Zenith2/Docs/Budgets.md`:1160 | Zenith2 Budgets › Rendering rows › Lighting environment and terrain pass rows | The row "Terrain geometry GPU" is the time of the terrain node draws inside the prepass, opaque and shadow-view passes, measured on W-Outdoor and W-OpenWorld; it is judged on its own and excluded from every bound, because those passes' own rows already contain it. Its value is an A/B difference, because MP-GPUPASS times whole passes and no pass holds the terrain draws alone: per (path, view kind), the p95 of the prepass, opaque and shadow-view pass timestamps of the workload run minus the same p95 of the same scripted run with terrain draws disabled by GEOM's development-only key `geom.terrain.enabled` = false (`Zenith2/Docs/Design/GEOM.md`), 10 repetitions of each, reported as the median and 95% interval of the per-repetition differences ([Z2-N-BUDGET-107]'s enabled and disabled comparison is the precedent). |
| Z2-N-BUDGET-277 | `Zenith2/Docs/Budgets.md`:1171 | Zenith2 Budgets › Rendering rows › Rendering capacity and latency rows | The row "Shadowed local lights per frame per tier" is the per-tier value of the configuration key `render.quality.maxShadowedLights`; a local light beyond it renders unshadowed for that frame and is counted in `Flux.Shadows.Dropped` (BR-DROP). |
| Z2-N-BUDGET-278 | `Zenith2/Docs/Budgets.md`:1172 | Zenith2 Budgets › Rendering rows › Rendering capacity and latency rows | The row "Placed foliage instances resident cap" is the configuration key `environment.foliage.instanceCap` (W-Outdoor's 100,000 placed instances is its sizing case); instances beyond it are shed render-only first, in ascending thinning rank, and counted in `environment.foliage.instances_shed` (BR-DROP). |
| Z2-N-BUDGET-279 | `Zenith2/Docs/Budgets.md`:1173 | Zenith2 Budgets › Rendering rows › Rendering capacity and latency rows | The row "Foliage activation catch-up frames" is the number of frames after a foliage cell's activation within which `environment.foliage.activation_backlog` drains back to zero in the MP-STREAM teleport and rapid-traversal runs. |
| Z2-N-BUDGET-280 | `Zenith2/Docs/Budgets.md`:1174 | Zenith2 Budgets › Rendering rows › Rendering capacity and latency rows | The row "Texture streaming request-to-resident latency" is the p95, in frames, from a texture residency requirement change to the frame on which the swap to the required mip level completes, over the WS-STREAM runs. |
| Z2-N-BUDGET-281 | `Zenith2/Docs/Budgets.md`:1188 | Zenith2 Budgets › Rendering rows › Approximation error rows | Every row of this table is a tolerance of an approximation-error test (MP-APPROX), never a time or memory budget; the test named by the owning design compares the feature with its reference and fails (BR-TEST) when the measured error exceeds the row. |
| Z2-N-BUDGET-282 | `Zenith2/Docs/Budgets.md`:1189 | Zenith2 Budgets › Rendering rows › Approximation error rows | The rows and their tests are: "Cloud layer approximation error" (`Test_Clouds_LayerMatchesReference`, against a CPU integrator of the same model at 256 steps); "Volumetric cloud approximation error" (`Test_Clouds_VolumetricMatchesHighStepReference`, against the same pass at 512 steps with no temporal reuse); "Underwater composite error" (`Test_Underwater_CompositeMatchesReference`, against a 1,024-step CPU single-scattering march over a flat seabed); "Ocean FFT to query-set height error" (`Test_Water_FftFitWithinBudget`, against the fitted Gerstner set, in metres); "Environment render test tolerances" (the FLIP and per-channel thresholds of the environment render tests' golden captures); "SSGI error budget" (the energy, accuracy, stability and off-screen criteria of the SSGI approximation-error scenes, one value per criterion); "Clip compression error" (`Test_ClipCompression_ErrorBoundHeldEveryFrame`, the measured hierarchical error of every joint at every frame against the default ε and the cinematic preset, in metres). |
| Z2-N-BUDGET-283 | `Zenith2/Docs/Budgets.md`:1158 | Zenith2 Budgets › Rendering rows › Lighting environment and terrain pass rows | The row Z2-B-ENV-001 "Sky and atmosphere GPU" times LIGHT's sky pass `SkyAtmosphere` together with ENV's cloud passes: `Env_AddCloudLayer`, recorded once per frame before `SkyAtmosphere`, and from Stage 5 `Env_AddVolumetricClouds` and `Env_AddCloudShadowMap`. |
| Z2-N-BUDGET-291 | `Zenith2/Docs/Budgets.md`:1199 | Zenith2 Budgets › Scripting rows › Script CPU budgets | Script CPU budgets count safepoints (Z2-D-052); the safepoint budget rows are the limits the interrupt callback enforces, and exhausting one raises a controlled Luau error inside the invocation's transaction. |
| Z2-N-BUDGET-292 | `Zenith2/Docs/Budgets.md`:1201 | Zenith2 Budgets › Scripting rows › Script CPU budgets | The chunk-system safepoint budget applies per entity (the counter resets at the start of each entity's logical transaction), so whether a budget is exhausted never depends on chunk size or layout; the behaviour safepoint budget applies per behaviour callback (this follows from Z2-D-019 and Z2-D-051 and is confirmed or refined by D-SCRIPT-TXN refinement (open, Stage 1), whose ruling the Stage 1 per-system budget ticket for SCRIPT is `blocks`-linked from). |
| Z2-N-BUDGET-293 | `Zenith2/Docs/Budgets.md`:1202 | Zenith2 Budgets › Scripting rows › Script CPU budgets | Tripping the wall-clock watchdog is a fatal, non-replayable diagnostic event, never a recoverable rollback, and its threshold is the watchdog row. |
| Z2-N-BUDGET-294 | `Zenith2/Docs/Budgets.md`:1203 | Zenith2 Budgets › Scripting rows › Script CPU budgets | The script phase CPU rows are measured in wall-clock with MP-SCRIPT for profiling and budgeting only; no runtime decision reads wall-clock time except the watchdog. |
| Z2-N-BUDGET-295 | `Zenith2/Docs/Budgets.md`:1207 | Zenith2 Budgets › Scripting rows › Luau memory and garbage collection | Every world has a per-world GC step budget row, in kilobytes of collector work per VM per tick, and the script runtime steps the Luau garbage collector of each of the world's VMs within that allowance every tick: a worker VM's allowance resets at its first chunk-system sub-job of the tick and the main VM's at the world's tick boundary, and the allowance is four times the row's value while the memory governor reports `MEMORY_PRESSURE_CRITICAL` (`Zenith2/Docs/Scripting.md → "VM model and threading"`). |
| Z2-N-BUDGET-296 | `Zenith2/Docs/Budgets.md`:1208 | Zenith2 Budgets › Scripting rows › Luau memory and garbage collection | Luau memory caps are enforced through the allocator each VM is created with: the world main VM against its row, and each per-worker chunk-system VM against the per-worker row; hitting a VM memory cap is fatal and not replayable (BR-FATAL: `SCRIPT_ERROR_KIND_VM_MEMORY`, the world halts), never a controlled Luau error, because a VM's heap occupancy depends on which invocations shared it and so varies with worker count (`Zenith2/Docs/Scripting.md → "VM model and threading"`). |
| Z2-N-BUDGET-297 | `Zenith2/Docs/Budgets.md`:1209 | Zenith2 Budgets › Scripting rows › Luau memory and garbage collection | The VM memory cap rows are sized with headroom over the per-entity snapshot and behaviour-state rows, so content within those budgets never reaches a VM cap, and a workload run that reaches one fails its acceptance. |
| Z2-N-BUDGET-298 | `Zenith2/Docs/Budgets.md`:1210 | Zenith2 Budgets › Scripting rows › Luau memory and garbage collection | World creation refuses a configuration whose per-worker VM cap times its worker count exceeds the row "VM memory times workers per world" (BR-REFUSE). |
| Z2-N-BUDGET-299 | `Zenith2/Docs/Budgets.md`:1211 | Zenith2 Budgets › Scripting rows › Luau memory and garbage collection | One VM per worker thread per world is loaded lazily for chunk systems (Z2-D-018), and the product of the per-worker VM memory cap and the world's worker count is capped by the row "VM memory times workers per world". |
| Z2-N-BUDGET-300 | `Zenith2/Docs/Budgets.md`:1215 | Zenith2 Budgets › Scripting rows › Snapshot and checkpoint rows | Snapshot memory has a hard per-entity cap and a per-tick total, both rows of this section; exceeding the per-entity cap raises a Luau error for that entity, which triggers the entity rollback. |
| Z2-N-BUDGET-301 | `Zenith2/Docs/Budgets.md`:1216 | Zenith2 Budgets › Scripting rows › Snapshot and checkpoint rows | The per-entity snapshot cap is per entity, never per call, so it does not vary with chunk size. |
| Z2-N-BUDGET-302 | `Zenith2/Docs/Budgets.md`:1217 | Zenith2 Budgets › Scripting rows › Snapshot and checkpoint rows | The behaviour-state checkpoint is serialized into reserved memory inside the transaction before commit, only when the declared state is dirty and at most once per tick, and its cost is budgeted by the checkpoint cost rows. |
| Z2-N-BUDGET-303 | `Zenith2/Docs/Budgets.md`:1262 | Zenith2 Budgets › Scripting rows › Scripting row table | The per-entity snapshot cap is the only snapshot limit a script can trip: exceeding it raises a `SNAPSHOT_CAP` Luau error inside that entity's transaction (BR-LUAU); the per-tick snapshot total is a measured and reported row (BR-REPORT) that never causes a rollback, because the per-chunk snapshot total depends on chunk layout (`Zenith2/Docs/Scripting.md → "Budgets safepoints and the watchdog"`; confirmed by D-SCRIPT-TXN refinement (open, Stage 1)). |
| Z2-N-BUDGET-304 | `Zenith2/Docs/Budgets.md`:1264 | Zenith2 Budgets › Scripting rows › Scripting row table | The string-intern, audio-trigger, VFX-trigger, save-request and per-source asynchronous capacity rows are capacity classes checked at the merge point in merge order (BR-MERGE), as `Zenith2/Docs/Scripting.md → "Merge-time capacity and asynchronous results"` defines; the recovery and module-load safepoint rows are enforced by the same interrupt callback as the invocation safepoint rows. |
| Z2-N-BUDGET-321 | `Zenith2/Docs/Budgets.md`:1270 | Zenith2 Budgets › Editor iteration rows › Iteration targets | Editor iteration targets are the numeric W-Editor rows: script hot reload, shader blob reload, asset reimport, scene open and play-in-editor start, each with a Windows limit. |
| Z2-N-BUDGET-322 | `Zenith2/Docs/Budgets.md`:1272 | Zenith2 Budgets › Editor iteration rows › Iteration targets | Each iteration row's operation is timed by MP-EDIT from the triggering event to the operation's completion event: script hot reload from the source file's save to the first tick that runs the new bytecode; shader blob reload from the `.slang` save to the first frame drawn with the rebuilt PSOs; asset reimport from the source file's save (or the reimport command) to the first frame showing the reimported asset; scene open from the open command to the first editor frame with the scene fully loaded; play-in-editor start from the Play command to the first simulated tick of the play world. |
| Z2-N-BUDGET-323 | `Zenith2/Docs/Budgets.md`:1273 | Zenith2 Budgets › Editor iteration rows › Iteration targets | A shader parameter-struct layout change needs a C++ rebuild and is outside the shader blob reload row; the editor reports that a rebuild is needed instead of timing a reload. |
| Z2-N-BUDGET-324 | `Zenith2/Docs/Budgets.md`:1308 | Zenith2 Budgets › Editor iteration rows › Usability walkthrough targets | Editor usability acceptance is a set of scripted task walkthroughs with time targets (build a scene, author a prefab variant, iterate a script, reimport an asset), each a row of this section, run by a human with MP-WALKTHROUGH. |
| Z2-N-BUDGET-325 | `Zenith2/Docs/Budgets.md`:1309 | Zenith2 Budgets › Editor iteration rows › Usability walkthrough targets | The complete character workflow (import, rig and retarget, author the animation graph, preview, in game) and the complete audio workflow (import, bank and event authoring, mix, audition in context) are timed scripted walkthroughs with their own rows. |
| Z2-N-BUDGET-326 | `Zenith2/Docs/Budgets.md`:1311 | Zenith2 Budgets › Editor iteration rows › Usability walkthrough targets | Each walkthrough's script is a numbered step list committed at `Zenith2/Docs/Research/Walkthroughs/<RowName>.md`, written by the walkthrough row's producer; the human follows it without prior rehearsal on the build under test, and the evidence record stores the per-step times. |
| Z2-N-BUDGET-327 | `Zenith2/Docs/Budgets.md`:1299 | Zenith2 Budgets › Editor iteration rows › Iteration targets | The row "Incremental no-op cook time per slice" is the MP-EDIT time of an incremental cook of a flagship slice whose sources are unchanged since the previous cook, from the cook command to its completion; it is part of the asset reimport row Z2-B-EDIT-004's cost and is allocated by the ASSET per-system ticket. |
| Z2-N-BUDGET-328 | `Zenith2/Docs/Budgets.md`:1301 | Zenith2 Budgets › Editor iteration rows › Iteration targets | The row "Terrain edit delta memory per undo step" bounds the editor memory one terrain sculpt or paint undo step holds, and the row "Terrain sculpt preview latency" is the MP-EDIT time from a brush stroke sample to the first editor frame that shows its displaced terrain; both are editor-only rows of the W-Editor workload, allocated by the EDIT per-system ticket. |
| Z2-N-BUDGET-341 | `Zenith2/Docs/Budgets/GateRows.md`:9 | Zenith2 Budgets: gate budget rows › Phase caps | Each warm gate phase run (measure, and verify) is capped at 30 minutes of wall-clock by the generic `phaseTimeoutMinutes` setting; the cap is enforced, not only reported (Z2-D-028). |
| Z2-N-BUDGET-342 | `Zenith2/Docs/Budgets/GateRows.md`:10 | Zenith2 Budgets: gate budget rows › Phase caps | The single tree-change retry is counted separately from the phase it retries, with the same 30-minute cap. |
| Z2-N-BUDGET-343 | `Zenith2/Docs/Budgets/GateRows.md`:11 | Zenith2 Budgets: gate budget rows › Phase caps | When a phase exceeds its cap, `tick_gates.ps1` kills the running line's process tree, fails the phase with exit 4, records the killed line in `steps`, and never flake-retries a phase-cap kill. |
| Z2-N-BUDGET-344 | `Zenith2/Docs/Budgets/GateRows.md`:12 | Zenith2 Budgets: gate budget rows › Phase caps | A phase that starts from an empty or invalidated build tree (a new lane, or a preset or CMake change), detected by the Build step, gets the separate, larger cold cap of the row "Cold phase cap", and the gate output reports that the cold cap applied. |
| Z2-N-BUDGET-345 | `Zenith2/Docs/Budgets/GateRows.md`:13 | Zenith2 Budgets: gate budget rows › Phase caps | Stage acceptance suites are exempt from the 30-minute cap; they run through `Run-Committed.ps1 -Acceptance -Stage N` under the `Zenith2Acceptance` category's longer `phaseTimeoutMinutes`, which is the row "Acceptance phase timeout". |
| Z2-N-BUDGET-346 | `Zenith2/Docs/Budgets/GateRows.md`:25 | Zenith2 Budgets: gate budget rows › Per-step hard timeouts | Each gate step has its own hard timeout, sized for a cold build (a new lane, or a preset or CMake change); incremental builds are expected far below it, and a step that exceeds its timeout fails the phase like a phase-cap kill. |
| Z2-N-BUDGET-347 | `Zenith2/Docs/Budgets/GateRows.md`:26 | Zenith2 Budgets: gate budget rows › Per-step hard timeouts | `Zenith2Memory`'s hand-over line is `pwsh -NoProfile -File Zenith2\Tools\Gates\Test.ps1 -Label memory`, a re-run of the base Test step filtered to the CTest tests labelled `memory`; its timeout is Z2-B-PROC-011. |
| Z2-N-BUDGET-348 | `Zenith2/Docs/Budgets/GateRows.md`:27 | Zenith2 Budgets: gate budget rows › Per-step hard timeouts | `ShaderChecks.ps1` (in `Zenith2Render`'s gates) and `EcsConformance.ps1` (in `Zenith2ECS`'s gates, running the ECS conformance suite against the custom, flecs and EnTT backend targets) are gate lines from hand-over, written by the Stage 0 base gate-step ticket, and pass with a report line while no shader source or ECS backend target exists; their timeout rows are allocated when a real run first exists and are sized for all three backends. |
| Z2-N-BUDGET-349 | `Zenith2/Docs/Budgets/GateRows.md`:28 | Zenith2 Budgets: gate budget rows › Per-step hard timeouts | A step's timeout row is allocated by the PROC ticket that activates the step, from a measured cold run of that step on the gate host, and the allocation table records the measured cold time. |
| Z2-N-BUDGET-350 | `Zenith2/Docs/Budgets/GateRows.md`:59 | Zenith2 Budgets: gate budget rows › Gates stay fast | Gates stay fast through four mechanisms, each of which Z2 gate steps preserve: incremental builds; change-triggered heavy suites (sanitizers through `Zenith2Memory`, parity through `Zenith2Render`); the 30-minute phase cap; and stage acceptance suites run outside the loop. |
| Z2-N-BUDGET-351 | `Zenith2/Docs/Budgets/GateRows.md`:60 | Zenith2 Budgets: gate budget rows › Gates stay fast | A compiler cache (sccache) is pinned for MSVC and NDK clang and shared across lanes, and `win-debug` always builds with `/Gy-` so that libgraph's symbol stage reuses the win-debug build with no fourth build flavour. |
| Z2-N-BUDGET-352 | `Zenith2/Docs/Budgets/GateRows.md`:61 | Zenith2 Budgets: gate budget rows › Gates stay fast | Android arm64 compile and the APK check are part of the base `Zenith2` category's gates on every ticket from Stage 2, and their step timeouts count toward the warm phase caps. |
| Z2-N-BUDGET-353 | `Zenith2/Docs/Budgets/GateRows.md`:63 | Zenith2 Budgets: gate budget rows › Gates stay fast | Every gate run's `tick_gates.json` step timings are the MP-GATE evidence for the gate rows; a warm phase whose measured time exceeds 80% of its cap on three consecutive tickets triggers a MAINT ticket to restore headroom, filed by the tick orchestrator. |
| Z2-N-BUDGET-354 | `Zenith2/Docs/Budgets/GateRows.md`:67 | Zenith2 Budgets: gate budget rows › CI integration smoke | The CI integration smoke boots every workload and flagship game in the device-free headless runtime configuration (no Flux, no window, no RHI device, `Zenith2/Docs/Architecture.md → "Multiplayer foundations"` [Z2-N-ARCH-785]), runs 300 frames of its scripted playthrough, and passes only when every correctness assert of that game passes (row "Integration smoke frame count"); WARP and lavapipe host the GPU test executables and render tests ([Z2-N-ARCH-793]), never this smoke. |
| Z2-N-BUDGET-355 | `Zenith2/Docs/Budgets/GateRows.md`:68 | Zenith2 Budgets: gate budget rows › CI integration smoke | The integration smoke is a CI-only step of `Run-Committed.ps1`, outside every category's `gates` list: a Stage 3 PROC ticket adds it to `Run-Committed.ps1`'s CI-only step list, `/tick` never runs it, and it runs in `zenith2-gates.yml` when a pushed change touches an engine path: `Zenith2/Engine/**`, `Zenith2/Player/**`, `Zenith2/Shaders/**`, `Zenith2/Tools/ZenithCook/**`, `Zenith2/Games/**` (a workload's scripted path or content) or `Zenith2/ThirdParty/manifest.json`. |
| Z2-N-BUDGET-356 | `Zenith2/Docs/Budgets/GateRows.md`:69 | Zenith2 Budgets: gate budget rows › CI integration smoke | The integration smoke's step timeout row bounds its CI job and is outside the 30-minute warm phase caps, and its timings are never evidence for a frame-rate row. |
| Z2-N-BUDGET-371 | `Zenith2/Docs/Budgets.md`:1336 | Zenith2 Budgets › Measured in every workload run | Every workload run measures: cold versus warm caches (shader, PSO and DDC); teleport and rapid-traversal streaming (where the workload streams); transient memory peaks; input-to-photon latency; individual hitches (the count of frames over 33.3 ms, and the maximum frame time); and, in W-Editor, editor iteration (script, shader and asset hot reload). |
| Z2-N-BUDGET-372 | `Zenith2/Docs/Budgets.md`:1341 | Zenith2 Budgets › Measured in every workload run › Cache state pairs | A cold-cache run starts with the engine's PSO cache files, the Vulkan pipeline cache or D3D12 PSO library, the driver shader cache and the derived-data cache (DDC) deleted for the build under test, exactly as after a fresh install; a warm-cache run starts after one complete run of the same build and workload version has populated them. |
| Z2-N-BUDGET-373 | `Zenith2/Docs/Budgets.md`:1342 | Zenith2 Budgets › Measured in every workload run › Cache state pairs | Every frame-rate workload is measured as a cold-cache and warm-cache pair; the warm-cache run is judged against every frame-rate, hitch and per-system row, and the cold-cache run against the cold-cache hitch rows and the frame-time rows. |
| Z2-N-BUDGET-374 | `Zenith2/Docs/Budgets.md`:1347 | Zenith2 Budgets › Measured in every workload run › Teleport and rapid traversal | MP-STREAM performs, in each WS-STREAM workload run, five scripted teleports to fixed destinations at least 1 km apart and one rapid-traversal leg at the workload's maximum traversal speed; teleport-to-resident time runs from the teleport command's sync point to the first frame at which every cell and texture the destination view requires is resident at its required LOD and mip, and a residency miss is a frame that draws any required asset below its required LOD or mip. |
| Z2-N-BUDGET-375 | `Zenith2/Docs/Budgets.md`:1348 | Zenith2 Budgets › Measured in every workload run › Teleport and rapid traversal | A teleport resets physics velocity, interpolation, animation root and the render motion-vector history, so the frames after a teleport show no velocity smear; the frames after a teleport count toward the frame-rate and hitch rows like any other. |
| Z2-N-BUDGET-376 | `Zenith2/Docs/Budgets.md`:1352 | Zenith2 Budgets › Measured in every workload run › Transient memory peaks | The transient memory peak is measured with aliasing on (the budgeted configuration) and with aliasing off (reported), and the on-screen profiler shows transient memory with and without aliasing, and the savings. |
| Z2-N-BUDGET-377 | `Zenith2/Docs/Budgets.md`:1356 | Zenith2 Budgets › Measured in every workload run › Input-to-photon latency | The input-to-photon latency row accounts for the extra frame of CPU render lag that pipelining through render extraction adds (Z2-D-050), and for the GPU frames in flight; its allocation table decomposes the value into input sampling, simulation, the pipelining frame, GPU frames in flight, and presentation. |
| Z2-N-BUDGET-378 | `Zenith2/Docs/Budgets.md`:1358 | Zenith2 Budgets › Measured in every workload run › Input-to-photon latency | MP-LATENCY injects one synthetic input event every 2 s at the platform layer with a recorded timestamp, identifies the first present whose simulation consumed it (through an input-sequence number carried from the Input phase into `Flux_RenderWorld` and on to the present), and takes the display time of that present from `VK_KHR_present_wait` on Windows Vulkan, `IDXGISwapChain::GetFrameStatistics` on D3D12 and `VK_GOOGLE_display_timing` on the S24; the latency of the event is the display time minus the injection timestamp. |
| Z2-N-BUDGET-379 | `Zenith2/Docs/Budgets.md`:1360 | Zenith2 Budgets › Measured in every workload run › Input-to-photon latency | Stage 6 qualification confirms MP-LATENCY against a physical measurement (a photodiode or 240 fps camera over the display with a hardware input trigger), run by a human for each platform; the engine-side estimate is evidence only once a qualification report shows it within 5 ms of the physical measurement. |
| Z2-N-BUDGET-380 | `Zenith2/Docs/Budgets.md`:1364 | Zenith2 Budgets › Measured in every workload run › Hitches | A hitch is a measured frame whose frame time is greater than 33.3 ms; each run reports the hitch count and the maximum frame time of its measured interval. |
| Z2-N-BUDGET-401 | `Zenith2/Docs/Budgets.md`:1383 | Zenith2 Budgets › Production-quality acceptance › Separation from research measurements | Production-quality acceptance is defined in Stage 1, implemented incrementally, and kept separate from the research measurements of ECS backends and shading paths; a research result never satisfies a production-quality acceptance item, and a production-quality measurement never substitutes for a research run. |
| Z2-N-BUDGET-402 | `Zenith2/Docs/Budgets.md`:1384 | Zenith2 Budgets › Production-quality acceptance › Separation from research measurements | Production-quality acceptance comprises exactly: the feature × platform quality and fallback matrix (`Zenith2/Docs/QualityMatrix.md`); the content specifications (`Zenith2/Docs/ContentSpecs.md`); the editor iteration targets (the W-Editor rows); and the acceptance package of each of the three flagship slices. |
| Z2-N-BUDGET-403 | `Zenith2/Docs/Budgets.md`:1392 | Zenith2 Budgets › Production-quality acceptance › QualityMatrix schema | `Zenith2/Docs/QualityMatrix.md` has exactly these `##` sections, in order: `## Purpose`, `## Quality tiers`, `## Rendering features`, `## Animation features`, `## Audio features`, `## UI features`, `## Change rule`. |
| Z2-N-BUDGET-404 | `Zenith2/Docs/Budgets.md`:1393 | Zenith2 Budgets › Production-quality acceptance › QualityMatrix schema | `## Quality tiers` holds one table with the columns `\| Tier ID \| Platform \| Description \| Output resolution \| Dynamic resolution range \| Upscaler \| Frames in flight \|`, with Tier IDs of the form `QUALITY_TIER_<PLATFORM>_<NAME>` (for example `QUALITY_TIER_S24_DEFAULT`), and is the tier list that the per-tier rows of this document (view count, texture views and captures) allocate against. |
| Z2-N-BUDGET-405 | `Zenith2/Docs/Budgets.md`:1394 | Zenith2 Budgets › Production-quality acceptance › QualityMatrix schema | Each feature section holds one table with the columns `\| Feature ID \| Feature \| Area \| Requirements \| Tier ID \| Windows technique \| S24 technique \| Fallback \| Fallback trigger \| Acceptance kind \| Reference \| Threshold \| Budget rows \|`, one row per (feature, tier). |
| Z2-N-BUDGET-406 | `Zenith2/Docs/Budgets.md`:1395 | Zenith2 Budgets › Production-quality acceptance › QualityMatrix schema | Feature IDs have the form `Z2-Q-<AREA>-NNN`, are unique across the file, are never reused, and every feature's `Requirements` cell lists the `Z2-R-<AREA>-NNN` requirement IDs it realises. |
| Z2-N-BUDGET-407 | `Zenith2/Docs/Budgets.md`:1396 | Zenith2 Budgets › Production-quality acceptance › QualityMatrix schema | `Acceptance kind` is `golden` or `human`: a `golden` row's `Reference` is the repo-relative path of its golden capture and its `Threshold` is a FLIP mean threshold plus per-channel thresholds written `FLIP<=x; R<=a G<=b B<=c`; a `human` row's `Reference` names the flagship slice and playthrough segment where the human reviews it, and its `Threshold` reads `human sign-off`. |
| Z2-N-BUDGET-408 | `Zenith2/Docs/Budgets.md`:1398 | Zenith2 Budgets › Production-quality acceptance › QualityMatrix schema | Golden captures live under `Zenith2/Games/<Game>/Acceptance/Goldens/<FeatureID>_<TierID>_<Win\|S24>.png`, are LFS-tracked, and are regenerated only by a ticket that cites the feature ID and records the reason in `## Compatibility`. |
| Z2-N-BUDGET-409 | `Zenith2/Docs/Budgets.md`:1399 | Zenith2 Budgets › Production-quality acceptance › QualityMatrix schema | `Fallback` names the technique used when the tier's technique is unavailable, and `Fallback trigger` names the capability flag, pressure level or thermal state that selects it; a feature with no fallback reads `none` in both cells and its row states why. |
| Z2-N-BUDGET-410 | `Zenith2/Docs/Budgets.md`:1407 | Zenith2 Budgets › Production-quality acceptance › ContentSpecs schema | `Zenith2/Docs/ContentSpecs.md` has exactly these `##` sections, in order: `## Purpose`, `## Asset classes`, `## Texel density and texture budgets`, `## Geometry and LOD complexity`, `## Material complexity`, `## Character classes`, `## Audio`, `## UI`, `## Validation`, `## Change rule`. |
| Z2-N-BUDGET-411 | `Zenith2/Docs/Budgets.md`:1408 | Zenith2 Budgets › Production-quality acceptance › ContentSpecs schema | `## Asset classes` holds `\| Class ID \| Asset class \| Examples \| Owning area \| Slices \|`, with Class IDs of the form `CONTENT_CLASS_<NAME>`. |
| Z2-N-BUDGET-412 | `Zenith2/Docs/Budgets.md`:1409 | Zenith2 Budgets › Production-quality acceptance › ContentSpecs schema | `## Texel density and texture budgets` holds `\| Class ID \| Texel density (texels per metre) \| Maximum texture resolution \| Textures per material \| Windows compression \| S24 compression \| Resident texture memory per instance (MB) \|`, where the compression cells name BC7/BC5/BC4/BC6H on Windows and an ASTC block size on the S24. |
| Z2-N-BUDGET-413 | `Zenith2/Docs/Budgets.md`:1410 | Zenith2 Budgets › Production-quality acceptance › ContentSpecs schema | `## Geometry and LOD complexity` holds `\| Class ID \| LOD0 triangles maximum \| LOD count \| Triangle ratio per LOD step \| LOD screen-size thresholds \| Materials per mesh maximum \| Vertex precision fallbacks allowed \|`. |
| Z2-N-BUDGET-414 | `Zenith2/Docs/Budgets.md`:1411 | Zenith2 Budgets › Production-quality acceptance › ContentSpecs schema | `## Material complexity` holds `\| Class ID \| Texture samples maximum \| Permutations maximum \| Two-sided allowed \| Alpha-tested allowed \| Transparent allowed \|`. |
| Z2-N-BUDGET-415 | `Zenith2/Docs/Budgets.md`:1412 | Zenith2 Budgets › Production-quality acceptance › ContentSpecs schema | `## Character classes` holds `\| Character class ID \| Bones maximum \| Blend shapes maximum \| Animation layers maximum \| IK chains maximum \| LOD0 triangles maximum \| Update-rate reduction tiers \|`, with IDs of the form `CHARACTER_CLASS_<NAME>`, and the W-Characters character (60 bones) is one of its classes. Each class row's content-rule selector in `Zenith2/Docs/ContentSpecs.rules.json` is the `.meta` label `character-<name>` (lower case, `^character-[a-z0-9-]{1,22}$`), the label CHAR's import wizard writes from the game's character project document (`Zenith2/Docs/Design/CHAR/Facial.md` → "Character project settings document"), so CHAR's per-game classes and the ContentSpecs rows meet in one string; CHAR's class presets `hero`, `npc` and `crowd` (`Zenith2/Docs/Design/CHAR/Debugging.md` → "Character class presets") are classes of the table, and the Update-rate reduction tiers cell of a class whose preset forces full rate (the default `hero`) lists tier 1 only, every other class 1, 2, 4, 8. |
| Z2-N-BUDGET-416 | `Zenith2/Docs/Budgets.md`:1413 | Zenith2 Budgets › Production-quality acceptance › ContentSpecs schema | `## Audio` holds `\| Slice \| Real voices maximum \| Virtual voices maximum \| Streamed voices maximum \| Banks \| Bank memory maximum (MB) \|`, one row per flagship slice. |
| Z2-N-BUDGET-417 | `Zenith2/Docs/Budgets.md`:1414 | Zenith2 Budgets › Production-quality acceptance › ContentSpecs schema | `## UI` holds `\| Slice or screen \| UI elements maximum \| Text runs maximum \| Visible glyphs maximum \| Draw batches maximum \|`. |
| Z2-N-BUDGET-418 | `Zenith2/Docs/Budgets.md`:1415 | Zenith2 Budgets › Production-quality acceptance › ContentSpecs schema | Every numeric cell of ContentSpecs.md is checked by the content validation the CONTENT and ASSET areas deliver, at cook time, and a cooked asset that exceeds its class's limit fails the cook (BR-COOK); `## Validation` names the validator for each table. |
| Z2-N-BUDGET-419 | `Zenith2/Docs/Budgets.md`:1416 | Zenith2 Budgets › Production-quality acceptance › ContentSpecs schema | The sum of the ContentSpecs memory figures of a slice's content fits inside the slice's per-subsystem memory rows; the ContentSpecs ticket and the per-system budget tickets for GEOM, ANIM, AUDIO and UI reconcile the two in their allocation tables. |
| Z2-N-BUDGET-420 | `Zenith2/Docs/Budgets.md`:1420 | Zenith2 Budgets › Production-quality acceptance › Flagship slice acceptance package | The three flagship slices (OpenWorldSlice, CinematicSlice, Game2D) are equally binding (Z2-D-021), and each has, written by its Stage 1 flagship acceptance ticket: a playthrough script, a feature checklist mapped to requirement IDs, per-platform budgets, a bug bar, and a human-review sign-off list for its subjective quality items. |
| Z2-N-BUDGET-421 | `Zenith2/Docs/Budgets.md`:1422 | Zenith2 Budgets › Production-quality acceptance › Flagship slice acceptance package | A slice's acceptance package lives under `Zenith2/Games/<Slice>/Acceptance/` as `Playthrough.md` (the human-readable script), `Playthrough.replay` (the input-replay file the Player plays back), `FeatureChecklist.md`, `Budgets.md` (the list of row IDs that apply to the slice, never numbers), `BugBar.md` and `SignOff.md`. |
| Z2-N-BUDGET-422 | `Zenith2/Docs/Budgets.md`:1426 | Zenith2 Budgets › Production-quality acceptance › Playthrough script format | A playthrough script is a scripted walkthrough of 20 to 30 minutes (row "Playthrough script duration"), divided into segments, and plays back deterministically from its replay file on fixed simulation ticks. |
| Z2-N-BUDGET-423 | `Zenith2/Docs/Budgets.md`:1428 | Zenith2 Budgets › Production-quality acceptance › Playthrough script format | `Playthrough.md` holds one table `\| Segment \| Start (mm:ss) \| Duration (s) \| Checkpoint \| Content exercised \| Feature IDs \| Requirement IDs \| Assertions \|`, where `Checkpoint` names the save or checkpoint the segment starts from, so a segment can be replayed alone, and `Assertions` lists the correctness asserts the integration smoke and the acceptance run check at the segment's end. |
| Z2-N-BUDGET-424 | `Zenith2/Docs/Budgets.md`:1429 | Zenith2 Budgets › Production-quality acceptance › Playthrough script format | Every segment is at least the minimum run length, so each segment is a valid measured interval on its own. |
| Z2-N-BUDGET-425 | `Zenith2/Docs/Budgets.md`:1433 | Zenith2 Budgets › Production-quality acceptance › Feature checklist format | `FeatureChecklist.md` holds one table `\| Item \| Requirement IDs \| Feature IDs \| Segment \| Evidence kind \| Evidence \|`, where `Evidence kind` is `automated` (an assert or evidence record), `golden` (a QualityMatrix golden) or `human` (a SignOff.md item), and every requirement the slice's area owners assigned to the slice appears in at least one item. |
| Z2-N-BUDGET-426 | `Zenith2/Docs/Budgets.md`:1437 | Zenith2 Budgets › Production-quality acceptance › Per-platform budgets for a slice | A slice's per-platform budgets are the rows of this document whose Workloads column names the slice's workload or a workload set containing it, plus the QualityMatrix rows of the slice's tiers; the slice's `Budgets.md` lists those row IDs and Feature IDs and never restates a value. |
| Z2-N-BUDGET-427 | `Zenith2/Docs/Budgets.md`:1447 | Zenith2 Budgets › Production-quality acceptance › Bug bar and severity weights | Each slice's bug bar is zero open crash or blocker defects and a severity-weighted open-defect count at or below the slice's cap row. |
| Z2-N-BUDGET-428 | `Zenith2/Docs/Budgets.md`:1449 | Zenith2 Budgets › Production-quality acceptance › Bug bar and severity weights | Defects are BUG tickets on the Z2 board whose body names the slice (or whose parent epic's work the slice exercises) and whose status is not in a DONE category; `S1_CRITICAL` (crash, data loss, or a hard block on progression) counts as a crash or blocker, and the weighted count is `5 × S2_MAJOR + 2 × S3_MINOR + 1 × S4_TRIVIAL`. |
| Z2-N-BUDGET-429 | `Zenith2/Docs/Budgets.md`:1468 | Zenith2 Budgets › Production-quality acceptance › Human sign-off items | `SignOff.md` holds one table `\| Item \| Feature IDs \| Segment \| Platform \| Question the reviewer answers \| Reviewer \| Date \| Verdict \|`, the human reviewer runs the named segment on the named platform, and a slice's acceptance is met only when every item's Verdict reads `accepted`. |
| Z2-N-BUDGET-430 | `Zenith2/Docs/Budgets.md`:1469 | Zenith2 Budgets › Production-quality acceptance › Human sign-off items | Sign-off items cover only subjective quality; anything a golden capture, an assert or an evidence record can check is not a sign-off item. |
| Z2-N-BUDGET-431 | `Zenith2/Docs/Budgets.md`:1470 | Zenith2 Budgets › Production-quality acceptance › Human sign-off items | A sign-off is performed through a `needs-human` ticket, and the human records the verdict in `SignOff.md` in that ticket's diff. |
| Z2-N-BUDGET-432 | `Zenith2/Docs/Budgets.md`:1474 | Zenith2 Budgets › Production-quality acceptance › OpenWorldSlice acceptance | OpenWorldSlice's acceptance package exercises streaming, world authoring, AI and navigation at scale, persistent-world saves, and the Luau quest, dialogue and inventory samples, which are Luau gameplay modules inside the slice and never engine features (Z2-D-077). |
| Z2-N-BUDGET-433 | `Zenith2/Docs/Budgets.md`:1476 | Zenith2 Budgets › Production-quality acceptance › OpenWorldSlice acceptance | OpenWorldSlice's sign-off list includes at least: traversal feel and camera behaviour; streaming pop-in visibility at the traversal speed; lighting and atmosphere over a day/night transition; AI crowd believability; quest and dialogue flow readability; and save/load continuity after a mid-streaming save. |
